# The oracle

melee-hd's simulation is checked against ssbm-rs at the `melee-hd-base` tag, whose ports are verified function by function against the original machine code. The oracle comes before any porting: every ported system is checked by it from its first commit.

## Levels

| Level | Reference | Compares | Status |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Replay events | Slippi replays, which ssbm-rs reproduces | Slippi's pre-frame, post-frame, item and frame-start events, field by field, through the timeline rollback settled on | `melee-oracle record` ([recordings](#recordings)), `melee-oracle compare` |
| Full state | ssbm-rs playing the same replay | The whole state every tick, as the captured-state checks compare it, against ssbm-rs's memory | `melee-oracle diff`: runs from the match's start or a trace tick, stops at the first difference or unported function |
| Sessions | ssbm-rs playing the same recorded session (`--inputs`) | The whole state every tick from power-on, and the controller reads each tick took | `melee-oracle session`: boots from power-on with the recording's reads and stops at the first difference or unported function ([sessions](#sessions)) |
| Captured states | ssbm-rs's lockstep captures (memory at a real call) | A game function run from a real game state on both sides, then the whole state | `melee-capture`: imports a captured state into melee-sim (scheduler, joint trees and everything on them, fighters, the stage) and compares melee-sim's state with memory; round trips on real captures come back clean. leaf-check's `stage` tests run Final Destination's and Battlefield's setup and processes from captures, its `camera` tests every camera function, and its `effects` tests every effect function and spawn every effect id from real states |
| Leaf functions | ssbm-rs's ports | Math, collision geometry and other code whose inputs and outputs map directly | `leaf-check`: the RNG, math, the SDK's and HAL's vectors, matrices and quaternions, collision geometry, stage and environment collision, controllers and rumble, and HSD's scheduler, animation, joints, references, display lists and shapes |

**Replay events.** ssbm-rs plays the 40-replay smoke set to the same recordings as the original code, with documented entry-frame and off-screen-flag differences in 12. melee-hd records a match as Slippi's recording codes do (`game::record`), and `melee-oracle record` compares its recording of a replay's match with the original replay through ssbm-rs's own comparison (`ssbm_slippi::compare`), as [recordings](#recordings) describes. This covers VS matches only, and only what Slippi records.

**Full state.** Replays leave out most of the game: menus, single-player modes, stage objects, the camera and most fighter fields. ssbm-rs's runner, with `STATE_TRACE=FILE`, writes the game's raw state at the end of every engine tick: the RNG seed, the player blocks, the stage, the camera, and every fighter, item and fighter joint. `melee-oracle trace-check TRACE REPLAY.slp` checks a trace against the replay it ran: each frame's post-frame fighter positions and percents must equal the trace's, bit for bit, one tick per frame. Two full smoke replays (Dream Land, 13,779 frames; Pokémon Stadium, 13,671) pass with no difference; a full match's trace is about 14 MB. Slippi's pre-frame data doesn't line up as exactly, because it is sampled partway through a tick. Function-level lockstep doesn't carry over, because melee-hd's data no longer has the original's layout.

With `STATE_TRACE_MEMORY=FROM..TO` (ticks, or `1` for all; trace version 3), each tick also carries main memory: the 4 KiB pages that changed since the tick before, XORed with what they held, every nonzero page at the window's first tick. Version 4 adds the time base at each such tick's end, which the audio timeline starts from when the diff takes up a tick or seeds the boot state. The main thread's stack and the GX FIFO are left out, as neither is game state: since version 5 their bytes are held at zero and the rest of the pages they share is recorded (before, the whole pages were left out, the OS heap descriptors after the FIFO with them). A whole match with memory is about 250 MB in the silent DSP model and about twice that in the sample-accurate one. The oracle's traces are made with Show Player Names off (`SLIPPI_PLAYER_NAMES=0`, a setting Slippi Dolphin has too): that optional playback code draws the players' names over the match and makes HUD objects for them, which melee-hd leaves to the presentation. They're made with ssbm-rs's default, sample-accurate DSP model, which `Settings::from_replay` plays replays with, as Slippi Dolphin recorded them. That doubles their size, because the voices' state in audio memory then changes every tick: the Fox vs Sheik match's is about 525 MB, against 258 MB silent. The Fox vs Sheik Final Destination replay's is made so, from ssbm-rs's checkout:

```text
SLIPPI_PLAYER_NAMES=0 STATE_TRACE=local/traces/v5-mixed/Game_20260129T133652.trc STATE_TRACE_MEMORY=1 \
  target/release/ssbm-run "$SSBM_DISC" --replay Game_20260129T133652.slp --fields 100000 \
  --port all --known local/replays/known-names0/Game_20260129T133652.txt
```

Runs with Show Player Names off take their known divergences from ssbm-rs's `local/replays/known-names0/`, not `known/`. The names' HUD objects are allocated ahead of others, so with names off a few heap addresses land 0x2500 bytes lower. Eight replays' known files record such addresses (in fields like a hitstun word that holds an object's address, never gameplay), and `known-names0/` holds those eight rewritten from names-off runs, plus copies of the other 178. If ssbm-rs's heap layout changes, those eight need regenerating in both folders.

`melee-oracle diff TRACE REPLAY.slp` plays the replay in melee-hd and compares its whole state with the trace's memory after every engine tick, through `melee-capture`'s comparer: the scheduler and everything its GObjs hold (joint trees with their animations, references, display objects and shapes, the fighters and every byte of their animation buffers the model knows, the stage objects), the stage, the camera, HSD's ID table, the audio driver's voices, the player blocks, the pad library, the button maps, the scene loop and its clocks, the VS match's controller, Slippi playback's state, the ruleset and UCF's pad buffer, the items' statics, and the display pass's statics (the current camera, the current and active lights with their slot masks, refraction's users, textures and projection, the background flash). Objects pair afresh each tick by where they are reached from (a dangling pointer pairs nothing, below). It reports the first tick (and its replay frame) that differs with the first 30 fields that do (`--all` for every one), or the first function no port covers (`code::unported`), named with its tick. The trace samples each tick before its frame's display pass, so the diff compares right after the tick and runs the display pass before the next one (`Match::step_replay_tick`, `Match::render`). The archives a started match loaded itself pair with where the original loads them by name (`Match::files`), a fighter's animations wherever its buffer holds them. `--fp hardware|slippi` picks the float mode (`gekko_fp`) the trace was made in: Slippi's for a replay as Slippi Dolphin plays it, the hardware's for a trace made with `--fp hardware`. To look past another part's seam, `--stand-in ADDR[=VALUE]` lets an unported function return (melee-sim's `stubs`), and `--ignore TEXT,...` drops the differences that contain one of the texts. Without options it starts the match from the replay's settings (`Settings::from_replay`), as Slippi's playback codes do, and runs from the trace tick that ran the match's first engine tick. With `--from TICK` it imports the trace's state at the end of that tick through `melee-capture`, compares the import back with the memory it came from, and plays on from there; `--against TICK` compares the import with a later tick's memory instead, which shows the comparison telling states apart. `trace-info`, `trace-dump` and `trace-world` inspect a trace with memory.

**Dangling pointers.** A field can keep pointing at an object after the object is freed (a timer's digits after the timer goes, Samus's beam tip entry after the beam, a fighter's last damage source, a hit victim), and the original's heap may hand the block to a new object while the field still holds its address. So a pointer to an object melee-hd has freed pairs nothing: the object's pairing ends with its free, and the field compares as dangling on both sides, by the address the allocator model gave the object (`Pool::addr`) against the original's word, whatever either side has put there since (`Comparer::compare_handle`). melee-hd's joints, display objects, materials and textures come from the class allocator, whose blocks are reused in another order than the pools' slots, so a slot may be reused, and freed again, at another address while a field still names its earlier object; the diff notes every slot's addresses after each tick (`PastAddrs`), and a field naming a slot compares equal to any address the slot's objects have had. That covers the joints, display objects, materials, textures, generators, GObjs, fighters, items, effects and deferred spawns. A freed object whose address the model doesn't know (one made outside a step) still pairs nothing, and differs only where the original's word is paired with another of melee-hd's objects or is null. Before 2026-10-22 a dangling joint pointer passed only while the original's block was still free and otherwise paired the address with the freed joint, which a later field naming the block's new joint then contradicted (`cpu-l1-luigi-l5-samus-ys`, below). Broken on purpose: a dangling address off by 4 fails `cpu-l1-luigi-l5-samus-ys` in tick 1,815 (a freed item's address) and, with that ignored, in tick 1,830 (a fighter's last damage source); without the slots' earlier addresses, `cpu-bowser-ps` fails in tick 10,680 (`hud time.digits[4]`).

On the Fox vs Sheik Final Destination replay (v5-mixed), the match runs from its start in Slippi's float mode with no stand-in: it compares every one of the trace's 11,928 ticks with no difference and no stale read, the whole audio state and the sound handles the fighters and items keep included (Sheik's needles, the fighters' hitboxes tested against them, her ledge hangs and Vanish among them; from tick 1223, replay frame 1099, Sheik turning into Zelda, `ftCommon_8007EFC8`, Zelda's specials and dress dynamics toggled by her scripts, back into Sheik, the KOs, smash charges and UCF's dashback turning the sleeping fighter's CPU stick, Fox's lasers and the fighters' attacks landing on Sheik's needles and the needles taking the hits, the items in reach of a fighter's grab, an item's tip), to the trace's end. Zelda is out until frame 2302 and again from 8440 to 9025. From tick 296 on, new thrown needles' variables hold words their flight never writes (`xDD4`, `xDDC`, `xDE0`, later `xDD8`), which the block keeps from the held needles it held before (whose owner pointer is the first word); they stay stale (`it::Var`, docs/engine.md), so the comparison leaves them out, and nothing reads them. Taken up from tick 200, play runs the remaining 11,728 ticks with no difference: a new item's unused hitboxes' joints (`x5D4_hitboxes[2]` and `[3]`, `hit.jobj`), words the item never writes and keeps from the freed item its block held before, now import as the live joints they point to. From ticks 120, 400 and 1000 play now reaches the trace's end too: before, it stopped in tick 609 (from 120 and 400) or 1061 (from 1000) on a new item's unused hitbox joint, which is now unknown until written (docs/engine.md). The audio state compares from the first tick because the match's start runs, on the boot state's timeline, the boot's last scene's end (its last frame's render and copy, and the wait for the next retrace), the announcer's bank load, the scene's file loads where the original makes them (the fighters' `PlCo.dat` and their effect files among them) and the music's start. The pad library's queue follows the timeline: the polls the pad alarm queued while the scene set up (14 on this replay, beyond the one the boot state's queue held, which wrap the five-entry queue's `qwrite` from 2 to 1) are queued before the loop's flush, and the first round ticks on the one it leaves. In play the alarm (every 675,000 time base ticks) runs a little faster than video fields (675,675), so every thousand frames or so the scene loop finds a second poll queued by the time it counts them: that round ticks twice and renders once, and for the frames before it a poll waits in the queue. The diff queues each poll the timeline counts and runs the display pass only after a round's last tick. Taken up from any tick, the import compares back with no difference (ticks 120, 200, 400, 1000, 2500 and 5000 checked, the background flash's state and the magnifier's bubbles included), and play goes on: from 9000 and 11000 to the trace's end, and from 2440, 3000, 5000 and 7000 to tick 7649, where a fighter's last damage source (`dmg.x1868_source`) points to a GObj freed before the import, which the import gives a dangling slot rather than a place on the GObj pool's free list (an open import limit), with no difference or stale read before. Before new items' hitbox joints were unknown until written, play from 2440, 5000, 7000 and 11000 stopped sooner (in ticks 2867, 6353, 7166 and 11022) on such a joint. The four-player Final Destination replay with two Foxes (`Game_20260531T174953`, traced the same way) imports and compares back clean at ticks 200 and 6000; before Jigglypuff's port, play stopped at her `ftPr_Init_UnkIntBoolFunc0` in the next tick. The Fox ditto benchmark (`local/bench/fox-fox-fd.slp`, two CPU Foxes on Final Destination) is traced in the hardware's float mode with ssbm-rs's trace-fifo build (`SLIPPI_PLAYER_NAMES=0 STATE_TRACE=local/traces/v5-mixed/fox-fox-fd.trc STATE_TRACE_MEMORY=1 ssbm-run "$SSBM_DISC" --replay local/bench/fox-fox-fd.slp --fields 100000 --port all --fp hardware`, whose replay check shows no divergence). With `--fp hardware` and no stand-in or ignore, it compares every one of the trace's 15,660 ticks with no difference, to its end at replay frame 15536: grabs, throws and held fighters' flinches, the rapid jab, clanks and their recoils, reflected blaster shots, powershields, Fox Illusion's afterimage, taunts, ledge catches, KOs past the side and bottom blast zones and as stars off the top (with their stats), and each respawn on the platform. Taken up from a tick, the import compares back clean, and from tick 2300 play reaches the trace's end (13,360 ticks). It stopped in tick 2356 before: a new item's unused hitbox kept the joint word of the freed item its block held (`Pool::alloc_stale`), a pointer to a joint freed before the tick whose block the original had handed to a new joint, and the import's dangling slot for the freed joint didn't pair with it. That word is never read, so it is now unknown until written (`HitJObj`, docs/engine.md). The three Fox–Marth benchmark matches (`local/bench/fox-marth-{fd,bf,ps}.slp`, CPU Fox against CPU Marth) are traced the same way into `local/traces/v5-mixed/fox-marth-{fd,bf,ps}.trc` (each replay check shows no divergence). Final Destination compares every one of its 15,355 ticks with no difference, to its end: Shield Breaker charged and released, Dolphin Slash from the ground and the air, Marth's shield (his sword sheathed), his sword trail's draws, the common states' item checks over Fox's lasers and Illusion, and his KOs and respawns, with no stale read; an Illusion made in a block a Fox laser left keeps the laser's word in its never-written `xDD8`, which stays stale (docs/engine.md). Battlefield compares every one of its 15,176 ticks with no difference and no stale read, to its end: a laser tipping Marth in tick 3132, and from tick 13,789 a KO off the top that falls toward the screen (`ftCo_800D4780`, DeadUpFall), whose fighter the display pass places from the game camera's view each frame (docs/engine.md). Pokémon Stadium compares every one of its 13,674 ticks with no difference and no stale read, to its end: its transformations load asynchronously, as the original's do without the Slippi ruleset. The match's entry times each fighter's five second-buffer animation loads (`Fighter_Create_Inline2`, out of ARAM): without them the music started an audio frame early there. The tournament stages' Slippi replays are traced the same way as the Fox vs Sheik one (v5-mixed, names off, `--fp slippi`). Yoshi's Story's (`Game_20260129T133446`, Fox vs Sheik) compares every one of its 7,245 ticks with no difference and no stale read, to its end: the Shy Guys' flights (`It_Kind_Heiho` enemy items, without food with items off), the fighters' attacks and Fox's lasers knocking them back and away, their KO bonus and the enemy hits in the players' statistics, Randall's puffs, and UCF's tumble wiggle (`800908F4`), which a fighter used in tick 873. Dream Land's (`Game_20260129T130802`, Fox vs Sheik) compares every one of its 13,851 ticks with no difference and no stale read, to its end: Whispy Woods turning toward the fighters (`ftLib_GetOpponentsDir` with the Slippi ruleset's WhispyBlowDirFix) and blowing, his wind pushing fighters through the fighter code's wind device (`ftColl_GetWindOffsetVec`) and swaying their parts as a wind source, the looping quake while he blows, and the clouds' and stars' flights behind the stage. Fountain of Dreams' (`Game_20260129T214216`, Sheik vs Fox) compares every one of its 9,707 ticks with no difference and no stale read, to its end: the side platforms rising, falling and sinking under the surface on the water's columns, their reflections, the water's reflection camera, which renders the fighters with the magnifier's callback and the stage mirrored below the surface into the stage archive's image (`grIzumi_801CCEA0`), its path-animated lights (whose positions the lights' setup reads, `HSD_LObjSetupInit`), and UCF's shield SDI (`80093294`), which a fighter used in tick 5368. Once a full tick runs, each frame's display pass follows; on these replays its first camera is the magnifier's (`ifMagnify_802FBBDC`), then the game camera's (`fn_800301D0`), both ported.

No replay at hand pairs Ness only with characters ported before him, so the oracle's Ness matches are CPU matches: `melee-bench oracle-codes DIR` writes the Gecko codes for `tools/bench`'s oracle set (`ORACLE_MATCHES`: Ness against Fox on Battlefield and on Pokémon Stadium, against Marth on Final Destination), ssbm-rs's runner records each (`BENCH_CODES=DIR/NAME.ini BENCH_AI=0,1 BENCH_SLP=NAME.slp ssbm-run --fields 31000`, into `local/bench/oracle-chars2/`) and checks it as a replay (no divergence), and it is traced like the Fox ditto into `local/traces/v5-mixed/oracle-ness-{fox-bf,marth-fd,fox-ps}.trc`. With `--fp hardware` each compares every traced tick with no difference and no stale read: Battlefield its 11,088 to the match's end, Final Destination its 17,697 and Pokémon Stadium its 13,615 to the traces' ends at the replays' last frames. They run the bat's forward smash, the yo-yo's up and down smashes, PK Fire and its pillar, PK Flash charged and let go, PK Thunder steered, hitting Ness into his recovery and rebounding off the stage, and the sound objects his scripts' positional sound command makes (`lb::sound_obj`, docs/engine.md).

Jigglypuff's Pokémon Stadium replay against Sheik (`Game_20260129T131206`, in her costume without a hat) is traced like the Fox vs Sheik replay (names off, Slippi's float mode) into `local/traces/v5-mixed/Game_20260129T131206.trc` and compares every one of its 13,732 traced ticks with no difference and no stale read: her double jumps (their rises scaled by her height, `ftCo_800D0CBC`), Pound and Sing in the air, and her sideways flick out of tumble, which UCF's tumble code decides (the table below). Her Final Destination replay against Sheik (`Game_20260524T180613`, in her costume with the bow, traced the same way) compares every one of its 17,575 traced ticks with no difference and no stale read: her hat, loaded with her model (`ftPr_Init_8013C360`: its joint, its DObj list and its variants' lookups, from the costume's archive, kept for the match in `ft_8045A1E0`), shown and hidden with her model's parts, its ribbon's dynamics and its draw on her left leg's matrix; Rollout charged, rolling, turning and ending on the ground and in the air; the shield's SDI nudges UCF's code decides (the table below), and her held item's special release (`ftCommon_8007E82C`).

Samus's oracle matches are CPU matches the same way (`ORACLE_MATCHES`: Samus against Fox on Final Destination and on Pokémon Stadium, against Marth on Battlefield; `local/traces/v5-mixed/oracle-samus-{fox-fd,marth-bf,fox-ps}.trc`). With `--fp hardware` each compares every traced tick with no difference and no stale read: Final Destination its 14,995, Battlefield its 14,276 and Pokémon Stadium its 14,096, to the traces' ends at the replays' last frames. They run the Charge Shot charged, kept, fired and reflected, homing and super missiles, the Screw Attack, the bombs and her own bomb's jump, her rolls as the morph ball, and her grapple beam: shot from her grab and dash grab, its links following the tip, bouncing back, falling, drawn back in, and holding a caught fighter at its tip. On Pokémon Stadium a tip's damage, held back through its hitlag, lands in tick 5998 (`ftColl_8007BE3C`).

The Pokémon Stadium Slippi replay with Ness (`Game_20260129T214824`, traced like the Fox vs Sheik replay), which stopped at his `ftNs_AttackHi4_YoyoCheckEnvColl` in tick 13,821 before his port, compares every one of its 14,212 traced ticks with no difference and no stale read in Slippi's float mode.

Ranked Slippi 3.16 and 3.17 replays on Pokémon Stadium run with the stage frozen, as their code set freezes it (FreezeStadium, the Slippi ruleset's table below): no transformation ever starts and the cycle's timer stays where the setup drew it. Before, melee-hd stopped such a match in the stage's setup. A 3.17 replay of Fox against Dr. Mario (`hf-fox-doc-ps-b5925e97`, `local/replays/hf/doc-diamond-diamond-a6/slp/diamond-diamond-b5925e971387b15a6c9c0141.slp`) is traced as Sarah's replays are; its replay check differs from the recording only in port 2's post-frame offscreen flag (12 frames from frame 928), which BrawlOffscreenDamage keeps out of play online. In Slippi's float mode with no stand-in it compares every one of the trace's 10,673 ticks with no difference and no stale read, with `--heap` too, to the match's end. The Ice Climbers' 3.16 replay against Marth (`hf-ics-marth-ps-5db3312e-rf`, below) compares all its 1,483 traced ticks the same way, and Young Link's 3.17 replay against Fox (`hf-ylink-fox-ps-cfb1b164`) all 12,174. Broken on purpose, the model fails: the cycle run unfrozen fails the Ice Climbers' replay in tick 85, the match's first cycle tick, where its timer counts down from 3781 and the trace's stays; the preload byte set in the setup fails it in tick 1 (`ground.u.stadium.xEC`).

Falco runs Fox's code with his own attributes, sounds and Phantasm. A Slippi replay of Falco against Sheik on Final Destination (`Game_20260129T170722`, the author's replays, with Falco's wall jumps) is traced the same way into `local/traces/v5-mixed/Game_20260129T170722.trc` (replay check clean). In Slippi's float mode with no stand-in, it compares every one of the trace's 8,026 ticks with no difference and no stale read, to the match's end: Falco's creation, lasers, Phantasm, Firebird and reflector, his lasers and Sheik's needles hitting shields (`ftColl_80077688`) and bouncing off them (`lbColl_80007DD8`), and UCF's shield SDI and tumble codes.

Captain Falcon is checked the same way on a Slippi replay of him against Sheik on Final Destination (`Game_20260524T181444`, the author's replays), traced into `local/traces/v5-mixed/Game_20260524T181444.trc` (replay check clean). In Slippi's float mode with no stand-in, it compares every one of the trace's 15,772 ticks with no difference and no stale read: his creation (his costumes have no texture animations, so the list the fighter keeps is null), Raptor Boost, the aerial Falcon Dive and Falcon Kick, a tip's damage held back through its hitlag (`ftColl_8007BE3C`, tick 9496), and his respawn in tick 4874 keying off the scream still playing on his voice track (`ft_80088A50`, which before only handed the frontend the key-offs). His Battlefield replay against Sheik (`Game_20260524T181118`, traced the same way; its replay check shows only the known file's off-screen flag) compares all 11,989 ticks with no difference and no stale read.

Ganondorf runs Captain Falcon's code with his own effects, the Warlock Punch's wind and his knockback hooks. The author's replays have him only in four-player matches on unported stages, so a CPU benchmark match pairs him with Captain Falcon on Final Destination (`data/bench/ganon-captain-fd.slp`, made as [performance](performance.md) describes; its replay check and controllers check show no divergence), traced the same way as the Fox–Fox match into `local/traces/v5-mixed/ganon-captain-fd.trc` (`--fp hardware`; replay check clean). With `--fp hardware` and no stand-in it compares every one of the trace's 12,173 ticks with no difference and no stale read, to its end: his creation and spawns (`ftGn_Init_OnLoad`, `ftCa_Init_OnLoadForGanon`, `ftGn_Init_OnDeath`, and `ftGn_Init_LoadSpecialAttrs` at each spawn), the Warlock Punch, Gerudo Dragon, Dark Dive and Wizard's Foot, and his taunt in tick 5743, whose wind blows from his top joint before the side taunt, entered twice (`ftCo_800DEA28`'s Ganondorf case falls through to the default's). A ranked Slippi 3.16 replay of Ganondorf against Captain Falcon on Final Destination from the `erickfm/melee-ranked-replays` dataset (`local/replays/hf/ganondorf-dd-a5/slp/diamond-diamond-4923c7b4697dd54b247afd7c.slp`) is traced as Sarah's replays are into `local/traces/v5-mixed/hf-ganon-captain-fd-4923c7b4.trc` (its replay check shows only the pre-frame seeds below). In Slippi's float mode with no stand-in it compares every one of the trace's 9,890 ticks with no difference and no stale read, 3.16's Final Destination background included (the scene's timer never runs; playing it as 3.19's makes the stage's timer differ in tick 85).

Luigi is checked on a Slippi 3.19 replay of him against Sheik (and Zelda) on Battlefield (`Game_20260524T175654`, the author's replays), traced as the others are into `local/traces/v5-mixed/Game_20260524T175654.trc` (replay check clean). In Slippi's float mode with no stand-in it compares every one of the trace's 17,336 ticks with no difference and no stale read: his creation and spawns, the Fireball (`it_802C01AC`), the aerial Green Missile through its charge, launch, flight and end, the aerial Super Jump Punch and the Luigi Cyclone on the ground and in the air, which run Mario's Super Jump Punch and Tornado code (`ft::mario::plumber`) with Luigi's attributes. His knockback and item hooks are shared ones. His `PlLgAJ.dat` holds a sub-archive identical byte for byte to one of Mario's `PlMrAJ.dat`; the capture loader's archive scan now prefers the known or named archive when two match (`tools/capture`), where it picked Mario's and played a wrong animation in tick 247. Two ranked Slippi 3.16 replays of Luigi against Ganondorf (`hf-luigi-ganon-fd-6141614c` on Final Destination and `hf-luigi-ganon-bf-779d0533` on Battlefield, from the `erickfm/melee-ranked-replays` dataset, replay checks clean) compare all 11,512 and 16,499 ticks with no difference and no stale read; the Battlefield one needed UCF's Shield Drop Extended (the table below). Mario's and Dr. Mario's code is ported alongside (their specials, the cape, the fireball, the pill and Dr. Mario's taunt). Their ranked replays (`hf-mario-falco-fd-8b2f8e20`, Mario against Falco on Final Destination, and `hf-doc-fox-bf-1651059b`, Dr. Mario against Fox on Battlefield, replay checks clean) compare all 14,516 and 8,751 ticks with no difference and no stale read, past the ceiling techs that stopped them before main's port (`ftCo_800C23A0`, ticks 9,087 and 1,000), and Dr. Mario's cape turning Fox around in the air in tick 4060 (`ftCo_800C3538`, `ftCo_800C3598`: the turn's spin and hitlag, then a push along the hit's angle; the reaction a cape's hit picks keeps the fighter's facing when the cape can't turn it alone, and a grounded fighter's push isn't reached yet). Leaf checks (`leaf-check` `luigi`, `mario`, `drmario`, `pikachu`, `pichu`) run every one of the five characters' callbacks from captured states of their replays (and debug dittos for the moves the replays don't reach) and compare the whole state.

Pikachu is checked on a ranked Slippi 3.16 replay of him against Fox on Battlefield from the `erickfm/melee-ranked-replays` dataset (`local/replays/hf/pikachu-diamond-diamond-a5/slp/diamond-diamond-0892a98876eb85bda044880e.slp`, replay check clean), traced as the others are into `local/traces/v5-mixed/hf-pikachu-fox-bf-0892a988.trc`. In Slippi's float mode with no stand-in it compares every one of the trace's 10,312 ticks with no difference and no stale read: his creation and spawns, the Thunder Jolt thrown from the air (`itPikachuThunderJolt_Spawn`), which ends before it meets the stage, the aerial Skull Bash through its charge, flight and end, and the Quick Attack's zips on the ground and in the air, turned once by the stick, ending on the ground and against surfaces met too squarely. In tick 5270 he is KO'd toward the screen, which runs Slippi's FreezeDeadUpFallPhysics codes (the Slippi ruleset's table below): the fighter's position stays where the KO started, so the blast-zone check that keeps its knockback magnitude reads the same place as the original. Pichu runs Pikachu's code with his own sounds, no Quick Attack sparks, and the self-damage his scripts deal (`ftAction_80072BF4`). His ranked Battlefield replays against Captain Falcon (`hf-pichu-captain-bf-328faec7`) and Ganondorf (`hf-pichu-ganon-bf-2c3a71f0`, replay checks clean) compare all 9,232 and 11,874 ticks with no difference and no stale read, ceiling techs included (from ticks 4,894 and 3,598).

Roy runs Marth's code (`ft::mars`, which tells the two apart in the specials' effects, the neutral special's charge flash and the damage Counter's strike adds) with his own callbacks (`ft::emblem`). Three ranked Slippi 3.16 replays from the `erickfm/melee-ranked-replays` dataset's `ROY_master-master_a6` archive (`local/replays/hf/roy-master-master-a6/slp/`, named by their hashes) are traced as the others are. Against Sheik on Final Destination (`hf-roy-sheik-fd-64324cc1`, replay check clean), in Slippi's float mode with no stand-in, play compares every one of the trace's 12,108 ticks with no difference and no stale read: his creation and spawns (`ftFe_Init_OnLoad` through `ftMs_Init_OnLoadForRoy`, `ftFe_Init_OnDeath`), Double-Edge Dance's swings, the aerial Blazer, and Counter catching a hit and striking back with the caught hit's damage added to his hitboxes. Against Jigglypuff on Fountain of Dreams (`hf-roy-puff-fod-9636cc09`) all 9,531 traced ticks compare the same way, Flare Blade on the ground and in the air and the aerial Counter among them; but there ssbm-rs parts from the recording: from frame 7759, where Roy's down aerial meets Jigglypuff, its replay check shows her percent, then both fighters' positions and action states, differing, and the replay ending at frame 9298 of 9422, so melee-hd follows ssbm-rs where the recorded match went elsewhere. Against Fox on Yoshi's Story (`hf-roy-fox-ys-b0c1d064`, whose replay check differs on Fox's position from frame 5308) play compares its first 9,327 ticks with no difference and no stale read, Flare Blade and the Counter's strike in the air among them, past tick 779, where it stopped on the audio state until the timeline took interrupts between an ARAM load's chunks (docs/engine.md, the timeline). With `--heap` it now compares all 11,512 traced ticks with no difference and no stale read, to the trace's end. It stopped in tick 9328 (frame 9204) before: five Shy Guys appear at once, and one's unused second hitbox held another joint than ssbm-rs's (`x5D4_hitboxes[1].hit.jobj`). The word is the joint pointer of the item its block last held, freed with it; melee-hd's handle and the joint the original's address now holds needn't pair, as melee-hd's joint pool doesn't reuse slots in the original's address order. Item code reads a hitbox's joint only while the box is on, and only the script's hitbox command, which writes the joint, turns a new item's box on, so the word is never read: it is unknown from the item's creation until written (`lb::hitcapsule::HitJObj`, docs/engine.md), and reading it unknown stops. With the item's creation leaving the joints as they were, the diff fails in tick 9328 again; with the command's write forgotten (the joint unknown after it), play stops at the first box's read. **Both recordings are inexact, not ssbm-rs**: replayed in Slippi's playback Dolphin with its resync off (`shouldResync` false), Dolphin writes copies that differ from the originals exactly as ssbm-rs does, from frame 5308 on Yoshi's Story (803 differences) and from frame 7428 on Fountain of Dreams (6,826; Roy's y position first, the percent at 7759 following from it). Both start at the end of a hitlag, where smash DI moves the fighter by 3 times the C-stick: the recorded match moved with a C-stick of 1.0, but the replay records 0.9875 for those frames, so a replay played from its inputs moves 0.0375 short. The cause is the recording format: 3.16's code set snaps a stick near a cardinal to exactly 1.0 (UCF 0.84's code at `Fighter_procInput+0x750`, from the raw input bytes, after the recorder has saved the pre-frame's 0.9875), and playback writes the replay's raw bytes back for the snap to read, but 3.16 replays don't record the raw C-stick, so the C-stick's snap never happens in playback (the main stick's, whose raw bytes are recorded, does). 3.17 added the raw C-stick to the format. A sample of 61 replays from the dataset showed it in 20 of 51 from 3.16 and in none from 3.15 or 3.17. Slippi's playback hides this by resyncing positions, percents, action states and the random seed from the replay every frame; ssbm-rs and melee-hd never do. **Decision: where a replay must also agree with its recording, it is a 3.17 or later or a 3.15 or earlier one;** a 3.16 replay whose first difference from its recording is a cardinal smash DI's ±3.0 at the end of a hitlag is this, and stays a comparison with ssbm-rs only. melee-hd's own `.slp` output will record the raw C-stick, as 3.17's format does. Both replays stay comparisons with ssbm-rs, the reference, and neither is evidence about the console past those frames. The captured-state check (`leaf-check` `roy`, sharing `marth`'s, `support/mslike.rs`) runs Marth's functions and Roy's from Roy's states in the three replays, and his item hooks from a debug VS ditto.

Peach is checked on a Slippi 3.19 replay of her against Sheik (and Zelda) on Battlefield (`Game_20260129T133041`, the author's replays), traced as the others are into `local/traces/v5-mixed/Game_20260129T133041.trc` (replay check clean). In Slippi's float mode with no stand-in it compares every one of the trace's 13,850 ticks with no difference and no stale read: her creation and spawns, the float and its aerials, the forward smash's frying pan, the vegetable pull (`spawnVeg`: a turnip's face by its weighted odds, `it_802BD32C`), held turnips thrown on the ground and in the air, smash throws among them (`checkItemThrow`), and dropped by a hit (`Fighter_8006CDA4`), the turnips' flight, bounces and life, and the up special: the parasol made in her hand, opened by her script (`ftAction_80072894`, `ftCommon_8007E83C`), closed when her state changes, and put away when she leaves it, the Peach Bomber on the ground and in the air, and Toad in the air (`ftPe_SpecialAirN_Enter`, where the comparison stopped in tick 12,377 before Toad's port): Toad made in her hand by her script, shown on his seventh frame, the jump's lift, and Toad gone once the move ends in a fall. A turnip's flag byte holds bits it never writes (docs/engine.md), which stopped the comparison in tick 6151 until only its own bit was compared. leaf-check's `peach` runs every ported function of her units, her items' (the turnip, the parasol, the Bomber's explosion, Toad and his spores), and the common pickup, throw and parasol states' (224 functions) from captured states, as saved and from their units' neighbours with the controls and script flags changed, and compares the whole state. Its states come from that replay and, for Toad's counter and spores, which that replay's one Toad never reaches, from a ranked Slippi 3.16 replay of Peach against Jigglypuff on Battlefield (`erickfm/melee-ranked-replays`' `PEACH_diamond-diamond_a5`, `diamond-diamond-0d59ba0a849753b895df8410`, in `local/replays/hf/peach-dd-a5/slp/`, replay check clean: four counters and 13 spores, one of which hits Jigglypuff). The archive's 3.14 and 3.15 replays (`PEACH_diamond-diamond_a1`) run UCF 0.8 (below), which melee-hd and the capture importer now play.

Her pull's other items, Mr. Saturn (`itdosei.c`), the Beam Sword (`itsword.c`) and the Bob-omb, are checked on eight of those 3.14 and 3.15 replays (`local/replays/hf/peach-dd-a1/slp/`, named by their hashes), traced as the others are into `local/traces/v5-mixed/` (replay checks clean, but for Final Destination's pre-frame seeds on `hf-peach-luigi-fd-0b7737d0`, as on the Ganondorf vs Captain Falcon replay below). In Slippi's float mode with no stand-in each compares every traced tick with no difference and no stale read:

- Against Fox on Battlefield (`hf-peach-fox-bf-e3e4bb7e`, 8,929 ticks): a Mr. Saturn pulled (sized as she is, `it_802BD4AC`), held, thrown, hit (`itDosei_Logic7_DmgReceived`: a hop back, its life a second shorter), standing dazed, walking, turning at ledges and walls where it last stood, falling off them, and picked up and thrown by Fox (`pl_8003E854`).
- Against Jigglypuff on Battlefield (`hf-peach-puff-bf-433f0fe4`, 12,114 ticks), Samus on Battlefield (`hf-peach-samus-bf-51fb8650`, 15,055: three Mr. Saturns, and a Bob-omb pulled, thrown at her size, `xDE8`, and exploding) and Fox on Final Destination (`hf-peach-fox-fd-2483cee7`, 5,079): more of the same, Mr. Saturn hit as it falls and picked up by Jigglypuff.
- Against Luigi on Battlefield (`hf-peach-luigi-bf-f05a237d`, 15,777 ticks): a Beam Sword pulled, swung on the ground (`ftCo_Attack_800CCF58`: the swing's state at `PlCo.dat`'s speed, the blade grown and eased back by her script, `ftAction_800729D4`, and its trail following bone 5, `ftCo_800C2FD8`, drawn by the display pass), thrown, lying, and picked up by Luigi.
- Against Samus on Final Destination (`hf-peach-samus-fd-f9a92433`, 12,232 ticks): a sword thrown three times and picked up again in between.
- Against Luigi on Final Destination (`hf-peach-luigi-fd-0b7737d0`, 7,788 ticks): a Bob-omb pulled and exploding in her hand, its blast's hits counted (`pl_80041B08`, which looks the Bob-omb up in the hit player's list), then a sword.
- Against Marth on Final Destination (`hf-peach-marth-fd-dc42c8bb`, 13,249 ticks): a sword pulled, thrown, lying and picked up by Marth, and in tick 8447 a held fighter knocked away as its holder is hit (`ftCo_800DCFD4`, below).

A 3.14 replay of the same archive against Fox on Battlefield (`hf-peach-fox-bf-03dd468e`, below) now compares all its 11,632 ticks, past the item caught in the air that stopped it. No replay at hand has Mr. Saturn skid off a slope (none of the tournament stages has one steep enough), stand, turn from the screen and walk after a spawn of its own (states 7 to 10, which only the item switch's drops reach), or meet a shield or a reflector, or a player pick up or catch a Bob-omb (which lists it for its hits, `pl_800419AC`). leaf-check's `pulls` runs every function of Mr. Saturn's and the sword's units, the sword's swings and the item hooks they call (`ftCommon_8007E79C`, `ftCommon_8007E7E4`), and the statistics a pickup and a Bob-omb's hit count, from captured states of seven of these replays, as saved and from their units' neighbours with the controls, Mr. Saturn's counters, facing and floor and the fighter's floor changed, and the pickups made Bob-ombs with the player's list of Bob-ombs filled, and compares the whole state (103 functions); leaf-check's `items` drops a sword at a point (`itSword_Spawn`, which only the item switch's drops call) and runs its trail's widths, look and joint from the item corpus's swords. The Home-Run Bat's render callback is in the sword's unit (`it_80284E10`, `it_803F1418`'s entry for kind 11): melee-sim dispatches it, but nothing ported makes a bat in a match, so it stays unclaimed. Each port broken on purpose fails a check: Mr. Saturn's hit taking 59 frames of life instead of 60 fails `hf-peach-fox-bf-e3e4bb7e` in tick 1515 and `pulls`; its pull not sized as Peach (the turnip spawn's `xDE0`) fails that replay in tick 1319; the swing's kind stored one off, the trail 10.0 times the blade instead of 10.03, and the script's blade grown by 1/128 instead of 1/256 each fail `hf-peach-luigi-bf-f05a237d` in tick 6213 or after, and `pulls` or `items`; Mr. Saturn's steps paced by a quarter of the floor's slope instead of a half, its spawn's turn starting at π/4, its skid's uphill test flipped, the swing's collision not falling, the Bob-omb's hit marking the wrong bit, the list's ring wrapping at 3 and a pickup leaving `xCA8` each fail `pulls` alone (flat floors, unreached states and an empty list hide them from the diffs). Two breaks change nothing anywhere: every sword swing's speed in `PlCo.dat` is 1.0, so reading the next one is the same, and a swing's collision leaving the ground without handing the item back is undone by the fall's own state change.

Link is checked on two ranked Slippi 3.17 replays of him on Final Destination from the `erickfm/melee-ranked-replays` dataset's `LINK_diamond-diamond_a6` archive (`local/replays/hf/link-dd-a6/slp/`, named by their hashes), traced as the others are: against Fox (`hf-link-fox-fd-037e45f5`) and against Marth (`hf-link-marth-fd-51a2bdce`). Their replay checks differ from the recording only where ssbm-rs's playback is known to (the random seed before the match and at frame 3877, the state flags' offscreen bit, and an item's position's low bits for twenty frames from frame 6071 against Fox, which ssbm-rs's interpreter shows without its ported code too). In Slippi's float mode with no stand-in, play compares every one of the Fox replay's 13,009 ticks to the match's end and every one of the Marth replay's 14,814 traced ticks with no difference and no stale read: his creation and spawns (the optional part his `ftData` names hung on his model, `ftParts_800753D4`), his shield's absorb, the down aerial's bounce, the second forward smash swing (`ftattacks4combo.c`), the Spin Attack on the ground and in the air, bombs pulled, held, thrown, dropped, rolling, knocked about and exploding, the boomerang thrown at the stick's angle, homing back and caught, with its two trails, the bow drawn and arrows loosed, flying, sticking and skidding along shields, and the hookshot: the grab's and dash grab's shot, its catch and miss, and the air grab latching on a wall, pulling him up and letting him hang. Items he catches in the air (`fn_800D6F58`) and picks up from the ground (`ftpickupitem_80094694`, light items only) run their common code. His bomb's flag byte holds bits it never writes, and his boomerang's throw writes a rotation whose w is never set (docs/engine.md). leaf-check's `link` runs every ported function of his units and his items' (the bomb, the boomerang, the hookshot, the arrow and the bow; 227 functions) from captured states of both replays, as saved and from their units' neighbours with the controls and script flags changed, and compares the whole state; `item_render` runs the boomerang's and the arrow's render callbacks from their captures in each pass, their trees sometimes dirty, and compares the whole state, which checks the item's own drawing; their trails' drawing leaves no state to compare. Young Link runs this code too, and `link`'s check runs from `support/lklike.rs`, which `young_link` shares.

Young Link runs Link's code (`ft::link`, whose code and items branch on the kind for the hookshot's and the arrows' sounds and the fire arrow's flame), with his own creation, his shield's absorb with its own sound and his side taunt, a drink of milk (`ft::clink`, the milk an item, `it::kinds::clinkmilk`). He is checked on three ranked Slippi 3.17 replays from the `erickfm/melee-ranked-replays` dataset's `YLINK_diamond-diamond_a6` archive (`local/replays/hf/ylink-dd-a6/slp/`, named by their hashes), traced as the others are, whose replay checks are clean: against Sheik on Final Destination (`hf-ylink-sheik-fd-bd2c97c0`), Marth on Battlefield (`hf-ylink-marth-bf-91d55df5`) and Fox on Dream Land (`hf-ylink-fox-dl-25f1c7b3`). In Slippi's float mode with no stand-in, play compares every one of their 14,991, 13,255 and 11,089 traced ticks with no difference and no stale read, to the traces' ends: his creation (wall jumps, the milk's article) and spawns, the shield's absorb set up as he waits and crouches, bombs, boomerangs, fire arrows and the hookshot's grabs and tether among them. The Battlefield replay stopped in tick 11,243 on a new hookshot link's `x20`, which only the links' reset writes and the hookshot never does, so a link in a block new from the heap keeps the heap's bytes (docs/engine.md); it is unknown until written now. A fourth 3.17 replay, against Fox on Pokémon Stadium (`hf-ylink-fox-ps-cfb1b164`), compares all 12,174 ticks with the stage frozen (below). None of the archive's 3.17 replays has his taunt, so a 3.16 replay against Sheik on Fountain of Dreams with the archive's one taunt (`hf-ylink-sheik-fod-d89ff958`) is traced too: play compares all 16,010 ticks the same way, the milk made, shown, scaled and let go of among them. Its replay check differs from the recording from frame 14,390, where Sheik leaves a hitlag with the C-stick recorded at 0.9875 on the x axis: 3.16's C-stick snap (above), so it stays a comparison with ssbm-rs. leaf-check's `young_link` runs Link's functions as he runs them, his own and the milk's (248 functions) from captured states of the four replays and of a match against Link (below), as saved and from their units' neighbours with the controls and script flags changed, and compares the whole state; `young_link_item_render` runs his boomerang's and fire arrow's render callbacks as `item_render` runs Link's. Link's item hooks, which his own replace, run only in `link`.

Link and Young Link in one match keep two sets of hookshot lengths (docs/engine.md). None of the local Link and Young Link sets has the matchup, so the dataset's `YLINK` archives of the diamond-diamond, master-master, master-diamond and master-platinum pairs were scanned for it (2,131 replays): 16 replays of Link against Young Link are in `local/replays/hf/link-ylink/slp/`. One, a 3.16 replay on Final Destination with both hookshots out at once and air grabs by both (`hf-link-ylink-fd-fbeda3fb`, Link on port 1), is traced as the others are; its replay check differs from the recording only in the random seed before the match and at frames 3877 and 7877, and Link's offscreen bit at frames 9564, 9599 and 9600, as ssbm-rs's playback is known to. In Slippi's float mode with no stand-in, `--heap`, play compares all 11,752 traced ticks with no difference and no stale read. The match can't tell one shared set from two: the archives hold the ground grab's lengths at height 1, so a hookshot reading the other character's lengths differs only while the other's last spawn was an air grab, and in none of the 16 replays does either grab while the other's air grab is out; with melee-sim's spawn writing one entry for both kinds, the diff stays clean. Three capture sets of the match in `young_link`'s directory check it: `hf-link-ylink-fd-fbeda3fb` (798 calls of every function `ylink-funcs.txt` lists), `hf-link-ylink-fd-fbeda3fb-air` (the hookshot's and the grabs' functions, `hookshot-funcs.txt`, from field 7180: Young Link's air grab at frame 7613, while Link's lengths are his ground grab's) and `hf-link-ylink-fd-fbeda3fb-grab` (every call of the grab's hookshot frames, `fn_800D8EC8`, from field 7900: Young Link's next grab writing over his air grab's lengths). All compare clean. With the importer reading only the first registered hookshot article, as it did, a call of `itLinkhookshot_UnkMotion8_Anim` in Young Link's air grab differs (the tip's spin and scale from 10 links instead of 15); with one entry for both kinds, his grab's spawn (`fn_800D8EC8`, call 7) leaves his air grab's lengths where memory holds the ground's.

Yoshi (`ft::yoshi`, with his victims' capture and egg in `ft::co::yoshi_egg` and his items in `it::kinds::yoshieggthrow` and `it::kinds::yoshistar`) is checked on six ranked Slippi replays from the `erickfm/melee-ranked-replays` dataset's `YOSHI_diamond-diamond_a6` archive (`local/replays/hf/yoshi-dd-a6/slp/`, named by their hashes), traced as the others are. Four are 3.17: against Falco on Final Destination (`hf-yoshi-falco-fd-1394844d`) and Battlefield (`hf-yoshi-falco-bf-3a068da7`), Sheik on Battlefield (`hf-yoshi-sheik-bf-1a63c5bc`) and Captain Falcon on Fountain of Dreams (`hf-yoshi-captain-fod-43843c48`). Their replay checks differ from the recording only in the post-frame hitstun word while it holds a heap address (above) and, on Fountain of Dreams, in the low bit of the fighters' heights as they enter, before the match's first frame. The archive's few 3.17 replays lack the ground pound's landing, so two 3.16 replays against Falco on Battlefield with clean replay checks are traced too (`hf-yoshi-falco-bf-22f665d4`, `hf-yoshi-falco-bf-86016647`). In Slippi's float mode with no stand-in, play compares every one of their 9,770, 11,694, 16,075, 12,235, 13,256 and 12,115 traced ticks with no difference and no stale read, to the traces' ends: his creation and spawns, the flutter jump's armor and turn, the egg shield (its texture cracking with its health, its stun, powershields, and the rolls and spot dodge out of it), the tongue grab's pull, the egg lay swallowing a fighter and the victim's egg (mashed, hit and broken out of), the egg throw's angle and power, the ground pound with its stars, and the egg roll on the ground and in the air, turning and ending. The Sheik replay first stopped in tick 205 on the shield stun's animation frame: Yoshi's stun names an animation his file lacks, so the frame the state change stores is the float register the animation step's walk over his model leaves, the egg shield's texture animation's length (docs/engine.md); his creation also writes that length into his loaded attributes. leaf-check's `yoshi` runs every ported function of his units, his items' and the common code's Yoshi parts (213 functions) from captured states of the six replays, as saved and from their groups' neighbours with the controls and script variables changed, and compares the whole state; `yoshi_shield_stun` runs the shield stun's entries against the original's machine code, since ssbm-rs's ports at the tag store 0 for that frame. No replay at hand has items, so his item hooks, the tongue catching an item and the egg that item becomes (`ityoshitongue.c`, `ityoshiegglay.c`) are ported but unchecked.

Donkey Kong is checked on eight ranked Slippi replays from the `erickfm/melee-ranked-replays` dataset's `DK_diamond-diamond_a6` archive (`local/replays/hf/dk-dd-a6/slp/`, named by their hashes), traced as the others are into `local/traces/v5-mixed/`: five 3.17 replays, against Samus on Battlefield (`hf-dk-samus-bf-9da5c633`, replay check clean) and on Fountain of Dreams (`hf-dk-samus-fod-c9478604`), Luigi on Yoshi's Story (`hf-dk-luigi-ys-3d2fd655`) and Jigglypuff on Fountain of Dreams (`hf-dk-puff-fod-6857ef78`, `hf-dk-puff-fod-b8c2bba2`), and, for the burying that none of those has, a 3.16 replay against Falco on Yoshi's Story with the archive's one bury among its pairings of ported characters (`hf-dk-falco-ys-a1896f12`). Their replay checks differ from the recordings only at the entry (the state flags' offscreen bit, and a byte of one fighter's position at frame -118 or -113), which Slippi's playback Dolphin with its resync off shows too. In Slippi's float mode with no stand-in, and with `--heap`, play compares every one of their 11,934, 11,154, 11,634, 15,419, 12,236 and 8,151 traced ticks with no difference and no stale read, to the traces' ends: his creation (the cargo walks' animation lengths written into his attributes, docs/engine.md) and spawns, the Giant Punch's swings charging it, stored with the shield, let go later and at full charge with its glow, on the ground and in the air, the Headbutt on the ground and in the air, the Spinning Kong on the ground and in the air, the Hand Slap's hitboxes walked along the floor, and the cargo carry: his forward throw lifting the victim onto his shoulders (`ftCo_8009B56C`), the cargo wait, walks, turn, knee bend, jump, fall and landing with the victim following in its shouldered states, and the cargo throws forward, back, up and down on the ground and up and down in the air (`ftCo_CargoThrow.c`, the victim thrown as `ThrownFF` to `ThrownFLw`); and from tick 4646 the Headbutt burying Falco (`ftCo_800C0D0C`): sinking, waiting, mashing and jumping free, the bury effect following the floor (`ftCo_800C0FCC`). The Samus Battlefield replay stopped in tick 2070 on a falling Samus bomb that lost its hitbox landing on the left platform: melee-hd's bomb landed through `it_8026E15C`, which turns the hitboxes off, where the original's `itSamusbomb_UnkMotion1_Coll` lands through `it_8026E248`, which keeps them. Two more 3.17 replays, against Fox on Yoshi's Story (`hf-dk-fox-ys-3465640f`, with up throws carried and thrown in the air) and Jigglypuff on Fountain of Dreams (`hf-dk-puff-fod-28fd506a`, the archive's only cargo back throw), compare every one of their 11,934 and 17,221 traced ticks the same way, but their recordings can't be reproduced: ssbm-rs's replay check, its interpreter without ports and Slippi's playback Dolphin with its resync off all part from them alike (8,221 and 2,036 differences, the first where Donkey Kong is thrown, by Fox's up throw at frame 202 and Jigglypuff's down throw at frame 928, in his position's low bits), so they stay comparisons with ssbm-rs. leaf-check's `donkey` runs his functions, the cargo, shouldered and buried states' and the throws his cargo throws run (183 functions) from captured states of the eight replays, as saved and from their units' neighbours with the controls, script flags, mash timers and state variables changed, and compares the whole state. No replay at hand takes a cargo throw off a ledge (`ftCo_8009C170`), drops through a platform carrying (`ftCo_8009C540`), lets a carried fighter mash free (`ftCo_8009C744`) or hits a carrier too lightly to drop its cargo (the cargo hurt wait, `ftCo_CargoWait2_*`), so those are checked from changed states only. His down special's second end (state 386) is entered by no code (`ledger/dead.txt`), and his heavy-item states need crates or barrels, which item-free matches never spawn, so they aren't ported. Each port broken on purpose fails a check: in `donkey`, the punch's damage per swing or lunge off by one, its swings capped one later, the store on A instead of the shield, the aerial Headbutt's speed multiplied instead of divided or its gravity taken before the script's flag, the Spinning Kong leaving a jump or falling at half gravity, a slap asked for with A, the slaps' hitboxes spaced at 1.25 or raised by 3, the cargo throw's sides swapped, the knee bend's or the turn's countdown off by a frame, the cargo walks' frames and speeds swapped, the carried fighter's mash timer or mash wrong, the bury's sink upside down, the jump free twice as fast, the glow's color animation one off, the Headbutt's state flags off the ground missing a bit and the attribute copy without the walks' lengths; in the diffs, the forward throw not carrying fails the Samus Battlefield replay in tick 284, the bomb landing through `it_8026E15C` again fails it in tick 2070, and a ground hit not burying fails the Falco replay in tick 4646. Creation's write of the walks' lengths changes nothing on its own: every spawn copies the attributes again (`ftDk_Init_LoadSpecialAttrs`, which `donkey` checks) before a cargo walk reads them.

Bowser (`ft::koopa`, with Koopa Klaw's victim's states in `ft::co::capture_koopa` and his flame in `it::kinds::koopaflame`) is checked on six ranked Slippi replays from the `erickfm/melee-ranked-replays` dataset's `BOWSER_diamond-diamond_a6` archive (`local/replays/hf/bowser-dd-a6/slp/`, named by their hashes), traced as the others are. Five are the archive's every 3.17 replay: against Captain Falcon on Dream Land (`hf-bowser-captain-dl-63df8613`), Fountain of Dreams (`hf-bowser-captain-fod-a874ea97`) and Pokémon Stadium (`hf-bowser-captain-ps-1dd99c3c`, the stage frozen, below), and against Sheik (`hf-bowser-sheik-ys-63ee0a54`) and Jigglypuff (`hf-bowser-puff-ys-f471526f`) on Yoshi's Story. Their replay checks are clean on Dream Land and Pokémon Stadium and differ elsewhere only before the match's first frame (the post-frame offscreen flag at frame -122, and the low bit of a fighter's height as it enters, as on Yoshi's Fountain of Dreams replay). Their players start the fire breath, Koopa Klaw and Bowser Bomb only in the air, so a 3.16 replay against Fox on Yoshi's Story with the grounded starts is traced too (`hf-bowser-fox-ys-75580e94`, whose replay check differs only in that pre-match flag). In Slippi's float mode with no stand-in, play compares every one of their 10,012, 11,213, 12,414, 12,170, 11,934 and 10,612 traced ticks with no difference and no stale read, with `--heap` too, to the traces' ends: his creation and spawns (his super armor, `dmg.armor0`, which the common damage code reads, and a full tank of fuel), the fire breath on the ground and in the air, its flames sized and sped by the fuel left, which each flame spends and every frame outside the breath refills (`ftData_UnkMotionStates3`), the flames' random angles and their turns along the surfaces they meet, Koopa Klaw catching in the air and landing with its catch, its bites, its forward throws on the ground and in the air and its back throw on the ground, its victim fixed to his model, flinching, shaken by the stick and mashing out, Whirling Fortress on the ground and in the air with its shell's intangible hurtboxes and its lean, and Bowser Bomb from the ground and the air with its fall's and landing's effects. leaf-check's `bowser` runs every ported function of his units, his flame's and the Klaw's victim's states and the throws' `ft_800850B4` (171 functions) from captured states of the six replays, as saved and from their groups' neighbours with the controls, script variables, bite and fuel changed, and compares the whole state. Thirty-one of them no replay at hand reaches and run only from their neighbours' states: the Klaw started on the ground, its hold without a bite, its back throw in the air (and its victim's thrown state), its switches between the ground and the air and off ledges, the aerial breath's end, and the flame reflected, absorbed, clanked or bounced off a shield. Nothing enters the victim's states 278 and 283 (the catch goes straight to the flinch), so their callbacks are in `ledger/dead.txt`. No replay at hand has items, so his item hooks are ported but unchecked. Each port broken on purpose fails `bowser`: the breath's flame drawn from the wrong angle group, spending two fuel a frame, its smaller sound at half the fuel instead of a third, a flame mirrored the wrong way, its speed's random range from the top, its full speed taken from the size's, its hitbox scaled by its base scale twice and its turn along a surface at 0.03 instead of 0.02; the refill's size step doubled; the bite's damage from the wrong attribute, the stick's throw both ways forward, the back throw turned by the wrong flag, the victim's timer drained by the mash's worth, its shake's height by the stick's x, its hold's grab immunity one bit short, its mash timer from the wrong attribute, the thrown state's switch losing its kept flags or its frame; the fortress steered at its top speed as acceleration, falling at its gravity's place, its shell's hurtboxes merely off; the bomb's fall from the wrong speed, its air start keeping the wrong share of its rise, and its landing's effect; and `ft_800850B4` along the fighter's facing. Two breaks change nothing anywhere: his attributes put the flame's offset at 0 ahead and 0 up (`x24`, `x28`), so mixing them up is the same, and the fuel's speed and size refill at the same 0.7 a frame (`x8`, `xC`).

Mewtwo (`ft::mewtwo`, with Confusion's victim's capture and throw in `ft::co::capture_mewtwo`, the bound state Disable leaves a grounded fighter in in `ft::co::damage_bind`, and his items in `it::kinds::mewtwoshadowball` and `it::kinds::mewtwodisable`) is checked on six ranked Slippi replays from the `erickfm/melee-ranked-replays` dataset (named by their hashes), traced as the others are. Five are 3.17, from the `MEWTWO_diamond-diamond_a6` archive (`local/replays/hf/mewtwo-dd-a6/slp/`): against Marth on Pokémon Stadium (`hf-mewtwo-marth-ps-3605b444`), Fox on Pokémon Stadium (`hf-mewtwo-fox-ps-eef2a498`) and Battlefield (`hf-mewtwo-fox-bf-c0c596d7`), Ganondorf on Battlefield (`hf-mewtwo-ganon-bf-6c208982`) and Falco on Final Destination (`hf-mewtwo-falco-fd-1690697d`). None of that archive's 3.17 replays has his forward throw, which fires a full Shadow Ball, so a 3.14 one against Pikachu on Fountain of Dreams from the `MEWTWO_master-master` archives (`local/replays/hf/mewtwo-mm/slp/`, `hf-mewtwo-pikachu-fod-aaf066d1`) is traced too. Their replay checks differ from the recording only in the state flags' offscreen bit against Ganondorf, and on Fountain of Dreams in the low bit of the fighters' heights as they enter, before the match's first frame, and in the post-frame hitstun word while it holds a heap address (above). In Slippi's float mode with no stand-in, play compares every one of their 22,863, 10,012, 15,718, 14,877, 15,236 and 9,291 ticks with no difference and no stale read, to the traces' ends (the Fountain of Dreams match to its end): his creation and spawns, his tail's swing, his floating double jump (Peach's code), Shadow Ball charged on the ground and in the air up to full, kept between uses with its glow, fired with its recoil, put away with a shoulder, the ball's wobble as it flies, its bursting on the stage, bouncing off a shield and reflected back, and the forward throw's ball; Confusion's rise in the air, its reflector, and its grab turning the victim to face his way and throwing it, on the ground and in the air; Teleport along the floor and through the air at the stick's angle, into a helpless fall or a landing; and Disable's flash binding Ganondorf on the ground (`ftCo_800C4550` and the bound state's callbacks) and knocking Fox, hit in the air, into a tumble. leaf-check's `mewtwo` runs every ported function of his units, his items', Confusion's and Disable's victims' and the common code's Mewtwo parts (182 functions) from captured states of the six replays, as saved, swept over the moments few saved calls fall on (a charge level reached, a charged release, the forward throw's shot, the ball's wobble turning, Teleport's landing), and from their groups' neighbours with the controls, the script variables, the charge and the reflector changed, and compares the whole state. His creation and `ftAnim_8006DF0C`'s hip offset (which his creation's `x2221_b2` turns on, and which runs in the states that bring his hip to his model's root, `x2226_b2`) are the oracle's; no replay at hand has items, so his item hooks (empty) are ported but unchecked, and Kirby's copy of the ball (`It_Kind_Kirby_MewtwoShadowBall`) isn't ported.

Mr. Game & Watch (`ft::gamewatch`, with his ten items in `it::kinds::gamewatch*` and what they share, their variables, spawn and colors, in `it::kinds::gamewatch`) is checked on six ranked Slippi replays from the `erickfm/melee-ranked-replays` dataset's `GAMEANDWATCH_diamond-diamond_a6` and `_a5` archives (`local/replays/hf/gnw-dd-a6/slp/`, `gnw-dd-a5/slp/`, named by their hashes), traced as the others are. Five are 3.17, picked for his specials: against Falco on Pokémon Stadium (`hf-gnw-falco-ps-64a54b19`: Oil Panic catching Falco's lasers, the full bucket's oil thrown on the ground and in the air, and the rapid jab), Jigglypuff on Fountain of Dreams (`hf-gnw-puff-fod-2be1b6a8`: Judgement's 3, 4 and 5 on the ground and in the air, and Chef), Zelda on Yoshi's Story (`hf-gnw-zelda-ys-4e9d13cb`: Judgement's 6 and 7), Fox on Final Destination (`hf-gnw-fox-fd-2264f2d2`: Judgement's 8, Chef in the air) and Falco on Battlefield (`hf-gnw-falco-bf-e43c32ae`: Judgement's 9); the archives' 3.17 replays catch little with Oil Panic, so a 3.16 one against Falco on Final Destination with seven catches on the ground and in the air is traced too (`hf-gnw-falco-fd-7b66bba4`, its replay check clean). The others' replay checks are clean but on Fountain of Dreams (the low bit of the fighters' heights as they enter) and Yoshi's Story (the post-frame offscreen flag at frames -122 and -121), both before the match's first frame. In Slippi's float mode with no stand-in, play compares every one of their 11,634, 11,336, 14,634, 11,393, 9,773 and 10,432 traced ticks with no difference and no stale read, with `--heap` too, to the traces' ends (Yoshi's Story's to the match's end): his creation (his flat model's depth, its color and outline, which his items take, `it_8027CE64`, and draw around themselves in the camera's outline passes, `it_8026EECC`) and spawns, the jab's and rapid jab's sprayer, the down tilt's manhole and its gust, the forward smash's torch, the aerials' parachute, turtle and Sparky kept through their landings, Chef's sausages (one of five but the last two, at random, each with its own throw, spin and fall, turning back off walls), Judgement's roll by the attributes' weights (never either of the last two numbers), its hammer showing its number, and its air lift once per landing, Fire's trampoline under him, the throw angled once by the stick and the fall helpless after, and Oil Panic's bucket: its absorber taking energy projectiles (`ftColl_8007925C`'s absorb, which fills the absorber's tally), its fill on his model, the full bucket's glow and its oil's damage from what it caught. Judgement 7's food spawns nothing with items off (`it_8028FAF4`), so the food itself isn't ported. His trampoline's animation destroys it and then asks for its destroy again, which the port runs as the original does ([the engine notes](engine.md#objects-and-identity)). leaf-check's `game_and_watch` runs every ported function of his units, his items' and the common code his moves reach anew (the rapid jab's start and loop with his end, `ftCo_800D6B00` and `ftCo_800D6C60`, and the fighter library's accessors his items read) — 282 functions — from captured states of the six replays (`local/calls/gamewatch`), as saved and from their groups' neighbours with the controls, script variables, Judgement's and Chef's last numbers, the bucket's fill and damage and the states' own variables changed, and compares the whole state; Judgement's 1 and 2, which no replay at hand rolls, run from those changed states, as does Oil Panic's loop back (`ftGw_SpecialLw_UpdateAction`, B held to frame 38). The importer takes a next process the running one freed (his take-damage hook destroying his torch mid-process) as none: the scheduler reads the next one again once the running process returns. No replay at hand has items, so his item hooks are ported but unchecked. Each port broken on purpose fails `game_and_watch`: Judgement's roll leaving out only the last number, its air lift only after the first, and the up aerial's landing with its own lag rather than the back aerial's; Chef's draw leaving out only the last sausage, the sausage thrown ahead whichever way he faces, its wall bounce from another attribute, and its loop a frame early; the oil's damage multiplier from its bonus, the bucket a third full showing two thirds, each catch filling it twice, its glow from a third full, and Oil Panic's turn frames from another attribute; Fire's angle the other way and its trampoline 2 below him instead of 2.5; his items' outline in his costume's color, the sprayer's states swapped as it's taken in hand, the torch at half speed while he charges, the parachute from his root or gone a frame late, the hammer's sign turned toward him, the manhole's gust half as long, and the trampoline destroyed once instead of twice; the down tilt crouching when the stick is down, the jab's state for the jab chain one off, and the common rapid jab's end on either of its conditions. The absorber's tally counting the boxes that don't count fails the Pokémon Stadium replay's diff, and the outline passes showing and hiding their bones the other way the Fox replay's. Two breaks change nothing anywhere: Judgement's nine weights are all 1, so reading the next number's changes nothing, and the absorbed item's direction (`AbsorbAttr.x1A40`) is only tested against zero by the hit's resolution, so turning it around changes nothing for him. `sync-test --rollback 7` on the Pokémon Stadium replay restores and replays 7 ticks at each of its 11,529 checked ticks with no difference.

Kirby (`ft::kirby`, with his victims' states, the inhaled hold, the stars and the swallowed throw, in `ft::co::capture_kirby`, his items in `it::kinds::kirbycutterbeam` and `it::kinds::kirbyhammer`, and his copies' hats in `ft::kirby::hat`) is checked on six ranked Slippi replays from the `erickfm/melee-ranked-replays` dataset's `KIRBY_master-diamond_a6` and `KIRBY_diamond-diamond_a6` archives (`local/replays/hf/kirby-md-a6/slp/`, `kirby-dd-a6/slp/`, named by their hashes), traced as the others are. Three are 3.17: against Marth on Pokémon Stadium (`hf-kirby-marth-ps-84dcdb02`) and Battlefield (`hf-kirby-marth-bf-cdeac923`: up throws, the hammer, aerial Stone), and Sheik on Battlefield (`hf-kirby-sheik-bf-e928c79a`: Inhale, swallows and spits, Final Cutter and its wave); three are 3.16, for what the 3.17 ones don't reach: Falco on Battlefield (`hf-kirby-falco-bf-49b22a11`: Falco's copy, his costume of parts on Kirby's bones and the blaster), Samus on Fountain of Dreams (`hf-kirby-samus-fod-674aca44`: Samus's copy, her hat and the Charge Shot) and Yoshi on Fountain of Dreams (`hf-kirby-yoshi-fod-27074109`: the mash out of his throw). Their replay checks are clean but on Falco's, where ssbm-rs's play leaves the recording at frame 2195 (UCF's C-stick snap, which 3.16 replays don't record), and the known noise of Samus's (the entry's height bit, a hitstun word) and Yoshi's (a hitstun word); the traces are ssbm-rs's play either way. In Slippi's float mode with no stand-in, play compares every one of their 12,172, 12,773, 15,473, 8,630, 19,981 and 7,549 traced ticks with no difference and no stale read, with `--heap` too, to the traces' ends: his creation and spawns, the copies' files the match loads for the characters present, the multi-jump, his dash attack carried into the air, the throws (the up throw's own camera, the thrower and victim landing frozen in the air and after the release, the mash out), Final Cutter and its wave, Stone with its slopes and slide, the hammer, Inhale with its pull, catch, swallow and spit, the held victim's escape and its star or swallowed throw, the bump effects, copies taken and lost on KO with their hats, and the two copies the replays use: Samus's Charge Shot (charged, kept, fired on the ground and in the air, its item's Kirby branches) and Falco's blaster (its Kirby kinds, its flash and shots). The Sheik replay reaches UCF 0.84's down-smash out of crouch fix (`800D65EC`), which the crouch's stand-up check now runs. His other copies and the copy star are checked on CPU matches (below), Giga Bowser's, which no VS match can give him, in leaf-check only. Inhaling an item and his item hooks aren't ported, and each stops as unported. leaf-check's `kirby` runs every ported function of his units, his items', his victims' states and the common code his moves reach anew (the throws' air landing and camera, the mash out, UCF's crouch fix, the side taunt, the swallowed victim let go) — 959 functions — from captured states of the six replays and the twenty-two CPU matches below (`local/calls/kirby`), as saved and from their units' neighbours with the controls, script variables, the hit flag, Samus's copy's charge and frames, the blaster's loop, and the other copies' timers, needles, sausages and aerial lift changed, and compares the whole state; the common throws run only as saved, because the throw's landing before its freeze (`fn_800DD5EC`) loops until the script's flag, which another state's script never sets, in the original as in ssbm-rs's port (such a call now stops the check by the per-call time limit). Each port broken on purpose fails `kirby`: Samus's copy's charge level a frame early and its aerial recoil forward, Fox's and Falco's copies' blaster state one off and their shot not mirrored facing left, a hat model's variants hidden where shown, Falco's costume's textures left animating, a copy's reset count at 8, the inhale's pull range from the next attribute, Final Cutter's wave's life from its speed, the up throw's camera box, the mash out's jump and cut swapped, and UCF's crouch threshold at 0.625. One break changes nothing: Stone's helpless fall after the aerial end reads an attribute that is 0, so leaving it out is the same. `sync-test --rollback 7` on the Pokémon Stadium replay restores and replays 7 ticks at each of its 12,076 checked ticks with no difference.

Kirby's copies beyond Samus's, Fox's and Falco's are checked on CPU matches. A survey of the local replays' post-frame action states (5,074 files in ssbm-rs's `local/replays/hf` and the author's Slippi folder, of which 89 have a Kirby, all from the dataset) finds his copies' states in only three: Samus's (the traced Fountain of Dreams replay and a 3.16 one on Yoshi's Story) and Falco's (the traced Battlefield replay); none reaches another copy or the copy star (item kind 52). So, as for Ness, `melee-bench oracle-codes` writes the codes for eight matches the level-9 CPU AI plays (`ORACLE_MATCHES`, recorded into `local/bench/oracle-kirby/`): Kirby against Mario, Captain Falcon, Ness and Peach on Final Destination and against Luigi, Pikachu, Mr. Game & Watch and Zelda on Battlefield. The CPU Kirby swallows its opponent early and often, so each match reaches its opponent's copy (Zelda's reaches Sheik's too) and the copy star. Their replay checks differ from the recordings only in the hitstun word's heap pointer (recorded with Show Player Names on, checked with it off) and, on Battlefield, the entry's height bit; they're traced as Ness's are (`--fp hardware`, names off) into `local/traces/v5-mixed/oracle-kirby-*.trc`. With `--fp hardware` and no stand-in, play compares every traced tick with no difference and no stale read, with `--heap` too, on the four whose copies are ported, to the traces' ends (Luigi's to the match's end): Mario (`oracle-kirby-mario-fd`, 11,632 ticks: Mario's copy's fireball and its flash, on the ground, in the air and off ledges), Luigi (`oracle-kirby-luigi-bf`, 13,370: his fireball), Captain Falcon (`oracle-kirby-captain-fd`, 13,313: the Falcon Punch, its flame from his hand, the aerial punch's lunge along the stick) and Pikachu (`oracle-kirby-pikachu-bf`, 18,360: the Thunder Jolt), each copy's hat on his head (Pikachu's with its ears' dynamic bones in place of his own, `ftCo_8009D2A4`, given back with `ftCo_UnloadDynamicBones`), and the copy star (`it::kinds::kirbycopystar`) a hit knocks out of him at the attributes' odds or his side taunt drops (`ftCo_800DEBD0`), falling until its lifetime ends. Two fixes came out of them. A thrown fighter's mash out (`ftCo_800DE5A4`) read its buffered jump as a word where the original reads a byte, so the union's next bytes made Pikachu jump free of Kirby's forward throw instead of cutting free (tick 505; [Melee's own bugs](melee-bugs.md#unsure)); and Kirby's copy was safe from element 11 rather than a cape's 10 (`ftKb_SpecialN_800F5BA4` compares with `0xA`), so Mario's cape drew the loss odds' random number one time too many (tick 4775). The other four (their copies ported in a second batch) compare the same way, with `--heap`, to the traces' ends: Ness (`oracle-kirby-ness-fd`, 12,592 ticks: PK Flash out of his waist, held and let go, the flash's checks of its owner, `ftKb_SpecialNNs_800FEC78` and `_800FECBC`, and its loss when he's hit), Peach (`oracle-kirby-peach-fd`, 13,072: Toad in his hand, its bubble turning a hit into the counter's spore, Toad's pause in his hitlag and its loss; and Peach let go from his mouth as he swallows her, `ftCo_800DC920`'s branch for Kirby, at her own size from where she is, in tick 300), Mr. Game & Watch (`oracle-kirby-gnw-bf`, 9,532: Chef with a pan of his own, `it::kinds::kirbygamewatchchefpan`, the sausages and the pan colored from the copy's data, `ftKb_Init_800EEB00` and `_800EEB1C`, and the copy's costume of parts with its variant lookup, his outline color and his model's scale) and Zelda (`oracle-kirby-zelda-bf`, 16,374: Nayru's Love with its reflector and effects, Zelda's hat with its dynamic bones from the copy's first dynamics, `ftCo_8009D5EC`; and Sheik's copy, once Zelda has turned into Sheik, with its needles drawn, charged, thrown and dropped when he's hit, and Sheik's hat with its chains, `ftCo_8009D704`). One defect of the original came out of them: Mr. Game & Watch's costume puts a one-entry lookup in Kirby's own variant slot 4, which his two model groups walk past into a pointer, a negative count in memory ([Melee's own bugs](melee-bugs.md#kirby-mr-game--watchs-copy-walks-past-its-lookup)); melee-hd read the file offset as a count and stopped at tick 1644. leaf-check's `kirby` takes the copies' functions from the eight matches' captures (`local/calls/kirby/oracle-kirby-*`, with `kb-copies.txt` and `done-copies.txt` for the first four, `kb-copies2.txt` and `done-copies2.txt` for the others, hardware floats; all eight packed), runs Pikachu's copy's throws with the script's flag raised and the copy star's landing from just below the stage. Each port broken on purpose fails `kirby`: Mario's copy off a ledge into the grounded throw and its fireball from another bone, Luigi's copy's flash and its fireball thrown behind him, Captain Falcon's copy's lunge slowing by its speed and its flame on another bone, Pikachu's copy's aerial jolt from the ground offsets, Pikachu's hat without its ears' chains, the copy star thrown ahead whichever way he faces and kept after touching the stage, and the side taunt dropping his copy without its star; the mash out read as a word fails the Pikachu match's diff and the cape as element 11 the Mario match's.
