Two breaks change nothing anywhere: Luigi's copy's transition flags without `0x1000`, and Pikachu's copy landing from its aerial throw with its fall kept (no capture or match lands in that state). For the second batch's copies, `kirby` also runs the needles' drop when he's hit and the landing that gives back the aerial Toad's lift from every capture of their units (`FROM_ALL`), since few neighbours reach them. Each of those ports broken on purpose fails a check: Ness's copy's flash 2 units up (the Ness match's diff, tick 338) and kept when B is let go (tick 6365), and its aerial fall from the next attribute (`kirby`; no match flashes in the air); Peach's copy's bubble's push from the top falling speed (tick 1082) and its aerial Toad's lift kept on landing (`kirby`), and the swallowed Peach left part of Kirby's model (`kirby`); Zelda's copy's reflector's speed from its damage multiplier (tick 4348) and its hover from the attribute before (tick 4345); Sheik's copy's needles on Sheik's own part (tick 9666) and its dropped needles thrown instead (`kirby`); Mr. Game & Watch's copy forgetting the sausage before last (tick 1697), its loop from the attributes' frame rather than the one before (`kirby`), the pan's colors swapped (tick 1680), the costume's lookup count read as a count (stops in tick 1644) and his outline color left as it was (tick 1643); Zelda's hat's chains from the third dynamics (stops in tick 4298), Sheik's hat without its chains (tick 8086) and Ness's hat as Peach's (stops in tick 281, Peach's copy unloaded). One break changes nothing anywhere: the swallowed victim's model scaled back to its own (`Fighter_UpdateModelScale`) is left out, as the victim's scale is still its own in every state at hand. `sync-test --rollback 7 --fp hardware` on each of the eight restores and replays 7 ticks at each of their 11,565, 13,250, 13,203, 18,264, 12,508, 12,978, 9,454 and 16,272 checked ticks with no difference.

The third batch's copies come from six more CPU matches of Kirby (`ORACLE_MATCHES`), recorded into `local/bench/oracle-kirby2/` and traced as the others (`local/traces/v5-mixed/oracle-kirby-*.trc`), each reaching its opponent's copy (the table below): against Fox, Bowser and Young Link on Final Destination and Donkey Kong, Link and Yoshi on Battlefield. Their replay checks differ from the recordings in the hitstun word's heap pointer and, against Donkey Kong and Bowser, the entry's height bit. Fox's also differs in Kirby's position for 74 frames from frame 9867 (and its y in 4 from frame 9910), which is the doubled round's phase ([the engine notes](engine.md#state-the-render-pass-writes)): Kirby is KO'd off the top at frame 9856 and falls toward the screen (`ftCo_DeadUpFall`), where his render callback places him through the camera, so he moves only on ticks after a display pass. ssbm-rs's playback doubles a round after tick 9990 (frame 9866), as melee-hd does, so Kirby's x holds still at frame 9867; the recording run, which boots with the benchmark's codes rather than Slippi's bootloader, doubled it a tick later and held still at frame 9868, and the camera's eased motion keeps the two falls a little apart until frame 9946, well before he comes back (frame 9985). Nothing else differs, his percent and stocks included, and the trace is ssbm-rs's own playback, so it serves to its end. With `--fp hardware` and no stand-in, play compares every traced tick with no difference and no stale read, with `--heap` too, to the traces' ends: Fox (`oracle-kirby-fox-fd`, 12,832 ticks: Fox's hat on his head; the blaster is Falco's copy's code), Donkey Kong (`oracle-kirby-donkey-bf`, 14,576: the Giant Punch's wind-up, swings, shield cancel and punch with the swings kept in `u` between uses, `ft::kirby::special_donkey`, and Donkey Kong's costume of parts), Bowser (`oracle-kirby-bowser-fd`, 12,712: the fire breath's flames from a point on his mouth, the flame item's branch for Kirby's kind with his attributes' full speed and size, their speed and size spent while he breathes and refilled each frame otherwise, `ft::kirby::special_koopa`, and Bowser's hat with its dynamic bones from the copy's second dynamics, `ftCo_8009D4D4`), Link (`oracle-kirby-link-bf`, 19,621: the bow and its arrow, nocked, drawn, charged and loosed, `ft::kirby::special_link`, the bow's and the arrow's checks of a Kirby owner, and Link's hat with its chains, `ftCo_8009D074`), Young Link (`oracle-kirby-younglink-fd`, 11,392: the same with Young Link's attributes and fire arrows, and his hat's chains, `ftCo_8009D18C`) and Yoshi (`oracle-kirby-yoshi-bf`, 15,417: the egg lay, his hat's tongue animations played on it from the copy's dynamics 1 to 4, `ft::kirby::special_yoshi`, and the victim caught, swallowed and laid in his copy's own egg states, `ft::co::yoshi_egg`'s `Layer::Kirby`, which read Kirby's attributes and his copy's egg model, then mashing out). Kirby's draw of the bow has no bones to follow, unlike Link's: its points come from a table of zeros (`ftKb_Init_803B7548`), so the bow's angle is `atan2(0, 0)` and the string's point the origin. `sync-test --rollback 7 --fp hardware` on each of the six restores and replays 7 ticks at each of their 12,750, 14,471, 12,625, 19,544, 11,316 and 15,315 checked ticks with no difference. Three defects of the original came out of them, none reached by a match: Bowser's copy's aerial start lands in the grounded loop, Giga Bowser's copy refills its breath while breathing, and a victim breaking out of Yoshi's copy's egg keeps the shrink's scale ([Melee's own bugs](melee-bugs.md#kirby-bowsers-copy-lands-from-the-aerial-breaths-start-into-its-loop)). leaf-check's `kirby` takes these copies' functions from the six matches' captures (`kb-copies3.txt` and `done-copies3.txt`, hardware floats, packed), with the punch's swings, the flame's speed and size, the breath's frames and the arrow's charge varied, and runs Yoshi's copy's victims' states only in those states. Each port broken on purpose fails a check: Fox's hat as Mario's (stops in tick 1448, no copy data for it) and kept when he loses the copy (tick 2880); Donkey Kong's copy's lunge at the attribute after its speed (tick 406), its ground punch's effect as the air's (tick 391) and the swings kept one short of full when a charge fills (`kirby`); Bowser's copy's flame from a point higher on his mouth (tick 999) and the breath's least speed from its least size (`kirby`); Link's hat's chains from the copy's second dynamics (stops in tick 292) and his arrows' spread at 0.1 (tick 346); Young Link's copy's attributes 4 bytes short (tick 674) and his hat's chains from the second dynamics (stops in tick 317); the bow's destroyed hook asking the other owner's code (ticks 7721 and 1557, Link's own bow and Young Link's); Yoshi's copy's egg's hold from the shrink's frames (tick 475), the hat's tongue played for the item's pulls rather than the fighter's (tick 448), and a hit's share of the hold over the shrink rather than the damage behavior (`kirby`). Breaks that change nothing anywhere: Bowser's copy's flame offset from his breath's other axis (both attributes are 0), the model's scale set back as Kirby's copy's egg breaks (in every match the shrink is over by then), and Donkey Kong's own full-charge glow (57) for Kirby's copy's (58), which leaves every compared field the same. One break no check catches: Bowser's copy landing from the aerial start in its own start, the bug's fix, since no match or capture lands from the aerial start.

The fourth batch's copies, the last, come from eight more CPU matches of Kirby (`ORACLE_MATCHES`), recorded into `local/bench/oracle-kirby3/` and traced as the others (`local/traces/v5-mixed/oracle-kirby-*.trc`), each reaching its opponent's copy (the table below): against Jigglypuff, Marth, the Ice Climbers and Ganondorf on Final Destination and Mewtwo, Roy, Dr. Mario and Pichu on Battlefield. Their replay checks differ from the recordings only in the hitstun word's heap pointer, but for one frame of Pichu's: at frame 14874 Kirby, sent flying, crosses the top of the screen, and the recording sets his offscreen flag that frame where ssbm-rs's playback sets it a frame later. The flag is set only by a display pass ([the engine notes](engine.md#state-the-render-pass-writes)), so this is the doubled round's phase, as in Fox's match above: ssbm-rs's 1,001-tick cycle puts a doubled round there, and the recording, booted with the benchmark's codes, doubles its rounds a tick off playback's; nothing else differs, his percent included, and the trace is playback's. With `--fp hardware` and no stand-in, play compares every traced tick with no difference and no stale read, with `--heap` too, to the traces' ends (Roy's to the match's end): Jigglypuff (`oracle-kirby-jigglypuff-fd`, 12,593 ticks: Rollout as Kirby rolls it, `ft::kirby::special_purin`, Jigglypuff's attributes 0x280 bytes on in his, the roll's squash on his model and its sounds, the turn and the bounces, and Jigglypuff's costume of parts with its dynamic bones on his own parts from the copy's fourth dynamics, `ftCo_8009D81C`), Mewtwo (`oracle-kirby-mewtwo-bf`, 16,018: Shadow Ball, `ft::kirby::special_mewtwo`, the ball in his hand charged, kept, fired with its recoil and put away, the ball's checks of a Kirby owner, and Mewtwo's costume of parts with the tail's dynamic bones from the fifth dynamics, `ftCo_8009DB50`), Marth (`oracle-kirby-marth-fd`, 10,969: Shield Breaker, `ft::kirby::special_mars`, with a sword of his own in his hand, the copy's accessory, the charge and the swing's damage by it, and Marth's hat with its dynamic bones, `ftCo_8009D920`), Roy (`oracle-kirby-roy-bf`, 19,140: Flare Blade, the same code with Roy's attributes and effects, and Roy's hat, `ftCo_8009DA38`), the Ice Climbers (`oracle-kirby-ics-fd`, 12,233: Ice Shot, `ft::kirby::special_iceclimber`, with a hammer in his hand, the copy's own ice and its check of a Kirby owner, and Popo's hat), Dr. Mario (`oracle-kirby-drmario-bf`, 12,714: the vitamins of Mario's copy's code, and his hat), Ganondorf (`oracle-kirby-ganon-fd`, 12,772: the Warlock Punch of Captain Falcon's, and his hat) and Pichu (`oracle-kirby-pichu-bf`, 16,558: the Thunder Jolt of Pikachu's, and his hat with its ears' dynamic bones, `ftCo_8009D3BC`). One finding beyond the ports: putting a copy costume on Kirby's bones rewrites the matrix indices of its display lists in the copy's file, 31% of Jigglypuff's, past the 70% the comparison's file scan asked of a loaded file's words, so the scan lost the file in the trace's memory at tick 274; a file found at the same place before, or the only one with its header on the disc, now needs half (`tools/capture`). Giga Bowser's copy, which no VS match can give him, shares Bowser's code (batch three) and hat; leaf-check runs it from Bowser's copy's states with his kind and states changed. Taking it reads a copy file nothing loads ([Melee's own bugs](melee-bugs.md#kirby-giga-bowsers-copy-reads-a-copy-file-nothing-loads)), and three more defects of the original came out of the batch: Jigglypuff's copy leaves the roll's look on him when hit, its landing dust takes the roll for the slope, and a fully charged Shadow Ball never plays its own sound, Mewtwo's or Kirby's. `sync-test --rollback 7 --fp hardware` on each of the eight restores and replays 7 ticks at each of their 12,483, 15,899, 10,880, 19,020, 12,142, 12,599, 12,686 and 16,450 checked ticks with no difference. leaf-check's `kirby` takes these copies' functions from the eight matches' captures (`kb-copies4.txt` and `done-copies4.txt`, hardware floats, packed), with the roll's frames, squash, charge and way, the swing's charge, the ball's charge and lag, and the ice's hop varied, runs the roll's end (which no capture reaches) from its neighbours' states with each of its arguments, and Bowser's copy's states as Giga Bowser's. The importer types Kirby's Shadow Ball, its effects and his ice in `u`, and the copies' ball's and ice's item variables. Each port broken on purpose fails a check: Jigglypuff's copy's roll speed from the slope's attribute (tick 348), its costume's dynamic bones from the fifth dynamics (stops in tick 274: there are none) and the aerial end's rise cut by the ground end's share (`kirby`); Mewtwo's copy's ball at his shoulder's bone (tick 1218), its lag from the frames per level (tick 1206), its recoils on the ground and in the air swapped (tick 4938), its costume's bones from the fourth dynamics (stops in tick 509) and the ball's put-away check without the full charge's state (`kirby`); Marth's copy's draw keeping the speed by its friction (tick 347), the swing's damage a half second ahead (tick 275) and his hat's bones from the first dynamics (tick 216); Roy's copy's flashes swapped between the ground and the air (tick 2507) and his hat's bones from the third dynamics (stops in tick 1620, none there); the Ice Climbers' copy's ice offset forward by its height (tick 1354), the hammer in his right hand (tick 1348), the aerial shot's hop from the landing lag (`kirby`; no match shoots in the air first) and a destroyed ice left for him to hold (`kirby`); Dr. Mario's and Ganondorf's hats as Mario's and Captain Falcon's (stop in ticks 318 and 235, no copy data for them); Pichu's hat's bones from the second dynamics (stops in tick 272, past the bones' limit) and his hat taken off without its dynamic bones (stops in tick 4199); and Giga Bowser's copy breathing in Bowser's copy's states (`kirby`, through the special's entry from his own unit's states). With every copy ported, Kirby is back on `game::roster`.

His copies, by what reaches them (the survey of 2026-10-16; frames in the copy's states and entries into them):

**Boot state.** A match that starts from the replay's settings can't hold what the boot and the menus left in the original's memory. Until they are ported, `diff` seeds it from the trace tick before the match's scene (the boot state; it needs a version 5 trace, for the time base and the audio heap), field by field from this list and never as a bulk copy (`tools/oracle/src/boot.rs`, `SEEDED`):

- the pad library: the queue's read and write positions, queued count and queued polls, the reset button, and the master, copy and game statuses (the seeding refuses a boot state where a rumble script the menus started still runs);
- the random seed (`*HSD_RandSeedPtr`) as the menus' draws leave it; Slippi's playback replaces it with the replay's when the match starts;
- the audio library: the AX library, the synth, the driver and the game's audio library as the boot initializes them and the menus leave them, with the sound bank they loaded and the audio timeline at the boot state's time base. Load timing isn't modeled: the bank is there from the start.

With `--boot-audio` the audio library isn't seeded: the match's start runs melee-sim's port of the boot's audio setup (`game::boot`, [the engine notes](engine.md)), and `diff` first compares what it leaves with the boot state, the audio state as the capture comparison renders it and the timeline (time base, pending events, pad samples, the DSP's and the disc's state). On all nine v5 traces (the four benchmark matches and the five Slippi replays) the two are the same, byte for byte, and whole matches from it compare tick by tick with no difference and no stale read: the Fox ditto's 15,660 ticks, Fox against Marth's 15,355 on Final Destination and 15,176 on Battlefield in the hardware's float mode, and the Fox vs Sheik replay's 11,928 in Slippi's. `melee-oracle diff TRACE REPLAY.slp --boot-audio --ticks 0` runs the comparison alone.

The fighters' addresses (which the address paragraph of [the engine notes](engine.md) explains) aren't seeded: the allocator model gives them, laid out as Slippi's playback lays out the heap, and `diff` refuses a match whose fighters' addresses at its first traced tick aren't the model's (on every v5 trace, the benchmark matches and the Slippi replays, they are). With `--heap` it also compares the whole model with the trace's memory after every tick (the OS heap's blocks, the memory pieces' free lists, every pool and every class's counts), and `--heap-log FILE` writes the model's allocations and frees in order, to set beside a log of ssbm-rs's allocator calls when the comparison finds a difference. All the v5 traces compare whole so with no difference: the eleven benchmark matches and the thirty-seven Slippi replays this page lists, 612,995 ticks. The Ice Climbers' ranked replay against Fox on Final Destination (`local/replays/hf/ics-dd-a5/diamond-diamond-c93e2e19a89a5d982c30bc2c.slp`) is traced as Sarah's replays are, with an ssbm-run built from ssbm-rs's main since its `d61135b`, whose `--port all` passes Nana's DI the register the console does ([the engine notes](engine.md)), into `local/traces/v5-mixed/hf-ics-fox-fd-c93e2e19-rf.trc`; the older `hf-ics-fox-fd-c93e2e19.trc` came from ports that pass a stale one. Its replay check shows 21 pre-frame differences (Popo's y in frame 89, Nana's c-stick from frame 92), the same as the interpreter's run without ports, and no post-frame one. It compares its first 7,518 ticks so, a thrown Fox let go from an Ice Climber's model in tick 4712 (`ftCo_800DC920`), Nana's recovery and ledge behaviors (from tick 5634), her turn toward an attack's target and Popo's up special alone (from tick 6316, Nana out of reach) among them, and from tick 6541 the side special (Squall Hammer: in the air and on the ground, Nana joining Popo frozen with him at his place, then let go and spinning on a little behind him, and Popo spinning alone, landing and lifted by B presses), and from tick 7519 Fox frozen by Blizzard in the air (`ftCo_DamageIce_Init`: the block's box, effect and ball hurtbox, the body moved within it, its spin and slowed fall), mashed out of after 16 frames (`ftCo_80091854`) and jumping out of the block (`DamageIceJump`), to the trace's end: all 8,089 ticks. leaf-check's `ice` runs the frozen states' functions from saved calls of four replays of the set where Blizzard freezes a fighter (this one, `hf-ics-fox-dl-a7365e16-rf` below, and two on Yoshi's Story, `e107a975` and `e3ff9bc0`, with hits while frozen and a block landing), and from those states with the controls changed, the mash timer near its end, the block moved toward the stage's sides and underside, the one grounded frozen state on the ground and off its floor's end, and a surface handed to `ftCo_DamageIce_Collide`. No replay at hand bounces the block off a surface or breaks it (faster than `ftCommonData` x780, 100, which no hit in play reaches), KOs a frozen fighter toward the screen (`ftCo_80090AC0`) or runs the frozen state's damage hook (`ftCo_DamageIce_OnHit`: a hit while frozen changes state before the hooks run, which clears it), so those are checked from changed states only; the hammer's jump out of the ice (`ftCo_800C5A98`) isn't ported. Taken up from tick 5600 or 6200 it runs with no difference to tick 7430, where a new item's unused hitbox holds the joint word its block kept from a freed item (the import limit the Fox ditto's paragraph describes). The up special with Nana (Belay: she rides Popo's hand, is thrown up on the string and he is pulled up after her, the string paid out, let fall and reeled in) is checked on more replays of the `ics-dd-a5` set, traced the same way into `local/traces/v5-mixed/`, each only as far as the moves it checks (`--fields`), and compared with `--heap`, with no difference: against Ness on Final Destination (`hf-ics-ness-fd-c719def4-rf`, all 1,982 traced ticks from the start, an air Belay from tick 1154), against Fox on Dream Land (`hf-ics-fox-dl-a7365e16-rf`, traced again for the whole match, all 10,492 ticks: an air Belay from tick 822, Nana landing from her throw in tick 875 and her computer player heading off her island from tick 889, Fox frozen by a hit in tick 994 and jumping out of the ice, and from tick 4095 a second animation Nana copied from Popo's buffer, which the original copies into her main animation buffer, where her next action's animation overwrites it, [the engine notes](engine.md#loaded-data-the-game-writes-into)), against Fox on Final Destination (`hf-ics-fox-fd-f7386ff7-rf`, taken up from ticks 7100 and 9100 to the trace's end, an air Belay from tick 7152 and a ground one from tick 9172, and the side special alone in the air from tick 8574, landing in tick 8686, and with Nana in the air from tick 9412). Popo's hop alone on the ground is checked by `hf-ics-marth-fod-a918794d-rf` on Fountain of Dreams (all 1,983 traced ticks, the hop from tick 1780) and `hf-ics-fox-bf-b2652aab-rf` on Battlefield (all 1,684 traced ticks, the hop from tick 1317); `hf-ics-captain-fd-16db3989-rf` on Final Destination (all 1,584 traced ticks) has Nana dropping from Popo's hand when he's hit. On all three Nana's computer player heads off her island toward its destination (`ftCo_800AB224`'s branch: jumping, walking off, dropping through a platform or turning back by the angle and what's in between); on Fountain of Dreams she also mashes out of a hold (`ftCo_800AC30C`, from tick 498), and on Battlefield copies Popo's ground Blizzard (tick 191). `hf-ics-marth-ps-5db3312e-rf` on Pokémon Stadium (3.16) compares all 1,483 traced ticks with no difference, the stage frozen (the Slippi ruleset's table below). The side special alone, with Nana out of reach in a damage or held state (the freeze glitch fix's branch, the Slippi ruleset's table below), is on two more replays of the set against Fox, traced the same way: Fountain of Dreams (`hf-ics-fox-fod-3673022d-rf`, all 984 traced ticks, from tick 784) and Dream Land (`hf-ics-fox-dl-1f721c41-rf`, all 2,434 traced ticks, from tick 2224), with no difference. The floors on these replays are flat, so the slope's push on the ground spin and the body's lean with the floor (`ftIceClimberAttributes::x6C` and the collisions' lean) come to nothing, and no spin meets a wall or has Nana leave hers before Popo's ends: those wait for a replay that does. Their replay checks show only the differences above (the frame start's random seed under 3.16's codes, Nana's c-stick). No replay at hand lands Popo during the start or the throw, walks him or Nana off a ledge in them, or runs the hook that takes the string away when he's hit or KO'd (`ftPp_SpecialS_80121164`), so those wait for one that does. Popo's ground pull (state 349) and the Ice Climbers' state 364 are never entered, so their callbacks are in `ledger/dead.txt`. Nana never DIs with no knockback in the replay against Fox (she DIs 79 times, all with the stick neutral), so leaf-check's `nana` test checks that path from saved calls. The Battlefield replay `hf-ics-fox-bf-db9ac460-rf` (the `ics-dd-a3` set, traced the same way, replay check clean) runs UCF 0.8 (below) and compares all 9,052 of its ticks with `--heap`, with no difference, Nana's computer player heading off her island (`ftCo_800AB224`) from tick 102. Nana tumbling (behavior 15, `ftCo_800ABBA8`: at level 5 and up she aims for the island end farthest from her target, `ftCo_800A6700`, and drifts toward it unless her fall's prediction lands her above it), her ledge pick on her way back to Popo while recovering (`ftCo_800A8DE4` from `ftCo_800B0760`) and her attack check against a shielding target (`ftCo_800B9F6C`, which picks from the computer players' shield table) are checked on six more replays of the `ics-dd-a3` and `link-dd-a6` sets, traced the same way and compared with `--heap`, with no difference and no stale read: the Ice Climbers' ditto on Final Destination (`hf-ics-ics-fd-688aa3dd-rf`, all 3,188 traced ticks: the ledge pick in tick 961, tumbling from tick 1351), against Luigi on Battlefield (`hf-ics-luigi-bf-422b40f2-rf`, all 3,786: the ledge pick in tick 1113, tumbling from 3029), against Captain Falcon on Dream Land (`hf-ics-falcon-dl-2a01c93d-rf`, the whole match, 6,769 ticks, tumbling from 3204), against Falco on Final Destination (`hf-ics-falco-fd-7bd4b671-rf`, the whole match, 10,011 ticks), and against Link on Dream Land (`hf-ics-link-dl-04dad1f2-rf`, all 4,786 traced) and Battlefield (`hf-ics-link-bf-2a40237f-rf`, all 11,494 traced), where she attacks a shielding Link from ticks 4554 and 11307. Their replay checks are clean but for the Link replays' post-frame off-screen flag (one frame and seven), the render-skip and widescreen noise above. Broken on purpose they fail: the island end taken 1 further inside fails the Dream Land replay in tick 3204, the drift's test turned around fails the ditto in tick 1351 and Dream Land in tick 3204, the tumble's level raised from 5 to 6 fails the ditto in tick 1351, the ledge pick on her way back left out fails the ditto in tick 961 and Luigi's in tick 1113, and the shield check or its table switched off fails both Link replays (ticks 4554, and 11313 or 11307). leaf-check's `nana` runs her frame from saved frames of the ditto and the Falco replay too, and changed (`nana_tumble_and_shields`) to reach what no replay does: tumbling at level 9, where she techs when the floor is under 10 frames away; over an enemy's up tilt right below her (a shield, a 50-frame wait and down); 4 frames from her destination at a height where the prediction's square root of the frames (the original's, where the square belongs) decides the drift; in the tumble's behavior at level 0; and at level 9 with her enemies shielding. Each of those branches broken on purpose fails it. The seeding runs on the new state before anything of the match's scene (`Match::start_from`), and goes away once the boot and the menus are ported. It isn't platform state. `diff --boot-diff` prints what still differs between the seeded state and the boot state among the globals and statics the comparer covers: on the Fox vs Sheik replay only the button maps and the rumble data pointer (`lb_804D63C0`), which the scene's setup sets itself. HSD's ID table is empty at the boot tick, so there is nothing of it to seed, and which slots the GObj and heap pools reuse doesn't show, as objects pair by structure.

**A match from the menus.** A VS match a console without Slippi's codes starts from its menus has no replay, so `diff` can't play it. `melee-oracle vs-heap TRACE --stage STKIND --player PORT:CKIND[:LEVEL]... [--item-freq F] [--ticks N]` starts it from the settings its menus chose (`Settings::vs_with` with a new save's rules; a LEVEL makes the player a computer player at that level, and the costumes are the trace's player blocks'), refuses settings whose players' characters, kinds or computer levels aren't the trace's, the random seed and the save's unlocks as the trace's memory has them before the match's scene, then checks the fighters' addresses at the match's first tick and the whole allocator model (as `--heap` compares it) against memory after each tick, every controller at rest, as the run held them; `--shift-heap BYTES` moves the model's heap, to see the check fail. The trace is ssbm-run's from a new save (`local/lockstep/cards/template-saved.raw`'s copy in slot A) through the title screen, the main menu, VS mode's Melee with Ice Climbers on port 1 and Fox on port 2 and Fountain of Dreams, its menus driven by `MONKEY_HOLD` (the holds are in `traces/vanilla/menus-ics-fox-fod.holds.txt`): `STATE_TRACE=local/traces/vanilla/menus-ics-fox-fod.trc STATE_TRACE_MEMORY=800.. MONKEY_HOLD=... ssbm-run "$SSBM_DISC" --card CARD --fields 1160 --port sysdolphin/baselib/perf --monkey 1`. `--item-freq F` sets the item switch's frequency the menus chose (`none` for the switch at none). Its 304 ticks of play compare with no difference, and so do the 313 of `traces/vanilla/menus-ics-fox-fod-noitems.trc`, the same run with the item switch set to none first in Custom Rules, with `--item-freq none` (`tools/oracle/tests/vanilla.rs`, with the disc alone also the fighters' addresses on Fountain of Dreams and Pokémon Stadium, and a heap 32 bytes off failing). With a computer player: `traces/vanilla/menus-cpu-fox-fox-fod.trc` is the noitems run with Fox on both ports, port 2's switched to a computer player on the character select screen (its toggle, level 1), every field held so the runner's random input never plays (`.holds.txt` beside it); with `--player 0:2 --player 1:2:1 --item-freq none` its 343 ticks compare with no difference, the computer player playing; [the engine notes](engine.md#the-allocators) give the other ways into the match that were checked.

**Captured states.** Game code needs real states to run from: a fighter mid-action, with its joints, animations and stage. ssbm-rs's lockstep captures (`CAPTURE=DIR CAPTURE_FUNCS=NAME,...`) save main memory and registers at real calls. `melee-capture` finds which disc archives are loaded where (by header and contents; the game writes into some, such as fighters' action tables), then imports the state into a `Game`: the GObj scheduler with its processes, every joint tree with its animations, references, display lists and shapes, the fighters through their generated mirrors, and the stage (`stage_info`, the stage objects' user data with their variables typed for the stage, the stage parameters the game writes into, the collision geometry and islands, fog, lights, the game camera with its camera boxes, the player slots, game mode and controllers the camera reads, the random seed and HSD's ID table, and the effects and the particle system: every effect, efLib's globals and the fighters' deferred spawns, the effect banks, particles, generators, app-SRTs, point joints and the display's sorting state). Afterwards it compares a `Game` with memory, pairing objects by where they're reached from, so objects either side created since pair too. A port runs from the imported state while ssbm-rs runs the call from the same memory, and the two states are compared. Pointers the importer doesn't model yet (static data in the executable, item user data) are listed rather than guessed. A check runs its saved calls on `LEAF_CHECK_WORKERS` threads (2 by default, each with its own reference), and a check made of many sets of saved calls runs `LEAF_CHECK_SETS` of them at once (4 by default; [leaf-check's speed](#leaf-checks-speed)).

**Debug VS heaps.** A debug VS run (`--mode e`, leaf-check's `debug*` sets) holds an earlier scene's bytes in its heap. The boot plays the opening movie (`GM_OPENING_MV`, `MvOpen.mth` through `lbMthp_8001F410`) for two fields before ssbm-rs's runner turns the title it ends in into the debug VS mode, and the movie's first frame, black, is decoded into a buffer from the heap: `__THPDecompressiMCURow640x480` stores its luma plane through the locked cache (`LCStoreBlocks`), 16 (`0x10`) in every byte, over the zeros `ClearArena` left at boot. Later scenes take that memory again uncleared, so an item's block holds `0x10101010` wherever its code never writes. The fill is the game's, not ssbm-rs's: three runs of Pikachu's `debug2` capture (two Pikachus on Battlefield, `--mode e --matches 21 --monkey 21`, 9,000 fields), one plain and two with the runner's `FILL_ARENA=00` and `FILL_ARENA=55` (the whole arena filled at `main`), played the same match, saved the same four calls of `ftPk_SpecialLw_SpawnEffect`, and held the same 54,500 words of `0x10101010` in the arena (the `55` run's other free memory was `0x55` instead of zeros); a write watch (`WATCH_ADDR`) on a word of it saw only `ClearArena` and the movie's decoder write there before the match. The replay sets' heaps don't hold the frame where items go, which is why they imported cleanly.

The importer carries these bytes over, so item code runs from them as the original does:

- **The blocks a pool grows into.** A new item takes the item pool's last freed block, or once the free list is empty a new block from the heap (`HSD_ObjAllocAddFree`, one at a time), uncleared either way. The importer brings in the next four blocks the pool would take after its free list (`fresh_items`, from the allocator model as memory holds it, `melee_capture::heap::read`), with whatever the heap holds there, and puts them behind melee-sim's free list as fresh slots (`Pool::free_fresh`), linked as `HSD_ObjAllocAddFree` links a new block: taken once the free list runs out, but not on it, so an item freed while only they remain links to none of them, as the original's `freehead` is null then. Before 2026-10-17 they were last on the free list itself, so such an item's first word named one (`import_compares_back_clean`, from tick 200 of the Fox vs Sheik replay, differed in tick 609 at an item that reused that item's block). A new item in one keeps the original's leftovers where its creation doesn't write, as one in a freed block does; in a replay run's fresh heap they are zeros, as before. Before, such an item started from zeros, so every call that made one in a debug VS state differed (`ftPk_SpecialLw_SpawnEffect`'s second call in Pikachu's `debug2` by 830 fields, the item spawns of the items set below).
- **Words that point nowhere.** A word of an item's stale bytes (a live item's, a freed block's or a new block's) outside main memory, of any handle kind, is unknown until written: it imports as a handle past every pool (from `melee_sim::hsd::pool::UNKNOWN`, the word kept in `Maps::unknown`). The comparer compares it by the word, so it agrees while neither side writes it and differs when only one does; following it stops with a stale read (`Pool`'s index panics, `stale read: followed a word unknown until written`), as the original would fault on the word, and the logic and state tables stop the same way on an address they don't know. Testing it against null gives what the original's test gives, since the word isn't null. Before, such a word dangled for six handle kinds (a blank object a read would follow without a sound) and was null for the others, left out of the comparison.

So nothing is exempted: every value an item check compares is the original's or a stale read stops it, and a stale read is a failure, not a skip. The item capture sets this added and what they check:

- `$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/calls/items/debug1`: two Pikachus on Battlefield with the item switch narrowed to the 13 ported kinds, 3,461 saved calls of the item code (the command is in `tools/leaf-check/tests/items.rs`). Every check in `items` takes it with the others; before, `create`, `spawn_air`, `spawn_ground` and `spawn_proc` differed on it, each at a new item's fields (`0x10101010`, or an older scene's heap bytes for the spawn process). Three checks took calls their ports don't model and now leave them out: `stage_crossing` the calls asking for a normal, `it_80276408`'s the calls writing a barrel's variables, `drop_at` the states without Fox's articles. `debug_round_trips` imports each of its calls but the display pass's material setups and the item code's setup (3,321), compares it back clean, and counts 26,610 unknown words among them.
- `$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/calls/pulls/debug1`: the same match with Mr. Saturn and the Beam Sword alone, 655 saved calls (`tools/leaf-check/tests/pulls.rs`). It compared clean before the change too (its calls make no item in a new block); every call round-trips, with 5,240 unknown words.
- Pikachu's and Pichu's checks now take `ftPk_SpecialLw_SpawnEffect`'s debug VS calls, which drop the Thunder's bolt into a new block; every one compares clean.
- `$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/calls/items/oracle-items-ported-fd` and `calls/pulls/oracle-items-ported-fd`, the replay sets: [milestone 4](milestone-4.md)'s items-on pilot (the ported kinds on Final Destination, [oracle sets](#oracle-sets)) played from its recording as the character sets are, 2,020 saved calls of the item code (every thousandth) and 379 of the pulls' (every fiftieth). Their heap is playback's. `items` (194 checks, 27 minutes at four test threads) and `pulls` take them with the other sets and pass.

Two changes break the checks, as they should: making the importer treat an in-range word of an item's stale bytes as unknown (the dynamic bones table) stops `anim_proc`, `bone`, `change_state` and `create_hitbox` with stale reads on the `debug1` set, and reading the pool's new blocks 32 bytes off makes `create`, `spawn_air`, `spawn_ground` and `spawn_proc` differ.

Besides single calls, a captured state can drive longer runs: leaf-check's `last_stage_ticks` runs Final Destination's stage processes alone for 6,400 ticks on both sides from one capture (phases 1 through 8 of its background cycle), `battle_stage_ticks` runs Battlefield's for 9,000, and the `on_state` tests call functions no capture reached on a real stage. The camera's tests also run perturbed copies of captured states (both sides start from the same changed memory) to reach values and branches the replays don't, and enter its special modes from VS states with random controllers. Where objects are freed and new ones made, both sides are paired afresh by where objects are reached from, since the heap reuses blocks by address and pools by last freed.

The audio driver is too (`leaf-check`'s `audio` tests): the importer brings in the driver's virtual voices and globals, the synth's nodes, loaded effects and globals, the AX library's voices, parameter blocks and stacks, and the sound bank (from its loaded copy), and the comparison checks them byte for byte. Their calls are saved with the runner's `CAPTURE_INTERACTED=1`, since every audio function masks interrupts. Their schedule has a check of its own: ssbm-rs's runner records every audio call game code makes, the audio frames and the audio state at every tick's end (`AUDIO_TIMELINE`), and the check replays the calls from a captured tick with melee-sim's timeline placing the frames, comparing tick start and end times and the whole audio state every tick over whole matches: a Final Destination singles and a four-player Battlefield teams replay (about 10,000 ticks each, clean), the singles again with ssbm-rs's sample-accurate DSP (`AX=1`, where the music refills read the disc), and a Pokémon Stadium match whose transformations load during play. The recorder also logs the game's own disc load requests, which the check replays with their waits. A fifth timeline starts at the boot's last tick (`AUDIO_START=0`) and replays the Final Destination match's scene entry: the library's bank choice and loads, the file loads and the music's start, with game code's own time between ticks taken from the log's time base and the entry's audio frames placed where the log has them. The sound bank functions also have saved calls of their own (`bank_calls`, `match_bank_choice`, `bank_group_remove`, `bank_deflag_moves`). The music stream's start and callbacks and the audio library's tick have saved calls of their own.

The VS match is checked the same way (`leaf-check`'s `vs` and `player` tests): the importer also brings in the player blocks, the pad library (queue, rumble and statuses), the button maps, the scene loop's state and frame clocks, the VS controller, the live standings, the pause screen and the player library's counters, and compares them back. Its captures come from the debug VS mode's random matches (`--mode e --matches`), whose code Slippi's playback codes don't patch (in replay runs they patch the VS scene's frame, setup and entry, and the pad library). Calls the runner never saves (those that load files or reach the audio driver) run from other functions' captured states; the player blocks' and the match's accessors run as random sequences from captured states, comparing every value read and the blocks after each step.

Fighter creation is checked from the states just before a VS match creates its fighters (`leaf-check`'s `fighter_create`): every slot's fighters are created on both sides and the whole state compared after each, the fighters' new joint trees, parts, attributes, DObj lists, dynamic bones, shadows and lights, the player blocks and the fighter globals with it. The debug VS mode reads fighters' files from the disc inside `Fighter_Create`, which the reference's side does from the disc while melee-sim's side is handed the same files.

The fighters' attack collision (`Fighter_procAttackColl`) is checked from its calls in running matches (`leaf-check`'s `fighter_procs`), each also run again with the other fighter's first hitbox switched on over the fighter and, by the call, the fighter's own box over it on the ground (clanks), its shield up, or both fighters' thrown boxes live: the hits, tips, clanks and shield hits, the damage logs, the knockback and damage result, the hit sounds and the statistics they reach, the effects they spawn compared with the rest, and the attack statistics stood in for on both sides. The same check runs the collision results (`Fighter_procCollResolve`), from real calls and again with one of the attack collision's results given (a tip, a shield hit, a clank, a dealt hit, a shield push, a held hitlag), the camera process and the fighters' light. The dynamic bones (`Fighter_procDynamics`) and the legs' reach (`Fighter_procIK`) are checked from their calls there and in a replay, again with wind sources given, a flat floor, other sizes, the air, a collider and Flat Zone, and with the legs' flags set and the model's root off the floor.

The fighter mirrors come from `tools/structgen`, which generates each game struct with an importer and a field-by-field comparer. `melee-oracle mirror-check TRACE` round-trips every fighter of a state trace through them.

**Replay playback and the Slippi ruleset.** A replay drives a melee-hd match through a port of Slippi's playback codes (`game::playback`): the replay's game start becomes the match's settings, its seed and each frame's recorded inputs and seed are inputs the frontend supplies (`Match::step_replay`, answering what Slippi's EXI device answers), the fighters get the recorded inputs in `Fighter_procInput`, the pad library renews from the newest poll each tick, team stock steals follow the replay, the match ends with the replay, and the playback and recording GObjs exist as in a replay run. The codes that don't change the simulation in a replay played forward are listed in `game::playback`'s documentation. The replay's code list makes the rules (`game::slippi`), and the codes the ported code reaches are ported natively.

**Slippi versions.** Slippi's online code set changed between versions, and a replay records the set it ran. melee-hd knows three (`game::slippi::CodeSet`): Slippi 3.19's (the author's replays); 3.16's, which 3.17 ships word for word and 3.15 already has (the `erickfm/melee-ranked-replays` dataset's replays); and 3.14's, which is 3.16's without WhispyBlowDirFix (`8008653C`), so Whispy's blow direction counts dead and sleeping fighters as the original does. 3.19's and 3.16's gameplay codes match but for three of the determinism fixes, read from the replays' code lists:

- **Final Destination's background** (`8021AAE4`, in `grLast_8021AAB0`, on `bl grLast_8021B2E8`): 3.19's `C2` code saves the random seed, makes the call and puts the seed back; 3.16's is a `04` write of a nop, so the scene's timer never runs: no 30-second wait, no fog blends, and the background stays in its first phase all match. `proc_main` follows `Rules::codes`.
- **Nana's DI** (`800AC5B8`, in `ftCo_800AC5A0`): both clear `r30`, the register whose low byte becomes her stick Y; 3.19 also clears `r5`. Its host takes the variant from `Rules::codes` (`ft::cpu_move`, [the engine notes](engine.md)).
- **Pokémon Stadium's transformations**: 3.19 loads the next transformation's file when it's due, synchronously (`800165AC` and the hooks at `801C65C8`, `801D4760` and `801D457C`). 3.16 freezes the stage: `Online/Core/PreventFileAlarms/FreezeStadium` (`801D4578`, the instruction after the cycle's scale read) jumps to the cycle's epilogue (`801D4FD8`), so the stage stays the default one all match, the cycle draws no random number and its timer (drawn in the setup) never counts. The set also carries `Common/Preload Stadium Transformations` (slippi-ssbm-asm's names): picking the next transformation ahead while the cycle waits (`801D45EC`: `HSD_Randi(4)` drawn until it differs from `xE2`, kept at the stage's `xEC`, its file's load started with the game's asynchronous `lbFile_80016580` and a byte at `xF0` set), taking that pick when the timer runs out (`801D460C`, `801D4610`) and skipping the original's load (`801D4724`), and the byte cleared in the setup (`801D14C8`) and when the default stage settles back (`801D4F14`). All but the setup's line sit past the freeze's return, so with the whole set they never run; the setup's clearing of `xF0` does, and the comparison checks `xEC` and `xF0` with the rest of the stage's variables.

`Rules::from_codes` reads the version from the codes themselves: each known code lists the versions that ship it, every code of a list must belong to one version (`CodeError::Mixed` otherwise), and each rule must have all of that version's codes. A list that fits several versions plays as the first it fits whole, in the order 3.19, 3.16, 3.14: a list of only the codes all share plays as 3.19's. `melee-oracle rules REPLAY.slp...` prints what a replay's codes make, or why melee-hd refuses them.

**UCF versions.** UCF comes in two versions in Slippi replays (`game::ucf::Ucf`), each recognized by its codes and either going with any code set:

- **UCF 0.84** (`External/UCF 0.84/UCF`): all of Slippi 3.17's and 3.19's replays and most of 3.16's.
- **UCF 0.8** (`External/UCF 0.8/Logic`): all of 3.14's and 3.15's and some of 3.16's. It has three codes, at three of 0.84's places: dashback (`800C9A44`), shield drop (`800998A4`) and tumble (`800908F4`). It has no pad buffer, SDI, shield SDI, extended shield drop or down-smash out of crouch fix. Its dashback and tumble take the raw stick's travel from the pad library's queue, from the poll two before the one the tick took to that one, where 0.84's read its own buffer of each human fighter's last four ticks. Its dashback needs the stick past the dash threshold either way, not the way the turn faces, and turns Nana's CPU stick only for Popo. Its tumble lets a NaN stick fail where 0.84's goes on. Its shield drop works on any floor, needs the stick's horizontal timer past 3 rather than the roll's window, and takes the stick's distance as floats (each axis in raw units plus 2, over 80, squared and summed, at least 1) rather than in whole units.

Of the 3,605 dataset replays in ssbm-rs's `local/replays/hf/`, 982 carry UCF 0.8: 383 from 3.14, 375 from 3.15 and 224 from 3.16. All the author's 203 replays (3.19) carry 0.84. 968 of the 0.8 replays played before melee-hd knew the codes players add; the other 14, like 61 of the 0.84 ones, carried such codes, and all of those but three play now (below, under the survey). Three dataset replays with UCF 0.8 are traced as the author's replays are, into `local/traces/v5-mixed/` (replay checks clean):

- Roy against Falco on Battlefield (`hf-roy-falco-bf-3fc44471`, `roy-dd-a3`, 3.16) compares all its 10,789 ticks with no difference and no stale read. 0.8's dashback runs 25 times there, its shield drop 20 times and its tumble flick on the push's second frame once.
- Peach against Fox on Battlefield (`hf-peach-fox-bf-03dd468e`, `peach-dd-a1`, 3.14) compares all 11,632 of its ticks with no difference and no stale read. It runs the dashback 10 times, the shield drop 5 times and the tumble flick once, and from tick 7935 catches an item in the air (`fn_800D6F58`, ported with Link).
- Peach against Fox on Dream Land (`hf-peach-fox-dl-126152b3`, `peach-dd-a1`, 3.14) compares all its 8,749 ticks with no difference and no stale read. Whispy never picks his direction there while a fighter is dead or asleep, so it doesn't tell 3.14's set from 3.16's: given WhispyBlowDirFix, it compares the same.

Each code broken on purpose fails the diff: the dashback's travel threshold raised from 75 to 150, or its earlier poll taken one poll back instead of two, fails Roy's replay in tick 1523 (Falco's first dashback); the tumble's flick on the push's second frame switched off fails it in tick 5796, and Peach's in tick 7802; the shield drop switched off fails Roy's in tick 1431. Raising the shield drop's timer floor from 3 to 6 changes nothing there, since all its 20 shield drops come with the stick held sideways longer than that.

The Ice Climbers' Battlefield replay `hf-ics-fox-bf-db9ac460-rf` (UCF 0.8) now plays too, to its end (above).

ssbm-rs plays 3.16 replays with Slippi's playback codes, which run the recorded codes as they are (it keeps the original machine code of the functions they patch), and its replay check shows one difference from the recording: the pre-frame random seed at frames -123, 3877 and 7877 of the Ganondorf vs Captain Falcon replay, where ssbm-rs records the frame start's seed one draw on and the recording has the frame start's seed itself. 3.16's online codes set each frame's seed (the frame index in the high half over the match's seed) and record it as the frame starts; playback puts that seed back from a process of its own (`playback_restore_seed`). In those frames Final Destination's particle generator spawns (`hsd_8039EFAC`, `hsd_8039F05C`, `HSD_Randf`) after playback's process and before the fighters' inputs are recorded, so playback's recording shows its draw; the online game's shows none, so there the frame's seed was set after that draw. 3.19 recordings show the draw (the Captain Falcon vs Sheik replay's pre-frame seed at frame -123 is its frame start's one draw on), so 3.19's codes set the seed where playback puts it back. Playback restores only the frame start's seed, so in those three frames every later draw comes one step on from the online game's; nothing gameplay draws then here (every post-frame event matches, and the next frame's seed is put back), only the particles, which no replay records. A gameplay draw in such a frame would part any playback of a 3.16 replay from the online game. It isn't an ssbm-rs bug: ssbm-rs does what Slippi's playback codes do.

Playback's own set also patches fighter code: in `ftCo_8009DD94` (`+0x2FC`, the bone dynamics' end) it sets up every dirty matrix of the fighter's model (each joint with `0x40` and not `0x800000` in its flags, children before siblings, instances included), which `Running::swing_chains` does in a replay, and in `ftCo_800DEBD0` it reads the debug level as 0, which a retail disc's is anyway. At the match's end the recording codes copy the results into the VS scene's exit data (`gmVsMelee_VsExitInfo`) and rank them with `gm_80166378`, which matters once the exit data is ported.

These ports are checked in leaf-check's `replay` tests against ssbm-rs's ports made with the codes (`ssbm_game::playback`), from captures of replay runs (their header shows how to make them): the recorded inputs with their raw sticks at UCF's thresholds, the first frame's settling, UCF's stick history and SDI, the off-screen limits, the frame reads, stock steals, the seed restored each frame, the general codes' costume rules and the settings themselves. leaf-check's reference runs the patched port of every function whose codes a capture's memory holds (`register_patched`), so the fighter and VS checks on replay captures check the patched behavior too; the VS checks' `place_players` covers neutral spawns, and `input`'s pad library sequences run with the playback patch on a quarter of the time. The replay's end (`replay_end`, ignored for now) agrees but for the audio driver: the quit plays the match's end sounds, which the VS code still hands the frontend as events where ssbm-rs's run drives the driver.

**Common stops.** On 2026-10-15, before the ports below, melee-vs-run played 1,448 replays whose characters and stage are all on `game::roster` (natively, no trace, each with its own code set's rules), sampled up to 12 per character pairing from the 3,200 such replays of `erickfm/melee-ranked-replays` in ssbm-rs's `local/replays/hf/` (1,291) and all 157 of the author's: 25 carried codes their players added and were refused, 1,039 played to their ends, and 384 stopped at code no port covered. A separate run over 186 Ice Climbers replays (their other characters ported) adds Nana's computer player (`ftCo_800ABBA8`, 37 matches; `ftCo_800A8DE4`, 6; `ftCo_800B9F6C`, 2) after Stadium's codes (55). The scratchpad's table (`common-stops/ranked.md`) has every stop with an example. With the common stops below fixed, the same 186 played 108 to their ends (5 refused, 73 stopped: Nana's tumble 59, her ledge pick on her way back 8, the shield check 2, and 4 others). With those three ported (the Ice Climbers' paragraph above), 176 play to their ends, 97% of the 181 melee-hd accepts, and 69 play further, none less far. Five stop: twice a held item's GObj gone on Pokémon Stadium (against Luigi and Young Link), once each `ftCo_800DD168` (a grab let go of, in a ditto on Pokémon Stadium), the Stadium stock icons' `ifStock_802FA118` (a 3.16 replay against Jigglypuff on Battlefield, at its start) and Link's variables read as bytes where his hookshot's GObj is.

The top nine are ported or fixed, each checked by a traced replay (`--fp slippi --heap`, traced as the others are into `local/traces/v5-mixed/`) and, for the fighter code, by leaf-check's `common_stops` from captured calls (`local/calls/common-stops/`, made on those replays and seven more):

- Stadium's 3.16 code set freezes the stage (the Slippi versions below): `hf-ylink-fox-ps-cfb1b164` (12,174 ticks) and `hf-fox-doc-ps-b5925e97` (10,673), both 3.17, compare to their traces' ends.
- Captain Falcon's red costume loads from `PlCaRe.usd`, as `lbFileGetFullName` completes a bare-dot name for English: `hf-ganon-captain-ps-c3c7e7c2` (3.16, frozen Stadium) compares all its 7,909 ticks.
- A KO's attacker is tested for no one before its team (`pl_8003DF44`): the author's four-player teams replay on Yoshi's Story (`Game_20260807T220230`) compares all its 6,162 ticks: a star KO with no attacker in tick 544, and stocks taken from a teammate, which bring the results up to date (`gm_GetMatchEndPlayerScore`) before the player respawns.
- `ftCo_800DCFD4` and `ftCo_800DE854` hit the held fighter with its holder's second throw hitbox: Peach vs Marth on Final Destination (`hf-peach-marth-fd-dc42c8bb`) compares all its 13,249 ticks, and the author's four-player Final Destination replay `Game_20260531T174953` all its 11,884: the throw-off in tick 629, a player out of stocks (`gm_8016AC44`, which in VS takes the player out of the match) in tick 10,141 and a stock taken from a teammate in tick 10,203.
- `ftCo_800C36DC`: Dr. Mario's cape turns Samus's shield on Fountain of Dreams (`hf-samus-doc-fod-3937907a`, all 9,826 ticks).
- `ftCo_8009E4A8`: Roy's cape as Whispy's wind ends (`hf-luigi-roy-dl-59f2d25b`, all 7,487 ticks).
- The broken shield (`ftCo_ShieldBreakFly.c` to `ftCo_Furafura.c`, by a hit with `pl_8003E058`, drained, or an overpowered reflector's `ftCo_80098C9C`): Marth vs Peach on Fountain of Dreams (`hf-marth-peach-fod-1a4f1bff`) compares all its 19,499 ticks, Peach's shield drained to breaking in tick 12,229, her flight, fall, landing on her front, getting up and dizziness; Peach vs Sheik on Battlefield (`hf-peach-sheik-bf-266c777a`) compares all its 10,547 ticks, Sheik's shield broken by a hit in tick 658 (landing on her back), and tick 6684's audio frame due as an ARAM load ends (below).

With them, the same 1,448 replays play 1,396 to their ends (357 more) and stop 27 times; 365 play further and none less far. What stopped them then: the read past a thrown Peach's animation (10, now modeled, below), Fox's and Falco's `fn_800C7DC4` (6, four-player matches that now get that far), an item absorbed (`it_8026FAC4`, 2), and once each the Stadium stock icons' `ifStock_802FA118` (a 3.16 Stadium replay at its start), a held item's GObj gone, Samus's ground beam homing, put to sleep by a hit, Bob-omb rain, and Link's and Young Link's variables read as bytes where a hookshot's GObj is (4).

The read past a thrown Peach's animation (10 matches) is modeled: thrown by Marth, she drops her item, and its hand pose (`ftAnim_80070FB4`, then `ftAnim_8006EED4`) binds her part 100 to the thrown animation through the remapping figatree path (`ftAnim_8006F954`'s, from skeleton 33 as `x594` says), whose skip to that part runs past the end of the node list and the animation's data block, into what the fighter's animation buffer holds there. melee-sim models the buffer's contents as the loads that left them (`ft::anim_buf`, [the engine notes](engine.md#loaded-data-the-game-writes-into)), and `melee-oracle diff` compares every byte of both of each fighter's buffers that the model knows with memory after every tick (`Comparer::archives`). Two of the replays are traced (`--fp slippi --heap`, ssbm-rs's `ssbm-run-rf`, replay checks clean) and compare every tick, buffers included, with no difference and no stale read: Peach against Marth on Battlefield (`hf-peach-marth-bf-433885bb-rf`, 3.14, all 11,691 ticks, the read in tick 1713) and against Mario on Battlefield (`hf-peach-mario-bf-7438b645-rf`, 3.15, all 15,117). All ten play to their ends in melee-vs-run. Every read there lands in the archive's own relocation table, which follows its data in the buffer, and only decides how long the walk runs (it binds nothing), so taking those bytes as zeros doesn't change the state; the buffer comparison catches the model's mistakes instead: with the oldest layer dropped, with every load forgetting the ones before, with pointers left unrelocated, or with the ARAM copy not rounded up to 32 bytes, `hf-peach-marth-bf-433885bb-rf` differs in tick 1 (tick 6 for the last), and with Nana's copy from Popo's buffer rounded up, `hf-ics-fox-dl-a7365e16-rf` differs in tick 11.

The audio stop found on the way is fixed:

- `hf-peach-sheik-bf-266c777a`, tick 6684: as Peach raises her shield (`ftCo_800921DC`'s sound 110), melee-sim played it on AX voice 49, whose effect ended the tick before, and ssbm-rs on none yet; the synth node, the driver's voice 92 and its playing count differed, nothing else, from the match's start and taken up from tick 6600 alike. The tick's second animation load ends 1 time base tick before an audio frame is due, and ssbm-rs runs that frame as the load's last chunk handler returns, before the sound starts, where melee-sim ran it at the tick's closing perf-timer read, after it ([the engine notes](engine.md#audio), "When an event runs"). With the frame taken there the replay compares all 10,547 ticks with `--heap`, no difference and no stale read, and taken up from tick 6600 to its end; without it, it differs in tick 6684 again. Sarah's untraced `Game_20260913T221846` meets the same case in tick 1582: traced to 1,900 fields, it compares all 1,881 ticks with the fix and differs in tick 1582 without it.

**Every roster replay.** On 2026-10-15 melee-vs-run played every local replay whose characters are all on `game::roster` (with the Ice Climbers, added the same day) and whose stage is a tournament stage, natively with each replay's own code set's rules: 4,027 replays, 3,862 of `erickfm/melee-ranked-replays` in ssbm-rs's `local/replays/hf/` and 165 of the author's (686 with the Ice Climbers). 75 carried codes their players added and were refused; with those codes known, all but three play (below). The first pass, over the 1,448 replays above and the 125 with Yoshi, after the Shy Guys' fix (below), left 17 stops: Fox's and Falco's wall bounce while knocked down (`fn_800C7DC4`, 6), an item absorbed (`it_8026FAC4`, 2), Link's and Young Link's hookshot word read as bytes in a grab check (4), a VS rule's target icons (`ifStock_802FA118`, 1, and 8 more in the rest), a tip's damage landing after its item was freed (1), Samus's ground grapple homing (`ftCo_800A4A40`, 1), a match with bob-omb rain on (1) and a fighter put to sleep by a hit (`ftCo_800C318C`, 1). The Ice Climbers' replays added a grab let go of as its holder is caught (`ftCo_800DD168`, 1) and a Blizzard puff freed in its first frame (`it_802750F8`, 1). All are fixed, each checked by a traced replay (ssbm-rs's `ssbm-run-rf`, names off, into `local/traces/v5-mixed/`, `--fp slippi --heap`) that compares every tick with no difference and no stale read, to its trace's end unless named:

- `Game_20260605T220809` (four players on Yoshi's Story, all 8,264 ticks): a knocked-down fighter sliding into a wall bounces off it in tick 4630 (`fn_800C7DC4`, `ftCo_DownReflect_*`; off a wall to its right the original moves it up by its TransN's y instead of setting it against the wall, and so does melee-sim).
- `hf-doc-ness-ys-ecdc4d64` (all 12,234 ticks): Ness absorbs an item in tick 10,895 (`ftColl_8007925C`'s absorb branch).
- `hf-falco-ylink-ys-d18f9dc3` (all 9,532 ticks) and `hf-link-falco-ys-e1bc8740` (all 9,111): a grab check with the hookshot out (`fn_800D8E94` tests the word as a GObj), in ticks 2600 and 8333; and the Ice Climbers' `hf-link-ics-bf-2a40237f-rf` in tick 13,473.
- `hf-doc-pika-ps-36c0b713` (all 8,091 ticks) and the Ice Climbers' `hf-puff-ics-bf-fe056970-rf` (all 9,533): their rules show the target icons (`gm_8016B1A8`), sixteen made at the match's start (`ifStock_802FA118`) and placed by the first's process from the stage's target counts (`fn_802F916C`, `Ground_801C1DE4`).
- `hf-doc-marth-bf-d1bc4efe` (all 19,381 ticks): in tick 669 a pill's tip damage lands after the pill is freed; its source GObj's first word is then the free list's link, so its classifier reads as neither a fighter's nor an item's and no statistics are recorded (`ftColl_8007BE3C`). The Ice Climbers' `hf-luigi-ics-ps-eed44ab0-rf` (all 13,136) and `hf-ics-ylink-ps-d78ba2b7-rf` (all 12,595) stopped on the same.
- `hf-roy-samus-fd-56052455` (6,340 ticks): Samus's ground grapple homes on Roy with Z held from tick 5485 (`ftCo_800A4A40`), to the stop below.
- `Game_20260524T174154` (all 760 ticks): the bob-omb rain rule is on in a match shorter than the rain's start (a bomb every 30 frames after the first 1200); melee-sim stopped at the rule's first check, and stops now only where a bomb would fall.
- `Game_20260807T214701` (four players on Final Destination, all 13,428 ticks): Jigglypuff's Sing puts a fighter to sleep in tick 10,352, who sleeps and wakes (`ftCo_DamageSong.c`).
- The Ice Climbers' `hf-ics-ics-ps-f3d90438-rf` (all 11,637 ticks): in tick 8981 an Ice Climber is grabbed while holding someone and lets go (`ftCo_800DD168`).
- The Ice Climbers' `hf-fox-ics-ps-03b0d4e2-rf`: in tick 7122 a Blizzard puff is freed by its first frame's collision, and the original sets its flag in the freed block (`it_802750F8`, which melee-sim looked up by the freed item's GObj).

leaf-check's `common_stops` runs the wall bounce's, the sleep's and the homing's functions from saved calls of four of those replays (`local/calls/common-stops/`, packed) and compares the whole state. Each fix broken on purpose fails its replay where the case first happens: the hookshot test inverted in tick 8070 of `hf-falco-ylink-ys-d18f9dc3`, the absorbed item's side flipped in tick 10,896, the wall bounce's `ftCommonData` x1BC slow-down left out in tick 4630, the sleep's mash timer not running down in tick 10,352, the homing at the fall speed's attribute in tick 5485, the rain from frame 64 in tick 189, the target icon's texture frame 56 in tick 1, and the holder's and the held fighter's branches of `ftCo_800DD168` swapped in tick 8982.

The last 17 stops are fixed too: Nana's computer player around items (16, all in Ice Climbers matches, 15 against Peach, whose turnips lie about) and Samus's ground grapple's button-sequence swing (1). Ten Ice Climbers replays are traced (ssbm-rs's `ssbm-run-rf`, `--port all`, names off, into `local/traces/v5-mixed/`) and compare every tick with `--fp slippi --heap`, no difference and no stale read, to their ends; the 17 stops are these replays (six appear in two sets) and the Samus one, and all 17 now play to their ends in melee-vs-run:

- Nana's item search (`ftCo_800A5F4C`, 12): she looks over the common items each frame she makes her way back to Popo, for the nearest of the most worth (`ftCo_803C5A68`) worth at least her threshold (`CpuFighter::x2C`, 5). The survey's note that its square root read a register its caller left was wrong: its zero test compares with `f29`, which it loads with 0.0 itself. `hf-ics-peach-ps-fdef375b-rf` (all 10,612 ticks), `hf-ics-peach-bf-00b85fb9-rf` (7,789), `hf-peach-ics-fd-0d861bcc-rf` (13,314), `hf-peach-ics-bf-9852011d-rf` (13,075), `hf-peach-ics-bf-c85bd188-rf` (10,852), `hf-ics-peach-ys-f4440f7a-rf` (11,212) and the 3.14 `hf-peach-ics-bf-7f505305-rf` (16,258).
- Nana holding an item (`ftCo_800ADE48`, 3): her decision throws an item it calls dangerous (behavior 14, `ftCo_800AD7FC`), through a test that reads the item's kind as a fighter's action state ([the engine notes](engine.md)); a turnip isn't one, so she keeps it. `hf-ics-peach-bf-dee42fc0-rf` (all 12,414 ticks) and `hf-ics-peach-bf-c5ac9d3f-rf` (12,174).
- Nana jumping at her target (`ftCo_800B7180`, 1, with `ftCo_800A05F4` and `ftCo_800A0798`): `hf-ics-fox-ys-35368f9f-rf` (all 9,832 ticks), from tick 1324.
- `it_802B7160` (1): Samus's ground grapple swing takes its grabbed-victim-only bit from a byte of `it_802B9328`'s frame that nothing between its caller's matrix setup and the swing writes. The original leaves `PSMTXConcat`'s save of `f14` there, 0.0, so the bit is clear ([the engine notes](engine.md)). `hf-roy-samus-fd-56052455` (traced before) compares all 16,856 ticks, the swing in tick 6341. ssbm-rs parts from the recording at frame 6336, after the swing, in its interpreter as with `--port all`, and Slippi Dolphin's playback with resync off parts from it the same way (1,226 differing events from frame 6336, the same values): the recording is at fault, and the trace is the reference.

ssbm-rs's replay checks of the Ice Climbers traces part from their recordings only in ways that leave gameplay alone or that the recording can't reproduce: the 3.16 entry's state flags and a position bit there, Nana's pre-frame C-stick (-0.0 recorded where ssbm-rs records another word, with no change after), and one 3.16 C-stick snap (`hf-ics-peach-bf-c5ac9d3f-rf`, Peach's x from frame 2449).

leaf-check's `nana` runs Nana's frame from five more sets of saved calls of these replays (`local/calls/ics/`, packed) against the machine code, and changes them to reach what the replays don't: every item made a grabbable beam sword worth going for, with Nana apart from Popo (the search's choice, between two turnips lying about in `bf-9852011d-turnips`), the held item made a bob-omb or an egg (the throw and its scripts), and her behavior a short hop at her target (`ftCo_800A0798`). leaf-check's `samus_swing` runs the shot beam's frames (`fn_802B805C`, `local/calls/samus-swing/`) the same way, the swing's among them. Each fix broken on purpose fails: the item search ignoring the worth threshold in tick 3417 of `hf-ics-peach-ps-fdef375b-rf` (and 129 saved frames), a full jump made a short hop in tick 1324 of `hf-ics-fox-ys-35368f9f-rf` (and its saved frame), the swing's bit set in tick 6341 of `hf-roy-samus-fd-56052455` (and its saved frame), the search keeping the first of two items in 57 changed frames, the hop's last wait a frame short in 255, and the held item never thrown, a bob-omb not counted dangerous, or a bob-omb's throw off to the side dropped instead of script 40 in 61, 61 and 49 changed frames (no replay throws).

Stops left: 0.

**Players' codes.** 119 of the 4,991 local replays (113 different ones) carried Gecko codes their players added to their Slippi setups, which melee-hd refused, the survey's 75 among them. `game::player_codes` knows them now ([the engine notes](engine.md#players-codes) say what each does and how it's ported), by unique replay: an X/Z swap (`C2 8034E2AC`, 13), invisible techs (`C2 80080A30`, 9), a fast-fall spark (`C2 8007D5A0`, 6), a quiet announcer (`04 800249A4`, 7), a camera (`C2 801C0A50`, 5), match rules at the scene's entry (`C2 8016E750` with `8016E91C`, 8), SDI colors (`C2 8008E588` with `8008E734`, 2), a dash grab color (`C2 8006A138`, 1), Fountain of Dreams' reflection at 640x480 (`04 801CCE4C`, `801CCE50` and `803E0F3C` to `803E0F48`, 5, 2 of them with `801CCECC` and `801CD078`), Pokémon Stadium's monitor weights (`04 801D2AC0`, `801D2AC8`, `801D2B98`, 6), and, without effect in a replay, Marth's sword trail colors (`C2 80136510`, 2, without Marth), the character select screen's codes (`04 80263384`, 15; `C2 80263064`, 7; `C2 80262F48`, 2; `C2 802668BC`, 3; `04 804DC578`, 2), a set for the title, main menu, sound test and debug menus (`04 801A1CC4` and twelve more, 22) and an empty `00 80000000` line (4). Three are still refused: `C2 8006B80C` (two variants) writes past whatever `r24` points at in `Fighter_procInput`, the fighter's GObj on the console and something else in ssbm-rs, so no port could be checked; they stay refused (one of them with a training-mode set), and no further players' codes will be ported. melee-vs-run plays the other 116 natively, all to their ends. Three of them are matches with items: the match rules code turns items on in a replay's scene (item frequency 3 and a 120-second limit), though not in the online recording's, so their playback, ssbm-rs's as Slippi Dolphin's, plays a different match from the recording, with items ([Items on](#items-on) below). Of all 5,074 local replays now, 3 are refused.

One replay per code is traced (ssbm-rs's `ssbm-run-rf`, names off, `--port all`, into `local/traces/v5-mixed/pc-HASH.trc`) and compares every tick with no difference and no stale read (`--fp slippi --heap`), to its trace's end unless named:

- `pc-7452c0d4` (the X/Z swap, Mario against Bowser on Final Destination, 20,341 ticks); no controller answers in a replay, so leaf-check's `player_codes` checks the decoder against `SPEC2_MakeStatus` with the code.
- `pc-9bf5119c` (invisible techs, Fox against Dr. Mario on Battlefield, all 9,826).
- `pc-a023b8ab` (the fast-fall spark, Luigi against Young Link on Battlefield, 9,292): the code reads `r28` and `r31` from its callers, which ssbm-rs's ports don't leave, so this trace runs `melee/ft/fighter`, `ft_081B`, `ft_084E`, `ftCo_AirCatch`, `ftCo_FallSpecial` and `ftCo_PassiveWall` as machine code (`--port all,-melee/ft/fighter,...`); with `--port all` its run stops at frame 88 at an unmapped read.
- `pc-b176f3ae` (the quiet announcer, Ice Climbers against Marth on Battlefield, 10,792).
- `pc-bebc5463` (the camera, Marth against the Ice Climbers on Battlefield, all 13,371).
- `pc-2dd3ed50` (the match rules' items variant, Fox against Dr. Mario on Pokémon Stadium, all 3,646; see [Items on](#items-on)). `pc-3ec93d19` (Link against Samus on Final Destination, Slippi 3.17, 20,581) and `pc-348bb403` (Dr. Mario against Samus on Battlefield, 20,703): the variants a replay skips.
- `pc-bc88b59c` (SDI colors, Fox against Peach on Dream Land, Slippi 3.15 with UCF 0.8, 13,974).
- `pc-6b26738c` (the dash grab color, Ganondorf against Falco on Battlefield, 10,372).
- `pc-7eb95849` (Fountain of Dreams' reflection, Bowser against Fox there, 10,312) and `pc-e841dca6` (its second pair with the Stadium codes and `804DC578`, Fox against Dr. Mario on Yoshi's Story, 10,133); neither replay with the second pair is on Fountain of Dreams, so no trace reaches that pair's port.
- `pc-62f76ed3` (Stadium's monitor weights, Peach against Falco on a frozen Pokémon Stadium, 13,374) and `pc-bbf2ec71` (on Battlefield, 10,131).
- `pc-7c954610` (Marth's trail colors without Marth, 11,214), `pc-48319424` (the empty line, 9,351), `pc-c2d0508d`, `pc-e59dc50e` and `pc-f0760b06` (the character select screen's, 7,368, 10,130 and 11,214), `pc-fcc4836d` (the menus' set, Mewtwo against Marth, 9,291).

leaf-check's `player_codes` applies each code ported in a function ssbm-rs has a port of to saved calls (the fighters', the display's, the replays' and the audio library's) and runs that function as its machine code against our port in both float modes: the fast-fall spark from `Fighter_procUpdate`'s states (both callers' registers), invisible techs from the fighters' render callback, SDI colors and their reset from the replays' hitlag calls, the dash grab color from `Fighter_ChangeMotionState`, the quiet announcer from the audio library's tick, and `SPEC2_MakeStatus` with the X/Z swap on every combination of X, Z and the response's error bits. Each port broken on purpose fails its check: the swap left out, the techs' state range or frame window narrowed, the spark's side, part or attribute word changed, the announcer at 2, an SDI color, flag or reset changed, the dash grab's flags or state changed (leaf-check), and the camera's zoom rate, the items variant's frequency or item switch, the skipped variants run in a replay, the reflection's height, and the monitor's weight or repeat check (the trace diffs).

### Items on

The three replays whose players' match rules code turns items on in playback are the oracle's first matches with random items: Dr. Mario against Fox, ranked Slippi 3.16 replays from `doc-diamond-diamond-a6` (`local/replays/hf/doc-diamond-diamond-a6/slp/diamond-diamond-HASH.slp`), traced as the other players' codes are (`local/traces/v5-mixed/pc-HASH.trc`). Each compares every tick with no difference and no stale read (`--fp slippi --heap`), to its trace's end, where playback's match ends:

- `pc-2dd3ed50` on Pokémon Stadium (3,646 ticks): a Party Ball (its dynamic bones swing, and it opens into four Bob-ombs), a Fire Flower, a Super Scope that a fighter picks up and fires (charged shots and bursts, the shots skidding along the floor), a Freezie sliding about, a Ray Gun, a Poké Ball, capsules and a box, with Fox's blaster, lasers and Illusion and Dr. Mario's pills and cape.
- `pc-339ac78e` on Yoshi's Story (3,346): an egg that a hit explodes, a Ray Gun, a green shell, a parasol, a box with a Beam Sword in it, and the Shy Guys, whose tops a fighter's feet are tested against every frame.
- `pc-86fbfe04` on Fountain of Dreams (3,886): a Lip's Stick, a Warp Star, a Poké Ball, five capsules (one exploding), a green shell and a Bob-omb.

What they needed, beyond the kinds already ported: an item's dynamic bones (`Item_80268560`, `Item_8026A788`, the destroy's links back), the Ray Gun, Fire Flower, Freezie, egg, Super Scope with its shots and the fighter's scope states, Poké Ball, parasol, Lip's Stick and Warp Star (`it::kinds`, their item code; each kind's use by the fighter other than the scope's isn't ported and stops), a fighter stepping onto an item (`lb_80014638`), an ownerless item's hit (`pl_8003D60C`), the scrolling stages' wind on items (`grLib_801C9E60`, none on the ported stages), the hold flags (`it_8026BDCC`, `it_8026BE28`), and in HSD, the expression argument buffer's allocation (`expEvaluate`'s `arg_buf`, [the engine notes](engine.md#the-allocators)) and the bytecode stack's list nodes, which the allocator model now replays: a Fire Flower's model is the first in these matches to run an expression. Each was found where the trace and melee-hd first parted. `sync-test --rollback 7` passes on all three (3,665, 3,342 and 3,902 ticks checked).

leaf-check's `items_on` runs the new ports from saved calls of the three replays (`local/calls/items-on/hf-pc-HASH`, packed; the capture recipe is in the test's header) against ssbm-rs's ports, each also from its kind's neighbours' captures: the scope's with the buttons, script flag, charge and burst counts changed, a Freezie's floor slope and speed and a Fire Flower's state varied, and the item functions that take a level, state or speed given one. All 333 compare clean. Two checks reach what no replay does: `stomp_lands` runs `ft_80081A00`'s saved calls with every item made one to step on and the fighter's feet coming down onto it, so the stomp's marks and the bounce compare, and `stepped_on_matches` runs `lb_80014638` on spans built for each of its branches; `explosive_kinds` checks `pl_8003D60C` on every kind. The check found the hold flags' `x1A` set the wrong way round, which no replay reaches. Each port broken on purpose fails a check or a diff: the Ray Gun's recoil, the Fire Flower's spawn, the Freezie's slope, the egg's explosion count, the scope's charge levels, shot speed, charge rate, shot sound and burst count, the Poké Ball's spawn, the parasol's spin, the Lip's Stick's spore flag, the Warp Star's spin, the dynamic bones' first link, the stomp's side and the stomp test's interpolation (leaf-check), and the argument buffer's size, the list nodes' frees, the stage wind and the dynamic bones' ground check (the diffs). The Fire Flower's held loops (its states 2 to 5) are reached by no saved state, a fighter never having picked one up: breaking them passes. Nor does any replay tell the ownerless hit's explosion flag apart (breaking it passes; `explosive_kinds` checks the kinds).

The Sudden Death replay the milestone's survey lists for the Bob-omb rain (`Game_20260524T174154`, Young Link against Fox on Final Destination) compares all 760 ticks with no difference: its sudden death ends before the rain starts, so it spawns no Bob-omb (`Ground_801C0A70` isn't reached).

### Items held

Milestone 4's task 7 ports the held and swung items, checked on the `held` family's oracle matches (`items-held-fd`, Samus against Luigi on Final Destination; `items-held-bf`, Mario against Pikachu on Battlefield; `items-held-ps`, Link against Jigglypuff on Pokémon Stadium; [oracle sets](#oracle-sets)) and three more the `held-extra` set adds, because the level 9 AI left most of the family's code unplayed in those: `items-cannon-fd` (Fox against Marth, the barrel cannon alone), `items-swing-bf` (Captain Falcon against Peach: the parasol, Star Rod, Lip's Stick, fan and Screw Attack) and `items-heavy-ps` (Falco against Sheik: the box and barrel). Each is recorded, replay-checked and traced as the other oracle matches are (`local/bench/oracle-m4/`, `local/traces/v5-mixed/oracle-items-NAME.trc`); the Battlefield ones' replay checks differ from the recordings only in one pre-match frame's entry position (frame -113), which their controllers' checks don't. With `--fp hardware --heap`, every one compares every traced tick with no difference and no stale read but `items-held-ps`:

- `items-held-fd` (7,429 ticks): boxes and barrels lifted overhead (`ftpickupitem_80094694`'s heavy pickup, into `ftCo_Lift`), the Screw Attack, fan and Star Rod spawning, falling and lying, and boxes a fighter opened counting in its player's records (`ftLib_800874CC`, `pl_8003E4A4`).
- `items-held-bf` (7,430): the fan, a barrel cannon lying and falling, and the Lip's Stick's swing: its spores (`it_8029A114`) drifting and slowing, and their hit planting a flower on Pikachu's head (`ftCommon_8007FC7C`, `it_8029A748`): his ears hidden for it, the petals grown with the damage, a little damage every few frames, mashed against (`ftCommon_8007FFD8`, whose mash, `ftCommonData` x6FC, is an integer read as a float) and falling when its time runs out.
- `items-swing-bf` (7,428): fan swings landing and growing the fan (`ft_800CD65C`) and the Star Rod's swings spending its charges on stars; `items-cannon-fd` (7,427): barrel cannons spawning, falling and lying, though no fighter picked one up; `items-heavy-ps` (7,425): heavy pickups and carrying, one of whose item blocks, new from the heap, showed an item's untouched words (docs/engine.md).
- `items-held-ps` stops in tick 3333 (replay frame 3209) at a ReDead (`it_802E8BCC`), an adventure enemy a container dropped from the monsters' table (`it_8026F6BC`), with no difference in the 3,332 ticks before.
