The ReDead isn't a held item: its port is about 50 functions (`itleadead.c`) with some 15 of the zako engine's and its grab state (`ftCo_800C7590.c`), left for the enemies' work.

`sync-test --rollback 7` passes on each to the same tick (on `items-held-ps`, the 3,326 ticks before the ReDead).

The items used as they're picked up (`ftpickupitem_8009447C`, and `ftpickupitem_80094B6C` for a pickup cut short by a hit) are ported for the healing items with ported variables, the heart and tomato (`Fighter_8006CF5C`: the heal counted out a point a frame, with its sound, the player's display and the healed count), and the coin, beside task 9's power-ups ([Power-ups](#power-ups)); food and the lucky egg heal by their amounts since task 8 (`it_8026B47C`, [Shooting and thrown items](#shooting-and-thrown-items)), and Whispy's apple stops there. With them, task 3's `items-ported-fd` match now compares every one of its 7,427 ticks with no difference and no stale read (it had stopped in tick 1424 at the Party Ball's record of what it gave, `ftLib_800874CC`), and with task 9's hammer merged in, so do `items-ported-bf` (7,425 ticks, which had stopped at the hammer in tick 649) and `items-ported-ps` (7,428, which had stopped at it in tick 6654). A heart's spawn cleared the top bit of a copy of its flag byte, so the bit never cleared and its destroy read the byte's stale bits (stale reads from tick 1744 once hearts were picked up); the byte is now a top-bit flag like the turnip's (`it::TurnipFlag`, spliced by `tools/structgen`), which an event's heart sets.

leaf-check's `items_held` runs the new ports from saved calls of all six matches (`local/calls/items-held/items-NAME`, packed; the recipe is in the test's header) against ssbm-rs's ports, each also from its unit's neighbours' captures with the fighter's buttons and stick varied, and the swing entries, the Screw Attack's aerial jump, the barrel cannon's grab, a fighter caught by it, an item's hit record and a thrown Lip's Stick's spores given arguments: 267 of 277 functions compare clean. The barrel cannon's ride is blocked, and its port's aliases are held back (`// Ports`, docs/porting.md): the AI never threw one at a fighter or held one, so no saved state has a fighter inside (`ftCo_Barrel_*`, the launch `ftCo_800C92E4`, `it_80295F38`) or a cannon in hand (`it_3F14_Logic5_Dropped`). Each port broken on purpose fails a diff or `items_held`: the lift's walk and turn, the heavy throws' smash directions, the thrown Screw Attack, its jump's height, the fan's growth and its frames, the Star Rod's charges and its stars' spin, the spores' speed and slowing, the barrel cannon's lock-out, the flower's growth, stem and mash, and the containers' records (the diffs caught the spores, the fan, the Star Rod, the flower and the records; `items_held` the rest). Two breaks pass both: the heavy pickup's height scaling (`Fighter_804D6524` x8), which no fighter of height 1 tells apart, and the barrel cannon's roll, grab and launch, which no saved state reaches. The checks found ftCommon_8007E79C and ftCommon_8007E7E4 calling the base item hooks (`ftData_OnItemDrop`, `ftData_OnItemPickup`), which for Link and Young Link leave their sheathed sword and shield alone, where the swings had called the Ext hooks.

### Power-ups

The milestone's power-ups oracle matches (`items-powerups-fd`, `-bf`, `-ps` in `$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/bench/oracle-m4`, [Oracle sets](#oracle-sets)) compare every tick to their traces' ends with no difference and no stale read (`--fp hardware --heap`): Roy against Zelda on Final Destination (7,423 ticks), Bowser against Pichu on Battlefield (7,430) and Mewtwo against Mr. Game & Watch on Pokémon Stadium (7,431), each two `BENCH_AI` players in a two-minute match with only the power-ups on. Between them they spawn 45 power-ups: Warp Stars (one ridden up and crashed on Final Destination), Starmen taken, Poison Mushrooms taken, Cloaking Devices and Bunny Hoods put on, Metal Boxes broken open, and hammers swung and let go. No one takes a Super Mushroom in any of the three, so the growth runs only in leaf-check.

What they needed: the six kinds' item code (`it::kinds::{star, kinoko, metalb, rabbitc, spycloak}`, with the Poison Mushroom's own landing, `it_8026E414`, not the Super Mushroom's) and the Warp Star's ride and crash (`it_80294364` to `it_802947CC`); on the fighter's side the items taken on touch (`ftColl_80077C60`'s Starman and mushrooms), the Starman's invincibility and its timer (`ftColl_8007B7FC`), the mushrooms' eight states and the fighter's scale joint they animate (`ft::co::kinoko`, `ft_0D27.c`, and the attributes at a height scale, `ftCo_800CF6E8`), metal (`ftmetal.c`: the look, the attributes and the shared metal material, `ftMaterial_800BF2B8`), the Cloaking Device (`ft_0C88.c`), the Bunny Hood (`ftCommon_8007F8E8` to `ftCommon_8007FA58`), the hammer's seven states (`ft::co::hammer`), the Warp Star's states (`ft::co::warpstar`), the use-on-pickup effects of these items (`ftpickupitem_8009447C`, `ftpickupitem_80094B6C`, which also hold task 7's heal and coin, [Items held](#items-held)), and the timers in `Fighter_procAnim`. Two findings beyond the ports: a cloaked fighter's materials set up refraction's textures, whose screen copy is a shadow-coordinate texture, so drawing one brings the camera's inverse view matrix up to date ([the engine notes](engine.md#state-the-render-pass-writes)); while another fighter cast a shadow the stage's materials did that anyway, and the diff found it only when the other was KO'd. And the Warp Star's descent turns its rider by π/2 rounded to single precision (`frsp`), which a plain store truncates. `sync-test --rollback 7` passes on all three (7,318 ticks each).

leaf-check's `items_powerups` runs the new ports from saved calls of the three matches (`local/calls/items-powerups/oracle-items-powerups-M`, packed, and `-pickup` sets for the pickup functions main had ported; the recipe is in the test's header) against ssbm-rs's ports, each also from its unit's other captures: the Warp Star's, hammer's (the drop's window opened with A pressed and a shoulder held, as no match dropped a hammer), metal and cloak, mushrooms', Bunny Hood and pickup units, and each item kind, with the arguments the larger functions take (an end's intangibility flag, a state to fall into, a scale, a cloak's or a metal's frames, a `Fighter*` from the GObj). All 248 compare clean; six helpers run inside their callers (`it_80293660`, `it_80294364`, `it_802944AC`, `ftCo_800CF6E8`, `it_80272818`, `pl_8003FDF4`). Each port broken on purpose fails a check or a diff: the Starman's bounce sound, the mushroom's walking speed, the Poison Mushroom's landing, the Metal Box's opening flag, the Cloaking Device's landing, the hood's parts, the Warp Star's size, the Starman's frames, the scale's interpolation and a shrink's frames, the cloak's and metal's joint flags and blending, the cloak's color animation, the hammer's turn, swings and drop statistic, the Warp Star's climb, drift and crash quake, the hood's and the mushroom's attribute weights and the metal sound (leaf-check), and the refraction setup's inverse view matrix and a Poison Mushroom's touch (the diffs).

The Battlefield match's replay check (`items-powerups-bf.check.log`) shows six divergences between the recording and ssbm-rs's playback; the controllers alone (`items-powerups-bf.pads.log`, the recording run's own layout) show none, and the trace is of the playback, which melee-hd matches. They don't matter: two are Bowser's entry frames (frames -118 and -117, position y before he appears), and four are Pichu's post-frame "hitstun" word (`fp+0x2340`) during Thunder's loop (state 360, frames 1846 to 1849), which holds a heap pointer in that state; the recording run and the playback lay out the heap differently ([Oracle sets](#oracle-sets)), so both are layout, not gameplay.

### Shooting and thrown items

The milestone's shooting and thrown items oracle matches (`items-shoot-fd`, `-bf`, `-ps` in `$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/bench/oracle-m4`, [Oracle sets](#oracle-sets)) compare every tick to their traces' ends with no difference and no stale read (`--fp hardware --heap`): Ness against Yoshi on Final Destination (7,428 ticks), Donkey Kong against Young Link on Battlefield (7,430) and Dr. Mario against Ganondorf on Pokémon Stadium (7,429), each two `BENCH_AI` players in a two-minute match with only that family's eight kinds on (the egg, Ray Gun, Freezie, food, motion-sensor bomb, Flipper, Super Scope and Fire Flower). Between them they spawn seven Ray Guns, six foods, ten motion-sensor bombs, four Flippers, five Super Scopes, nine Fire Flowers and a Freezie (no egg): rays fired and hitting, a food eaten in each match, bombs thrown, stuck to the stage and set off, a Flipper thrown and settled in the air, Super Scope shots and Fire Flower flames.

What they needed: the kinds' item code (`it::kinds::{foods, msbomb, flipper, lgunray, lgunbeam}`, the flame being `itlgunbeam`'s, `It_Kind_L_Gun_Beam`, which the Fire Flower spawns; `It_Kind_F_Flower_Flame` is the Lip's Stick's flower, task 7's), the Ray Gun's and the Fire Flower's shots from the item (`it_8028E774`, `it_8028E79C`, `it_80292EF8`, `it_80292F14`), the fighter's shooting states (`ft::co::{item_lgun, item_fflower}`, `ft_0CDF.c`, `ft_0CE3.c`), food's heal amount (`it_8026B47C`, which the pickups' shared use now takes for every healing item, into task 7's `Fighter_8006CF5C`), a food's model swap (`it_80273318`) and count (`it_80274EE8`), an item's attack in its owner's statistics (`it_8027B564`, `pl_80037DF4`), an inert box (a stuck bomb's) touching a fighter or an item (`ftColl_8007925C`, `it_802706D0`: it marks the item touched, `xDCE` bit 6 and `toucher`, for the item's own touch callback), the Ray Gun bonus's count (`it_8026C1D4`, from `pl_8003EAAC`), and a second KO mark's render, which reads the display before the first ([Melee's own bugs](melee-bugs.md#a-second-ko-mark-reads-the-display-before-the-first)). And one model change: an item's `x8` and `x21` to `x23`, which no code reads or writes, are left out of the mirror, since an item in a block the item pool grows from reused heap holds the heap's old words there (a Fire Flower flame in tick 5940 on Battlefield; [the engine notes](engine.md#uninitialized-values)). `sync-test --rollback 7` passes on all three (7,318 ticks each).

leaf-check's `items_shoot` runs the 144 new ports from saved calls of the three matches (`local/calls/items-shoot/oracle-items-shoot-M`, packed; the recipe is in the test's header) against ssbm-rs's ports, each also from its unit's other captures: the Ray Gun's and the Fire Flower's states with A held, pressed or not, the script's throw flags and the Fire Flower's counters varied, the Flipper's spin, flight timer and (settled) hitbox timer, the bomb's armed bit, a reflected ray's speed factor, the flame's turns with each combination of surfaces, the Flipper's spin and push with the item's own points, and the statistics' attack flags beyond the matches' (whose blasts count an attack id alone). All 141 compare clean; three helpers run inside their callers (`it_8028E774`, `it_80292EF8`, `it_80273318`), and `it_8027542C`, which only a Flipper fixed to a stage calls, runs from the Flipper's captures. Each port broken on purpose fails a check or a diff: a dish's heal, the food's landing sound, the heal's color animation, the bomb's stick sound, blast flags and thrown box, the Flipper's spin decay and settled life, the ray's stretch, speed and reflected speed (the reflector's factor varied), the flame's turn rate and its random draws' order, the Ray Gun's shot sound and landing switch, the Fire Flower's wait after a press, puff sound and attack ids, the statistics' counts and the owner check, the heal amount, the food count and the Ray Gun bonus's count (leaf-check), and the heal's percent a frame and a second KO mark's visibility, whose draw changes the display pass's matrices and heap (the diffs). Not caught: the Flipper's wall bounce (no captured flight meets a wall), an inert box's `toucher` (cleared with the item's other hit records each frame before anything reads it), an inert box against an item's hurtbox (no match reaches it) and the Ray Gun bonus's point (no player fires all 16 shots of one gun).

**The items-shoot replay checks.** Final Destination's replay check (`items-shoot-fd.check.log`) shows 274 divergences between the recording and ssbm-rs's playback, and the controllers alone (`items-shoot-fd.pads.log`, the recording run's own layout) none; the trace is of the playback, which melee-hd matches. All 274 are one field of one fighter in one state: port 1's post-frame "hitstun" word (`fp+0x2340`, Slippi's misc action state) while Ness is in Yoshi's egg (`YoshiEgg`, state 277), on frames 96–151, 570–623, 1530–1583, 3476–3531 and 5326–5379 (every frame of his five eggs). In that state the word is `mv.co.yoshiegg.x0`, Yoshi's GObj, a heap address, which each run lays out differently: 0x80E0C1A0 in the recording run, 0x80E0ED80 in the replay check's playback (Show Player Names on, whose codes take 0x4C0 more of the heap) and 0x80E0E8C0 in the trace's playback and the capture runs (names off). The check compares the word's low half and the next bytes (its field window starts two bytes into the word), so only `C1A0` against `ED80` shows. Gameplay only ever follows the pointer, so this is layout, not gameplay (as Pichu's word during Thunder is in the power-ups' Battlefield match, above), and ssbm-rs's playback of the match is a sound oracle for every item; nothing else is needed. Battlefield's two divergences are Young Link's entry frames (frames -113 and -112, position y before he appears), as in the power-ups' Battlefield match; Pokémon Stadium's check has none.

### A coin match's coins

The coin oracle matches (`coins-fd` and `coins-items-fd` in `$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/bench/oracle-m4-coins`, the `coins` set of [Oracle sets](#oracle-sets)) compare every tick to their traces' ends with no difference and no stale read (`--fp hardware --heap`): Fox against Marth on Final Destination, two `BENCH_AI` players in a two-minute coin match, with items off (7,427 ticks, 365 coins) and with capsules on (7,427 ticks, 384 coins, broken capsules scattering some). They needed the coins themselves (`it::kinds::coin`, `it_2E5A.c`'s `It_Kind_Unk4`: the three tiers, the flight out of a fighter, the bounces, the lifetime and the waits before the owner and the others may take one), a hit's and a KO's drops (`ftCommon_8007EA90`, `ftCo_800D4E50`), a broken container's (`it_8026F8B4`, `it_802E5F00`), and the pickup by touch (`ft_8007C77C`). A coin's flag byte keeps only the two bits the coin and the pickup use (`it::CoinFlags`, spliced in by `tools/structgen`), and the pickup's sparkle reads the contact point the touch test writes past the coin's variables. `it_802E609C` returns a register its caller left when it makes no coin; only `it_802E5F8C` uses the result, and its register then holds the count it was given, which the port returns. `sync-test --rollback 7` passes on both (7,318 ticks each).

leaf-check's `items_shoot` runs the 24 coin functions from both matches' saved calls (`local/calls/items-shoot/oracle-coins-*`) and their unit's, coin functions only on coins. Each port broken on purpose fails it: the wait before others may take a coin, the count's random spread, the flight's sideways push, a tier's threshold, the landing's slowdown, a hit's mirrored angle, a KO's half, a container's coin count, a scatter's facing and the bone's last position; the pickup's owner check fails both diffs instead (tick 240 and 236), no saved pickup coming in the waits. Not caught: the room made below the coins' limit (`it_802E6658`), which no saved state comes near.

Both matches' replay checks and controllers checks (`*.check.log`, `*.pads.log`) agree with their recordings through frame 7,200, no divergence. The set's first seeds were the held items' extra matches' (`oracle_sets` wants each seed once), so it was recorded again with its own (`0x1F0F_0601`, `0x1F0F_0602`); the first `coins-items-fd` had shown 70 divergences, a coin's x position a unit in the last place from frame 1,877, which its controllers check didn't (the recording run's `BENCH_AI` hook going another way, not ssbm-rs's playback). The traces are of the playback, which ssbm-rs's lockstep of the capture runs checks against the machine code (no mismatch in the 43 and 45 functions they reached).
### Pokémon
Milestone 4's task 10 ports the Poké Ball's release and the Pokémon, checked on the `items` set's six Poké Ball matches ([Oracle sets](#oracle-sets)): `items-pokeball1-fd` (Fox against Marth), `items-pokeball2-bf` (Falco against Sheik), `items-pokeball3-ps` (Captain Falcon against Peach), `items-pokeball4-fd` (Samus against Luigi), `items-pokeball5-bf` (Mario against Pikachu) and `items-pokeball6-ps` (Link against Jigglypuff), two-minute matches with only the Poké Ball on, seeded so that between them 23 of the 30 Pokémon come out (88 releases). Bellossom comes out eight times, Togepi and Weezing seven, Unown, Clefairy and Chansey six, Goldeen, Marill and Blastoise five, Porygon2 and Charizard four, Cyndaquil, Scizor, Wobbuffet, Venusaur and Snorlax three, Moltres, Chikorita and Lugia twice, Entei, Electrode, Zapdos and Ho-oh once; Articuno, Raikou, Suicune, Ditto, Staryu, Mew and Celebi never.
What's ported: the release (`it_279C.c`'s Pokémon part, `it::pokemon`: the weighted draw that leaves out the last two Pokémon, the release's variables from the ball's attributes, the owner's records, and every Pokémon's growth out of the ball, its fall, its root bone's motion taken as its own, its launch when hit through the enemies' common data, `it_804D6D40`) and all 23 Pokémon the six matches release, each unit whole with its items: Goldeen, Chikorita and its leaf, Snorlax, Blastoise and its water, Weezing and its gas, Charizard and its flames, Moltres, Zapdos, Wobbuffet, Scizor, Unown and its swarm, Entei, Bellossom, Electrode, Lugia and its three Aeroblasts, Ho-oh and its Sacred Fire, Clefairy, Togepi, Chansey (her eggs are the common egg, `it_80288C88`), Porygon2, Cyndaquil and its flame, Marill (whose unit also holds Cyndaquil's flame's callbacks) and Venusaur, besides the items' background flash command (`Command_09`, Moltres's and Zapdos's scripts). A Pokémon's variables are the union's bytes, read and written at the decomp's offsets (`it::pokemon::vars`), because every Pokémon shares the release's first 0x60 bytes and overlaps them with its own fields (Bellossom's `x8` is the release's bone motion's y); each byte is stale until written, with a bitfield's written bits tracked alone ([the engine notes](engine.md#uninitialized-values)).
With `--fp hardware --heap`, all six compare every tick to their traces' ends with no difference and no stale read: `items-pokeball1-fd` 7,427 ticks, `2-bf` 7,425, `3-ps` 7,428, `4-fd` 7,429, `5-bf` 7,430 and `6-ps` 7,430, so every one of the 88 releases is checked in a straight run; the `--from` windows that checked each Pokémon while an earlier unported one stopped the straight run are no longer needed. `sync-test --rollback 7` passes on all six (7,318 ticks each).
leaf-check's `items_pokemon` runs every function of the ported units from saved calls of the six matches (`local/calls/items-pokemon/oracle-items-pokeballN`, packed, 466 MB; the recipe is in the test's header), each also from its unit's neighbours' captures on items of the kinds whose tables name it: 519 functions compare clean. Two exceptions to the kind rule: the forgetting callbacks (each only `it_8026B894`) take any Pokémon or Pokémon's item, so Lugia's Aeroblasts' is reached, and Lugia's search for a point to fire at (`it_802D208C`, and the firing callback and shot that run it) runs from neighbours only in Lugia's firing state, the only one in which it ends; from any other state the call gets a null GObj, which stops the reference at once. Chansey's own egg (`It_Kind_Lucky_Egg`, 19 functions) is ported but blocked, its aliases held back: she laid only common eggs in the six matches. The training mode's and the event matches' Pokémon draws (`it_8027A780`, `it_8027A9B8`) and the card's rare-Pokémon records (`gm_80165084`, `gm_80172C04`, `gm_80172BC4`) aren't ported and stop: a release reaches the last only when both of its 1-in-251 draws come up, which none of the 88 did. Each port broken on purpose fails `items_pokemon` or a diff window (between three and five breaks per Pokémon, and eleven of thirteen on the release and Bellossom). The breaks that passed reach no saved state: Bellossom's ledge test at exactly zero and its fall at exactly its limit, Marill's step end, turn and damage limit (it never lands from a step, meets a wall or is hit), Togepi's draw on a weight boundary, Wobbuffet's swing and counter (never hit in a reachable window), an egg's or leaf's life running out (every one hits the stage first), Chikorita's fall cap, Weezing's first gas's slowing (both gases' attributes hold the same value), Cyndaquil's flame turning off a sloped surface, Scizor's target choice (one opponent) and fly-off, and fused multiply-adds whose products are exact (a facing of ±1). Goldeen and Charizard took four breaks each, Clefairy and Porygon2 three, and all failed `items_pokemon` but Goldeen's fall-off sound's margin below the bottom blast zone: no Goldeen falls out of the stage, and its one saved destroy is its life running out. Snorlax, Entei, Lugia and Ho-oh took three to six breaks each; those that passed were the Aeroblasts' speed factor by kind and the second Aeroblast's count step (no saved state tells the kinds' attributes apart) and Ho-oh's stop 10 below where it came out (it never sinks that far, in `items_pokemon` or a diff).
Two harness fixes came with it: the importer reads the executable's static string buffers (`grPs_8049F040`, Pokémon Stadium's), so `--from` works on its matches, and `diff --from` keeps the importer's dangling slots, so a pointer stale since before the starting tick compares by address every tick, as it does at the import. The Battlefield matches' replay checks differ from their recordings only in entry frames (frame -113's position y) and, in `items-pokeball2-bf`, in Sheik's x position for the 40 frames from 1857, which the controllers' check (the recording run's own boot) doesn't show; the trace is of the playback, which melee-hd matches.
**The rest (2026-10-20, `feat/pokemon-rest`).** The `pokemon` set's three matches release the five the six never did: `items-pokeball7-fd` (Fox against Marth; Articuno twice, and Mew in its playback), `items-pokeball8-fd` (Mario against Pikachu; Raikou twice, Staryu three times) and `items-pokeball9-fd` (Fox against Marth again; Suicune, Staryu four times), two-minute matches with only the Poké Ball on at frequency very high (121 releases), on seeds a search found: `melee-bench`'s candidates `0x1F0F_0801` on, recorded by ssbm-rs and each recording's releases read from its item events, until every target had come out (20 candidates recorded, 699 releases: Articuno 5 times, Raikou 9, Suicune 3, Staryu 39, Mew never). The search's recordings never released Mew, and it took a playback to see why: ssbm-rs's recording runs of these matches don't apply Slippi's General Codes, so nothing is unlocked and Mew's draw (`gm_80164ABC`) refuses, while the playback that the traces are of applies them. `items-pokeball7-fd`'s replay check shows it: from frame 6,994 its playback releases Mew where the recording released Goldeen, and differs from the recording from there on (its controllers check, the recording's own run again, doesn't). Celebi can't come out with Slippi's codes (their Disable Special Messages answers the card's records test with the index it is asked, so the trophies' check fails on record 0; [the player](player.md#items-toggle-whats-left), "The Pokémon"), so no match can release it and it isn't ported. `items-pokeball8-fd`'s replay check differs from its recording in port 2's post-frame misc value for 149 frames from 1,922 (a value the playback reads differently, its controllers check clean); its playback releases the same Pokémon.

What came with them: Articuno (`itfreezer.c`), Raikou and Suicune (`itraikou.c`, `itsuikun.c`, copies of Entei's unit, which now runs all three as an `entei::Beast` with its callbacks, effect and sounds), Mew, and Staryu with its star (`ithitodeman.c`, and `ftLib_FindLowestPercentOpponent` for whom it follows, its fighter kept as a handle in its variables), and the rare draws as the game makes them: `it_8027A4D4` asks the card for Celebi and Mew (`gm_80165084`, `gm_80164ABC`) and records the one drawn (`gm_80172C04`, `gm_80172BC4`), which `gm::Unlocks` models with the records word and Slippi's two codes on it (`gmMainLib_8015D94C`, `gmMainLib_8015D818`); the stop on every first 1-in-251 draw is gone.

With `--fp hardware --heap`, all three compare every tick to their traces' ends with no difference and no stale read: `items-pokeball7-fd` 7,427 ticks, `8-fd` 7,429 and `9-fd` 7,427; `sync-test --rollback 7` passes on all three (7,318 ticks each). leaf-check's `items_pokemon` runs every new function from the three matches' saved calls (`local/calls/items-pokemon/oracle-items-pokeball7-fd` to `9-fd`, packed, 417 MB, the same recipe), each also from its unit's neighbours' captures: 606 functions compare clean, the 87 new ones among them (Staryu's easing, `it_802D48B0`, runs inside its following, which covers it), and the 19 of Chansey's own egg stay blocked (she laid only common eggs in the nine matches). Two tests check the draws: `items_pokemon_rare_draws` runs every saved draw (`it_8027A4D4`) again with its seed moved to where Celebi's or Mew's 1 in 251 comes up, the card's codes Slippi's or the original's, every trophy recorded, all but one (Mew's own, for Mew's draw) or every record, and the characters unlocked by the code, the save or not at all (320 runs compare clean), and `card_records` runs the records' test, the trophies' check and the records (`gmMainLib_8015D94C`, `gmMainLib_8015D818`, `gm_80165084`, `gm_80172BC4`, `gm_80172C04`, and `gm_80164ABC`) as the original's machine code over every combination of the codes and random records, since ssbm-rs's ports model the two records codes only together. The importer now follows a stale word to a joint only when the block it names still has a joint's class: an item's box in `items-pokeball7-fd` kept the address of a freed joint whose block no longer held one, and following it read garbage as a joint (the word is unread, `HitJObj`, so it dangles now, compared by address). Each port broken on purpose fails `items_pokemon`: Articuno's rise by the wrong attribute and its frost's count, Raikou's second roar, Suicune's effect, Mew's side and rise, Staryu's easing, offset and kick back, and its star's life, and on the draws the trophies' check without Celebi's exclusion, the cut-short test answering 1, a record left unwritten, the rare draw leaving `rare_spawned` and Mew's draw left unrecorded. Not caught: whom Staryu follows on a tie of percents (the first fighter), which no saved release meets.

Left: Ditto and Celebi, whom no VS match can release, Chansey's own egg, and the training and event draws.

### The items toggle's leftovers

The items audit's tasks 1 to 3 ([the player](player.md#items-toggle-whats-left)) are checked on the `leftovers` set ([Oracle sets](#oracle-sets)), recorded, replay-checked, controllers-checked and traced as the other oracle matches are (`local/bench/oracle-m4/`, `local/traces/v5-mixed/oracle-NAME.trc`). With `--fp hardware --heap`, each compares every traced tick with no difference and no stale read, and `sync-test --rollback 7` passes on it:

- `items-leftovers-fd` (Fox against Falco, 11,032 ticks; sync test 10,918): Falco spun up by the Screw Attack (`ftCo_800D3004`, `DamageScrew` with its landing flag), the common parasol opened and closed in the hand 73 times (`ftCommon_8007E83C`), a motion-sensor bomb reflected, and a hit knocking off a Bunny Hood (`Fighter_8006CDA4`'s `x197C`).
- `items-leftovers-bf` (Captain Falcon against Bowser, 11,033; sync test 10,918): five Bunny Hoods knocked off and the parasol opened in the hand.
- `items-box-kongo-jungle` (Mario against Luigi, 7,430; sync test 7,318): a box riding the river (the river's event 2, `it_80286088`), opened, and its path destroyed with it (`itBox_Logic1_Destroyed`, `grKongo_801D8058`).
- `items-foods-yoshis-story` (Samus against Yoshi, 7,430; sync test 7,318): 15 Shy Guys carrying food, placed every frame (`it_8028F968`, `it_8028F9D8`), four dropping it when hit (`it_8028F8E4`) and ten leaving the stage with it (`it_8028F9B8`).

The Kongo Jungle match first stopped in tick 532, when Mario's and Luigi's attacks clanked and Luigi's rebound loaded its animation into his buffer: Luigi's animation file repeats some of Mario's byte for byte, and the trace's scan named the copy in Luigi's buffer as Mario's. The comparison now takes the same offset into archives with the same bytes as the same data (`Comparer::compare_data`); nothing in the simulation differed.

The replay checks: Final Destination's has no divergence; Battlefield's two are Bowser's entry frame (-113's position y), as in the other Battlefield matches; Kongo Jungle's two are Mario's entry frames (-118 and -117, position y); Yoshi's Story's 137 are the entry frames (-122 and -121's off-screen flag, -113 and -112's position y) and 133 frames of Samus's post-frame "hitstun" word while she's in Yoshi's egg (state 277), where the word holds Yoshi's GObj, a heap address each run lays out differently (as in `items-shoot-fd`, [Shooting and thrown items](#shooting-and-thrown-items)). The controllers' checks have none. The traces are of the playback, which melee-hd matches.

leaf-check's `items_leftovers` runs every port from saved calls of the four matches (`local/calls/items-leftovers/oracle-NAME`, packed; the recipe is in the test's header) against ssbm-rs's ports, and each function that takes only a GObj also from every other capture of its side, the fighters' or the items', with the KO states' frozen flag set and clear, the Screw Attack's landing flag both ways and a fast fall (the kinds' own functions only on their kind, and the items' also from `items_shoot`'s captures of motion-sensor bombs, Flippers and foods in flight, hitting and lying): all 42 functions compare clean, from 156 saved calls. Each port broken on purpose fails it or a diff: the Screw Attack's launch speed and its check's answer, the parasol tumble's kept fast fall and its IASA, the common parasol's step speed, the tumble's parasol branch (a saved tumble's entry holds a parasol), the frozen KOs' rumble, effect and spin, the food's pickup when let fall, its flag when carried, its going, its height when placed, the Shy Guy's food facing at its spawn, the fall when hit, the depth when carried and the going when it leaves, Kongo Jungle's river event and the box taking its path with it, the bomb's burst and its reflection, the Flipper's reflection and shield bounce, and the Bunny Hood taken off (`items_leftovers`). Not caught: the Screw Attack's landing branch (`cmd_vars[0]`), which no saved state lands from (the Final Destination match runs it for 22 frames, none landing), and the hood's draw at its boundary (`<` against `<=`), which no saved hit lands on.

### The containers' monsters

A box, barrel or Party Ball that opens sometimes releases a monster instead of items (`it_8026F6BC`: 1 opening in 128, a Goomba, ReDead, Octorok or Topi weighted 2:3:1:1). The `monsters` set ([Oracle sets](#oracle-sets)) has two matches seeded for each, recorded by melee-hd and made the oracle by ssbm-rs's replay check and trace of the recording.

**The ReDead** (`it::kinds::leadead`, `itleadead.c` whole but `it_802EA9FC`, the stage enemy spawner's): standing, walking toward the nearest fighter, turning at walls and ledges, lunging, holding a fighter (`ft::co::capture_leadead`, `ftCo_800C7590.c` but the Like Like's `fn_800C7890`, with `ftCo_800C7434.c`'s damage callbacks `fn_800C74CC` and `fn_800C7568`) until it mashes free, dodging a hit to lie down and get up, knockbacks, and the KO flights; with what it needs of the enemies' common code (`it::zako`: the model turned to the floor, `it_8027BBF4` to `it_8027C56C`, the walk along a slope, `it_8027C8D0`, the hurt sounds, the bounces and landings that end a KO's flight, the drop and throw, the element's extra damage, the 1P mode's trophy roll), and of the item engine (`it_80271590`, `it_80274FDC`, `it_80275070`, `it_802759DC`, `it_8026BD3C`, `it_8026BD54`, `ftLib_GetPartJObj`). The monsters' variables are the union's bytes, each stale until written (`VarsKind::Zako`, the Pokémon's store), since the enemies' common words overlap each kind's own. With `--fp hardware --heap`, every tick compares with no difference and no stale read:

- `items-held-ps` (7,430 ticks, the ReDead the held family's match released at frame 3,209; it had stopped there);
- `monsters-redead-fd` (7,429 ticks: the ReDead from frame 752 to 1,759 grabs Mario and holds him from frame 1,010 to 1,090 until he mashes free);
- `monsters-redead-bf` (7,430 ticks: the ReDead from frame 1,087 to 2,625, knocked down, lying and getting up);
- `monsters-codes-redead-fd` (7,429 ticks: the first batch's ssbm-rs recording of `monsters-redead-fd`'s seed, a ReDead from frame 2,825 to 3,716).

`sync-test --rollback 7` passes on all four (7,318 ticks each). The first diffs found two port mistakes: the angle `it_8027BBF4` and `it_8027C0F0` hand `lbVector_Rotate` is rounded (`frsp`) from a double product, which a plain store truncates, and the knocked-down state's animation lies down again (`it_802E98E0`), not up. `monsters-redead-bf`'s replay check is clean; `monsters-redead-fd`'s differs in 111 post-frame "hitstun" words, Mario's held state's word (`mv.co.captureleadead.x0`, the ReDead's GObj address), which the check's playback (player names on) lays out 0x940 bytes higher than melee-hd's recording and the trace (names off): layout, not gameplay.

leaf-check's `items_monsters` runs every ported function of the units from saved calls (`local/calls/items-monsters/oracle-*`, packed: `items-held-ps` and the three ReDead matches; the recipe is in the test's header), each also from its unit's neighbours' captures with the arguments the game passes: 106 compare clean. The ReDead's drop and throw and the enemies' (`it_8027CB3C`, `it_8027CBA4`, `it_8026C220`), which only a fighter's grab reaches, were blocked until the `catch` set ([Catching items](#catching-items)). The ReDead's release when its fighter is hit (`it_802EADD8`) runs inside the fighter's damage callbacks, which compare clean. The importer types the held fighter's word (motion 0x14D). Each port broken on purpose fails the check: the dodge's box and its frames, the walk along a slope (a fused multiply-add), the held fighter's mash timer and the hurt sounds. Three breaks passed: the lunge's reach in height (5 to 6: no fighter stood between), the turn to the floor's last frames (2 to 3: no saved turn ends there) and the speed of a shrugged-off knockback (1.5 to 1.6: no saved hit is shrugged off), which the diffs reach only where these matches do.

No match has a ReDead hit while the line it last stood on has tilted into a wall, where its floor test (`it_802EA804`) walks from a wall and adds the wall's length to f27 its caller left (0.0, `hsd::gobj::PROC_F27`; [the engine notes](engine.md#uninitialized-values)): it needs a dynamic line (Brinstar Depths, Great Bay, Poké Floats) and a ReDead falling off it hit before it lands, and 408 seeds of opener matches didn't reach it ([the player](player.md#items-toggle-whats-left)). leaf-check's `redead_walk_from_a_wall` runs it from the ReDead's saved calls (its unit's, as neighbours'), the line turned into a left wall, a right wall and a ceiling in the call's memory: the floor test with f27 at the saved calls' 0.0 and at -40, -1000 and NaN, which let a walk run on past walls, and the damage callback (`it_802E8CD8`) with damage that weighs a dodge. 4,845 prepared calls had the ReDead's line tilted; the floor test compares clean 5,536 times and the callback 2,256 (both float modes), and at the game's f27 neither ever reaches the first walk's unwritten normal (the items audit's reason for that stop), which the test asserts. Each break fails it: the second walk ignoring f27, the walk's wall length not starting from f27 (`mpLib_80056C54`) and `PROC_F27` set to -40, -1000 or NaN. The first walk ignoring f27 passes: at the game's 0.0 it's the same walk, and at other values it only turns comparisons into that stop, which those values reach.

**The Goomba** (`it::kinds::oldkuri`, `itoldkuri.c` whole): standing, walking one way and back (a round done, it may turn at the script's point or a wall), falling, landed on (flattened on the ground, knocked down in the air: its jumped-on callback `it_802D8098`, which `it_802D848C` sets with each state it can be landed on in), knocked back, and the KO flights, with the forget callbacks the adventure Goomba's and kind 190's tables name (`it_802D84D8`, `itOldKuri_Logic29_EvtUnk`) and the script-flag test `it_802750E8`. With `--fp hardware --heap`, every tick compares with no difference and no stale read:

- `monsters-goomba-fd` (7,429 ticks: the Goomba from frame 1,204 to 1,454, falling, standing, walking right and flung away);
- `monsters-goomba-ps` (7,428 ticks: from frame 1,438 to 1,663, walking left, falling off a platform and flung toward the screen);
- `monsters-codes-octorok-ps` (7,428 ticks: the first batch's ssbm-rs recording of `monsters-octorok-ps`'s seed, a Goomba from frame 2,009 to 2,208, flung away soon after it lands).

`sync-test --rollback 7` passes on all three (7,318 ticks each), and both melee-hd recordings replay-check clean (no divergence). No fighter lands on a Goomba or hits one without KOing it, so the stomp (`it_802D8098`, `it_802D813C`, `it_802D81FC`) and the knockback that leaves it standing run in leaf-check alone. The Pokémon Stadium match's captures found the importer taking a texture object for a joint: from tick 4,270 to 6,505 a HUD element keeps a link to a joint whose block a texture object (`hsdTObj`) took over, and the importer read it as a joint because its first word named a class in the executable's data; a block naming another HSD class's info is no longer a joint to the importer (`is_jobj`), so the link dangles and compares by address, and `diff --from` works through those ticks.

leaf-check's `items_monsters` runs the Goomba's functions from the three matches' saved calls (`oracle-monsters-goomba-fd`, `-goomba-ps`, `-codes-octorok-ps`) and from its unit's neighbours' captures, the stomp with the first fighter as the one that landed (`xCFC`, which `Item_80269B60` sets before it calls back) and the forget callbacks with the first fighter to forget: 156 functions compare clean. The Goomba's drop and throw (`it_2725_Logic0_Dropped`, `it_2725_Logic0_Thrown`) waited for the `catch` set with the ReDead's. Each port broken on purpose fails the check: the standing timer's count, the frames it stands after landing (`xC`), the knock-down's slowing (`x8`), the stomp's sound, `xDEC` cleared by the flight and the walk's speed. Four breaks passed: walking left's round keeping its hit records (no saved round ends with any), a hit that leaves its damage exactly at its health KOing it (none does), the right wall's side (no saved walk right meets a wall once its round is done) and the knockback's least upward speed (no hit is survived), which no match reaches.

**The Octorok and its stone** (`it::kinds::octarock`, `itoctarock.c` whole; `it::kinds::octarockstone`, `itoctarockstone.c` whole): standing, checking each period for the nearest fighter within its range (`it_802E52E0`), turning to face one behind it, hopping to spit a stone at one ahead from its mouth's bone (aimed at the fighter's camera bone, a tenth of the height between them added at random, `it_802E89D0`), moving by its model's bone (`it_802E595C`), knocked back, and the KO flights; the stone falling, bouncing off the stage and ending on a hit. With `--fp hardware --heap`, every tick compares with no difference and no stale read:

- `monsters-octorok-ps` (7,428 ticks: the Octorok from frame 553 to 1,412, knocked back twice, spitting two stones, turning, and flung away);
- `monsters-octorok-bf` (7,430 ticks: from frame 1,080 to 6,735, spitting 26 stones, some bouncing off the stage, until a hit flings it away).

`sync-test --rollback 7` passes on both (7,318 ticks each), and both melee-hd recordings replay-check clean. The first diffs stopped at the first stone's spin speed, `-0.0` where ssbm-rs has `0.0`: the stone's spawn never sets its `SpawnItem`'s velocity, and the spawn's spin takes its way from that stack vector's x, a heap address the joint lookup before it left (negative as a float; [the engine notes](engine.md#uninitialized-values)).

leaf-check's `items_monsters` runs the Octorok's and the stone's functions from both matches' saved calls (`oracle-monsters-octorok-ps`, `-bf`) and their units' neighbours' captures, the forget callbacks with the first fighter to forget: 214 functions compare clean. The Octorok's drop and throw (`itOctarock_Logic2_Dropped`, `itOctarock_Logic2_Thrown`) waited for the `catch` set with the other monsters'. Each port broken on purpose fails the check: the shot's and the turn's timers, the bone motion's sideways axis, the fall's slowing (`x4`), the spit and bounce sounds, the turn's new facing, the stone's arc (its half and its tenth) and the spawn's plane. Two breaks passed: the range's width read from its height (`xC` for `x8`: no saved check has a fighter between the two) and the knockback's least upward speed (no saved knockback points off the floor that low).

**Topi** (`it::kinds::oldottosea`: `itoldottosea.c` and Topi's part of `itwhitebea.c`, `it_802E31F8` to `it_802E37A4`, whole): standing, walking a round (80 frames, 42 with the Japanese language setting), sometimes charging (one draw in `x10`), turning at walls, ledges and a round's end, knocked back, the KO flights and gone past the blast zones by itself (`it_802E35CC`); and with the Freezie on in the item switch, one Topi in `x28` makes a Freezie as it first walks and pushes it ahead (the Freezie's `it_8028EB88`, `it_8028F434`, `it_8028EC98`, `it_8028ECE0`, `it_8028ECF0`, in `it::kinds::freeze`), letting go of it once it's farther than `x18` or when it's hit, picked up or gone, which the Freezie's own letting go (`itFreeze_ClearLinkedItem`, `it_802E37A4`) mirrors. The walk and turn read the language setting (`lbLang_IsSettingJP`, `lbLang_IsSettingUS`), not the saved language: the boot sets it from the disc (English, the US disc having `usa.ini`, `gmMainLib_8015FBA4`) and nothing changes it, so `game::gm::language_setting_is_us` is a constant. With `--fp hardware --heap`, every tick compares with no difference and no stale read:

- `monsters-ottosea-fd` (7,429 ticks: Topi from frame 892 to 997, falling, knocked back in the air, standing, walking a frame and falling off the stage);
- `monsters-ottosea-bf` (7,430 ticks: from frame 2,018 to 2,203, falling, knocked back, walking, turning and flung away);
- `monsters-ottosea-freezie-fd` (7,429 ticks: a Topi from frame 2,585 to 2,743 makes a Freezie and walks it along, knocked back and walking again, until a hit flings it away, letting go; the Freezie slides on and is picked up and thrown; 47 Freezies come out of the item switch besides, 15 of them picked up by the openers).

`sync-test --rollback 7` passes on all three (7,318 ticks each), and all three melee-hd recordings replay-check clean. No saved state has a Topi charging (states 6 and 7) or its Freezie stopped against a wall, which leaf-check reaches from neighbours only. leaf-check's `items_monsters` runs Topi's functions and the Freezie's a Topi reaches from the three matches' saved calls (`oracle-monsters-ottosea-fd`, `-bf`, `-freezie-fd`, made with a second function list, `items-monsters-funcs-topi.txt`, and `done-nonmonsters-topi.txt` without those functions) and their units' neighbours' captures: 271 functions compare clean; Topi's drop and throw (`it_802E32B4`, `it_802E3314`) waited for the `catch` set with the others', and `it_8028ECE0` and `it_8028ECF0` run inside Topi's collision end and hit. Each port broken on purpose fails the check: the turn's frames, the Freezie's place ahead of it (`x14`), its deceleration's sign, its life starting over, whether a Topi makes one (the draw's test) and the standing model's facing. Breaks of what no saved state reaches passed: the walk's round (80 frames: no saved walk lasts it, so neither its length nor the charge's draw, `x10`, is read), the charge's frame 25 and its Freezie push scale (`x20`: no Topi charges), the letting go exactly at `x18`, the Freezie draw's odds (`x28`: the saved draw comes up 0 for one more as well) and a stand's turn over one frame or two (no saved stand is on a new slope).

### Catching items

Only a container's monster is catchable: a fighter's catch box (a grab, Kirby's inhale, Yoshi's tongue) tests an item only when its `xDD0` bit 4 is set, which only the enemies' setup sets (`it_80275270`, from `it_8027CAD8` and the kinds' own spawns). The `catch` set ([Oracle sets](#oracle-sets)) has a catcher take each, on seeds `seed-search` found (`--watch caught` with the spit star, kind 53, for Kirby; Yoshi's egg, kind 87; each monster in its held state for the grab: Goomba 5, ReDead 13, Octorok 5, Topi 8); 200 Kirby seeds had 7 catches, 200 Yoshi seeds 5 eggs, 400 grab seeds 13 monsters held (no Octorok, which took 1,200 more). melee-hd recorded each and ssbm-rs made it the oracle as the monsters' matches are (`$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/bench/oracle-m4-catch/`, `local/traces/v5-mixed/oracle-NAME.trc`). With `--fp hardware --heap`, each compares every traced tick with no difference and no stale read, and `sync-test --rollback 7` passes on it (7,318 ticks each):

- `catch-kirby-fd` (Kirby against Mario, 7,428 ticks): Kirby inhales a monster, pulls it in (`it_802F23EC`, `it_802F258C`) and spits it out as a star (`it_802F295C`, `it_2F28.c`) at frame 3,365;
- `catch-kirby-swallow-fd` (7,428): a monster inhaled from frame 3,126 and swallowed (`ftKb_SpecialNDrink0_Anim`, `it_802F28C8`);
- `catch-yoshi-fd` (Yoshi against Link, 7,428): the tongue catches a monster (`fn_8012CEE0`, `it_802F2BFC`) and Yoshi lays it as an egg at frame 2,354 (`it_802F2F34`), which sits, takes hits and breaks;
- `catch-grab-goomba-fd`, `-redead-fd`, `-octorok-fd`, `-ottosea-fd` (Fox against Mario, 7,429 each): Fox grabs the monster (`fn_800D8BFC`: taken into the hand and lifted, the heavy pickup), carries it, and throws it (each kind's throw, the enemies' `it_8027CB3C`, a stage item's throw counted for its thrower, `it_8027B4A4` and `it_8027B378` with `it_802758D4`) or drops it (`it_8027CBA4`).

The replay checks of the grab and Yoshi matches have no divergence; the two Kirby matches' four and five are Mario's post-frame "hitstun" word from frame 396 while Kirby holds him (his inhaled state's word is Kirby's GObj, a heap address each run lays out differently), layout as in the other matches.

What they needed: the catch box against items (`ftColl_8007BC90`'s `updateItemGrabTarget`, the first box that touches an item taking it if it's the nearest, the fighter's `x1A64` beside its target, now a GObj handle) and the catch (`Fighter_procGrabColl`: the grab sound, `it_8027B4A4`, the fighter's `x2194` callback); Kirby's item inhale (`ftKb_SpecialN_800F5DE8`, `fn_800F6178`, `fn_800F6280`, the capture states' animations), the item in his mouth (`itkirby_2F23.c`: `it::kinds::kirbyinhale`, its variables the union's last words over the monster's own) and the star he spits it out as (`it_2F28.c`, `It_Kind_Unk2`: `it::kinds::kirbyspitstar`), the swallow and the let-go (`it_802F28C8`, `ftKb_SpecialN_800F9090`); the item state tables code sets itself (`it_803F9450`, and Kirby's copy's tongue's `it_803F9490`); the grab's item callback (`fn_800D8BFC`) and a thrown enemy's statistics (`it_80273F34`'s tail).

leaf-check runs the new ports from saved calls of the seven matches (`local/calls/kirby/oracle-catch-kirby-*` with `kb-items.txt`, `local/calls/yoshi/oracle-catch-yoshi-fd` with `ys-items.txt`, `local/calls/items-monsters/oracle-catch-grab-*` with `items-monsters-funcs-catch.txt`, packed; the recipes are in the tests' headers): `kirby` 976 functions clean, `yoshi` 229 (the tongue's item and the egg lay, 22 rows that had waited for a check), `items_monsters` 292 (the grab's catch, the lift and each monster's throw and drop, which had been blocked). A few run from another function's saved calls whose states are theirs (`ALSO_FROM`): the item pull's test from the pull's animation, the tongue's aerial catch from its ground catch, the Goomba's drop from its throw (no match drops one) and the item engine's stop (`it_80273408`) from a throw. Kirby's copy of Yoshi catching an item (`it_802F2D98`, `fn_801095DC`, `fn_80109714`, `it_802F2E7C`) is ported and still waits for a check: no match gives Kirby Yoshi's power with a monster near.

Each port broken on purpose fails a check or a diff (27 breaks): the pull's speed clamp, its shrink and its speeds' order, the pull's reach (the diffs), the item hidden in his mouth (the diffs), the star's slowing, speed, facing and the item's destruction kind, the swallow's sound and the catch's flag (`kirby`); the tongue's blink, the egg's breaking time and a hit's toll on it (`yoshi`); the grab target's nearest test, the catch counted, the lift run to its flag, a throw's damage in the statistics and the hitboxes' most damage, a throw's source player, the Goomba's throw and drop, the Octorok's timer and Topi's thrown state (`items_monsters`). Three pass both, each changing what no match reaches: the held item placed at Kirby's previous position instead of his current one (the scripts stand still while he holds it), a drop that doesn't mark the monster's hit records (a held monster's hitboxes are off) and the stop's nudge left uncleared (no item has one when it's stopped).

### Fighters with items

The items audit's task 4, part A ([the player](player.md#items-toggle-whats-left)) is checked on the `fighters` set ([Oracle sets](#oracle-sets)), recorded, replay-checked, controllers-checked and traced as the other oracle matches are (`local/bench/oracle-m4/`, `local/traces/v5-mixed/oracle-NAME.trc`). The replay checks agree with their recordings through frame 7,200 but for the Battlefield Zelda and Sheik matches' entry frame (frames -113 and -112, the second fighter's position y), as in the other Battlefield matches; the controllers checks show no divergence.

**Donkey Kong's heavy carry.** `items-dk-heavy-fd` (Donkey Kong against Donkey Kong, 7,432 ticks) and `items-dk-heavy-bf` (Donkey Kong against Marth, 7,429) compare every traced tick with no difference and no stale read with `--fp hardware --heap`, and pass `sync-test --rollback 7` (7,318 ticks each). Between them Donkey Kong lifts boxes, barrels and Party Balls 17 times, carries them in his heavy wait (state 341), walks slowly and at middle speed with them, falls off a ledge with one and lands (347, 349), throws them, and is hit carrying one seven times, each hit light enough to leave him in his hurt wait (350) with it. The first diff stopped at the first hit: the heavy states count from his cargo attributes' first word (`motion_state`, 341), where his forward throw's cargo states count from the second (`x4_motion_state`, 351).

leaf-check's `donkey_heavy_functions` runs every function of the heavy states (`ftdonkeyheavy*.c`, `ftdonkeyms3450.c`, and `ftCo_Turn_Anim_Inner`, which his heavy turn shares with the common turn) from saved calls of both matches (`local/calls/donkey-heavy/oracle-NAME`, packed; the recipe is in the test's header) against ssbm-rs's ports, and each from every other heavy state's saved calls with the buttons, stick, a jump's press, a fast fall, the states' variables and a hit's knockback varied, so the fast walk, the turn, the knee bend and the jump, which no match reached, run from real states: all 42 compare clean. Each port broken on purpose fails it: a hit's knockback level that keeps the item, the turn's frames, the knee bend's lag, the landing's lag at zero, the fall's kept fast fall, the turn's jump before it has turned, the hurt wait's landing, the walks' speed scales, the dropped item's speed, the turn's just-turned flag, the hurt wait's state, the wait check's stick and the wait's landing from the air. Not caught: the heavy jump's voice flag (`ftCo_800CB110`'s second argument), since Donkey Kong's sound table has no jump voice (its entry is the none value), so the flag plays nothing either way.

**Zelda and Sheik transforming with items.** The level 9 AI asks Zelda and Sheik to transform every 400 frames half the time (`ftCo_0A01.c`'s `xF8_b5`), so a Zelda against Sheik match transforms a few times a minute, but rarely with something worn. `items-zelda-sheik-fd` and `items-sheik-zelda-bf` have every kind that changes the transformation on; their 10 transformations carry the Starman three times, the Cloaking Device once and held items seven times, and no metal, hood or flower. The other four came from a search: ssbm-rs recorded 16 candidate matches (seeds `0x1F0F_0F11` on, eight with Lip's Stick, the Bunny Hood and the Metal Box, eight with Lip's Stick alone), melee-hd played each recording's playback with a local probe on the transformation, and the four kept are those whose transformations wore metal and a Bunny Hood together and apart (`items-zelda-hood-fd`, `items-sheik-hood-bf`) and a flower on Zelda's head and on Sheik's (`items-zelda-flower-fd`, `items-zelda-flower2-fd`). Between the six: 29 transformations, the Starman 3, the Cloaking Device 1, metal 4, a Bunny Hood 3, a flower 2 and an item held 12 (six Beam Swords, five Lip's Sticks, a Ray Gun). With `--fp hardware --heap` each compares every traced tick with no difference and no stale read (7,428 ticks on Final Destination, 7,429 on Battlefield), and each passes `sync-test --rollback 7` (7,318 ticks).

What they needed (`ftCommon_8007EFC8`'s six refused cases): the Starman passed on (`ftColl_8007B7FC`, its color animation copied, `ftCo_800C0358`, and the source's cheer taken back, `ft_800880D8`, split out of the dead flags it had been folded into), metal and the Cloaking Device passed on and ended on the source, the Bunny Hood and the flower handed over (`it_8026B9A8`: let go, held by the woken fighter at its head part, the throw it counted taken back, and its state's animation started again with `HSD_JObjAddAnimAll`) and worn, the held item handed over at the hand part, and a flower's size in `ftCommon_80080174`. The original reads the source live at each step, so the port no longer copies it ahead: ending the source's metal recomputes its attributes and collision, which the collision copy then reads; and the hood is fitted, and the flower shaped, by the source's attributes, height and growth, not the woken fighter's (`ftCommon_8007FA00(gobj)`, `ftCommon_8007FDA0(gobj)` on the source, as the original calls them).

leaf-check's `zelda_transform_items` (in `zelda`) runs every saved call of the transformation, the hand-over, the color animation's copy and the worn items' size from the six matches (`local/calls/zelda-transform/oracle-NAME`, every call saved, packed; the recipe is in the test's header): 29 transformations and 17 hand-overs. Each transformation runs again with the Starman, metal, the Cloaking Device (steady, and flashing toward its end, which no match has) and all three given to its source, the cloak counted among refraction's users as a real one is, and the worn items' size runs on each source with its flower's growth moved on: all four functions compare clean. Each port broken on purpose fails it: the hand-over's throw count, its animation's first frame and its flag `x14`, the Starman's frames and its cheer, the metal's end on the source, the cloak passed on flashing, the flashing cloak's test, the hood's shine, the hood fitted by the woken fighter, the flower shaped by the woken fighter, the held item's pickup hook flag and its part, the flower's size, and the color animation's overlay. Not caught: the copied color animation's id (`ftCo_800C0358` with 9 rather than the Starman's 0x6B: both play on the same overlay, `x408`, so the same overlay is copied either way) and the held item's visibility flag carried over (`x221E_b3`: the woken fighter's next state change, the transformation's second half, sets it again before anything reads it).

### The Super Scope's one-shot charge

The Super Scope charged past level 0 with less ammunition left than a level 1 shot costs (one shot of its 48; a burst's shot costs 1, the charged levels 2, 3, 4, 6 and up to 16) takes its GObj address for a level, and a hit while charging spends that address, which leaves the scope over two billion shots: the infinite-ammunition glitch ([Melee's own bugs](melee-bugs.md#the-super-scopes-affordable-level-returns-its-gobj-infinite-ammunition)). melee-hd models it with the allocator model's address. The `sscope` set ([Oracle sets](#oracle-sets)) has two matches of two gunners (`Control::Gunner`, below) on seeds a search found, recorded by melee-hd (`RECORDER=melee-hd`) and made the oracle by ssbm-rs's replay check and trace of the recording (`local/bench/oracle-m4-sscope/`, `local/traces/v5-mixed/oracle-NAME.trc`). With `--fp hardware --heap`, each compares every traced tick (7,427) with no difference and no stale read, and `sync-test --rollback 7` passes on it (7,318 ticks):

- `sscope-fd` (seed `1F0F1153`): Fox, charging with one shot left, is hit at frame 3,010, and the scope's ammunition goes from 1 to 2,131,526,977 (1 less the scope's GObj address, 0x80F37AC0 in the trace's layout); the scope fires 222 more shots, 104 by Fox and 118 by Marth, who holds it later.
- `sscope-click-fd` (seed `1F0F1007`): Fox's charge with one shot left fires at frame 4,776 and only clicks, his state word holding the scope's address (0x80F7C780) through the shot; his last shot follows as a burst's.

The search: `melee-bench seed-search sscope-fd FROM 400` with a temporary stop at the hit while charging found one seed in 400 (6 s for 8 seeds on four threads); with a stop at the address taken in the charge, one in the first eight. Breaking `it_80291DAC` to return no level fails `sscope-fd`'s diff at frame 3,010 (`item.xD4C`: ours 1, theirs 2,131,526,977) and `sscope-click-fd`'s at its shot. `sscope-fd`'s replay check is clean; `sscope-click-fd`'s differs in 29 post-frame "hitstun" words from frame 4,776, Fox's state word holding the scope's GObj address, which the check's playback (player names on) lays out 0x520 bytes higher than melee-hd's recording and the trace: layout, not gameplay, as Yoshi's egg's word in `items-shoot-fd` ([Shooting and thrown items](#shooting-and-thrown-items)).

leaf-check's `items_on` runs the glitch from real states, which no replay reaches (`scope_one_shot_charge`): the scope's charging interrupt from each saved call with the held scope at one shot and a charge of 15 ticks, A held or let go (and past the charge that fires on its own), the hit while charging, the charged shot's entry and its shot with the address for a level, and `it_80291DAC`, `it_80291F14` and `it_80291D38` with one shot and charges past level 0 or levels outside their cases; each compares the whole state, and some calls must refill the scope, keep the address and return it. The importer now gives each live GObj its address (`Pool::set_addr`), as the allocator model would. Each port broken on purpose fails it: `it_80291DAC` returning no level (which `items_on_functions` alone missed), `it_80291F14` spending nothing for a level outside its cases, `it_80291D38` returning the level, and the address off by 0x20.

### The characters' own items

The fighter audit ([the player](player.md#characters-whats-left-in-their-own-items)) found the characters' items ported but 136 of their callbacks and helpers waiting for aliases. Their checks are the verify list's replays: a `melee-oracle` build that counted every item callback it dispatched and every `// Ports` function it ran (a temporary counter, not committed) played each replay with `--golden-check`, which simulates the match and compares every tick's state hash with the golden its clean full diff wrote, and `ledger/checks.txt` names, for each function some replay ran, the replay that runs it most and how often (244 of the 246 replays matched their goldens; the ReDead and Octorok monster matches' goldens predate their re-recorded replays). Seven mutations, one build selecting them by an environment variable, each fail their replay's golden check in the tick the broken callback first runs: a reflected Mario fireball destroyed (`kirby mario-fd`, tick 6901), an absorbed Falco laser kept (`gnw-falco-fd-7b66bba4`, tick 126), a clanked Luigi fireball kept (`items-pokeball4-fd`, tick 4903), a Samus bomb not bouncing off what it hit (`dk-samus-bf-9da5c633`, tick 1175), the Ice Climbers' reflected ice destroyed (`oracle-cpu-ics-fd-interp`, tick 2816), a reflected PK Fire destroyed (`oracle-cpu-yoshi-bf`, tick 8063) and Pikachu's bolt ended by its hit (`oracle-cpu-mario-ps`, tick 4371); unbroken, the build matches. The same counts show the hookshot's link counts (Link 15 on the ground and 22 in the air, Young Link 10 and 15) and that no replay of the list swings Samus's grapple beam (`hf-roy-samus-fd-56052455`, outside the list, swings it from the shot). No new matches were made: the 98 functions no replay runs (absorptions of the fireballs, pills, thunder and Din's Fire, clanks and shield bounces, PK Flash's burst, the blaster's throws) wait for matches that reach them, such as `BENCH_AI` Ness against those characters.

**Samus's grapple swing (2026-10-21).** The audit's one reachable gap was the button-sequence beam's swing outside the shot ([the engine notes](engine.md#uninitialized-values)): the swing copies its grabbed-victim-only bit from a stack byte its caller never sets. The `grapple` set's `grapple-samus-bf` swings it ten times on the ground: bounced (`fn_802B8384`, through `it_802B99A0`) on its first, seventh and last frames (match frames 1524, 330, 974), hanging (`fn_802B8524`, through `it_802B9CE8`) on its first, eighth and last (1108, 515, 1724), and shot (`fn_802B805C`, through `it_802B9328`) on its first, fourth (homing on the foe), 24th and last (617, 1370, 790, 1257). Recorded by melee-hd (`RECORDER=melee-hd`) into `local/bench/oracle-m4-grapple/`, ssbm-rs's replay check compares 12,491 events through frame 1800 with no divergence, and its trace (`ssbm-run-rf`, names off, `--port all`, `local/traces/v5-mixed/oracle-grapple-samus-bf.trc`, 78 MB) took 5.5 minutes. `WATCH_ADDR` on the byte's words, in the interpreter and with every port, found each swing's word last written as the engine notes say: `PSMTXConcat`'s save of `f14` (zeros) in the shot, and in the other two `HSD_JObjMakeMatrix`'s save of `r31`, her `ThrowN` bone's address (`0x80D9E900`: the bit set). melee-hd models both (`samusgrapple::SwingSlot`), the address from its allocator model. With `--fp hardware --heap` the diff compares every traced tick (2,025) with no difference and no stale read, and `sync-test --rollback 7` passes (1,918 ticks). leaf-check's `samus_swing` runs the three frames against the machine code from captures around each swing (`local/calls/samus-swing/grapple-samus-bf-F`, ten packed sets; the test's header has the commands), every saved call clean. Broken on purpose (one build, a mutation per run), each is caught by both: the address's bit 19 flipped fails the diff in tick 454 (frame 330, the first bounced swing: `x914[0].x134`) and leaf-check's bounced swing; the shot's bit set fails the diff in tick 741 (frame 617) and leaf-check's shot swing; and the hanging frame taken as the shot's (0.0) fails the diff in tick 639 (frame 515) and leaf-check's hanging swing. The fighter audit has no gap left ([the player](player.md#characters-whats-left-in-their-own-items)).

**Samus's grab off the ground (2026-10-22).** Peach's Castle's CPU sweep ([the player](player.md#cpu-players-whats-left)) stopped where Samus's ground grab left the ground before her beam came out: `fn_800D8E30` (both grabs' collision, off the ground) calls `fn_800D9C64`, which hands `it_802B7B84` her beam slot (`u.ss.x223C`) unchecked, and `it_802B7B84` does nothing with null, where melee-hd's port expected a beam; it now puts away only a beam that's there. A ground grab stops at ledges (`ft_800827A0`), so it leaves the ground only when the ground goes from under it. Yoshi's Island N64's clouds do that with no random draw: a cloud stood on drops its collision about 150 frames after the landing. `grapple-cloud-yi64` (the `grapple` set) scripts Samus onto the left cloud from her spawn and grabs so that the cloud goes on the grab's third frame (tick 374, replay frame 250), before the beam comes out on the seventh; she falls and is KO'd, and reborn she lands on the cloud again once it's back and grabs so that it goes on the grab's 15th frame (tick 933, replay frame 809), the beam out, which `it_802B7B84` puts away. Recorded by melee-hd (`RECORDER=melee-hd`) into `local/bench/oracle-m4-grapple/`, ssbm-rs's replay check compares 11,553 events through frame 1800 with no divergence, and its trace (`ssbm-run-rf`, names off, `--port all`, `local/traces/v5-mixed/oracle-grapple-cloud-yi64.trc`, 65 MB) took 24 seconds. With `--fp hardware --heap` the diff compares every traced tick (2,025) with no difference and no stale read (its golden written), and `sync-test --rollback 7` passes (1,918 ticks). Broken on purpose (one build, a mutation per run): the old `.expect` stops the diff in tick 374, and the beam left out fails it in tick 933 (the heap's AObj pool). The other grapple paths that read the slot either test it as the original does (`ftCo_800D9C98`, the beam's fighter hooks `it_802BAC3C`, `it_802BAC80` and `it_802BACC4`, the pull's wait) or, as the original, use it unchecked where it can't be null (the grabs' frames after the spawn, the air grab's, the pull's start).

**The bow (2026-10-21).** The audit's one unsure stop, the bow loosing an arrow whose owner is no longer the archer (`itLinkArrow_802A850C`), can't be reached in an items-off match: a nocked arrow sits in its state 0, whose table entry has no animation (`anim_id` -1), so no script turns its hitbox on and no reflector or absorber can turn it; its pickup (`it_802742F4`) clears its pickup flag, so no fighter takes it; the item code's other owner writes are the shells', barrel's and Metal Box's callbacks, a drop or throw of a fighter's held item (the arrow isn't one: it's `u.lk.arrow_gobj`), a destroy, and the unload walk. Nor can a destroyed arrow's stale pointer be loosed: the arrow clears its own `xE0` only as it destroys itself with the archer out of the bow's states, and the archer leaves them with an arrow nocked only by loosing it, through `ftCommon_8007DB58` (a hit, a capture, the barrel) or a KO, whose callbacks destroy the arrow and clear his pointer first. The audit records the reason for Link, Young Link and Kirby.

**The rows no replay ran (2026-10-22).** The 54 functions the verify list left in the characters' item units (the "98 distinct rows" counted the units' unreached rows and `evt_unk`s too) group by the meeting that runs them: projectiles absorbed by PSI Magnet (16), clanked (8), hitting a shield (5) or bouncing off one (5, a Counter's bubble deflects an airborne item whose box can bounce), the thrown Thunder Jolt reflected, PK Flash landing, Samus's bomb settling on a slope and sliding (3), her grapple beam frozen in a hit (`it_802BA760`, which `it_802BACC4` calls only in hitlag), the blaster's states 9 and 10 (6), Dr. Mario's held pill (4) and three helpers (the Blizzard's launch, the Ice Shot's drop, the blaster's removal). A walk of each kind's state scripts and the hitboxes they open settled 19 unreachable, now in the fighter audit with their reasons: PK Flash and Din's Fire never open a hitbox (their `Absorbed` and `Clanked`), the boxes of PK Flash's burst, PK Thunder's ball, Thunder's bolt, the Blizzard and Din's Fire's burst never have the clank bit both boxes need, the blaster's state 9 needs a pickup at an action its spawns never come in and state 10 the demo's command variables, and the pill's held states only the demo's spawn enters. The others are reached by the `own-items` set, scripted players (`Control::Program`: a list of parts, each a run of steps a number of times, mirrorable) against Ness holding PSI Magnet, Marth countering or shielding, and Fox's Reflector: Luigi (`own-luigi-fd`), the Ice Climbers (`own-climbers-fd`), Zelda (`own-zelda-fd`) and Ness (`own-ness-fd`), each with one shooter at each target on Final Destination; Pikachu's Thunder through Ness on Battlefield's left platform, Pichu's jolts at Marth's Counter, and Ness's PK Flash landing on the platform (`own-thunder-bf`); Pichu's jolt at Fox's Reflector and Pikachu's at Ness (`own-jolt-bf`); Samus's Charge Shot, her Z-air grapple into a jumping Ness, and Dr. Mario's pills (`own-samus-fd`); Yoshi's stars and Mario's fireballs (`own-yoshi-fd`); and Samus's bomb on Great Bay's sloped platform (`own-bomb-gb`, ten seconds). Recorded by melee-hd (`RECORDER=melee-hd`, `local/bench/oracle-m4-own/`), the first four (round 1) replay-check clean through frame 1800 (21,018, 32,021, 21,823 and 20,542 events), are traced (`local/traces/v5-mixed/oracle-own-NAME.trc`, 106 to 139 MB, 3.3 to 5.2 minutes each), compare every traced tick (2,014 to 2,022) with no difference and no stale read with `--fp hardware --heap`, and pass `sync-test --rollback 7` (1,918 ticks); a counting `melee-oracle` build confirmed what each runs, and `ledger/checks.txt` names them for their 16 rows (and `kirby fox-fd` for the blaster's removal). Broken on purpose (one build, a mutation per run), each fails its match's golden check: Luigi's fireball kept when absorbed (`own-luigi-fd`, tick 176), Luigi's fireball not bouncing off the Counter (tick 157) and PK Flash's burst kept when absorbed (`own-ness-fd`, tick 466); unbroken, the same build matches both goldens. The other five (round 2) replay-check clean through frame 1800 (20,151 to 21,340 events; `own-bomb-gb` 5,222 through frame 600; `own-thunder-bf`'s with Slippi's name plates off, as the traces are made), are traced (35 to 112 MB), compare every traced tick (2,016 to 2,021; 822 for `own-bomb-gb`) with no difference and no stale read with `--fp hardware --heap`, and pass `sync-test --rollback 7` (1,918 ticks; 718), and `ledger/checks.txt` names them for their 18 rows. Their mutations, by the same recipe: Yoshi's star not bursting when it clanks (`own-yoshi-fd`, tick 980), the thrown Thunder Jolt not turned around when reflected (`own-jolt-bf`, tick 196) and Samus's bomb not sliding on the slope (`own-bomb-gb`, tick 287); unbroken, the build matches all three goldens. leaf-check's `ness`, `zelda`, `samus` and `yoshi` tests also replay the item functions' saved calls from the matches (`local/calls/CHAR/own-NAME`, captured with only those functions listed). So of the 54, 35 are checked and 19 settled unreachable.

### Stages

The milestone's stage oracle matches ([Oracle sets](#oracle-sets)) are diffed as each stage's port lands, by the recipe in [Adding a stage](#adding-a-stage).

**Yoshi's Island N64** (`stage-yoshis-island-64`, Fox against Marth, two `BENCH_AI` players, four minutes; `groldyoshi.c`, `game::gr::oldyoshi`) compares every one of its trace's 14,635 ticks with no difference and no stale read (`--fp hardware`), with `--heap` too, to the trace's end at the match's last frame. The match runs the three clouds the fighters stand on: two of them sink, puff away, stay gone and come back (the first cloud once, the second twice), their collision joints taken out of the search list partway into the puff and back in partway into the return, each cloud's joint telling it a fighter stands on it (`fn_8020F2A8`, a collision joint's callback, which mpcoll's hooks now reach with the stage objects to write); the creatures behind the stage drawn and passing twice; and the stage's shadow check every display pass. `sync-test --rollback 7 --fp hardware` passes on it (14,518 ticks checked, to the replay's last frame). leaf-check's `stages` runs every function of the unit with code from the match's saved calls (`local/calls/stages/oracle-stage-yoshis-island-64`, packed; the recipe is in the test's header) against ssbm-rs's ports: the clouds' process also with every cloud set to each branch (stood on at and past its count's limit, puffing as its collision goes, gone long enough, shown again, its collision back, a count that wraps, a state past the four), the creatures' process with the wait over, running and each creature passing, the joint callback on each joint and with another kind of object's collision, each object's creation from the setup's first, the cloud speed's step also on 4,000 random inputs per float mode, and the empty processes and destroy callbacks, the load and the demo's setup from the clouds' calls. All compare clean.

Its replay check (`stage-yoshis-island-64.check.log`) shows one divergence between the recording and ssbm-rs's playback, which the controllers check (the recording run's own boot) doesn't: at frame 11870 port 2's post-frame state flags (the fifth flag byte, `+0x29` in the check's report, bit 0x80: the fighter's off-screen flag, `x221F_b0`) are set in the playback and clear in the recording. Frame 11870 is the second tick of one of the playback's doubled rounds: its rounds double at frames 1859 and 1860 and every 1,001 frames after (a probe of melee-hd's timeline, which follows ssbm-rs's: 1859, 2860, … 11869, 12870, 13871), so frame 11870 runs without a display pass of its own and keeps the off-screen mark the pass before it left ([State the render pass writes](engine.md#state-the-render-pass-writes)), while the recording run, which boots another way, doubled elsewhere. Port 2 is Marth. The flag feeds the off-screen bubble damage in vanilla play, but the recording's percents and every other field agree to the end, so here it changed nothing; either way the trace is of the playback, which melee-hd matches. Kongo Jungle N64's off-screen divergence is the same flag (port 1, frame 10866).

What the stage needed beyond its own code: the stage objects' sounds, `stage_info`'s settings and queries, the stage parameters' colors, the camera's and the fixed camera's getters, the spawner's switches, the bob-omb rain's starting point (`Ground_801C0A70`; the bomb itself is an item, task 3's) and the rest of `ground.c`'s and `stage.c`'s small functions (`game::gr::ground`, `gr::stage`, `gr::grlib`, `gr::zako`, `gr::material`), which leaf-check's `stage` runs on both sides of the match's states in turn (`ground_settings_and_queries`, `ground_object_sounds` with the audio state compared, `ground_load_map_joint`, `ground_euler_from_axes`, `stage_ground_kinds`, and on Final Destination `ground_background_color`), and the tournament stages' leftovers: every stage object's destroy callback, the processes and setups of objects play rarely or never makes (Pokémon Stadium's transformations' and windmills', Fountain of Dreams' background pieces, Battlefield's backgrounds) and the demos' setups, which `tournament_unreached_callbacks` runs by address on the objects they belong to from each tournament stage's saved calls (and `stadium_piece_destroys` right after the grass stage's setup, so the joints the destroys take out are in the list). Every stage's `callback1` and `callback4` are dead (nothing reads them), and the preload requests are replaced (`ledger/`).

Each port broken on purpose fails a check: a cloud puffing a tick early, its collision coming back a tick late, the puff's effect, the creatures' height spread, a passed creature left shown, a touch counted for another kind of object, the shadow check's height, the background's render bit, the stage's shadows off, the clouds' callback without its object, the setup's order, the stage's process without its collision and the speed step's braking distance and test (leaf-check's `stages`); the watch box's half sizes, `x724`'s step turned round, the angles' clamp dropped, a sound's pan scale, a joint load's depth test, the point test's height, the trophy slot, the materials' flags, the draw `Ground_801C5840` takes, the rain's sideways offset, the part reset's walk, the clear color from the fog, demo 26 and the grass stage's joint (leaf-check's `stage`). Four first tries survived and showed checks too weak, now strengthened: no saved call put a fighter near the shadow check's threshold, the random inputs never reached the speed step's braking, the pan checked was one whose two scales round alike, and Yoshi's Island N64 has no fog, so its clear color is black whatever the fog's.

**Brinstar Depths** (`stage-brinstar-depths`, Dr. Mario against Ganondorf, two `BENCH_AI` players, four minutes; `grkraid.c`, `game::gr::kraid`) compares every one of its trace's 14,636 ticks with no difference and no stale read (`--fp hardware`), with `--heap` too, to the trace's end. Kraid rises at his six places (shown with his copy behind the rock), idles with each idle's sounds and swipes; eleven swipes land in their windows, rumble every controller and turn the rock, which spins and eases back to rest with the camera shaking and its collision following. `sync-test --rollback 7 --fp hardware` passes on it (14,518 ticks checked). leaf-check's `stages` runs every function of the unit with code from the match's saved calls (`local/calls/stages-hazard/oracle-stage-brinstar-depths`, packed; 69 calls; the capture recipe is in the test's header) against ssbm-rs's ports: the rock's process also set to start a turn either way, to turn in its delay, to ease off, to stop and to ease off to exactly a step; Kraid's process in each state at its ticks' edges (each idle's sound ticks, both swipes around their windows' ends and their hits, the rock still or turning); where he rises after a turn from each last place and without one; each of the seven animations; the swipe's reach just short of and at each side's hit; a landing swipe either way and on a turning rock; the copy's debugging button (below) at each animation and at the wrap; and the empty processes and destroy callbacks, the load and demo hooks and the shadow check from the rock's calls. `OSReport` (a landing swipe reports Kraid's hand to the debugger) is stood in for on both sides and its calls compared. All compare clean.

Its replay check (`stage-brinstar-depths.check.log`) shows 16 divergences between the recording and ssbm-rs's playback; the controllers alone (`stage-brinstar-depths.pads.log`, the recording run's own layout) show none. Two are Dr. Mario's entry frames (frames -118 and -117, position y before he appears), the heap layout's as on the power-ups matches. The other fourteen are the pre-frame random seed, both ports, on seven frames (1008, 3299 and five more). Kraid's copy's process (`grKraid_801FE2D0`) is a debugging leftover: A pressed on the second controller's master status (`HSD_PadMasterStatus[1]`, the pad library's, not the fighter's inputs) steps the copy's animation ([Melee's own bugs](melee-bugs.md)).
