In the recording run the `BENCH_AI` Ganondorf presses A through that controller, and the run's log reports the debug request 352 times (`*** Req Effect Anime`); in playback the inputs reach the fighters through Slippi's playback codes (`Fighter_procInput`) and the master statuses press nothing, so neither the check's nor the trace's log reports one. The copy's other animations draw random numbers, so the recording's seed moved apart from playback's, which playback puts back every frame from the recording; only on those seven frames did a draw fall between the frame's start and its pre-frame events. A probe (not committed) proves it: fed port 2's recorded A presses, a straight run of melee-hd ends every one of the replay's frames with the seed the recording's next frame restores, where without them 9,369 frames part from it. Kraid's eleven landing swipes, which the logs report with the hand's position, are the same in both. Slippi Dolphin's playback injects inputs the same way, so ssbm-rs's playback is the trustworthy oracle here: it's what any Slippi playback of this recording does.

What the stage needed beyond its own code: every fighter's rumble (`ftLib_StartRumble`, `ftLib_StartRumbleAll`) and `OSReport` as a seam a check can stand in for (`code::os_report`). Each port broken on purpose fails a check: the rock's turn rate, its stop, its delay's draw and its rumbling part's animation; Kraid's swipe windows, his swiping side, his wait for a still rock and his wait's length; where he rises (the draw, and the middle place's coin), his copy's state and animation, the swipe's reach, the rumble's id and the turning speed; the copy's button's wrap; the copy's and the background's layers, the rock's rumbling part, the setup's order; an empty process and the shadow check (leaf-check's `stages`). One break survives: Kraid's furthest-right place taken on a tie (`>=` for `>`), which none of the stage's six places makes. Three first tries survived and showed checks too weak, now strengthened: the button's wrap from its sixth animation, a turn easing off to exactly its step, and the turning speed, which every saved swipe clamps to a bound (Kraid's process's swipes catch it).

**Fourside** (`stage-fourside`, Fox against Marth, two `BENCH_AI` players, four minutes; `grfourside.c`, `game::gr::fourside`) compares every one of its trace's 14,635 ticks with no difference and no stale read (`--fp hardware`), with `--heap` too, to the trace's end. The crane swings its girder over the stage and back and raises or lowers it six times, bobbing about each target; the UFO, waiting its turns and sooner while a fighter stands on the skyscraper's roof (the roof's collision joint counts them, `grFourside_801F30A0`, through standing and through hanging from its ledges), comes down over a building with a camera subject framing it and flies off again; the helicopter crosses behind when the UFO isn't out; the city's render callback turns three layers of the skyline with the camera's heading every display pass, and the stage's fog is fitted to the camera from its guide points (`Ground_801C4FAC`, which Fourside is the first stage to need). `sync-test --rollback 7 --fp hardware` passes on it (14,518 ticks checked). leaf-check's `stages` runs every function of the unit with code from the match's saved calls (`local/calls/stages-hazard/oracle-stage-fourside`, packed; 109 calls): the crane's process also in each of its eight states at their edges (each swing, the girder going down and up and arriving or not, bobbing either way, settled) and a state past them; the UFO's waiting (its wait over or not, a fighter on the roof or not, its challenge's count reached, after each building, the helicopter about or not), staying and leaving at their edges, and its camera subject over each building; the helicopter's states at their counts' edges with the UFO out or not, and whether it's about at 700 and 701 ticks; the roof touched by each collision kind; the render callback in the display pass and another; the shadow check with the feet just above, below and at the joint; the fog fit and the guide points; and the empty processes and destroy callbacks and the load and demo hooks from the city's calls. All compare clean.

Its replay check (`stage-fourside.check.log`) shows two divergences, Marth's entry frames (frames -113 and -112, position y before he appears), the heap layout's as on the power-ups matches; the controllers alone show none.

What the stage needed beyond its own code: a collision joint's callback data as either a stage object's `Ground` or its GObj (`gr::CollUser`; Fourside's roof passes the GObj) and the stage objects by map id where a callback writes another object than its own (`ft::coll::StageMaps`); the stage's fog fitted to the camera (`Ground_801C4FAC`); and a fix to mpcoll's ledge hang (`mpLib_8005811C`), which called a ledge's joint's second callback where the original calls its first, the one `mpJointSetCb1` sets: no stage before Fourside had a first callback on a joint with ledges.

Each port broken on purpose fails a check: the crane's bob's pull, its arrival going down, its settling and the girder's distance; the UFO's leaving, its camera subject's box, its challenge and its waits' count; the helicopter's sound's height, its leaving's count and the 700 ticks of `grFourside_801F3F10`; the roof's collision kind, the skyline's middle layer, the shadow check and the fog's far bound; the crane's rest, the guide points' order and the city's layer; and an empty process (leaf-check's `stages`). Four first tries survived and showed checks too weak, now strengthened: the helicopter's sound's height (its part's matrix now set about the bound), the girder arriving exactly on its target, the bob settling exactly at its speed, and the fog's far bound (the guide points and eye now placed inside it).

**Great Bay** (`stage-great-bay`, Roy against Zelda, who turns into Sheik, two `BENCH_AI` players, four minutes; `grgreatbay.c`, `game::gr::greatbay`, and Tingle's item, `ittincle.c`, `game::it::kinds::tincle`) compares every one of its trace's 14,630 ticks with no difference and no stale read (`--fp hardware`), with `--heap` too, to the trace's end. The turtle dives and surfaces with its splashes and sounds, carrying its side of the stage's collision; the floating platform sinks, rises and tilts under whoever lands on it; Tingle floats on his balloon, drifts and bobs, is stood on, popped, falls into the sea and is made again; the moon falls on its wait with the four giants; and the sea is a fighter device (`ftCo_800C07F8`, registered by the island's start hook) that splashes whoever crosses its surface. `sync-test --rollback 7 --fp hardware` passes on it (14,518 ticks checked). leaf-check's `stages` runs every function of the unit and of Tingle's with code from the match's saved calls (`local/calls/stages-hazard/oracle-stage-great-bay`, packed; 228 calls): the turtle's process also in each state at its edges (its wait over or not, diving at its splash and surfacing at its sounds and its fins' splashes each way, staying down) and a state past them, and whether it's out; the floating platform pushed away from rest and back each way, sinking and rising, and its collision joint's callback with each collision kind and side up to its limits; Tingle's stage process stood on or not (hanging from a ledge or not), popped and splashed or not, and his balloon's callback with each collision kind; the moon's wait about to end, ended and counting, the sea with a fighter crossing its surface downward or not, or hidden, and the shadow check; Tingle's states' callbacks, his floating state's bob and drift at their edges, his pop's countdown, his sitting's wait and depth and his lying's last loop, every entry and order the stage or his states call (pressed, let go below his height or not, popped either way, turned back or not), the still states' callbacks no saved call reaches (from their state's other callback's calls) and his destruction (no balloon fell below y = -50 in the match), from the floating state's calls; and the empty processes and destroy callbacks and the load and demo hooks from the island's calls. All compare clean.

Its replay check (`stage-great-bay.check.log`) shows two divergences, port 2's entry frames (frames -113 and -112, position y before the fighter appears), the heap layout's as on the power-ups matches; the controllers alone show none.

What the stage needed beyond its own code: the giants' scale joints file themselves in the ID table under the moon's setup's frame, below the stage's setup's by the create function's and the moon's setup's frames (`greatbay::MOON_FRAME`; filed under the stage's setup's own, they replaced its entry and the heap came out an entry short); `gm_IsCKindUnlocked`, which picks Great Bay's alternate song by Young Link's unlock; a second fighter device with its callback (`ftCo_800C07F8`, `ft::coll::FtDevices::register_coll`); the item hitbox flag Tingle clears as he spawns (`it_8027542C`) and the joints' rewind check the moon asks (`lb_8000B134`). It also showed two things wrong elsewhere. The ceiling tech's animation callback (`ftCo_PassiveCeil_Anim`) takes its script's flag as `ftCheckThrowB3` does, clearing it; melee-hd read it without clearing, which a Sheik's ceiling tech in tick 10810 showed. And an item's words that no code writes (`Item::x8`, `x21` to `x23`) keep whatever the heap held where a new block lands: in tick 13576 the item pool grows into memory a KO mark's palette table and a freed heap cell had used, which the shooting items had already found: they're left out of the mirror like padding (`UNUSED` in `tools/structgen/config.py`), so nothing compares them.

Each port broken on purpose fails a check: the background's and the water's layers; the turtle's dive's and surfacing's sound ticks; the floating platform's sinking and its landing kind; Tingle's pop's effect height and the balloon's collision kinds; the moon's giants' flags and its wait's end; a drift's end and its 1 in 3 turn away; and an empty process (leaf-check's `stages`). Two first tries survived and showed checks too weak, now strengthened: the balloon's third collision kind (its saved calls covered two; each kind is now set) and the turn away (the saved seed turned the same way either side of the break; the drift's start now runs from eight seeds).
**Green Greens** (`stage-green-greens`, Bowser against Pichu, two `BENCH_AI` players, four minutes; `grgreens.c`, `game::gr::greens`, and Whispy's apples, `itwhispyapple.c`, `game::it::kinds::whispyapple`) compares every one of its trace's 14,636 ticks with no difference and no stale read (`--fp hardware`), with `--heap` too, to the trace's end. Whispy Woods runs his cycle of idles, blowing and apples: eight idles, six spells of blowing and three of dropping apples (eight apples), his wind pushing a fighter on 2,036 ticks; his wind is a fighter device (`fn_80213B1C`, polled through `ftColl_GetWindOffsetVec`) that pushes the fighters on his side, and a wind source that sways their parts. The blocks fall into their grid of five rows of six columns on the stage's two sides: the match starts with 18 in place (four of them bomb blocks), 116 more fall in (12 bomb blocks), 114 break (14 bomb blocks, 68 of them hit), and fighters land on falling blocks 13 times and on resting ones 12. Each block is a collision joint and a hurtbox item (`ityaku.c`) on the stage's model, and its object (map 1, or map 3 for a bomb block); a falling block landed on breaks, a hit breaks any, a bomb block's item explodes through the stage's item script, and the blocks above a broken one fall into its place with their collision joints linked to their neighbours'. The grid and the blocks are heap blocks the stage allocates (`HSD_MemAlloc`), kept in the stage object's variables with their addresses (`gr::GreensVars`). `sync-test --rollback 7 --fp hardware` passes on it (14,518 ticks checked). leaf-check's `stages` runs every function of the unit and of the apples' with code from the match's saved calls (`local/calls/stages-hazard/oracle-stage-green-greens`, packed; 216 calls): Whispy's process waiting for the blocks or a demo and set to each state and step at its counts' edges (idle, turning and blowing, dropping apples at the first apple's tick, an interval after and between, none left), his wind with him blowing either way or not and the fighter in his box, outside it and on its edges, and the box test with its bounds in either order; the blocks' tick with a new block due (from several seeds), with a side full or both and with a block broken, a block's move falling slowly, fast, landing now, landed on, on a falling block below, bombing, broken and with falling blocks above it, a block added of the other kind and at rest or falling, a column settling over an empty place, the links around a broken place with every neighbour, the diagonals or the sides resting and at the grid's first place (below), the links of resting blocks, a break of the other kind and of a block already broken, the landing callback on another block's joint and on a joint no block has; the setup after a demo's, the layout from several seeds; an apple made kept or bursting for sure and from several seeds, falling at the stage's plane and short of it, ageing near its end, behind the stage and burst, and the states and events no capture reached from a lying apple's calls; and the empty processes, the destroy callbacks, the demo and load hooks and the shadow check from the stage's calls. All compare clean.

Its replay check (`stage-green-greens.check.log`) shows 528 divergences between the recording and ssbm-rs's playback; the controllers alone show none. Two are Pichu's entry frames (frames -113 and -112, position y before he appears), the heap layout's as on the power-ups matches; 526 are his post-frame "hitstun" word from frame 4880 on: his Thunder state keeps its item's address in the fighter's misc action-state word, 0x2720 apart between the recording run's heap and playback's, and nothing reads it as a number. Every position, action state and percent agrees: ssbm-rs's playback is a trustworthy oracle here.

What the stage needed beyond its own code: the stage items' glue for a state change, its script's end and an item's destruction (`grMaterial_801C8D98`, `801C8E48`, `801C8CDC`), and Whispy's healing apple's heal for the fighter's pickup (`it_8026B47C`). The stage's objects' scale joints file under the setup's frame as deep as Dream Land's, and the blocks' objects under the blocks' setup's and process's frames (`greens::SETUP_FRAME`, `SETUP_BLOCK_FRAME`, `PROC_BLOCK_FRAME`). The original links a broken block's last corner through the wrong neighbour and, at the grid's first place, reads before the blocks' array ([Melee's own bugs](melee-bugs.md)).

Each port broken on purpose fails a check: the grid's row spacing, a falling block's acceleration, Whispy's wind's direction, a blowing step's animation, the apples' sideways reach, what broke a block, a bomb block's hurtbox radius, the columns' weights, the landing callback's flag, the last corner's neighbour, the links' last column, a bounce's sound and an apple's sideways speed (leaf-check's `stages`).

**Brinstar** (`stage-brinstar`, Donkey Kong against Young Link, two `BENCH_AI` players, four minutes; `grzebes.c`, `game::gr::zebes`) compares every one of its trace's 14,636 ticks with no difference and no stale read (`--fp hardware`), with `--heap` too, to the trace's end. The lava rises and falls through its table of levels (moving on 7,069 ticks), its fog and lights following its height, with lava bubbles from the stage's and the island's spawn lines (`grZakoGenerator_801CA394`'s configurations, which Brinstar is the first stage to need); the lava is a fighter device (`grZebes_801DCBFC`, registered by the lava's start hook) that burns whoever falls in: four times in the match, each with its splash and sounds, and each time the frame's strongest hit (`ftCo_800C08A0` and the device's case of `ftColl_8007A06C`). The organic platform's bubbles move, take their places and span its hurtbox item; a pop shows on its top 58 times, five hits pop a bubble, and each time a bubble from below takes its place (`grZebes_801DB088`). The pillar and the island shake when hit (seven and eight hits), none past breaking. `sync-test --rollback 7 --fp hardware` passes on it (14,518 ticks checked). leaf-check's `stages` runs every function of the unit with code from the match's saved calls (`local/calls/stages-hazard/oracle-stage-brinstar`, packed; 193 calls): the lava's process waiting (with and without the quake, at its end, on its table's last entry), starting, speeding toward a level above or below and overshooting, slowing to a stop, and a state past them, its fog and light at levels across their tables, and its device with the fighter above it, below it, just fallen in and with the splashes at their limit; the pillar's and the island's shaking at each state's start and tick (at rest decaying, swinging past either side, settling, breaking past its limit; breaking, broken at its count's end, coming back) and their hits; the stage's process with the organic platform whole with a pop due, breaking (its animation going and over), broken (counting, at its end), growing back at each spawn's count and its end; the bubbles' tick as saved, with a top bubble popped (one below taking its place or none) and one falling apart, a pop shown from several seeds, and bubbles growing back due, with 15 bubbles and waiting; the lava bubbles on each of their lines and the spawn configurations at the lava's level and other heights; the creature, the background and the splashes; the setup and every map's object; and the empty processes, the destroy callbacks, the demo and load hooks and the shadow check from the island's calls. All compare clean.

Its replay check (`stage-brinstar.check.log`) shows no divergence between the recording and ssbm-rs's playback.

What the stage needed beyond its own code: a stage device's hit on a fighter (`ftCo_800C08A0`: its damage, `ftColl_800765F0`; its log entry, `ftColl_80076764`, whose damage word keeps the log slot's last one, [the engine notes](engine.md#uninitialized-values); the hit copied from its description, `lbColl_80008D30`; its sound, `ftColl_80078384`; and the players' records, `pl_8003EC30`) and its case in the weighing of the frame's hits (`ftColl_8007A06C`: its knockback at an attack ratio of 1, the fighter's facing as its direction, and its record with no attacker); the enemy generator's spawn configurations (`grZakoGenerator_801CA394`, `801CA43C`), a stage item at a point (`grMaterial_801C8D44`) and the camera's floor on Brinstar (`grZebes_801DCCC8`). The island's object files its scale joint under the stage's setup's frame (`zebes::ISLAND_FRAME`), and the bubbles' objects under the platform's setup's and the stage process's (`SETUP_BUBBLE_FRAME`, `GROW_BUBBLE_FRAME`, `REGROW_BUBBLE_FRAME`). A bubble's sound goes through the stage's GObj where its `Ground` belongs, and the platform's collision reads unset columns where no bubble reaches ([Melee's own bugs](melee-bugs.md)); a bubble that moves up leaves its pop's pointer behind, which nothing reads ([the engine notes](engine.md#uninitialized-values)).

Each port broken on purpose fails a check: the lava's direction, the shaking's damping, the organic platform's broken wait, the pillar's hit side, the bubbles' wait after a pop, a new bubble's depth, the bubbles' sinking, the lava bubbles' effect by line, the lava's splash sound, the camera's floor and a bubble's falling-apart sound (leaf-check's `stages`).

**Onett** (`stage-onett`, Mewtwo against Mr. Game & Watch, two `BENCH_AI` players, four minutes; `gronett.c`, `game::gr::onett`) compares every one of its trace's 14,638 ticks with no difference and no stale read (`--fp hardware`), with `--heap` too, to the match's end. Fifteen cars come from the right: each waits, and once a fighter is on the street waits again by that fighter's standing (`gm_8016C6C0`; twelve times), then drives through with the hazard sign up and its horn (`un_802FD604`), a fighter's scream past the camera's right edge with someone on the street (nine times; the edge comes from the camera the display pass leaves, [Melee's own bugs](melee-bugs.md)), and the camera's subject framing the street while the warning is up (`stage_info.x740`, which the computer players read too); cars hit the fighters ten times, spinning off or driving on, and twelve cars honk as they cross behind from the left. The drugstore's roof takes 66 landings: it cracks 23 times, breaks seven times and falls in seven times with its platforms gone, coming back blinking six times before the match ends. The awnings sag and spring back under the fighters, rustling 13 times, and the chimney smokes ten times. `sync-test --rollback 7 --fp hardware` passes on it (14,518 ticks checked). leaf-check's `stages` runs every function of the unit with code from the match's saved calls (`local/calls/stages-hazard/oracle-stage-onett`, packed; 138 calls): the cars' process with the car from the right in each state, its counts over or not and at each of its honk's states, and the car from the left in each state with its count over or not; a car's hit spinning or driving fast or slow either way, from several seeds; the horns for each car and side and the screams for each car; the drugstore's process in each state at its ticks' edges (its sounds, its platforms going) and its count's, the blink's bit, changing into each state and on its first tick; the roof's callback with each collision kind in each state, the landings just short of and at the crack's and the fall's; the awnings' springs pushed past their limit, sagging either way, springing back fast and slow, settled, capped either way, rustled by a landing or a changed count, and quiet, and their callback with each joint, collision kind and collision state; the street's process with the warning down and up, the chimney's wait running out with a smoke and without, and the shadow check; the setup, every map's object and the cars' and the street's setups from several seeds; and the empty processes, the destroy callbacks and the demo and load hooks from the street's calls. All compare clean.

Its replay check (`stage-onett.check.log`) shows no divergence between the recording and ssbm-rs's playback.

What the stage needed beyond its own code: the hazard sign for Onett (`un_802FD8A0`, moved right, through the HUD's `un_802FD708`) and a fighter's standing (`gm_8016C6C0`, through `gm_8016C5C0`); the cars are stage items (`grMaterial_801C8CFC`, `it_80274C60`). The stage's archive is named `/GrOt` in its table, which the file system completes with the language's extension (`GrOt.usd` for English). The cars' table of joints holds a fifth entry past the four cars' (the last car's again), which the first tick's car number 4 reads.

Each port broken on purpose fails a check: a car's resting place, the drugstore's breaking sound, its time down, the tick its platforms go, the landings it takes to fall, the edge the scream waits for, a car's scream, the awnings' spring's sign and their quiet after a rustle, and a spun-off car's least speed (leaf-check's `stages`).

**Kongo Jungle N64** (`stage-kongo-jungle-64`, Falco against Sheik and Zelda, two `BENCH_AI` players, four minutes; `groldkongo.c`, `game::gr::oldkongo`) compares every one of its trace's 14,632 ticks with no difference and no stale read (`--fp hardware`), with `--heap` too, to the trace's end, and passes `sync-test --rollback 7 --fp hardware` (14,518 ticks checked). The match runs the barrel below the stage the whole time, turning in spurts toward directions drawn by weight and swinging along its path at a speed that rises, holds and falls; the birds fly across the background in both directions; and the barrel is registered as a device that catches fighters (`ftCo_800C0764`), which the fighter code asks about each fighter every tick (`ftColl_8007BA0C`, the barrel's `grOldKongo_80210454`). Nobody falls into the barrel in the match; the barrel set's match has fighters taken in and shot out (`ftCo_8009EB18`, `ftCo_8009EC70`, "The barrels' ride" below). leaf-check's `stages` runs every function of the unit from the match's saved calls (`local/calls/stages/oracle-stage-kongo-jungle-64`, 84 calls, 470 MiB packed to 19 MiB): the barrel's process with its turn, swing and hold set to each branch (a turn starting either way, its time up and a direction drawn, the clamp, coasting and stopping, each swing state's timer ending, a hold with no fighter, a fighter's countdown running and over, the shot's start and a shot at something that isn't a fighter), the birds' process with the wait ending from many seeds, the direction drawn from 24 spreads of seeds, the catch with the fighter at the barrel and just inside and outside its radius (the barrel's sound, animation and flash, the effect and the controller's rumble), the fighter's device loop and its test of each device type, each object's creation, and the empty processes, destroy callbacks, the demo and load hooks and the barrel letting go and dropping from the barrel's calls. All compare clean.

Its replay check (`stage-kongo-jungle-64.check.log`) shows five divergences between the recording and ssbm-rs's playback: four are the fighters' heights on their entrances' first tick (frames -118 and -113 and the pre-frames after them), one ulp apart and equal from the next tick, and one is the off-screen flag on a doubled round (port 1, frame 10866, as on Yoshi's Island N64 above). Every later event agrees.

**Hyrule Temple** (`stage-hyrule-temple`, Samus against Luigi, two `BENCH_AI` players, four minutes; `grshrine.c`, `game::gr::shrine`) compares every one of its trace's 14,637 ticks with no difference and no stale read, with `--heap` too, and passes `sync-test --rollback 7` (14,518 ticks). The stage does little in play: its setup adds a breeze over the whole stage (`lb_80011A50`), the temple's collision follows its model and the wind sources age each tick, and its start hook does nothing. The computer player's route through the temple (`grshrineroute.c`, the CPU AI's) isn't reached: both players are humans played by `BENCH_AI`. What the match needed beyond the stage: the stage music's choice asks whether Marth is unlocked (`gm_IsCKindUnlocked`, the save data's mask by unlock index), and the ceiling tech, which the temple's low ceilings reach (Luigi teching under the temple at frame 1750): its animation callback (`ftCo_PassiveCeil_Anim`) cleared the script's drift flag when it took it (`ftCheckThrowB3`), which the port had read without clearing, so the drift ran every tick after; the match found it at tick 1886. leaf-check's `stages` runs every function of the unit from the match's saved calls (`oracle-stage-hyrule-temple`, 33 calls, 189 MiB packed to 11 MiB), the ceiling tech's check (hugging a ceiling and not), cling and animation callback (the flag set and clear) among them.

Its replay check shows two divergences, the first player's height on its entrance's first tick (frames -118 and -117), one ulp apart, and nothing after.

What these two stages needed beyond their own code: the catching devices' registry and the fighter's loop over it (`ftCo_800C0764`, `ftColl_8007BA0C`, `ftCo_800C0A28` for each device type, `ftCo_800C0874`), `ftLib_StartRumble`, the character unlock test, the ceiling tech's fix (`ftCo_PassiveCeil_Anim` clearing its script's flag as `ftCheckThrowB3` does, the fix Great Bay's match found too), and one in the camera: `Camera_800307D0` (the view's edges on the stage plane, which Kongo Jungle N64's birds fly in from) returns ±8.5070587e37 for an edge that doesn't meet the plane, a constant one ulp below the one the port had (0x7E7FFFFF, not 0x7E800000); no capture had reached that branch.

Each port broken on purpose fails a check: the barrel's turn ending a tick early, its stop not snapping to its heading, its swing speeding up twice as fast, the birds showing the wrong part or flying at another height, a direction's angle, the catch's sound and radius, the device test's bit, the catch tested before the device check, the shadow check's comparison, the barrel's `xE8`, the setup's order, the barrel's release and drop tests, the camera span's constant (Kongo Jungle N64's checks); the breeze's scale, the background's draw flags, the start hook dropped, the shadow check's comparison, a load hook that writes, the ceiling tech's flag kept and its throw flags kept on clinging (Hyrule Temple's). One first try survived and showed a check too weak, now strengthened: the match's one saved cling had no throw flag set, so the cling's clearing them went unseen; the check now sets them first.

**Yoshi's Island** (`stage-yoshis-island`, Captain Falcon against Peach, two `BENCH_AI` players, four minutes; `gryorster.c`, `game::gr::yorster`) compares every one of its trace's 14,635 ticks with no difference and no stale read, with `--heap` too, and passes `sync-test --rollback 7` (14,518 ticks). Its nine blocks are each a collision joint and a hurtbox item (`ityaku.c`, `game::it::kinds::yaku`, `It_PKind_Random`'s kind number), made by the stage's start hook (at frame -39). In the match the fighters' hits flip blocks 47 times and landings on them 15 times (a probe run; every block flips at least once); each flipped block spins and comes back, 15 times only on its retry because a fighter stood where it would come back. leaf-check's `stages` runs every function of the unit and the stage items' from the match's saved calls (`oracle-stage-yoshis-island`, 69 calls, 376 MiB packed to 15 MiB): the blocks' process with every block set to each branch (a hit or a landing just over, well over and just short of the parameters', the delay over and running, the spin and the retry well short of, a tick short of and at their 323 and 25 ticks, a state past the five, the blocks in mixed states), the block hit and the landing callback (by something other than a fighter, far over and under the speed, on another joint), the items made through the stage glue and directly (on a joint, at a point, falling or not, with neither), their physics following a joint and at a point, a hit taken, the hit and touch callbacks and the forgetting, the glue's small functions, `Ground_801C32D4` for other maps and parts, and the fighters' hits on items' hurtboxes with the item's number set to the two its sound tests. All compare clean, but for ten of the blocks' calls in the landing prepares, where ssbm-rs's reference faults on a hardware register (0xCC006800) as the landing's sound plays outside a match.

Its replay check (`stage-yoshis-island.check.log`) shows 418 divergences between the recording and ssbm-rs's playback, all in the first player's entrance: on its first tick (frame -118) the recording run sets Captain Falcon down 1.3 below his spawn point (y 4.64), where he stays, drifting 0.0003 in x, until he drops to the stage at frame -54, while the playback has him rise from his spawn point as an entrance does and drop at -50 (27 heights, 180 x positions and two action states, pre-frames with them); the 0.0003 stays in his x until frame 89, and every event after that agrees, percents throughout. ssbm-rs's playback is a trustworthy oracle here: its interpreter (the original's machine code, `--port sysdolphin/baselib/perf`) plays the replay's first 260 fields with the same 280 divergences as its ports do, so the trace isn't an ssbm-rs port's doing; the controllers alone in the recording run's own boot show none (`stage-yoshis-island.pads.log`). The two runs already differ before the entrance starts, in Falcon's collision data: the spawn's floor probe (`ft_80082A68`, an airborne pass from 10 above his spawn point to 10 below) finds a floor (line 23) in the recording run and none in the playback, so the recording run starts his entrance standing on it. The blocks' collision joints are the stage's from its setup, and their items come at the match's start (frame -39) in both runs; what places a floor under Falcon's spawn in one boot and not the other is the recording run's own boot (`BENCH_CODES` and the recording codes), not the stage's or the fighters' code, and the playback is what Slippi's playback of the recording does.

What the stage needed beyond its own code: the stages' hurtbox items (`it_802E6AEC` and its callbacks), whose spawn points `It_PKind_Random`'s article at the executable's attributes (`it_803F8C08`), a write into `ItCo.dat`'s data that melee-hd keeps as state (`it::Globals::yaku_attr`, read by `item::attr_of`); `grmaterial.c`'s stage item glue (`grMaterial_801C8CFC`, `801C8DE0`, `801C8E08`, `801C8E28`, `801C8E68`); `Ground_801C32D4` (a map's part's collision joint); the hit sound on a hurtbox of that kind (`it_802703E8`'s and `it_802706D0`'s tail, which reads the item's first halfword as a Pokémon's number); `it_8026B718`; and a collision joint callback that reads and writes the object that landed: mpcoll runs it mid-pass with the fighters and items out of reach, so a pass on Yoshi's Island notes each fighter's entrance and move first and gives the landing's hitlag after it returns (`gr::yorster::Landing`); nothing in a pass reads either.

Each port broken on purpose fails a check: the flip's start frame's scale, the spin's and the retry's lengths, the stage's animation, the blocks' hurtbox radius, the hit's damage sign, the landing's hitlag parameter, a load hook that writes (Yoshi's Island's checks); the item's collision kind, its joint's rotation not followed, its hit count kept, the hurtbox radius the glue sets, the collision joint's lookup by the wrong field and the hit sound's number (the stage items').

**Jungle Japes** (`stage-jungle-japes`, Mario against Pikachu, two `BENCH_AI` players, four minutes; `grgarden.c`, `game::gr::garden`) compares every one of its trace's 14,636 ticks with no difference and no stale read, with `--heap` too, and passes `sync-test --rollback 7` (14,518 ticks). The river below sweeps a fighter in it along (a wind device the fighter code polls, registered by the stage's start hook) with a splash as it falls in; Cranky Kong cycles through his four animations, the first and third held for random counts; and the Klaptrap, a stage hurtbox item (`it::kinds::yaku`) on the river, leaps at a random point along it 21 times in the match, biting with its item's state 2, whose script is the stage's own (`ALDYakuAll`, which `Ground_801C0800` writes into the random stage item's state descriptions; the item's script lookup reads it, `it::anim::state_script`). The match first stopped at tick 297 on that bite, which ran the article's empty script. The background's six guide points make the stage fit its fog to the camera every display pass (`Ground_801C4FAC`: the fog starts at the near points' distance from the eye and ends at the far ones', the left or right pair by where the eye looks, blended with the front pair by how far it looks back), and the camera's eye stays above the stage's floor (`grGarden_80203624`, -20). leaf-check's `stages` runs every function of the unit from the match's saved calls (`oracle-stage-jungle-japes`, 98 calls, 505 MiB packed to 17 MiB): Cranky's process with each animation's count run out and left, the Klaptrap's process waiting (its wait over from several seeds, and running) and leaping at each splash's tick, the river with the fighter above the surface, below it and just falling in (its current checked through the vector it fills), the splash at three sizes, the shadow check, the fog fit with the eye to either side and behind, the guide points at the eye and crossed, the items' state changes in the match (the Klaptrap's bite among them), a fighter's camera box depth (`ftLib_80086B80`, the splash's size), and the empty processes, the demo hook, the Klaptrap's hit callback and the camera's floor from Cranky's calls. All compare clean.

Its replay check (`stage-jungle-japes.check.log`) shows 230 divergences between the recording and ssbm-rs's playback: four are the fighters' heights on their entrances' first ticks (frames -118 and -113 and the pre-frames after them), one ulp apart and equal from the next tick, and 226 are the second player's post-frame "hitstun" word from frame 3017 on: Pikachu's Thunder state keeps a heap pointer in the fighter's misc action-state word (`0x80F1B840` in the trace), which differs with the heap's layout between the recording run and the playback (the playback's player names alone move it), and nothing reads it as a number. Every position, action state and percent agrees: ssbm-rs's playback is a trustworthy oracle here.

Each port broken on purpose fails a check: the Klaptrap's bite's state, Cranky's animation count, the river's current parameter, the shadow check's comparison, the camera's floor, the fog's least start, the stage item script's index and the guide points' joints.

**Kongo Jungle** (`stage-kongo-jungle`, Ness against Yoshi, two `BENCH_AI` players, four minutes; `grkongo.c` and `itklap.c`, `game::gr::kongo` and `game::it::kinds::klap`) compares every one of its trace's 14,636 ticks with no difference and no stale read, with `--heap` too, and passes `sync-test --rollback 7` (14,518 ticks). The rope bridge is fifteen planks on a static table (`grKg_803E188C`, kept as state with its two posts, `gr::kongo::Globals`) that swing back toward rest, pull on their neighbours and are kicked by whatever lands on them, its collision lines following their heights and its posts leaning and swaying with the slope; the landing callback runs mid-pass, so a pass notes the planks it kicks and gives the kicks once it returns (`gr::kongo::Kicks`), and nothing in a pass reads a plank's speed. The stage's process draws events: floating platforms that rise, carry fighters, count landings and sink, logs that float past behind, and once (frame 6656) a Klap Trap riding a river path (a spline the path follows at an even speed, turned along it, `Ground_801C4B50` with its tangent, `lbShadow_8000E9F0`), which swims and snaps, falls off the path, is caught by the barrel (which turns and swings as Kongo Jungle N64's does) and shot out at the parameters' hit (`it_802E2330`, its knockback from `it_80270CD8`), spinning away. No fighter falls into the barrel (the barrel set's match has them, "The barrels' ride" below); no box rides the river (items are off). Two things the match found beyond the stage's code: the objects an event makes put their scale joints' descriptors deeper on the stack than the setup's (`EVENT_FRAME`), a new key in HSD's ID table (the heap comparison found the missing entry at tick 2135), and a destroyed object's collision joints keep pointing at its model's freed joint (`Ground_801C4A08` doesn't clear them), which the reference's heap later gives a new joint; nothing reads them until they're bound again, so they keep the freed joint's address alone (`gr::Globals::freed_coll_jobjs`), compared as an address. leaf-check's `stages` runs every function of the units from the match's saved calls (`oracle-stage-kongo-jungle`, 230 calls, 1.2 GiB packed to 36 MiB): the barrel's process at each branch of its turn, swing and hold (a held fighter's countdown, the shot at neither a fighter nor an item, waiting for no Klap Trap near), its direction from 24 spreads of seeds, the fighter catch at and just inside and outside the barrel's reach, the release and drop holding a fighter, something else and nothing, the stage's process with the event's wait running out from many seeds (each event, a repeat drawn again or not), the planks swung a little and far, the posts' sway at each branch, the bridge's landing callback of another ground kind and at and past each end, the platforms at each state and at their landings' and ticks' limits, a landing on a platform of each ground kind, the logs and the river path near and at its end, the spline's pose and tangent at the ends and outside, the shadow check, every Klap Trap function (snapping, a hit taken and its knockback from its falling and spinning calls, which the match never ran), the shot at four angles and its knockback, and the empty processes and the demo and load hooks from the barrel's calls. All compare clean.

Its replay check (`stage-kongo-jungle.check.log`) shows 480 divergences between the recording and ssbm-rs's playback: four are the fighters' heights on their entrances' first ticks (frames -118 and -113 and the pre-frames after them), one or two ulps apart and equal from the next tick, and 476 are the first player's post-frame "hitstun" word from frame 994 on: one of Ness's action states keeps a heap pointer in the fighter's misc action-state word, which differs with the heap's layout between the recording run and the playback (the playback's player names alone move it), and nothing reads it as a number. Every position, action state and percent agrees: ssbm-rs's playback is a trustworthy oracle here.

Each port broken on purpose fails a check: the planks' easing, the bridge landing's last plank, the barrel's stopping distance (which needed a heading between half and the whole of it), the catch's rumble, the platforms' landing limit, the path's step, the event's platform, the posts' joints, the shadow check's comparison and the Klap Trap's depth under its path.

**The barrels' ride** (`barrel-kongo-jungle-64`, Fox against Marth, and `barrel-kongo-jungle`, Ness against Yoshi: the `barrel` set's two four-minute time matches, items off; `ftCo_BarrelWait.c`, `game::ft::co::barrel_wait`). The level 9 AI recovers too well to fall into a barrel (neither stage match had a fighter caught in its four minutes), so these matches' players are walkers ([Oracle sets](#oracle-sets)): each walks off its side of the stage, drifts back under it as it falls, where the barrel sweeps, and taps A on every other round. In each match the barrel takes a fighter in eight times and shoots it out eight times (the first catch at tick 940 on Kongo Jungle N64 and 200 on Kongo Jungle), and on Kongo Jungle N64 waiting fighters press A, which has the barrel shoot at once. Each compares every one of its trace's ticks (14,635 and 14,636) with no difference and no stale read, with `--heap` too, and passes `sync-test --rollback 7 --fp hardware` (14,518 ticks each). The ride: the barrel's catch (`ftCo_800C0874`, a type 2 device) has the fighter let go of what and whom it holds and wait in `BarrelWait` (`ftCo_8009EB18`), still, invisible and intangible, its camera box's extents the common data's; it keeps to the barrel (`ftCo_8009EC44`), and A or B asks the barrel to shoot at once (`ftCo_BarrelWait_IASA`, through `Ground_801C4DD0` to the stage's release); the shot (Kongo Jungle N64's at the barrel's angle, Kongo Jungle's at its heading, each with the parameters' hit) puts the fighter at the barrel with its collision and camera box, keeps it from the barrels for the common data's while, and gives it the hit's knockback at that angle (`ftCo_Barrel_ApplyKnockback`, an inline the item barrel cannon's launch shares) and damage, tumbling (`ftCo_8009EC70`). A fighter that dies inside has the barrel let it go (`ftCo_8009EAF8`, `Ground_801C4E20`), which no match reaches: the barrel stays far inside the blast zones. The replay checks show four divergences each, the fighters' heights on their entrances' first ticks one or two ulps apart, as in the stage matches; the controllers checks show none. leaf-check's `stages` runs every port from the two matches' saved calls (`local/calls/stages/oracle-barrel-NAME`, 67 and 66 calls, 372 and 348 MiB packed to 14 and 15 MiB; the capture runs' lockstep found ssbm-rs's ports of every captured function equal to the original's on every call, ten and thirteen million calls): `barrel_ride` runs the barrels' processes from these matches' saved states, the entry as saved and from every state the catch loop saved a fighter in, the waiting callbacks, A, B, both, neither and another button pressed with the barrel holding the fighter, not holding and shooting, the death hook and both stage calls from the waiting fighter's calls with the barrel holding it, another fighter, shooting and empty, and the shot as saved, at seven angles facing either way and with its damage and knockback changed; `oldkongo_barrel` and `kongo_barrel` now shoot a fighter from the stage matches' states too, and `oldkongo_devices` takes the fighter at the barrel in. All compare clean. Each port broken on purpose fails a check: the entry's intangibility mask, its camera box's extents and its `x209A`, the wait's buttons (A alone; a first try survived, since no saved call paired B with a holding barrel, and the check now runs each button against each hold), keeping to the barrel, the death hook's letting go, both stages' release and letting go by stage kind, the shot's lock-out timer, its damage and its knockback's mirroring, and Kongo Jungle N64's shot angle (the heading for the angle) and its parameters' offset.

**Poké Floats** (`stage-poke-floats`, Ness against Yoshi, two `BENCH_AI` players, eight minutes; `grpura.c`, `game::gr::pura`) compares every one of its trace's 28,990 ticks with no difference and no stale read, with `--heap` too, and passes `sync-test --rollback 7` (28,918 ticks). The author's Poké Floats replay (`Game_20260524T175101`, Young Link against Marth, 3.19, traced into `local/traces/v5-mixed/Game_20260524T175101.trc` as the other Slippi replays are, names off, `--port all`, its replay check clean) compares all 15,055 of its ticks the same way with `--fp slippi` and passes `sync-test --rollback 7 --fp slippi` (14,942 ticks). The stage is one animated model (map 4) that carries the floats past; each float is an object of its own (maps 2, 3 and 5 to 26, 25 of them, map 13 three times) that copies a stage joint's translation, rotation, scale and visibility every tick and draws with a toon texture from the executable (`grPu_803E7620`): its setup marks its materials toon-shaded and compiles their TEV with the texture set (`HSD_MObjSetToonTextureImage`, which makes the toon TObj the first time, and `grPura_80213250`'s recursion, which compiles a list's materials once per list it is in), and its render callback sets the texture around the stage object's draw. The stage frames each float with a camera subject of its own (25 of them, active while the float shows), moves the 41 collision vertices that ride on the floats to their joints every tick (`grPu_803E6C0C`, a table in the executable the setup writes the joints into, kept as state with the stage's globals), widens four joints' bounds and reclassifies joint 24's lines; the sky fades the fog and the camera's background between four colors, 3,600 ticks each. The setup pulls the camera limits in to 0.8 of their distance from their center. What the stage needed beyond its own code: the joint search by collision joint (`Ground_801C33C0`, `Ground_801C32D4` the other way) and the comparer's pairing of the toon TObj, which the floats' compiled TEV stages name before the display statics compare it. The floats' scale joints file themselves in the ID table under the stage setup's frame less the create function's, the stage object's setup's and `grPura_802125F0`'s (`pura::FLOAT_FRAME`); under the setup's own they replaced its entry and the fighters came out 0x40 bytes low. leaf-check's `stages` runs every function of the unit from the match's saved calls (`oracle-stage-poke-floats`, 63 calls, 333 MiB packed to 11 MiB): the sky's process at each point of its fade with pairs of colors, the floats' process with their joints and the stage's hidden and shown, the stage's process and the subjects' upkeep with the subjects' joints hidden and shown, the floats' setup with the stage's joints scaled past 2, the TEV compiles, the render callback in two passes, `Ground_801C33C0` for other maps and joints, and the callbacks no capture reached (the empty processes and destroy callbacks, the lights' setup, the shadow check, the load and demo hooks) run by address from the sky's calls. Each port broken on purpose fails a check: the fade's length and its interpolation's base, the float scale's threshold, a subject's extent, a vertex's joint, a joint's bounds left out, a subject's state, the reclassified joint, a float's visibility test, a material compiled once too few, the part a float copies and the search's field.

Its replay check shows 630 divergences, none of them gameplay: 626 are Ness's misc state word holding a heap pointer (post-frame hitstun from frame 1709, 0x80EBF140 against 0x80EC1D20, the recording's and playback's heaps), and 2 pre-frame and 2 post-frame are a fighter's x on frames 15875 and 15876; the controllers check is clean.

**Rainbow Cruise** (`stage-rainbow-cruise`, Donkey Kong against Young Link, two `BENCH_AI` players, eight minutes; `grrcruise.c`, `game::gr::rcruise`) compares every one of its trace's 28,991 ticks with no difference and no stale read, with `--heap` too, and passes `sync-test --rollback 7` (28,918 ticks). The course is map 3 twice: a hidden, frozen copy whose animation leads, and the copy shown, which each tick finds where the leading copy's camera joint looks through its interest joint onto the stage's plane and moves the ship, the flag, the blocks and the scenery by the opposite (the step from the last is the wind the stage blows on airborne items, `grRCruise_80201918`, now answering `grLib_801C9E60` for Rainbow Cruise). The ship holds two blocks from the heap (`Map_Chikuwa`'s seventeen planks and the twenty parts that appear with the camera, kept in its variables with their addresses), starts moving its parts at the match's start hook, tilts its deck toward the heavier side of its riders (the deck's collision callback weighs each rider's distance from the pivot), drops its planks after a fighter stands on them (they shake at random heights, fall below the camera limits, come back and blink), shows and hides the parts the camera reaches (their collision in and out of the search), and steers a wind source that sways the flag on its dynamics chain (`grLib_801C9B20`, `grLib_801C9B8C`, the links compared with the stage object). The three blocks play their animation once stood on and blink away. What the stage needed beyond its own code: `Ground_801C32AC` (a map's collision settled where it stands), the collision callbacks' access to a stage object's joint and `Ground` from its GObj (`ft::coll::StageMaps::jobj`, `ground_of`), and an animation rate set from the objects alone (`anime::set_rate_below`). leaf-check's `stages` runs every function of the unit from the match's saved calls (`oracle-stage-rainbow-cruise`, 136 calls, 750 MiB packed to 24 MiB): the ship's process with the match started and not, the tilt from each state with riders either side, none, swinging and level, every plank, block and appearing part in each state with counts at and past their waits, the wind's turns and gusts, the deck's, planks' and blocks' touches by each collision kind, the course frozen and not, the render callback in two passes, and the callbacks no capture reached (the empty processes and destroy callbacks, the shadow check, the load and demo hooks, the course's wind and the planks' line test, which only the CPU AI asks) run by address from the course's calls. Each port broken on purpose fails a check: a block's wait and the states a touch starts, the deck's reach and a rider's weight cap, the tilt's level threshold and its sides, a plank's fall cap, shake height and touch, the wind's turn period and gust strength, a part going shown instead of hidden, the course's lift and the objects it moves, the tree walk's siblings, a part's display object list, the gusts at start, the joint taken out of the search, a part's first state, the chain's first link and the planks' first joint. Three first tries survived and showed checks too weak, now strengthened: no saved rider stood between 4 and 5 from the pivot, no tilt lay between 0.2 and 0.3 degrees, and no line tested was joint 27's; the checks now place a rider either side of the reach, set those tilts, and test the first line of each joint about the planks'.

Its replay check shows four divergences, each player's height one ulp apart on its entrance's first tick (frames -118 and -117, -113 and -112), and nothing after; the controllers check is clean. With the Ice Climbers it is offered too since Nana's computer player reads the wind through the stage's own `grLib_801C9E60` ([the Ice Climbers on the stages](#the-ice-climbers-on-the-stages)).

**Flat Zone** (`stage-flat-zone`, Young Link against Jigglypuff, two `BENCH_AI` players, four minutes; `grflatzone.c` and its falling tools, `ittools.c`, `game::gr::flatzone` and `game::it::kinds::tools`) compares every one of its trace's 14,637 ticks with no difference and no stale read, with `--heap` too, and passes `sync-test --rollback 7` (14,518 ticks). A controller (map 2) waits a random while, then starts one of two events, drawn so it never repeats the last: the helmet game (map 8), which drops a random one of the five tools from 5 below the top blast zone every few ticks for a while, each falling spinning its own way until it meets a floor, a fighter, a shield or another hitbox, then lying there blinking (a fighter's hit knocks a falling tool back up and away, its hitbox's damage raised by the hit's); or the oil panic (map 3), whose man walks in from a random side step by step (the controller's screen turning for the left), pours his bucket and leaves a slick on the floor below where it lands, a dynamic attribute area of terrain 0x11 for a while (`grDynamicAttr_801CA0F8`), which makes the floor slippery. Between events the platforms (map 5) animate in and out: a set is drawn (`grFz_803E7A68`'s rows) and a row of it at random each wait, every collision joint relinked when the animation changes; during an event the platforms hold their set's pose. Events 1 and 2 (maps 4 and 6, with their own processes) are never drawn: the draw is from 3 and 4 only. The setup brings the camera's near and far planes to 1 and 1,000 and the bottom blast zone 50 lower; Flat Zone starts no enemy spawner. The match ran both events twice (40 tools, 11 hits dealt, one knocked by Young Link; the oil both times from the left, each on a floor), and sets 0 to 2 of the platforms. What the stage needed beyond its own code: the fighters' joint class in the presentation (below); `grdynamicattr.c` was already ported, and its area is now a stage object's variable too (compared by its address in `grDynamicAttr_8049EFA0`). Melee's bug kept: an oil panic started again while the oil lasts frees the area only when there is none (`grFlatzone_802176BC` tests `xCC == NULL`), which `grDynamicAttr_801CA1C0` ignores, so the oil stays until its timer runs out ([Melee's own bugs](melee-bugs.md)). The match's replay check shows two divergences, port 2's height on its entrance's first ticks (frames -113 and -112), and nothing after; the controllers check is clean. There is no local Slippi replay on Flat Zone. leaf-check's `stages` runs every function of the two units from the match's saved calls (`oracle-stage-flat-zone`, 106 calls, 536 MiB packed to 17 MiB): the controller with each event drawn after each and from several seeds, waiting, counting and the count already out; the platforms waiting, hidden, posed, counting and changing in every set with each of its rows; the oil panic started from each state on both sides, waiting, walking, pouring (also off the stage, where no floor is below, and with no area free), the oil lasting and the man hiding; the unused events started; the tools' process counting on and off a drop and ending; each tool spawned facing either way, falling to its life's end and still, lying at the blink's turns, knocked from a fighter on either side, without one, by something not a fighter, too fast and capped, and the reactions no capture reached (clanked, a shield hit, absorbed, bounced off a shield, reflected) run from a falling tool's calls; and the callbacks no capture reached (the empty processes, setups and destroy callbacks, the shadow check, the load, start and demo hooks) run by address from the controller's calls. Each port broken on purpose fails a check: the events' draw order and the last event not kept (the controller's); the count's limit, a set's row's animation and the joints not relinked (the platforms'); the setup's first set and the pose's wait (the setup's, the pose's also the controller's); the oil's reach, its radius and terrain, the right side's wait not halved, the step's tick, the slick's last tick and a restart's hiding skipped (the oil panic's); the drop's height, its collision joint and the event's end not reported (the tools' process); and the knock's side, its limit and damage base, the blink's bit, the spin's sign, an absorbed tool's life and the lying life taken from the fall's (the tools'). One survived two runs: the lying life's mask (0xFFFE made 0xFFFF), which the disc's data can't tell apart, its lying life being 30. The flat fighters' presentation: Flat Zone's fighters have a depth scale (`Fighter_80068E64`), and the fighters' joint class makes their root joint's position matrix leave the matrix that undoes it (`ftParts_JObjMakePositionMtx`, `ft_jobj_scale`), which their shapes put before their normals' matrices (`ftParts_PObjSetupMtx`); the display pass now names a fighter's root joint's drawing (`Draw::FighterJoint`) and the presentation follows. Six frames of the match (600, 2800 with a tool falling, 6000, 6900, 9000, 9300) draw identical to ssbm-rs's, every draw and both images; with the depth scale left out every lit fighter shape's normal matrices differ and frame 600 measures PSNR 36.4 dB ([the player](player.md#checking-frames)).

**Mushroom Kingdom** (`stage-mushroom-kingdom`, Samus against Luigi, two `BENCH_AI` players, four minutes; `grinishie1.c`, `game::gr::inishie1`) compares every one of its trace's 14,637 ticks with no difference and no stale read (`--fp hardware`), with `--heap` too, to the trace's end, and passes `sync-test --rollback 7 --fp hardware` (14,518 ticks checked). The stage holds its 19 brick blocks in a block from the heap (`HSD_MemAlloc`, kept in its variables with its address, `gr::vars::Inishie1Vars`); each block is a collision joint and a hurtbox item (`ityaku.c`). The match breaks 86 of them, 65 by hits on their items (`fn_801FBF6C`) and 21 bumped from below faster than 0.7 a tick (the ceiling's joint callback, `fn_801FBEB8`), each with its effect, sound and small quake, and each comes back after its random wait once nothing is within 15 of it, blinking in. The ceiling's callback runs inside mpcoll's pass, where the rest of the game isn't at hand, so the pass's caller gives its breaks once mpcoll returns (`gr::inishie1::Bumps`, beside Kongo Jungle's kicks); each is the pass's last act and nothing a pass reads changes with a break (the block's collision stays, and a pass draws no random numbers, makes no effects and plays no sounds), so they happen in the original's order. The two lifts on their pulley weigh what stands on them every tick (their joints' callback, `fn_801FC9AC`) and drift back level; no one pulled them apart far enough to fall in this match, which leaf-check covers. Under 20 seconds left the stage changes its song to the fast one (`GetMatchTimer`, `lbAudioAx_80023F28`), here at tick 13,326: the match's start now loads the fast song's file too (`vs_match`), as it loads the stage's song, or the audio driver stopped there. With items on, two random blocks start under ? blocks and more come after one is broken (map 2, its scale joint's descriptor 0xE0 below the setup's frame, or 0x140 below the process loop's); the match has items off, so leaf-check runs them. leaf-check's `stages` runs every function of the unit with code from the match's saved calls (`local/calls/stages/oracle-stage-mushroom-kingdom`, 73 calls, 369 MiB packed to 13 MiB; `capture-mk.sh` beside it): the setup with items off and on, every block in each state with its counts run out and not, a bump from each height, the ? blocks' waits running out one at a time and a tick apart (every block's item, the blocks broken, blinking in or at rest under their new ? blocks), a block bumped at speeds under, at and past 0.7 and hit, each holding nothing and each item, its giving up and breaking on their own, a ? block put over a block broken, blinking in and at rest, taken off, its item popped with items off and on, a ? block's setup and process blinking in (at its last count too), done and hidden for good; the lifts' process in each state (balancing with no weight, one side's, both sides', past the cap and past the most they part; falling above and below the blast zone; gone with the wait running out and not; coming back from either side) and their callback on either lift, another joint and each ground kind; the music as saved and sped up already (the song's change stood in for, as a captured state's audio has no timeline to start a stream on); and the empty processes and destroy callbacks, map 1's setup, the shadow check and the load and demo hooks from the stage's calls. All compare clean. Each port broken on purpose fails its leaf-check test (one build and run each): a giving-up block's shake turning at 6 instead of 5 and a ? block coming after a break holding the second kind of item for the first (`inishie1_blocks`), the break's sound one off and a bump needing more than 0.8 a tick (`inishie1_breaks`), the setup's two ? blocks' items swapped (`inishie1_setup`), a rider's landing weight added for the other ground kind and a lift's drift back going the wrong way (`inishie1_lifts`), and a ? block's blink skipping its last count (`inishie1_hatena`, missed until the test ran a count of 1).

Its replay check shows 12 divergences, the fighters' state flags on their entrance's first ticks (frames -121 and -120 on), none of them gameplay.

**Mushroom Kingdom II** (`stage-mushroom-kingdom-2`, Mario against Pikachu, two `BENCH_AI` players, four minutes; `grinishie2.c`, `game::gr::inishie2`, Birdo, `itkyasarin.c`, `game::it::kinds::kyasarin`, and her eggs, `itkyasarinegg.c`, `game::it::kinds::kyasarinegg`) compares every one of its trace's 14,637 ticks with no difference and no stale read (`--fp hardware`), with `--heap` too, to the trace's end, and passes `sync-test --rollback 7 --fp hardware` (14,518 ticks checked). The stage sends four kinds of objects after random waits and waits again once each is gone, all of which the match reaches: 27 logs down the waterfall (maps 3 and 4, their collision on after a while, gone at their animation's end), 8 Birdos on their platform (map 15, its collision following her; she walks in, waits, backs off, waits, walks in again, stands, spits 8 eggs, turns away and walks off, flinching from 5 hits), and 9 of Pidgit's carpets (map 1, flying in from a random side, turning about between the camera's edges, then leaving). Birdo and her eggs are enemy items over the enemies' common member (`itZako_ItemVars`), as Tingle and the Klap Trap are. The objects the stage's process makes file their scale joints in the ID table 0x68 (a log) and 0x58 (Birdo's platform, Pidgit's carpet) below the process loop's frame. Under 20 seconds left the music speeds up as Mushroom Kingdom's does (its own copy of the check), here at tick 13,326. leaf-check's `stages` runs every function of the three units with code from the match's saved calls (`local/calls/stages/oracle-stage-mushroom-kingdom-2`, 182 calls, 897 MiB packed to 29 MiB; `capture-mk2.sh` beside it): the setup making every map's object, the stage's process and each sending with its wait running out, at zero and running, its flag on and off, the logs with their collision back and not, their wait running out, for either log, Birdo's platform with her there and gone, her going and her knock-off, Pidgit's carpet with its flags and turns set to each branch (coming in, on its way out, turning first and later, leaving) and the random draw; Birdo's every state's callbacks with her place, facing, waits, eggs and flinch's state set either side of their branches, her spawn facing either way, her hits for a flinch, a turn away and a fall each way (by the seed), and the fall's states from her stand's calls; her eggs spat either way and with no Birdo, and each egg callback with the egg in each state, broken already or not, by the seed (a thrown egg breaking empty or dropping items), from its roll's calls; the music as saved and sped up already (the song's change stood in for); and the empty processes and destroy callbacks, the line hook, the shadow check and the load and demo hooks from the stage's calls. All compare clean. Birdo's and her eggs' `evt_unk` (`it_802EE1E0`, `it_802F0320`) aren't ported: as Tingle's, only a fighter's unload at the scene's end runs them. Each port broken on purpose fails its leaf-check test (one build and run each): Birdo facing out from her side instead of in (`inishie2_setup`), Pidgit's carpet turning about a third time (`inishie2_pidgit`), a log's collision joint swapped with the other's (`inishie2_logs`), Birdo's going starting Pidgit's wait instead of hers and her platform put at its offset's height instead of above her (`inishie2_birdo_platform`), a hit flinching her up to her fall's damage instead of her turn's, her flinch's state running her stand's callback and her mark walking in from the right taken as the far edge (`kyasarin_states`), and an egg's bounce not turning it back and a thrown egg breaking empty on the other draws (`kyasarin_eggs`). The platform's offset subtracted instead of added isn't caught and can't be: the stage's parameter is 0.0 on the disc.

Its replay check shows 367 divergences, none of them gameplay: the first are both players' heights one ulp apart on their entrance's first ticks (frames -113 and -112) and, from frame 2352, Pikachu's misc state word (post-frame hitstun), which holds a heap pointer in some special states.

**Icicle Mountain** (`stage-icicle-mountain`, Dr. Mario against Ganondorf, two `BENCH_AI` players, eight minutes; `gricemt.c`, `game::gr::icemt`) compares every one of its trace's 28,991 ticks with no difference and no stale read, with `--heap` too, and passes `sync-test --rollback 7` (28,918 ticks). In VS mode the stage is the scenery (map 0), the background (map 8) and the climb (map 9), which keeps two of the six fields (maps 1 to 6) in place, one above the other a field's height apart, and moves both by a speed it changes now and then: at rest a wait counts down, then the next of the parameters' twelve speeds is drawn among those not resting (each rests a few draws once picked) and the climb eases to it over the parameters' ticks. When the upper field comes down past its place, the lower one is destroyed and a field drawn among those not resting and not shown is set up above; past the lower one's place the other way round; the field nearer its place and the one past the middle get their points of interest (the camera's), and the scenery's parts are the other four. Each field holds blocks, stage hurtbox items (`ityaku.c`) on a block model the stage's archive holds (its map 7) that a hit hides with an effect, a sound and a small quake; fields 2 and 4 have ice that a landing starts falling, its collision joint leaving the search and coming back at fixed ticks. The background scrolls by 0.3 of the climb's speed and wraps within 3,500. The match runs 42 speed draws, 19 field changes (18 one way, one the other), every field set up (fields 1 to 6 three, three, three, three, five and four times), 12 blocks broken, the ice falling five times, and the background wrapping once. The climb's speed is the wind the stage blows on airborne items (`grIceMt_801FA728`, through `grLib_801C9E60`, which Nana's and the other computer players' recovery read too, `ftCo_800A9CB4`'s Icicle Mountain case, ported with it). VS mode never makes the adventure's climb (map 10, the stage kind 76 setup), its first three fields, its spawner or the Polar Bear's landing hook (`grIceMt_801FA6D8`), which aren't ported. What the stage needed beyond its own code: the stage objects' animations restarted and their loops cleared below a root joint, for the ice's collision callback (`anime::req_anim_below`, `clear_loop_below`). The objects the climb's setup and process make file their scale joints under frames 0x88 below the setup's and 0xD8 below the process loop's (`icemt::CLIMB_INIT_FRAME`, `CLIMB_PROC_FRAME`). leaf-check's `stages` runs every function of the unit with code from the match's saved calls (`oracle-stage-icicle-mountain`, the first 14,640 fields, 151 calls, 875 MiB packed to 26 MiB): the climb's process held and in each of the speed's phases, with a burst (nothing in VS mode starts one) and the upper field moved past and near each place; the speed's step in each phase; the draws with rest counts left; the field change from each side and with no field below; the gap between each pair of fields; the fields' processes with their points due; the falling ice still, running and at and past its joint's ticks; its landings by each collision kind and with it running; the background at rest and past either end of its wrap; the first fields' setup with other pairs; a part's index for a joint not in the model; and the callbacks no capture reached (the empty processes and destroy callbacks, the shadow check, the demo hook and the wind) run by address from other calls. Field 6 shows only after the captures' fields, so its setup, process and destroy callback are checked by the diff. Each port broken on purpose fails a check: the gap's sign, the speed change's divisor, a speed's rest count left unset, the next field allowed to be the upper one, the threshold for a field near its place, the ice's joint coming back, the blocks' quake and hurtbox radius, the background's scroll factor and wrap, the ice's landing kind, the field change's half point, a field's ice part and the scenery's first point. Three first tries survived and showed checks too weak, now strengthened: the fields' and background's heights were written to their joints' x, no state put the upper field between 10 and 12 from its place, and the next field was drawn only with both shown fields the same (as the field change calls it); the checks now write the joints' y, place the field 11 either side, and draw with the two apart.

Its replay check shows no divergence in 388,764 events, and the controllers check is clean.
**Mute City** (`stage-mute-city`, Bowser against Pichu, two `BENCH_AI` players, eight minutes; `grmutecity.c` and `grfzerocar.c`, `game::gr::mutecity` and `game::gr::fzerocar`) compares every one of its trace's 28,990 ticks with no difference and no stale read, with `--heap` too, and passes `sync-test --rollback 7` (28,918 ticks). The course (map 30) carries the fighters' platform (map 29) round the race track on an animation of 3,600 frames, and a script read by that animation's frame (`grMc_803E34E0`, 132 commands) runs the stage: it hides and shows the platform's parts, takes collision joints out of the search and puts them back, moves the camera limits and the blast zones by their offsets from the camera's center, raises the hazard sign with its sound before each stop on the track, starts the course's and the platform's lights' color scripts, switches the music's aux B send (`lbAudioAx_80024D78`, the table's third column, which only Mute City and Big Blue use), makes two of the track's lines a terrain of their own, and steers the platform's sway. While the platform stands on the track, the track's collision joint (4) follows the cars' two splines: each end searches 0.0004 at a time from its last place for where its spline crosses the camera's plane (`grMuteCity_801F094C`), the ends' lines take one of three shapes, the middle line spans the middle three tenths, and the lines hurt (`grMuteCity_801F2BBC`, which `ftCo_800C0B20` now takes as a hit of a burying device with no object: 37 hits in the match), items on them burst (`fn_801F2B58`), and joints the track covers take a fighter's shadow only above it (`grMuteCity_801F2C10`). Thirty cars race on their own (`grFZeroCar_801CAFBC` builds them with their pilots under map 2's joint): each keeps how far it has gone, its place on the splines, a speed and a lane; the race sorts them by distance and marks each car close behind, beside or faster than its neighbours and the first and last five, and each brakes, swerves, speeds up or drifts back to the middle by its marks (`grMuteCity_801F1328`, `801F106C`); each stands between the splines by its lane, turned along the track; one beside a faster car honks (at most one sound a tick, by its distance in front of the camera); passing the track's middle by the stage it shakes the screen with one of three sounds (louder while the script asks); in front of the stage it carries a hurtbox item, whose hits on fighters crash (23 in the match) and whose hits taken damage it, past a share of the parameters it smokes and past more it is wrecked, its model swapped for the wreck's. No fighter damaged a car in the match, so the smoke, the wreck and the cars' hit callbacks run only in leaf-check. Two of the original's defects are modeled ([Melee's own bugs](melee-bugs.md#mute-citys-race-order-reaches-the-word-before-it)): the race's sort reaches the word before its order (Brinstar's last bubble's object, `Globals::order_before`), and the car's smoke generator returns the stage parameters' address its last call left in r3 (`Car::generator`). What the stage needed beyond its own code: `Ground_801C4100` (the tree walk), the F-Zero hazard sign (`un_802FD8C4`), the stages' line hazards in `ftCo_800C0B20` and the comparer's view of grmutecity.c's statics, the word before the order on Mute City alone (`grZe_8049F170[19]`'s object is left out there). leaf-check's `stages` runs every function of the units from the match's saved calls (`oracle-stage-mute-city`, 171 calls, 966 MiB packed to 30 MiB): the script from each command kind and waiting, the track's ends from eleven places on the spline and in each shape, the race with the order's first passed, bunched cars, wrecks and the track's wrap, each car's steering in every state of its marks, lanes and speeds, the cars' moves with cars past smoking and wrecking, wrecked in view and out, shaking the screen loud and not, honking and in the pit lane, the platform's sway and lights in each state, the track's callback on fighters and items, the line hazards and shadow check in each case, each car's hit taken, the line damage with the track live and not, and the callbacks no capture reached (the empty processes and destroy callbacks, the scenery's setup, the demo hook) run by address from the cars' process's calls. Each port broken on purpose fails a check, but for six: the wreck's tilt, which `HSD_JObjSetTranslate` overwrites at once (only its random draw shows); the generator's answer taken as made whatever the parameters, which always exist; the light count's limit, as no saved state reaches a fourth diffuse light; and three comparisons made strict or not where no state lies on their edge (the track end's step direction, the horn's 300, the camera top's offset).

Its replay check shows 410 divergences, none of them gameplay: 408 are Pichu's post-frame misc state word holding a heap pointer from frame 1780 (0x4D000000 against 0x74200000, the recording's and playback's heaps), and the first player's height on its entrance's first tick (frames -118 and -117); the controllers check is clean.

**Big Blue** (`stage-big-blue`, Roy against Zelda, two `BENCH_AI` players, eight minutes; `grbigblue.c`, `game::gr::bigblue`, with `grFZeroCar_801CAFBC` building its cars' models) compares every one of its trace's 28,985 ticks with no difference and no stale read, with `--heap` too, and passes `sync-test --rollback 7` (28,918 ticks). Its reference is an interpreter trace (`oracle-stage-big-blue-interp.trc`, the original code but the trace's hook, `TRACE_PORT=sysdolphin/baselib/perf`), whose stack holds the console's words where the Falcon Flyer's setup reads them; the `--port all` trace's ports leave other words there. The road (map 34) is a chain of twelve pieces (`grBb_TrackEntries`, whose measured lengths the setup writes into the executable's data, kept in `bigblue::Globals`), two shown at a time, moving left under the camera and turning with the ground's slope: the next piece is drawn at random as the current one's end passes (the loop only above -700, only climbs or dives with the road's, none past the climb's limit), the loop turns the road with its own ground in four stages that switch its collision joints, and where the ground falls away the road drifts down until it lands; maps 1 and 2 move with it, and the wind blows airborne items left (`grBigBlue_801EF7D8`, through `grLib_801C9E60`). Four big cars (map 33) drive on it, each one of thirty models under a collision joint of its own (two heap blocks hold the joints and their slots' use): they come in from a side the others aren't leaving by, keep their order by rank with half-unit steps, pull one in toward the middle when none is near it, brake toward their places, bob, bounce under landing fighters, fly off where the road ends and fall with it until they land, and fade out past the sides; up to ten passes push apart those closer than their gap. Three flying platforms (map 32) wait for the Falcon Flyer to pass, then count down and cross from the side the road's heights allow, above the cars and each other, tilting with the road, dipping where fighters land and pushed by their weight, kept at their height over the road, the cars and the other platforms; the count of placed platforms calls the Falcon Flyer back (map 35, which the players start on, waits over the stage at the start and flies off) and the relay platform (map 36), which crosses at speeds drawn now and then. With items on a placed platform may carry an item (`grBigBlue_801E8A1C`: one of the Beam Sword, Super Scope, Bunny Hood, Fire Flower and Party Ball the switch has on, made by kind with `it_8026BE84` and holding still with its flags held, so it rides in from outside the blast zones; the `bigblue-items` matches, below). Four of the original's defects are modeled, and three latent ones listed ([Melee's own bugs](melee-bugs.md#big-blues-falcon-flyer-looks-for-the-road-from-a-stack-vector-it-never-set)): the Falcon Flyer's setup searches for the road from a stack vector it never set (`flyer_stale_pos`: the addresses of two of map 32's joints, which the allocator model gives, as the console leaves them; [the engine notes](engine.md#uninitialized-values)), the relay platform's check reads the flying platforms' object (`near_relay`), a car brought in starts its engine in its collision slot's sound slot (`bring_car`), and the road's layout writes 0xFFFF into a seven-bit field.
