What the stage needed beyond its own code: the trophy count (`Toy_803048C0`) for its alternate song, with the trophy word and Slippi's unlock-all code (`li r3, 0x63` at `0x8030490C`) read with the other unlocks (`Unlocks::trophies_all_code`), the wind's case in `grLib_801C9E60`, and the comparer's view of grbigblue.c's parameters and road pieces. leaf-check's `stages` runs every function of the unit from the match's saved calls (`oracle-stage-big-blue`, 343 calls, 2,269 MiB packed to 59 MiB): the setup and each object's creation, the road's layout, its next piece in each climb or dive state at heights round its limits, waiting and not, and its move in each turn, on the loop in each stage and falling away; the cars' upkeep with the slots used up either side, the wait run out with cars driving out either way and pulling in, each car's decision, change, acceleration and drive in every state, in the air and on the road, alone on the road easing toward its place, and the cars pushed apart; the searches (the highest car in boxes, platforms and the relay platform near points, the obstacle in boxes with the relay platform beside them), the platforms waiting, counting, placed with each event pending and flying (landed, pushed, dipping, tilted past their limits, leaving), their and the cars' collision callbacks, the Falcon Flyer and the relay platform in each state, the shadow check, the background's render callback, the wind, and the callbacks no capture reached (the empty processes and destroy callbacks, the cars' destroy callback, the demo hook) run by address. Each of 18 ports broken on purpose fails a check.

Its replay check shows 59,162 divergences: the first player's height from its entrance (60 post-frame and 58 pre-frame words from frame -118, as on Mute City for two frames), 46 pre-frame and 46 post-frame action states from frame -59, 13 action frames, and then the post-frame and item instance counters and the post-frame hit-by instance (58,939, from frames 0, 11 and 59). Its controllers check, the recording's own run again, is clean, so the trace, taken from the playback, is what melee-hd follows. The recording run (original code, as the playback) parts from the playback only in the entrance: port 1 stays up and then falls (0x1D) at frame -59 and lands (0x2A) until -14, where the playback falls at once and stands from -59; positions, actions and percents are equal from frame 0 on, and the counters differ by the two extra action changes. melee-hd's own recording of the replay (`melee-oracle record`) shows the same 59,162, and the Falcon Flyer's words aren't the cause (the recording run's are the same joints, `0x2720` lower in its heap, and the search lands at the origin either way): entrance noise of the recording run, as Mute City's, longer.

**Big Blue's flying platforms' items (2026-10-23, `feat/bigblue-items`).** The `bigblue-items` set's two matches ([Oracle sets](#oracle-sets)): `items-platforms-big-blue` (Mario against Sheik, both `BENCH_AI`) and `items-platforms-cpu-big-blue` (Marth, `BENCH_AI`, against a level 9 CPU Captain Falcon), eight-minute time matches with only the five kinds the platforms carry on, at frequency low, made as the other oracle sets' matches were (ssbm-rs's `ssbm-run-rf`, traced `--port all`, 1,389 and 1,378 MB), compare every one of their 28,987 and 28,989 ticks with no difference and no stale read (`--fp hardware --heap`), and pass `sync-test --rollback 7` (28,918 ticks each). Seven placed platforms carry an item (`grBigBlue_801E8A1C`, through `it_8026BE84`): three Party Balls and four Beam Swords. A fighter picks up a Beam Sword the platform brought in and swings it, and another a Party Ball; one Party Ball rides its platform out past the left blast zone, and one made on a platform placed above the camera starts falling at once, which ends its hold (`Item_80268E5C`), so the right blast zone it was made beyond takes it in the next tick; the other swords run out their lifetimes. The item holds still with its flags held, as the bob-omb rain's do (`it_8026BE28`), so the blast zones and the floor's slide leave it alone while it rides in from beyond the side; its first change of state lets go (`it_8026BDCC`) and clears the hold (`it_80274EF8`), which melee-hd's `Item_80268E5C` had left out, a difference in the item's flags the first match found at tick 6080 (nothing before had held an item still). The other three kinds only the leaf checks make on a platform. Their replay checks show 62,927 and 61,495 divergences, the class of `stage-big-blue`'s: the first player's height from frame -118 and the action states from frame -59 in the entrance (with, in the first, two pre-frame seeds at frame -9), then only the post-frame, hit-by and item instance counters; the first match's controllers check is clean. leaf-check's `items` runs `it_8026BE84` and the five spawns at a point from the corpus's swords (`drops_by_kind`: each kind with its flags held and not, a kind it makes none of, the Party Ball switched off, a sudden death), and `stages` runs `grBigBlue_801E8A1C` from the two matches' saved calls (`oracle-items-platforms-big-blue` and `-cpu-big-blue`, two calls each, 7 MiB packed each) as saved and on each platform with each kind alone, none, all, two, the Party Ball off and in a sudden death, from several seeds (`bigblue_platform_items`). Each port broken on purpose fails a check: the Party Ball's lift at 5, the first kind on always taken, every kind counted on, the sudden death's early return dropped, the hold-still mark or the held flags left out or inverted, the Party Ball spawned without standing up, the Party Ball's own switch check dropped and the Super Scope dropped onto the plane (those two in `items` alone: a platform makes only kinds that are on, at depth 0), and `Item_80268E5C` without clearing the hold (the first match's diff, at tick 6080).

**Corneria** (`stage-corneria`, Falco against Sheik, two `BENCH_AI` players, four minutes; `grcorneria.c`, `itarwinglaser.c` and `itgreatfoxlaser.c`, `game::gr::corneria`, `it::kinds::arwinglaser` and `it::kinds::greatfoxlaser`) compares every one of its trace's 14,631 ticks with no difference and no stale read (`--fp hardware --heap`), and the `starfox` set's Corneria matches ([Oracle sets](#oracle-sets)) do too: `long-corneria` (eight minutes, 28,989 ticks), `taunt-fox-corneria` (2,023), `taunt-falco-corneria` (1,422) and `cpu-corneria` (11,024; its replay check differs only in port 1's entry height bit at frames -118 and -117). The Great Fox drifts in its box with its copy (map 0) and its splash lines where its hull meets the sea, the city's three parts scroll under it and make each other in turn, and the sky and the parameter colors follow the part under the camera; its guns charge, aim and fire volleys and single big shots (25 lasers and 5 big shots in the four minutes), and Arwings fly in on their paths one at a time, the close types (2, 3, 4, 6 and 8 here) rolling, standing in for platforms through their wings' collision (maps 13 to 18) and firing ahead, the far ones (type 13) firing at a player's fighter. The long match sinks the Great Fox twice over the city's first part, and its far Arwings fire from both groups. Fox's side taunt calls the Star Fox team, whose talk (map 12) opens a window with its own camera (map 5, the first stage object with one: `lb_80013B14`, `Ground_801C2BD4`, `Ground_801C2C8C` and `grDisplay_801C5F60` came with it) and plays its lines with their text and voices; Falco's match ends during his talk, which the match's end stops (`grCorneria_801E2A6C`). What else the stage needed: the side taunt's taunt count and stats (`pl_800386D8`, `pl_80040120`) and its end (`ft_8008A324`), the text system's font closing (`HSD_SisLib_803A5DA0`, `HSD_SisLib_803A5F50`), a collision's unwritten flag bits ([the engine notes](engine.md#uninitialized-values), which a link of Sheik's chain held in tick 11950) and the far laser's stack position (tick 7399). The objects' scale joints file themselves in the ID table under their makers' frames: the Great Fox's copy under the setup's less the Great Fox's setup's and the create function's, the load hook's city part under its own (0x28 above the setup's), and the Arwings, their wings, the city's parts and the talk's window under the process loop's less their makers' frames (`corneria::ARWING_FRAME` and the others). `sync-test --rollback 7 --fp hardware` passes on all five (14,518, 28,918, 1,918, 1,318 and 10,918 ticks checked). leaf-check's `stages` runs every function of the three units from the matches' saved calls (`oracle-stage-corneria`, 327 calls, 2,070 MiB packed to 48 MiB; `oracle-taunt-fox-corneria` and `oracle-taunt-falco-corneria`, the stage's functions and Fox's and Falco's side taunt's, `cn-taunt-funcs.txt`): the Great Fox's process as saved and waiting; its drift turning or not from several seeds, past its speed caps and its box's edges; its sinking over each part with its chance had or not and sinking down and back up at the turn's tick and either side; its guns in each state at their waits' ends and short of them, each shot's kind and side; the guns' hurtbox items' hits short of, at and past their damage; the Arwings' spawns after each wait, type and group, in a match and as the demo's slots; a flight path's first tick, sound states and types, and its slot taken; an Arwing held, its path gone, in each roll state and firing in a match and the demo; a wing's visibility either side of its roll and depth limits; the city's parts waiting, still, and stepping just short of, onto and past their edges; the talk's process at its lines' ends and past them, a line in each state at its timer's edges, the window's render callback in two passes and paused for debugging, and its camera's; the lasers' spawns for each type, their callbacks, their lifetimes running out, and the hit events no match reached (reflections turned back at a fighter); and the empty processes and destroy callbacks, map 6's setup and the demo's city from the Great Fox's calls. All compare clean. Each port broken on purpose fails a check: a close type drawn from one fewer, a flight path's height off by one, the wings' visibility limit at 1.1, a roll drawn from four, the city's edge 100 farther, the talk clamped one lower, the guns breaking only past their damage, a volley's shot wait one longer, the sinking's turn a tick late, the window shown while paused, a line's wait a tick shorter, a close laser's lifetime one longer, the Great Fox's laser facing the other way, and the drift's speed cap left out. Unreached in a VS match: the demo's setup (`grCorneria_801DD2C0`, through the title demo's scene code), map 11's process (`grCorneria_801E1060`, destroyed by the load hook before its first tick), the opening movie's Arwing (`grCorneria_801E1BF0`) and the Great Fox laser's `evt_unk` (`it_802EB5A8`).

**Venom** (`stage-venom`, Captain Falcon against Peach, two `BENCH_AI` players, four minutes; `grvenom.c`, `game::gr::venom`) compares every one of its trace's 14,635 ticks with no difference and no stale read (`--fp hardware --heap`), and so do the `starfox` set's Venom matches: `long-venom` (eight minutes, 28,989 ticks), `taunt-fox-venom` (2,023), `taunt-falco-venom` (1,422) and `cpu-venom` (11,021; its replay check differs only in the entry height bit of three ports before the match). The Great Fox's animation (map 7) flies the stage through the canyon and its tunnels and is its clock: at set frames it sounds, holds the Arwings' groups back and lets them go, and under the lower deck's tunnel opens the deck's collision (joint 2) with its hum, turns the stage's colors black (red when flag 6 is up, the canyon's otherwise) and the music's aux send to the stage's second (`lbAudioAx_80024D78`, ported with the audio table's third column); its lights' animations loop and restart with it. The platforms (map 5) ride it with their copy (map 0), and Arwings fly in one at a time on their paths while no group is held, the close ones standing in for platforms through their wings (maps 10 to 15), the far ones firing at a fighter on their side of the stage. Fox's and Falco's side taunts call the team as on Corneria, the talk (map 8) and its window (map 1) played by Corneria's code; Falco's match ends during his talk. What else the stage needed: the far laser's stack position, which turned out to be what the shot's sound call leaves (Corneria's fixed stand-in was its sound's sample; [Melee's own bugs](melee-bugs.md)), and the register Venom's excluded-floor check leaves for the computer player's DI, the address of the collision joints' array (`Geometry::joints_addr`, from the allocator model). `sync-test --rollback 7 --fp hardware` passes on all five (14,518, 28,918, 1,918, 1,318 and 10,918 ticks checked). leaf-check's `stages` runs every function of the unit from the matches' saved calls (`oracle-stage-venom`, `oracle-taunt-fox-venom` and `oracle-taunt-falco-venom`, 494 calls, 3.1 GiB packed to 71 MiB, with `ve-funcs.txt`, which takes Corneria's talk code and the side taunt's too): the Great Fox's process with each cue crossed, the animation rewinding and each flag and colors' state, the colors and the aux send by column; the platforms' process with the event's line about to start, opening and waiting; the Arwings' spawns with each held group, wait, type and group, an Arwing out and the demo's slots; a flight path's first tick, sound states and types and its slot taken; an Arwing's roll states and a far one in each type's firing window and outside it; a wing either side of its visibility's limits; the talk at its lines' ends, its start for each taunt and its stop; Corneria's talk calls on Venom; the shadow check and the touch-line hook; and the empty processes, destroy callbacks and the demo hook from the Great Fox's calls. All compare clean. Each port broken on purpose fails a check: the far group held drawing from three types, the deck's cue a frame late, the red color on another slot, the event's window a tick early, the sound margin at 21, a far type's firing window starting a step late, the wing's depth limit at 49, a roll drawn from three, the talk's first wait at 9, the shadow check without its lower platform's case, the platforms' upper and lower swapped, and the aux send left at its first column.

**Princess Peach's Castle** (`stage-peachs-castle`, Link against Jigglypuff, two `BENCH_AI` players, four minutes, and `castle-walkers`, Fox against Marth, two walkers, four minutes; `grcastle.c`, `game::gr::castle`) compares every one of its traces' ticks (14,637 and 14,635) with no difference and no stale read, with `--heap` too, and both pass `sync-test --rollback 7 --fp hardware` (14,518 ticks each). The castle (map 3) steers a wind source (`grCs_804D6974`, its turn and gusts in two more statics, `grCs_804D45E0` and `grCs_804D45E4`) that sways its four flags on dynamics chains (`dynamicsdata_flag6`), each flag's pole first turned toward the wind where it stands (`grCastle_801D0D84`) and the wind's strength drawn again after each swing; it registers the moat as the fighters' wind device (type 6), which splashes a fighter falling in past 14 (in the stage's scale) and holds it up by 0.1 while it falls faster than 0.5. Its timers send the three switches (maps 18 to 20) one at a time to one of eleven spots on the castle and the lifts, drawn by weight, the last switch's weight divided; a switch blinks in, waits with its hitbox item, and hit or touched it plays its animation with a small quake and brings out its blocks (maps 17, 7 and 5), which grow back (not while a Bullet Bill flies within 40), join the collision, take hitbox items and, hit from below, bump and drop a random item from a ? block (none with items off), then flicker and shrink away. A Bullet Bill (maps 8 to 16, one per path, never one of the last three flown) comes with a camera subject and its hitbox item, its blaster (map 2) rising with a looping quake after its path's wait; when its flight ends it lingers with a color script, then blasts (map 1: a camera subject, a hitbox item, a wind blast) and the castle waits for the next. The two lifts (map 6) rise under the weight their collision joints report, after a wait, and sink back once left. The stage match runs three Bullet Bills, five switches out and pressed (one by a touch), blocks growing beside a flying Bullet Bill 470 times and the lifts 8 rises; the walkers' match, which melee-hd's walkers took past the moat 57 times (560 ticks held up), four Bullet Bills and six presses; neither bumps a block. What the stage needed beyond its own code: the camera's eye floor on Princess Peach's Castle (`grCastle_801D0FF0`), a stage chain's release (`grLib_801C9B6C`), and the CPU's Bullet Bill case (`ftCo_800A229C`'s, through `grCastle_801CDF54`). The objects made after the setup put their scale joints' descriptors deeper on the stack (the switches 0x60, their blocks 0xA8, a blaster and a blast 0x20 below the setup's; a Bullet Bill as deep as it), which the allocator model's ID entries show (`--heap` parted at tick 85 with them all at the setup's). leaf-check's `stages` runs every function of the unit from both matches' saved calls (`oracle-stage-peachs-castle`, 197 calls, 1,114 MiB packed to 28 MiB, and `oracle-castle-walkers`, 202 calls, 1,201 MiB to 30 MiB): the castle's process and its timers from each branch (no switch out, one out at rest or not, the wait run out, a path drawn with the last three each way), the wind's turns and gusts and the flags' turn from poles turned every way, the lifts in each state stood on and not near both ends and their touches by each collision kind, the Bullet Bills, blasters and blasts in flight and lingering with their waits run out, the blocks in each state with their timers at and past their ends, growing with items on and off and the bob-omb rain on, bumped at every step, hit and touched by a fighter and a stage item, the switches in each state with their waits at and past their ends and pressed by a hit and a touch, the moat with the fighter above, below and falling in, the shadow check about its limits, and the callbacks no capture reached (the empty processes and destroy callbacks, the castle's destroy callback, the load and demo hooks, the Bullet Bill's and blast's item callbacks, the castle's render callback, the splash and the camera's floor) run by address from the castle's process's calls. The castle's start hook runs from `Ground_801C0FB8`'s loop, which frees each node after its call and clears the list's head after the loop, so the calls made in it find a list running into reused memory; the checks leave the head out. With items off the blocks never hold items (only a shown ? block takes one, so they can't be hit), and no capture holds blocks out with items on, so the blocks' hit and touch callbacks run from the Bullet Bills' calls with the bill's item given to a block. Broken on purpose, each of these fails a check: in the setup, joint 14 left in the search, the scenery drawn in front, the moat's device type, the fountain's effect, a switch's scale and order, the lifts' timer, a Bullet Bill's camera extent, a blast's item state, a blaster's first timer, the Bullet Bills' first wait, a flag's chain a link short and the chain's release left out; the switches' wait a tick early and the last switch's weight, the Bullet Bills' wait a tick early, a path ring of two, the wind's turn reversed, its calm's strength and the flags' side; the lifts' rise from the sink's wait, the sink's terms swapped and the collision kind; three blaster animations, the blaster's height, a Bullet Bill's lingering a tick short, its blast's wind a tick short and the paths off by one; the blocks' flicker a tick short or gone, the item at the second bump step; a block's sound, bump state and item kept, and a stage item's touch knocking; a switch's blink, hitlag, hiding a tick early and hurtbox; the moat's threshold, surface and splash; the shadow check's limits; the camera's floor, the splash's scale and the demo's part. The first runs found the checks short of the switches' wait at 1, the lifts' timer between the waits (20 and 30), the blocks' flicker at its limit (60), a Bullet Bill lingering at 0 and the block callbacks' items; each now has its case. Not shown to fail after two runs: a switch's part reset on part 1 for part 0 (the switch's position and turn are set from its spot after it). Three changes can't fail with the stage's parameters: the rise's cap (`x20` is 0, so the rise is `x24`, 0.5, whatever the weight, under either cap) and the growth's and shrink's rates swapped either way (both 0.1). The render callbacks' draws aren't compared (the reference's GX writes go to null hardware), so the blocks drawn while flickered off and the castle's passes skipped aren't caught; what they draw is presentation the renderer check would compare, which has no reference of the stage yet. Melee's bugs the port found are in [Melee's own bugs](melee-bugs.md): a Bullet Bill keeps pointing at its freed blaster, and the path draw's empty case (latent).

Both replay checks are clean, and so are both controllers checks.

**Leaf functions.** `leaf-check` loads the disc's `main.dol` into ssbm-rs's runtime, registers every port, and calls them one at a time, so melee-sim's functions are compared with ssbm-rs's input by input, in both float modes. Inputs are special values, random bit patterns and values spread over each function's useful range. Changing one bit of a constant makes the checks fail.

## Oracle sets

Milestone 4 ([its plan](milestone-4.md)) needs oracles for items, the other 23 VS stages and the CPU AI, which the local replays barely reach, so they come from matches ssbm-rs plays from generated settings, as the benchmark and CPU oracle matches do ([performance](performance.md#benchmark-matches)). `tools/bench/src/matches.rs` describes a match by its stage (any of the 29 VS stages), its rules (stock with an optional time limit, or time), the item switch (frequency and which of the spawner's 35 kinds are on) and two to four players, each a human, whose controller ssbm-rs's `BENCH_AI` plays (the level 9 CPU AI through the controller), or a computer player of a CPU kind and level, which the game's own AI plays. The benchmark matches' and the oracle CPU matches' codes come out byte for byte as before.

`melee-bench oracle-sets DIR [SET|NAME...]` writes the codes of the milestone's sets (`SETS`), whole sets or single matches, every set when none is named, and prints a line per match: its name, the ports `BENCH_AI` plays, the video fields its runs need, and whether every player is human. The sets (183 matches, milestone 5's `special` among them):

- `items` (18): each item family on Final Destination, Battlefield and Pokémon Stadium (`items-FAMILY-STAGE`), then the Poké Ball alone over six seeds (`items-pokeballN-STAGE`), since it can't choose its Pokémon. The families follow the milestone's tasks: `ported` (the 13 switch kinds already ported, task 3's set: the capsule, box, barrel, Party Ball, Bob-omb, Mr. Saturn, heart, tomato, Home-Run Bat, Beam Sword, both shells and the hammer; the box, the barrel and the hammer also start fighter states not yet ported, heavy pickups and the hammer's), `held` (task 7), `shoot` (task 8) and `powerups` (task 9). Two-minute time matches, item frequency high, two humans.
- `stages` (23): one match per VS stage beyond the tournament six (`stage-NAME`), items off, two humans, four minutes, eight on the moving stages (Poké Floats, Rainbow Cruise, Icicle Mountain, Big Blue, Mute City).
- `coins` (2): coin matches on Final Destination, two minutes, two humans (Fox and Marth), items off (`coins-fd`) and capsules on (`coins-items-fd`), task 8's coins.
- `held-extra` (3): the held family's code the `held` matches left unplayed: the barrel cannon alone, the swung items with the Screw Attack, and the box and barrel ([items held](#items-held)).
- `pokemon` (3): the Poké Ball alone at frequency very high, `items-pokeball7-fd` to `-9-fd`, on the seeds a search found releasing the Pokémon the `items` set's six never do ([Pokémon](#pokémon)).
- `barrel` (2): Kongo Jungle N64 and Kongo Jungle with two walkers each, players whose controllers follow a script instead of `BENCH_AI` (`Control::Walker`, below), so the stages' barrels catch them ([Stages](#stages), "The barrels' ride").
- `castle` (1): `castle-walkers`, Princess Peach's Castle with two walkers (Fox and Marth), four minutes, items off, so fighters ride the lifts and fall past the moat, which the stage match's `BENCH_AI` players never did ([Stages](#stages), "Princess Peach's Castle"). Seed `1F0F0703`.
- `leftovers` (4): the items toggle's leftovers ([the player](player.md#items-toggle-whats-left), tasks 1 to 3): `items-leftovers-fd` (Fox against Falco, the reflectors, with the Bob-omb, parasol, Freezie, motion-sensor bomb, Flipper, Screw Attack and Bunny Hood) and `items-leftovers-bf` (Captain Falcon against Bowser, the same but the bomb and the Flipper), three minutes at frequency very high; `items-box-kongo-jungle` (Mario against Luigi, the box alone on Kongo Jungle, whose river carries boxes) and `items-foods-yoshis-story` (Samus against Yoshi, food alone on Yoshi's Story, whose Shy Guys carry it), two minutes at frequency high. Seeds `1F0F0Exx`.
- `monsters` (9): the containers' monsters ([The containers' monsters](#the-containers-monsters)): the box, barrel and Party Ball alone at item frequency very high, two openers each (`Control::Opener`, below), two matches for each of the Goomba, ReDead, Octorok and Topi (`It_Kind_Ottosea`) on Final Destination, Battlefield or Pokémon Stadium (`monsters-KIND-STAGE`), and `monsters-ottosea-freezie-fd`, with the Freezie on too, for a Topi pushing one; each seeded by a seed search to release its monster and recorded by melee-hd (below).
- `catch` (7): catching the containers' monsters ([Catching items](#catching-items)): the box, barrel and Party Ball alone at item frequency very high, a catcher against an opener on Final Destination (`Control::Catcher`, below): Kirby inhaling a monster and spitting it out (`catch-kirby-fd`) or swallowing it (`catch-kirby-swallow-fd`), Yoshi's tongue making one an egg (`catch-yoshi-fd`), and Fox's grab lifting a Goomba, ReDead, Octorok or Topi to throw or drop it (`catch-grab-KIND-fd`), each seeded by a seed search.
- `fighters` (8): the fighters with items ([the player](player.md#items-toggle-whats-left), task 4): `items-dk-heavy-fd` (Donkey Kong against Donkey Kong) and `items-dk-heavy-bf` (Donkey Kong against Marth) with the box, barrel, Party Ball and barrel cannon; `items-zelda-sheik-fd` and `items-sheik-zelda-bf` (Zelda against Sheik, each on either side) with the Starman, Lip's Stick, Bunny Hood, Metal Box, Cloaking Device, Beam Sword and Ray Gun; `items-zelda-hood-fd` and `items-sheik-hood-bf` with Lip's Stick, the Bunny Hood and the Metal Box; `items-zelda-flower-fd` and `items-zelda-flower2-fd` with Lip's Stick alone. Two minutes at frequency very high; seeds `1F0F0Fxx`, the last four from a search ([Fighters with items](#fighters-with-items)).
- `sscope` (2): the Super Scope's one-shot charge ([The Super Scope's one-shot charge](#the-super-scopes-one-shot-charge)): the scope alone at item frequency very high on Final Destination, Fox against Marth, two gunners, two minutes, on seeds a search found (`1F0F10xx` and on), recorded by melee-hd.
- `bigblue-items` (2): Big Blue's flying platforms' items ([Stages](#stages), Big Blue): only the five kinds the platforms carry on (the Party Ball, Beam Sword, Super Scope, Fire Flower and Bunny Hood) at frequency low, eight-minute time matches, two humans (`items-platforms-big-blue`: Mario against Sheik) and a human against a level 9 CPU (`items-platforms-cpu-big-blue`: Marth against Captain Falcon). Seeds `1F0F26xx`.
- `special` (11): milestone 5's task 12(a), the Special Melee modes' gameplay that the start data carries ([Special Melee](#special-melee)): one match per mode (`special-MODE-STAGE`: `tiny-bf`, `giant-fd`, `stamina-ps`, `ssd-dl` for Super Sudden Death, `invisible-ys`, `single-button-fod`, `fixed-camera-bf`, `slomo-fd`, `lightning-ps`), `special-mix-bf`, Giant, Lightning, Invisible, Single-Button and Fixed-Camera at once, and `special-stamina-ko-fd` (Marth against Captain Falcon), a check of the stamina knockout rather than the mode's setup: 30 HP and a two-minute timer kept, so a fighter runs out of HP before a blast zone takes its one stock (`stamina-ps` never ran one out: in playback's debug VS mode a stamina knockout isn't an elimination, and a blast zone took a stock first). `Match::special` (`matches::Special`) changes the start data as each mode's VS entry does (`gmtiny.c` and the others): every player block's scale and ratios, starting damage or invisibility, the rules' speed, single button or unpause hook, and Stamina Mode's own rules and match start. Two `BENCH_AI` humans, items off, two-minute time matches (Super Sudden Death's too, as the VS rules' default); Stamina Mode a stock match of one stock and 150 HP with no timer, as its entry makes it. Seeds `1F0F27xx`.
- `own-items` (9): the characters' own items' rows no replay ran ([The characters' own items](#the-characters-own-items)), scripted players (`Control::Program`) against Ness's PSI Magnet, Marth's Counter and shield and Fox's Reflector, items off, 30 seconds (ten for `own-bomb-gb`); the matches share one seed.
- `grapple` (2): Samus's grapple beam swung on the ground in each frame that swings it ([The characters' own items](#the-characters-own-items)): `grapple-samus-bf`, a grappler (`Control::Grappler`, below) against an idle Fox on Battlefield, items off, 30 seconds. Seed `1F0F2201`: the match was recorded with `1F0F2001`, which the `cpu-items` set took too, so a recording made from the set now gets another seed (no random draw decides what its scripts do). And `grapple-cloud-yi64`, Samus's ground grab ending off the ground before and after the beam comes out: a scripted Samus (`Control::Program`) grabbing on Yoshi's Island N64's left cloud as it puffs away, against an idle Fox, items off, 30 seconds, seed `1F0F2202`.
- `starfox` (8): the Star Fox stages' own matches ([Stages](#stages), Corneria and Venom): the team's talk, a taunter (`Control::Taunter`, below) against an idle Marth, items off, on Corneria and on Venom: Fox's side taunt a second after "GO" in 30 seconds, so the talk plays through (`taunt-fox-corneria`, `taunt-fox-venom`), and Falco's eight seconds before time runs out in 20, so the match ends during the talk (`taunt-falco-corneria`, `taunt-falco-venom`); and eight minutes of two idle players on each (`long-corneria`, `long-venom`), `long-corneria` on a seed a search found sinking the Great Fox twice with the far Arwings firing from both groups; and real CPUs at mixed levels against a `BENCH_AI` human on each, as the `cpu-stages` set's (`cpu-corneria`: Peach against Falco 9, Samus 5 and Roy 2; `cpu-venom`: Sheik against Mario 8, Pikachu 3 and Bowser 6), for the computer players' stage code (the excluded floors, the turn under Corneria's Great Fox). Seeds `1F0F23xx`, the long matches `1F0F2401` and `1F0F2501`.
- `ics` (7): the Ice Climbers against another `BENCH_AI` human, Nana the game's own AI, on the five stages whose hooks her computer player reaches, `ics-br` (Fox), `ics-on` (Marth), `ics-rc` (Sheik), `ics-gb` (Falco) and `ics-ht` (Peach), and with items on, `ics-items-bf` (Fox, item frequency high, every kind), stock matches, 4 stocks, 3 minutes; and `ics-items-open-fd`, two openers (the Ice Climbers and Fox, item frequency very high, every kind, two minutes), which melee-hd records ([the Ice Climbers on the stages](#the-ice-climbers-on-the-stages)).
- `cpu` (29): every roster character as a real level 9 CPU against a human, rotating over the six tournament stages (`cpu-CHAR-STAGE`, Kirby last: `cpu-kirby-bf`, against Marth), and a level sweep, three four-player matches of a human Fox against one character as CPUs at levels 1, 4 and 7 (`cpu-sweep-CHAR-STAGE`: Samus, Donkey Kong, Mewtwo). Stock matches, 4 stocks, 3 minutes.
- `cpu-levels` (16): every roster character as a CPU at level 1 or 5, the `PAIRS` and the Ice Climbers with Kirby, two CPUs a match against a human Fox (Falco when Fox is a CPU), one at each level, rotating over the tournament stages (`cpu-l1-A-l5-B-STAGE`); two matches of four CPUs and no human, at levels 2, 3, 6 and 8 (`cpu-four-l2368-dl`: Pikachu, Ganondorf, the Ice Climbers, Kirby) and 4, 5, 7 and 9 (`cpu-four-l4579-bf`: Luigi, Jigglypuff, Link, Mr. Game & Watch); and a 2v2 team match of four CPUs (`cpu-teams-fd`: Marth at 9 and Samus at 6 against Fox at 9 and Ness at 4; `is_teams` on, friendly fire off, as the menus' team match). The `cpu` set's rules, seeds `1F0F044x` and `1F0F049x`.
- `cpu-stages` (9): real CPUs on stages beyond the tournament six where the CPU AI has code of its own, a `BENCH_AI` human against three CPUs at mixed levels, 4 stocks, 3 minutes, items off (`cpu-stage-NAME`): Poké Floats (Kirby 9, Pikachu 5, Luigi 2) and Yoshi's Island (Jigglypuff 8, Link 3, Ganondorf 6) for a CPU off camera on the ground, Kongo Jungle N64 (Ice Climbers 8, Donkey Kong 5, Zelda 2) and Kongo Jungle (Yoshi 9, Ness 4, Mewtwo 7) for the barrels, and Brinstar (Sheik against Bowser 3, Roy 6, Dr. Mario 8), Rainbow Cruise (Fox against Peach 8, Mr. Game & Watch 3, Captain Falcon 6), Great Bay (Marth against Samus 6, Young Link 8, Pichu 3) and Icicle Mountain (Falco against Mario 8, Ice Climbers 5, Link 2; a time match of eight minutes, so the climb runs) for the safe-ground search ([The CPU AI](#the-cpu-ai)), and Big Blue (`cpu-stage-big-blue`: Luigi against Falco 9, Ness 5, Donkey Kong 2, a time match of eight minutes, as the stages set's moving stages, with a seed of its own, `1F0F2110`) for its sides and the cars' floors. Seeds `1F0F21xx`.
- `cpu-items` (4): milestone 4's task 15, a real level 9 CPU against a `BENCH_AI` human with every switch kind on at item frequency high, so the CPU AI goes for items, picks them up and uses them (`cpu-items-CHAR-STAGE`: Marth against Fox on Final Destination, Mario against Falco on Battlefield, Samus against Sheik on Pokémon Stadium, Captain Falcon against Luigi on Yoshi's Story), three-minute time matches. Seeds `1F0F20xx`.
- `cpu-approach` (4): the same, but with only the Starman, the Poison Mushroom and the hammer on at item frequency high, so the CPU's enemy often has a Starman or a hammer, which makes it the target to close in on, and a CPU Yoshi is often shrunk (`cpu-approach-CHAR-STAGE`: Yoshi against Fox on Battlefield and against Marth on Fountain of Dreams, Captain Falcon against Sheik on Dream Land, Ness against Luigi on Final Destination), three-minute time matches. Seeds `1F0F23xx`.

The items and stage matches pair every roster character but the Ice Climbers, whose matches are the `ics` set's ([the Ice Climbers on the stages](#the-ice-climbers-on-the-stages), [with items](#the-ice-climbers-with-items)). Seeds are fixed per match (`1F0F02xx` items, `1F0F03xx` stages, `1F0F04xx` CPUs). A seed needn't be unique to its match: everything made from a match is keyed by its name (the codes `NAME.ini`, the recording `NAME.slp`, the script, the trace `oracle-NAME.trc`, its goldens, the leaf-check captures), and the codes are the settings and the seed together, so matches whose settings differ play different matches on one seed. `oracle_sets` (`tools/bench/src/matches.rs`) checks what a shared seed could break: that no two definitions play the same match, the same codes with the same players' controls (a `BENCH_AI` human, a CPU or a script). So the `own-items` matches share one seed, and the `cpu-approach` matches the `starfox` set's taunt matches' seeds (`1F0F2301` to `2304`, a Yoshi, Captain Falcon or Ness CPU with items on against Fox's or Falco's taunter on Corneria or Venom).

A walker walks off its side of the stage for 120 frames, holds back toward the middle for 180 (drifting under the stage as it falls, or walking back), and taps A every 37 frames on every other round of 300 (`walker_pad`). `oracle-sets` writes the walkers' controllers beside the codes (`NAME.script.slp`: a raw Slippi event stream of pre-frame events with each walker's buttons and sticks, which ssbm-rs's replay reader takes without the UBJSON wrapper), and `make-oracle.sh` hands it to the recording run as `BENCH_PADS`, which plays the ports it has events for; the list's fifth column says so.

An opener runs right for 30 frames, stands for 50, runs left for 30 and stands for 50, a round of 160 frames offset by 40 a port, and taps A every 12 frames (`opener_pad`), so it picks up, throws and hits the containers that land near it; its script goes to `BENCH_PADS` as a walker's does.

A catcher (`catcher_pad`) runs the opener's round but taps A only while it runs, and holds its button for 12 frames from its stands' frames 30, 70 and 110; on odd rounds it holds the stick down for 6 frames from frame 60, so Kirby swallows what he holds rather than spitting it out. With B that is Kirby's inhale or Yoshi's tongue. A grabber (Z) runs only 20 frames each way and presses Z for 3 frames of every 16 it stands, since a monster has to walk into a grab's short reach. The processed inputs a script stands for (`processed` in `tools/bench/src/search.rs`) are what `Fighter_procInput` makes of the pad before playback records them: Z is also A and a shoulder pressed at the common data's light press, 0.35, as a real Z press records (checked on the author's replays); without that the grab never started.

A gunner runs as an opener does, with A tapped three times 12 frames apart from its first stand's third frame (a pickup, or a held scope's burst) and held for 24 frames from its second stand's third (a level 3 charge), and every fourth round A with the stick's smash into its first run, a smash throw of what it holds when the game reads one (`gunner_pad`).

A grappler (`grappler_pad`) is Samus in port 0: she runs left under Battlefield's left platform, turns, jumps onto it facing right, and grabs once with the grapple beam's button sequence (D-pad up, down, up, then A while the beam is folded), which leaves `u.ss.x2240` at 4, so every later grab from the ground makes the beam whose hitbox stage A swings (`x16` 1). Then she grabs every 150 frames, A pressed on a chosen frame of each beam (`GRAPPLES`), with L held from the grab when the beam should bounce: L homes the ground beam on the nearest foe (`it_802B9328`), the foe below, so its tip hits the platform she stands on. Port 1's grappler drops through the top platform and stands. No random draw steers either.

A taunter (`taunter_pad`) presses the D-pad down for one frame (`at`, from "GO") and nothing else, so Fox's or Falco's side taunt starts on the next (`ftFx_AppealS_CheckInput`: the button pressed a frame before, not held); five seconds into the taunt its script cues the team's talk. A taunter with `at` -1 stands idle.

**Seed searches.** A container's monster comes out of 1 opening in 128 (`it_8026F6BC`, the common data's `x124`), and the four are weighted 2:3:1:1 (`x128`: Goomba, ReDead, Octorok, Topi), so the level 9 AI's matches release one rarely: the 76 oracle matches before the set had one ReDead (`items-held-ps`). A match whose players all follow scripts needs no CPU AI, so melee-hd can play it: `melee-bench seed-search MATCH FROM COUNT [--jobs N] [--codes REPLAY.slp] [--record DIR]` plays the match with each candidate seed, headless, as Slippi's playback would play its recording but with no frame's seed restored (the random seed runs free), each frame's inputs the processed sticks and buttons the game makes of the script, and prints the frame each reached and the unported code it stopped at. Once a monster is ported, `--watch KIND[:STATE]` names what to look for instead: each match prints the frame an item of that kind (in that state) first exists, and stops once every watched item has; `--watch caught` looks for a monster a catch box took (Kirby's inhale or Yoshi's tongue holding it). The Topi-with-a-Freezie match came from `--watch 17:4` (a Freezie in its pushed state, which only a Topi starts): 400 seeds took 5 minutes on four threads and found two. 316 seeds of the Final Destination matches' setup (Mario against Link) took 4 minutes on four threads and stopped at 38 monsters (15 ReDeads, 14 Goombas, 5 Topis, 4 Octoroks); 200 each of Battlefield's (Fox against Samus) and Pokémon Stadium's (Peach against Ganondorf), at 15 and 25; every stop was a monster.

**melee-hd records them.** ssbm-rs's recording run doesn't play a match with items on as playback does, so its recordings of the seeds found didn't release their monsters (two of the eight the first batch made held a monster, neither the one predicted). Its boot draws the item spawner's first countdown (`it_8026D018`) before the codes set the match's seed, where playback restores the seed first: the recording run's draws from the seed run one behind playback's, and its first countdown (228 to 231 frames at very high, from whatever the boot left) isn't the seed's. With the seed run back a draw and that countdown put in, a seed played in melee-hd follows ssbm-rs's recording to frame 3,414 (`monsters-redead-bf`) or 82 (`monsters-redead-fd`) before it parts again; items off (`barrel-kongo-jungle-64`), the two agree through frame 14,400. So the set is recorded by melee-hd instead: `melee-bench record-match MATCH OUT.slp` plays the match as `seed-search` does with the recorder on, and `make-oracle.sh` with `RECORDER=melee-hd` makes it the oracle as any other match: ssbm-rs's replay check of the recording and its trace of the recording's playback (which restores each frame's seed from the recording, so the trace is ssbm-rs's game whatever melee-hd computed). The recording run's controllers check doesn't apply. A match melee-hd can't play to its end (an unported monster) can't be recorded until that monster is ported; its ssbm-rs recording from the first batch is kept beside the others (`local/bench/oracle-m4-monsters/codes/`).

`tools/bench/make-oracle.sh DIR TRACES [SET|NAME...]` makes them with ssbm-rs's runner, `JOBS` at a time, each on one core at low priority: it writes the codes, records the match (`NAME.slp`), runs ssbm-rs's replay check (`NAME.check.log`, `--fp hardware`), plays the recorded controllers again when every player is human (`NAME.pads.log`), and traces the recording's playback as the oracle's other traces are made, names off, `--port all`, memory every tick (`TRACES/oracle-NAME.trc`). `NAME.times` keeps each step's time and the trace's size. `RUN_TRACE=FIELDS` also traces the recording run itself for its first fields (`NAME.run.trc`, the interpreter as in the recording but for the port the trace hooks). The recordings go to `$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/bench/oracle-m4/`:

```sh
SSBM_RUN=.../ssbm-run-rf.exe SSBM_DISC=... JOBS=2 bash tools/bench/make-oracle.sh \
  "$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/bench/oracle-m4" "$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/traces/v5-mixed" items-ported-fd cpu-marth-bf
```

**The pilots (2026-10-16, ssbm-rs `ssbm-run-rf`, d61135b).** `items-ported-fd` (Fox against Marth, both `BENCH_AI`, items high with the ported kinds) played its two minutes with 59 items, 23 of them from the switch (capsules, boxes, barrels, Party Balls, 12 Bob-ombs, a heart, the bat, swords, shells) and the rest the fighters' own: the replay check and the controllers alone both compared 61,908 events through frame 7,200 with no divergence. `cpu-marth-bf` (a real level 9 CPU Marth against a `BENCH_AI` Fox) played its three minutes to the time limit, and the replay check compared 65,877 events through frame 10,800 with no divergence: ssbm-rs's playback reproduces a real-CPU recording (the AI runs every tick while playback injects the CPU's recorded inputs). Traced into `local/traces/v5-mixed/oracle-items-ported-fd.trc` (315 MB) and `oracle-cpu-marth-bf.trc` (470 MB). `melee-oracle diff` with `--fp hardware` compares the items pilot's first 510 ticks with no difference and stops in tick 511 (replay frame 387) at an item's dynamic bones (`lb_8000FD48`, task 3), and the CPU pilot at its first tick, at `ftCo_800B2AFC` for computer kind 4 (task 5). Each pilot took ssbm-rs about 11 to 12 minutes of one core in all (recording 201 and 272 s, replay check 174 and 264 s, controllers 179 s, trace 118 and 190 s), on a machine other agents were building on.

**A recording run doesn't lay out the heap as playback does.** `melee-oracle trace-heap` on the recording run's trace and the playback's: Slippi's bootloader carves the code list's heap from the arena, and playback's code set is larger (OS heap 0 is `0x2EC0` bytes in playback against `0x13C0` in the recording run), so the arena and every heap start `0x1B00` bytes higher in playback; playback's codes also hold one more block from boot and, by the match's first tick, seven more blocks and one more GObj with its process (a playback code's, in `p_link` 7). The fighters land `0x2720` bytes lower in the recording run (Fox at `0x80D02DA0` against `0x80D054C0` in the items pilot), and `diff` refuses the run's trace at its address check, whereas the playback trace matches the allocator model. So a trace of the recording run can't serve the free-running CPU diff as it stands (milestone 4's task 6): either melee-hd's allocator model learns the recording boot's layout, or ssbm-rs plays the recording back with the CPU ports' input injection off (the AI driving them in playback's layout), which ssbm-rs's runner can't do today.

### The CPU AI

**The level 1 replays are several games in one file.** The three ranked replays with a level 1 CPU (milestone 4's survey) each hold several Slippi games in one raw event stream, each from its event payloads message to its game end: the CPU games come first and end in a no contest (LRAS), and the last game, unfinished, is a human match, which ssbm-rs and melee-hd both play from such a file. `hf/ness-mm/slp/master-master-25eaa574…` holds five games of Link against a CPU Ness (Final Destination, Battlefield, Fountain of Dreams, Yoshi's Story, Pokémon Stadium; 124 to 154 frames), `hf/mewtwo-mm/slp/master-master-3e1a8c1d…` a CPU Mewtwo against Falco on Battlefield (2,108 frames) and `hf/ness-mm/slp/master-master-69664b46…` a CPU Ness against Marth on Battlefield (3,687). Each finished game is split into a replay of its own (the raw events alone), in `$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/replays/hf-cpu/` (`hf-link-ness-{fd,bf,fod,ys,ps}-25eaa574-g1` to `-g5`, `hf-mewtwo-falco-bf-3e1a8c1d-g1`, `hf-ness-marth-bf-69664b46-g1`), traced as the others are into `local/traces/v5-mixed/` (names off, `--port all`, memory every tick). Their replay checks compare every event with no divergence but for the Fountain of Dreams game's first-frame position bit (port 1's y, frames -118 and -117), Yoshi's Story's first frames' state flags (0x80 at frames -122 and -121) and Pokémon Stadium's (port 2's flag and the frame start's scene frame from frame 4), the entry and frozen-Stadium kinds the replays above already show. The CPUs are computer kind 4 (VS), level 1.

In Slippi's float mode with no stand-in each compares every tick with no difference and no stale read, with `--heap` too, to its end, and passes `sync-test --rollback 7`: the VS decision (`ftCo_800B24B8`, `game::ft::cpu_vs`: the target entering the stage, the best standing human enemy, heading for it, back from off camera), the jump at the target, the ranged attacks (`ftCo_800B9CBC`, Ness's PK Fire, PK Flash and PK Thunder steered, Mewtwo's Shadow Ball charged and let go, a table's weighted pick), Ness's recovery and the attack's follow-ups.

All 29 of the level 9 oracle matches (the `cpu` set, with `cpu-kirby-bf` below) compare every tick with no difference and no stale read, with `--heap` too, and pass `sync-test --rollback 7` (`--fp hardware`). The first five: `cpu-puff-fod`, `cpu-luigi-bf`, `cpu-mario-ps`, `cpu-mewtwo-dl` (11,034 or 11,035 ticks each) and the level sweep `cpu-sweep-dk-bf` (three Donkey Kongs at levels 1, 4 and 7 against a human Fox). They run the shield and dodge decision (`ftCo_800BB9B4`: an enemy's hitbox about to reach it, an enemy move coming down on it, an item's hitbox on its way, Fox's lasers among them) and its behavior (`ftCo_800BA9A0`: rolls, the shield held by level, air dodges), the characters' own rules on traveling specials (`ftCo_800B77E8`), the charge for Donkey Kong, Samus, Mewtwo and Kirby's copies (`ftCo_800ACD5C`), the grabs that count as holding (`ftCo_IsGrabbing`) and the standings between CPUs. Then 17 more, once the DI's leftover from the floor check was modeled (below; 2026-10-20): `cpu-captain-dl`, `cpu-dk-ps`, `cpu-doc-dl`, `cpu-falco-ps`, `cpu-fox-fd`, `cpu-ganon-fod`, `cpu-gnw-fod`, `cpu-marth-bf`, `cpu-ness-fd`, `cpu-pikachu-ys`, `cpu-roy-fd`, `cpu-samus-fd`, `cpu-sheik-ys`, `cpu-zelda-bf` and the sweep `cpu-sweep-mewtwo-ps` (11,027 to 11,035 ticks), `cpu-pichu-ys` (10,313, a stock match's end) and `cpu-yoshi-bf` (9,771); their DIs with no knockback read 2 to 26 such leftovers each. And `cpu-bowser-ps`, once the comparer compared the timer's digits as dangling pointers after the timer is freed (below). Then five more, once a CPU holding its own item and the island end with none in reach were ported (2026-10-21): `cpu-peach-fod` (9,230 ticks, a stock match's end), `cpu-link-dl` and `cpu-ylink-ys` (11,034), `cpu-ics-fd` (8,929) and the sweep `cpu-sweep-samus-fd` (11,033), the last two against interpreter traces (below). A CPU holding an item skips the swung items' attacks (`ftCo_800A59E4`; a swung item stops at `ftCo_800B52AC`, which only items on reach) and uses it from range instead of its ranged attack (`ftCo_800B9A04`): a bomb about to go off thrown, a shooting item shot or thrown away empty, containers and thrown items by angle, anything else thrown ahead at a target more than 30 away (Peach's turnips, Link's and Young Link's bombs). The island end beside with none in reach leaves the destination what the stack held (below). The approach behaviors (`ftCo_800AB224` with `xF9_b1`, `ftCo_800BA674`, `ftCo_800BA2E8`) no diff stopped at yet, but captured frames reached them (ported since, below).

**The DI's registers.** With no knockback, a CPU's DI (`ftCo_800AC5A0`) pushes the stick by r5 and r30, which its frame leaves (the stale-register rule, [the engine notes](engine.md)). The oracle matches' code sets have no NanaDeterminism, so r30 is the fighter's `Fighter*` (the allocator model) and r5 what the VS decision's last call, the behavior pick (`ftCo_800ADE48`), left: the seed pointer `HSD_Randf` loads (`&seed`, `0x804D5F90`) when a coin flip was its last call (the DI's own switch, or the 400-frame flip), `MatchEnd + 0x30` (`0x8046B91C`) when its standing query compiled the results this tick (`fn_801661E0`'s last team), and 1 when a charge's release ran before the DI (`ftCo_800ADC28`'s last wait; a charging state can keep the hitstun flag). Otherwise it is what the destination's last floor check (`mpCheckFloor`) left, which its last tested line decides (`mp::FloorR5`, `Geometry::check_floor_r5`): with no line let through its joint and flag checks, its caller's r5, the flags pointer the pick passes (its frame's `r1 + 0x58`); after a level line, the pointer to its own frame's slot it passed `mpLib_8004ED5C` (`r1 + 0x44`); after a sloped one, `mpLineIntersection`'s flag for the segment's end just above the line (0 or 1). The pick runs one check, or two when the destination is reset in the air, and `ftCo_800A1B38` and the blast zone getters leave r5 alone on every stage but Big Blue, Peach's Castle, Corneria and Venom, whose excluded-floor checks call stage code (not followed). The stack addresses come from `r1` at the CPU frame's entry, `0x804EEAB0` in every saved call (`ftCo_800B3900`'s only caller is the fighters' input process), and the frames on the way: under the VS decision `0x804EE948` and `0x804EE844`, a frame deeper under `ftCo_800AE7AC` (off camera) `0x804EE8E0` and `0x804EE7DC`. The 17 matches' DIs read `0x804EE844` (65 times) and the flag's 0 (16), after the DI's switch left the seed pointer two ticks before (100); the leaf check's changed frames reach the flags slots and the addresses under `ftCo_800AE7AC`, and no match or saved frame leaves the flag's 1 (melee-sim's `intersect_r5_flag` checks it).

**The island end's stack vector.** With its target in the air over no floor and no island end beside it in reach (under Final Destination, between its ends), `ftCo_800A75DC` sets the destination from a stack vector `ftCo_800A4768` never wrote: the back chain and the saved return address the target search's frame (`ftCo_800A4BEC`) left there, `0x804EE980` and `0x800A4D48` (`ftCo_800A0F00`'s return) or `0x800A4CB4` (`ftCo_IsAlly`'s) as floats ([the engine notes](engine.md#uninitialized-values)). `cpu-ics-fd` reads it 25 times, once with `ftCo_IsAlly` the search's last call, first in tick 3,260; `cpu-sweep-samus-fd` in tick 5,721. ssbm-rs's `--port all` traces (`oracle-cpu-ics-fd.trc`, `oracle-cpu-sweep-samus-fd.trc`) hold 0 in the y instead: its ports don't store the return address a call saves in its caller's frame, so the slot keeps an older word (an island end's height). Its interpreter keeps the console's, so both matches were traced again with the original code (`--port sysdolphin/baselib/perf`, the trace's hook alone; 367 and 690 s), into `local/traces/v5-mixed/oracle-cpu-ics-fd-interp.trc` and `oracle-cpu-sweep-samus-fd-interp.trc`; their replay checks show what the first traces' do (`cpu-ics-fd`'s five divergences in Nana's stick from frame 7,175, `cpu-sweep-samus-fd` none). Against those, both compare every tick with no difference and no stale read, with `--heap` too; against the `--port all` traces each stops at the first such read (`cpu.x54.y`, 0 against `0x800A4D48`), the only difference up to there.

**The timer's digits in `cpu-bowser-ps`.** At five seconds left, the timer's process (`ifTime_UpdateTimers`) loads the countdown's model and frees the timer's GObj, whose digit joints `ifTime_data` keeps pointing at; nothing reads them after (the timer's digits are set only while `match_timer` is there, [the engine notes](engine.md#uninitialized-values)). In tick 10,649 a joint the same tick allocates takes the freed block of the second digit in the original (`0x810a0640`), where melee-hd's digit points to its freed joint 383. The comparer then passed a dangling joint pointer only while the reference's block was still free, so it reported `hud time.digits[1]` there, and for a while the digits were left out while `match_timer` was null. Since the dangling pointers' rule (2026-10-22, "Dangling pointers" above) they compare by address like any other: a digit names the address its freed joint had, even where melee-hd's slot has since held a joint elsewhere (digit 4's slot is reused at `0x810a0640` and freed again by tick 10,680, while the original's digit still holds `0x810a0820`, its first joint's address). The match compares all 11,033 traced ticks with no difference and no stale read, with `--heap` too, to the trace's end, and passes `sync-test --rollback 7` (10,918 ticks checked).

**Off camera on the ground and in the barrels (2026-10-21, `feat/cpu-ship`).** `cpu-stage-poke-floats` (8,862 ticks, a stock match's end) and `cpu-stage-kongo-jungle-64` (11,023) compare every tick with no difference and no stale read, with `--heap`, and pass `sync-test --rollback 7` (8,776 and 10,918 ticks checked). Their replay checks show the first frames' entry position bits on ports 1, 3 and 4 (frames -118 to -102), and Poké Floats' 198 post-frame hitstun words from frame 848, the misc-AS heap words the recording run's layout gives (the playback's are what melee-hd matches). On Poké Floats the CPUs take the off-camera ground case (`ftCo_800AE7AC` into `ftCo_800A6FC4`) 2,950 times: 1,756 find another island toward the blast zones' middle and 1,194 find none and head for their target, 604 of them for one in the air, each with an island end beside it in reach, so no match reads the stack vector (the frame's save of f20) there; leaf-check's `OffCameraGround { target_under }` does. On Kongo Jungle N64, Nana rides the barrel for 381 frames (ticks 6,784 to 7,166) until a tap of A with it pointing just right of up shoots her; no VS CPU entered it, and the barrel cannon needs items on: leaf-check's `Barrel` changes reach those. `cpu-stage-kongo-jungle` (11,026 ticks; its replay check the same entry bits and 247 hitstun words from frame 233) compares clean too, with `--heap`, and passes `sync-test --rollback 7` (10,918 ticks), but no CPU entered a barrel or stood off camera on the ground there; so does `cpu-stage-yoshis-island` (9,465 ticks, no replay check divergence; sync 9,396), whose CPUs never stood off camera on the ground either. leaf-check's `cpu_vs` adds the two matches' saved frames (`oracle-cpu-stage-poke-floats` from field 1410, `oracle-cpu-stage-kongo-jungle-64` from field 7090, 400 calls each) and the `OffCameraGround` and `Barrel` changes. Broken on purpose: the off-camera search's 45-degree cone narrowed to 0.6 radians, an end's inset made 6, the middle's probe reach 5, the fighter's own island not skipped and its random draw dropped fail `cpu-stage-poke-floats` (in ticks 1,457, 2,875, 5,278, 719 and 719) and, but for the probe's reach, `cpu_vs`; the ends counted with no middle found and the stack vector's f20 made 1.0 fail only `cpu_vs`; the barrel's angle window narrowed to 0.1 below and no tap fail `cpu-stage-kongo-jungle-64` (ticks 7,157 and 7,155) and `cpu_vs`; the window's top made exclusive, the barrel cannon's coin made 0.4 and its period 4 frames fail only `cpu_vs` (the period once `Cannon(405)` was added: 400 is a multiple of both). Not shown to fail: the blast zones' middle the search aims at moved (its x weighted 0.55), in either.

**Brinstar's acid, Rainbow Cruise's edges and Great Bay's turtle (2026-10-22, `feat/cpu-stages3`).** `cpu-stage-brinstar` (11,021 ticks), `cpu-stage-rainbow-cruise` (10,966) and `cpu-stage-great-bay` (11,025), made as the other `cpu-stages` matches were (ssbm-rs's `ssbm-run-rf`, `make-oracle.sh`, `JOBS=2`; traced `--port all` into `local/traces/v5-mixed/oracle-cpu-stage-*.trc`), compare every tick with no difference and no stale read, `--fp hardware --heap`, to their traces' ends, and pass `sync-test --rollback 7` (10,918, 10,880 and 10,918 ticks checked). Their replay checks compare every event but Rainbow Cruise's first-frame entry height bit (port 2, frames -113 and -112). They reach `ftCo_800A229C`'s stage cases and `ftCo_800A8210` from `ftCo_800AE7AC`: on Brinstar every decision takes the acid's case (the acid is always above the blast zones' bottom), 7,263 on the ground, 191 of them with every island under the acid, and 10,335 in the air, where the search doesn't run; on Rainbow Cruise 3,745 on the ground near the edges, 117 of them searching against the wind and 52 finding nothing, and 5,558 in the air; on Great Bay 852 by the surfaced turtle on the ground (182 with a route's timer running, which keeps the destination) and 2,185 in the air. No trace reads the island end's stack vector (`cpu.x54.y`), so no interpreter retrace was needed, and the stages' cases read nothing unwritten ([the engine notes](engine.md#uninitialized-values)). leaf-check's `cpu_vs` adds 400 saved frames of each (`oracle-cpu-stage-brinstar` from field 2090, `-rainbow-cruise` from 4110, `-great-bay` from 4720, packed): all compare clean as saved in both float modes, and every change that applies without items compares clean on them. Broken on purpose: the acid test inverted fails `cpu-stage-brinstar` in tick 1 and the acid point's height raised 100 in tick 2,429; the far end measured from the fighter instead fails it in tick 1,188; Rainbow Cruise's 40% band made 45% fails `cpu-stage-rainbow-cruise` in tick 1, its 20% sides made 25% in tick 742, the wind's x and y directions swapped in ticks 1 and 177, and the search against the wind dropped in tick 2,337; the safe-ground search run in the air fails `cpu-stage-great-bay` in tick 1,281 and the destination's reach made 6 in tick 1,377. Not shown to fail, after two runs (`cpu-stage-brinstar` and `cpu_vs`' frames): the acid point's x off the middle (0.6 of the blast zones' sum for 0.5), which Brinstar's symmetric blast zones leave at 0 either way.

**Big Blue's sides and the cars' floors (2026-10-22, `feat/bigblue-followup`).** `cpu-stage-big-blue` (Luigi, `BENCH_AI`, against Falco 9, Ness 5 and Donkey Kong 2, an eight-minute time match, items off), made as the other `cpu-stages` matches were (ssbm-rs's `ssbm-run-rf` with its return-address fix, `make-oracle.sh`; traced `--port all`, 1,771 MB), compares every one of its 29,038 ticks with no difference and no stale read, `--fp hardware --heap`, and passes `sync-test --rollback 7` (28,918 ticks checked). Its replay check shows 117,451 divergences, and melee-hd's own recording of the replay (`melee-oracle record`) the same 117,451, so they are the recording run's against the playback the trace follows: port 1's entrance (heights from frame -118 and actions from -59, as in `stage-big-blue`), the instance counters from frame -34, and Ness's position for 46 frames from 21,874, as he falls against the camera after a KO off the top (action 7), up to 0.45 apart at first and closing; actions and percents are equal from frame 0. A probe counted what it reaches: `ftCo_800A229C`'s Big Blue case (`cpu_near_blast_side`) 11,927 times (3,496 on the ground), the safe-ground search 2,591 times, which found an island toward the middle every time but once, at frame 999, when it searched against the wind and found one; Big Blue's excluded floors' check (`grBigBlue_801EF844`) left r5 1 367 times (joints 33, 35 to 39 and 41) and 0 86,605 times; of the six DIs with no knockback, four read the seed pointer a coin flip left and two (frames 8,696 and 18,763) the check's 0, none its 1. leaf-check's `cpu_vs` adds 400 saved frames from each of two windows (`oracle-cpu-stage-big-blue` from field 1086, with the search against the wind, and `oracle-cpu-stage-big-blue-r5` from 2867, where the check leaves 1; packed): both compare clean as saved in both float modes, and every change that applies without items compares clean on them. Broken on purpose: the 20% sides made 25% fails `cpu-stage-big-blue` in tick 511, the search against the wind dropped in tick 1,123 (frame 999), and the check's 1 and 0 swapped in tick 8,820 (frame 8,696, the DI's stick) and `cpu_vs_changed` (its DIs with no knockback, in seven frames). Not shown to fail, after the diff and `cpu_vs`: joint 42 counted with 35 to 41 (the probe's 171 floors on joint 42 fed no DI with no knockback, and neither window has one), and the bottom test without its NaN case (`<` for `bge`'s negation: no position is NaN).

**Items on (2026-10-22, `feat/cpu-switch-items`).** The `cpu-items` set's four matches (a real level 9 CPU against a `BENCH_AI` human, every switch kind at frequency high) are ssbm-rs recordings (`ssbm-run-rf`'s `BENCH_CODES` run), as the `cpu` set's are: melee-hd couldn't play a CPU with items before this work, and playback restores each frame's seed from the recording, so the recording run's late item-spawner countdown ("Seed searches", above) changes which items come out, not whether playback reproduces the recording. Their replay checks: `cpu-items-marth-fd` and `-samus-ps` compare every event (92,537 and 97,991) with no divergence, `-mario-bf` (85,360) shows port 2's entry position bit at frames -113 and -112, and `-captain-ys` (134,049) Yoshi's Story's first-frame state flags (0x80, frames -122 and -121) and the position bit (-118 and -117), the entry kinds the level 1 replays show. They were traced with the original code but for the trace's hook (`make-oracle.sh`'s `TRACE_PORT=sysdolphin/baselib/perf`, as the interpreter traces above, so no port's lost return address reaches the stack words the CPU reads), into `local/traces/v5-mixed/oracle-cpu-items-*.trc` (422 to 477 MB; recording, replay check and trace about 15 minutes of one core a match). All four compare every tick (11,029 to 11,034) with no difference and no stale read, with `--heap` too, and pass `sync-test --rollback 7` (10,918 ticks each). A probe run counted what they reach: the CPU heading for an item (`ftCo_800A866C`, 1,615 frames: the floor under it every time, an island's left end in 219), checking a pickup (`ftCo_800A3710`, 1,905 with an item to go for, 482 of them on its island, 52 in reach by `ftpickupitem_80094150`), and a swung item's attacks (`ftCo_800B52AC`, 296 frames: Captain Falcon's fan and parasol, Mario's Beam Sword, Samus's bat; 16 picked one). They don't reach an item to go for on an island to the CPU's right, a container's monster (`ftCo_800A648C`'s candidates and the attacks on one, `ftCo_800B5AB0`), a cloaked enemy, Lip's Stick's or the Star Rod's reach, the shield behavior's rolls by a Flipper, barrel cannon or Poison Mushroom (`ftCo_800BA9A0`, `ftCo_800A2998`), or the item to go for no longer grabbable (`ftCo_800A3710` forgetting it: the VS decision picks only grabbable items in the same frame, so only Nana's paths can reach it); leaf-check's changed frames reach the rest (below). The swung item's attacks also cover Peach's Beam Sword pulls with items off.

**Every level, Kirby and teams (2026-10-22).** `cpu-kirby-bf` and the `cpu-levels` set ([Oracle sets](#oracle-sets)) were made with ssbm-rs's `ssbm-run-rf`, as the `cpu` set was. All 17 compare every tick with no difference and no stale read with `--fp hardware --heap`, to the trace's end, and pass `sync-test --rollback 7` (10,918 ticks checked each, 9,940 for the team match): `cpu-kirby-bf` (11,032 ticks), `cpu-l1-sheik-l5-falco-bf` (11,025), `cpu-l1-captain-l5-peach-ps` (11,028; no Beam Sword from Peach's down special reached a CPU), `cpu-l1-mario-l5-pikachu-dl`, `cpu-l1-puff-l5-link-fod`, `cpu-l1-ylink-l5-dk-bf`, `cpu-l1-doc-l5-ganon-ps`, `cpu-l1-bowser-l5-pichu-dl` (11,030 each), `cpu-l1-zelda-l5-roy-ys` (11,024), `cpu-l1-gnw-l5-mewtwo-fod` (11,031), `cpu-four-l2368-dl` (11,026), `cpu-four-l4579-bf` (11,029) and `cpu-l1-luigi-l5-samus-ys` (11,030, below). Four read the island end's stack vector, so their `--port all` traces stop at the first such read (`cpu.x54.y`, 0 against `0x800A4D48`, the only difference up to there), and were traced again with the original code as `cpu-ics-fd` was (`oracle-NAME-interp.trc`): `cpu-l1-fox-l5-marth-fd` (first read in tick 10,608), `cpu-l1-ness-l5-yoshi-fd` (1,775), `cpu-l1-ics-l5-kirby-fd` (9,320) and `cpu-teams-fd` (809, a stock match's end at frame 9,822); against those they compare 11,027, 11,028, 11,028 and 10,060 ticks clean. The replay checks show only the kinds above: the hitstun word's heap addresses (the recording run's layout, `cpu-kirby-bf`, `cpu-l1-ness-l5-yoshi-fd`, `cpu-l1-bowser-l5-pichu-dl`, `cpu-l1-ics-l5-kirby-fd`, `cpu-four-l2368-dl`), an entering fighter's position y bit and Yoshi's Story's first frames' state flags.

**`cpu-l1-luigi-l5-samus-ys` and the beam tip.** It first stopped in tick 9,375 (replay frame 9,251) at `generator 10 jobj` and `generator 11 jobj`: `JObjId` ours 704, theirs `0x8109f5c0` (paired with 697). Samus's beam tip entry (`fighter.parts[139].joint`, the one entry past her bones the comparer reads) names the tip joint of a beam freed in tick 3,820 on both sides, 697 in melee-hd and `0x8109f5c0` in the trace, the allocator model's address for 697. In tick 9,279 the original's heap gave that block to a new joint, and melee-hd's model put its new joint 704 at the same address; the two generators attach to it on both sides. The comparer passed a dangling joint pointer only while the reference's block was still free, and from tick 9,279 on its fallback paired the address with the freed 697 through the beam tip, so the generators' 704 disagreed. Nothing reads the entry while its joint is freed ([the engine notes](engine.md#uninitialized-values)). With the dangling pointers' rule (above) the entry compares by address, and the match compares all 11,030 traced ticks with no difference and no stale read with `--fp hardware --heap`, and passes `sync-test --rollback 7` (10,918 ticks checked). It reads no island end's stack vector, so its `--port all` trace is the reference.

**A target with a Starman or a hammer, and a shrunk Yoshi (2026-10-22, `feat/cpu-items-last`).** The CPU audit's last two gaps, both items-on. An enemy with a Starman (`x221D_b6`) or a hammer (its music, `x2168`) in reach is the VS decision's target to close in on (`ftCo_800A5CE0`), with the approach allowed (`xF9_b1`) and `xF8_b6` set when it stands within twice its character's distance threshold (`ftCo_800A20A0`). Walking on the ground (`ftCo_800AB224`) the CPU then jumps at the target when it faces it on its island within 37.5 (`ftCo_800A0148`), or, near it, at its level's chance (5% a level), rolls away from it toward the end of its island with room (`ftCo_800BA2E8`, by `ftCo_800A2A70`'s distances to the ends against the threshold), as the attack behavior does (`ftCo_800B658C`); standing (`ftCo_800ACD5C`) it rolls away while that side's end is farther than twice the threshold, else toward the target (`ftCo_800BA674`). A shrunk Yoshi falling short of a destination within 20 jumps and air-dodges up and forward 30 frames later (`ftCo_800A0508`). None of them reads anything unwritten (the approach's roll always has its target, [the engine notes](engine.md#uninitialized-values)), but the attack reads `xF8_b6` stale ([Melee's bugs](melee-bugs.md)). The `cpu-approach` set's four matches, made as the other oracle matches are (ssbm-rs's `ssbm-run-rf`, `make-oracle.sh`, `JOBS=2`; traced `--port all` into `local/traces/v5-mixed/oracle-cpu-approach-*.trc`), compare every tick with no difference and no stale read, `--fp hardware --heap`, to their traces' ends, and pass `sync-test --rollback 7` (10,918 ticks checked each): `cpu-approach-yoshi-bf` (11,034 ticks), `-yoshi-fod` (11,033), `-captain-dl` (11,029) and `-ness-fd` (11,032). Between them the CPUs (the `BENCH_AI` humans' AI frames among them) approach 997 times, jump at the target 29 times and roll away 27 times walking and 3 attacking (twice on a stale `xF8_b6`), and the shrunk CPU Yoshi jumps once (`cpu-approach-yoshi-bf`, tick 1,752); no match rolls standing or finds no room either way. Their replay checks compare every event but the post-frame hitstun words, the misc-AS heap words the recording run's layout gives (`-yoshi-bf` 70 from frame 4,919, `-yoshi-fod` 117 from frame 1,644), and the entry's first-frame height bit (`-yoshi-fod` and `-captain-dl`, port 1, frames -118 and -117); `-ness-fd` none. leaf-check's `cpu_vs` adds three sets of saved frames, packed (`oracle-cpu-approach-yoshi-bf` from field 1740, `-yoshi-bf-2` from 3870, `-captain-dl` from 1060; 754 calls, every call in its window), all clean as saved in both float modes and under every change that applies without items, and `Approach` changes: on the ground at level 9 in the walk, standing or attack behavior with its enemies under a Starman on its floor 10 ahead, 10 behind or 33 ahead, each clean on all 6,494 frames it changes across the sets. Broken on purpose: the jump's reach made 30 fails `cpu-approach-yoshi-bf` in tick 3,302 and `-captain-dl` in 1,619 (and `Approach` 33 ahead); the roll's chance made 0.04 fails them in 10,887 and 4,770; `xF8_b6` left set after the roll in 3,187 and 1,116; the facing test dropped in 3,920 and 4,770; the roll's sides tried in the other order in 3,187 and 4,770; the island's ends swapped in 3,920 and 1,074; the attack going on after its roll in 4,967 and 1,074; the room measured against 2 more than the threshold `cpu-approach-yoshi-bf` in 3,929; the shrunk Yoshi's wait made 29 and its stick 0x51 fail it in 1,752; each but the chance and the reach also fails the saved frames, and those two fail `Approach`. Standing's roll (`ftCo_800BA674`), which no match reaches, fails only `Approach` (its factor made 1, its sides swapped). Not shown to fail: the roll's no-room return (`ftCo_800BA2E8` finding no room on either side), which no match or change reaches.

**Icicle Mountain's edges (2026-10-22, `feat/cpu-icemt`).** `cpu-stage-icicle-mountain` (28,985 ticks, an eight-minute time match), made the same way with ssbm-rs's `ssbm-run-rf` rebuilt from main with the return-address fix (port-mode traces equal interpreter ones), compares every tick with no difference and no stale read, `--fp hardware --heap`, and passes `sync-test --rollback 7` (28,918 ticks checked). Its replay check compares all 518,650 events with no divergence. The VS CPUs take `ftCo_800A229C`'s Icicle Mountain case 33,313 times (26,134 near the bottom while the climb's wind blows down, 7,179 near the top), and on the ground `ftCo_800A8210`'s 12,529 times: 9,822 search for the nearest platform above (`ftCo_800A6A98`) and 2,707 for the nearest island below (`ftCo_800A6D2C`, a random draw per island). Every search found a place and no route's timer was running, so the destination was always set. The case reads nothing unwritten ([the engine notes](engine.md#uninitialized-values)). leaf-check's `cpu_vs` adds 400 saved frames (`oracle-cpu-stage-icicle-mountain` from field 17,860, where the CPUs turn from searching above to searching below; packed, 37 MiB): all compare clean as saved in both float modes, and every change that applies without items compares clean on them. Broken on purpose, every change fails the diff: the band's 0.4 made 0.45 in tick 88, the wind's share of it made 0.5 in tick 99, the wind's direction swapped in the case in tick 102 and in the search in tick 102; above, the platform flag dropped and the 2-unit margin over the feet dropped in tick 146, the blast zones' bounds dropped in tick 11,765 and the farthest taken for the nearest in tick 102; below, the jump's height dropped in tick 3,167, and the random draw dropped, the fighter's own island searched and the farthest taken each in tick 1,184; the middle's probe reaching 10 below for 20 in tick 575.

leaf-check's `cpu_vs` runs the CPU frame (`ftCo_800B3900`) from saved calls of the seven level 1 games and ten level 9 oracle matches (`local/calls/cpu-vs`, packed; the last five, 2026-10-21, where a CPU holds a turnip or a bomb and where a target falls under Final Destination) against the machine code, as saved and changed to reach what they don't ([the test's header](../tools/leaf-check/tests/cpu_vs.rs)). Broken on purpose, the ports fail a diff or the leaf check: the fear flag left clear fails `cpu-puff-fod` in tick 1, the reach made a difference fails `hf-link-ness-fd-25eaa574-g1` in tick 65, a 20-unit camera margin both long level 1 games, the standings reversed `cpu-sweep-dk-bf`, the shield's radius scaled `cpu-puff-fod`, the roll's side swapped `cpu-puff-fod` and `cpu-luigi-bf` in ticks 1067 and 636, Mario's rule's reach `cpu-mario-ps` in tick 2106, Din's Fire's aim `cpu-zelda-bf`, the jump's level floor `cpu-puff-fod`, and the charge's start `cpu-mewtwo-dl`; the near-target and beside-target reaches, the off-camera flags, the island end beside, the ranged chance, the wall check's side, Yoshi's and the table's picks, PK Thunder's aim, the up smash's dodge, Ness's recovery height and frames, Zelda's, and both DI leftovers fail `cpu_vs_changed`. The floor check's leftover (`mpCheckFloor`'s r5, `check_floor_r5`): `cpu_vs_changed` adds DIs with no knockback that read it as saved, with the destination moved far off the stage in the air and on the ground, high above the stage off camera and far to its side; with a probe they read `0x804EE844` and, under `ftCo_800AE7AC`, `0x804EE7DC`, and broken on purpose the pick's flags slot (0x58 to 0x5C), `ftCo_800AE7AC`'s frame size and the second check's leftover ignored fail them; `mpCheckFloor`'s own slot (0x44 to 0x48), its frame size, the pick's, the VS decision's and the CPU frame's entry `r1` (each off by 8 or 16) fail `cpu-gnw-fod` in tick 256, `mpLineIntersection`'s flag inverted, ignored, or taken only from lines the segment crosses fail `cpu-pichu-ys` in tick 425, and the flag never set or not cleared on entry fail melee-sim's `intersect_r5_flag`. Not shown to fail: the leftover on Peach's Castle (unfollowed, `Leftover::Unknown`; Big Blue's, Corneria's and Venom's are followed), and the flag's 1, which no match or saved frame reaches (the unit test does). Not shown to fail: the same-island test's air check, the ranged count (`x90`), Pikachu's far recovery, and Donkey Kong's carry in `ftCo_800B630C` and `ftCo_IsGrabbing`. The held item and the island end's stack vector: `cpu_vs_changed` adds `Held` (a CPU holding its item with its ranged attack due, the item as held and made a capsule, a box and a Poké Ball) and `TargetUnderStage` (its enemies under the middle of the stage, which on Final Destination leaves no end in reach; with the last other fighter made untargetable, the target search's last saving call is `ftCo_IsAlly`). Broken on purpose: `ftCo_800A0F00`'s or `ftCo_IsAlly`'s return address off by 4, `ftCo_IsAlly`'s noted only past the untargetable test, and the back chain off by 8 fail `cpu_vs_changed` (and the first fails `cpu-ics-fd`'s interpreter trace in tick 3,260, the back chain `cpu-sweep-samus-fd`'s in 5,721); the throw-ahead's distance (50) or command (0x2B), the container's command, the Poké Ball thrown ahead and the thrown items' cone widened to 30 degrees fail `cpu_vs_changed`, as does the held item falling through to the ranged attack (which fails `cpu-ylink-ys` in tick 8,194; the throw-ahead's command fails `cpu-peach-fod` in tick 151). Not shown to fail: a bomb's fuse threshold (30 for 60; no held bomb checked is that near going off) and a thrown item's up and down swapped (no target checked is more than 75 degrees above or below), and the shooting items' branch and `it_8026B924` (no fighter's own item is shot), which stays awaiting a check.

**The item paths (task 15's first part).** Four more sets come from the `cpu-items` matches (`oracle-cpu-items-marth-fd`, `-mario-bf`, `-samus-ps`, `-captain-ys`: every 54th frame of each match, `CAPTURE_EVERY=54`, 207 frames each), and `cpu_vs_changed` adds items-on changes, which run on those sets alone (a change runs every frame of a set, changed or not): `Swung` (the held item made each swung kind, so each reach slot), `SwungAround` (a swung kind and the enemies placed in front and above, varied by the frame), `EnemyItem` (every item made a Goomba or a Topi at growing distances in front: the nearest enemy item and the attacks on it), `Cloaked` (the enemies under a power-up's colors, the cloak holding, flickering, and without its refraction) and `MirroredX` (the CPU moved right of the middle: an island's right end); `ItemThreat` (the CPU moved onto the first live item hitbox, every item made a Flipper, barrel cannon or Poison Mushroom) runs on every set, since the items-off sets' lasers are what come at a grounded CPU.
