Mutation-tested, each mutant against the four `cpu-items` diffs and then leaf-check (2026-10-22): the diffs fail with Mario's Beam Sword's slot or the ground table in its place (`cpu-items-mario-bf`), the far side's reach dropped facing right or added facing left (`cpu-items-captain-ys`, `-samus-ps`), the same-island test dropped (`cpu-items-samus-ps` in tick 8,093), the pickup box's left edge without its half width (`cpu-items-marth-fd`, `-samus-ps`), the destination reach (`x38`) 2 rather than 1 (all four, first `cpu-items-captain-ys` in tick 411) and the left end's 5 made 6 (`cpu-items-mario-bf` in tick 1,940, `-captain-ys`); `cpu_vs_changed` fails with the right end's 5 made 6, the farthest enemy item, a Topi's kind off by one, the enemy item's half size or its box's bottom dropped, the cloak's flicker threshold or hold changed, its refraction ignored, `ftCo_800A2998`'s 5 made 50 and the rolls by a Flipper or barrel cannon and by a Poison Mushroom swapped. Survived twice, uncaught: the swung table's top without the item's reach (`SwungAround`'s targets never sit between the two tops of a picked entry), the pickup box ignoring a heavy item's offset (no checked CPU is in reach of a heavy item it goes for), the pickup ignoring `x2222_b4` (no checked CPU in that state reaches a healing item), the floor check under an item from 5 above it rather than 1 (no stage has a floor 1 to 5 above another's), and `ftCo_800A866C` skipping its same-island test (a fighter on the item's island stands within its ends, so neither end's test can pass: equivalent). `ftCo_800A3710` forgetting an item no longer grabbable isn't reached: the VS decision picks only grabbable items in the same frame.

### The Ice Climbers on the stages

Nana's computer player asks some stages about themselves, which the launcher refused the Ice Climbers on until it was ported (2026-10-22, `feat/nana-items`; [the player](player.md#stages-the-player-offers)): whether an island is safe on Brinstar (an end under the acid, or under where it rises to in 90 frames at its last step, `ftCo_800A1F98`) and Onett (an end at the street's height, 5 or lower, while a car is coming) in `ftCo_800A2718`; Rainbow Cruise's wind carrying her destination when it's over no floor (`ftCo_800B33B0` with the stage's `grLib_801C9E60`, where melee-hd had a copy of its own that stopped); Great Bay's and Hyrule Temple's routes (`ftCo_800A1CC4` with its table `ftCo_803C6594`, and `ftCo_800A21FC`), which send a computer player on the ground at a route's start to the route's next point by its destination's angle, for 300 frames; and Hyrule Temple's search for an island's end above and ahead when a ceiling is between it and its destination (`ftCo_800AAF48`). The same functions serve every computer kind. On Brinstar and Onett her item search also tested the stage items' kinds as monsters: `ftCo_800A648C`'s port compared the Pokémon's kinds where the machine code compares the containers' monsters and the adventure's Koopas, Like Like and Topi (`feat/cpu-switch-items`' fix, taken as it is).

The `ics` set's five stage matches were made as the other oracle matches are (ssbm-rs's `ssbm-run-rf`, `make-oracle.sh`, traced `--port all` into `local/traces/v5-mixed/oracle-ics-*.trc`, the recordings in `local/bench/oracle-ics/`). Each compares every tick with no difference and no stale read, `--fp hardware --heap`, to its end, and passes `sync-test --rollback 7`: `ics-br` (11,033 ticks; Brinstar's island unsafe 532 times), `ics-on` (11,033; Onett's 1,699), `ics-rc` (11,030; the wind carries her destination 76 times), `ics-gb` (8,446, a stock match's end; 111 routes taken) and `ics-ht` (11,033; 32 routes). Their replay checks show only the entry's first-tick height bit (two or four events), but for `ics-on`, where Nana's stick parts from the recording at frame 6,919 (680 events after): her DI with no knockback reads her `Fighter*`'s low byte, and the recording run lays the heap out otherwise than playback ([a recording run doesn't lay out the heap as playback does](#oracle-sets)), so playback plays its own match from there, which the trace is.

leaf-check's `nana` runs Nana's frame from seven more sets of saved calls of them (`local/calls/ics/`: `br-acid`, `on-car`, `rc-wind`, `rc-wind2`, `gb-route`, `ht-route`, `ht-route2`, 1,005 frames) against the machine code, as saved and with each change, and two changes reach what the matches don't: `ShrineAbove` (on Hyrule Temple's floor under its upper floors, apart from Popo, walking to the temple's roof: the search runs, 26 times) and `WindDrift` (on Rainbow Cruise, her destination 3.5 above where she stands, which the wind carries and her floor keeps: in the matches the wind's push is always overwritten the same frame, by her way to Popo or the destination's reset). Broken on purpose: the acid's 90 frames made 89 fails `ics-br` in tick 2,420 and its height test dropped fails it too; Onett's car flag ignored fails `ics-on` in tick 134 and the street's height made -5 fails it; the routes' timer (300), reach (5), facing, span, up and down angles, a route point (Great Bay's (-30, 34)), the destination island test, and `ftCo_800A3554` returning true after a route each fail `ics-gb` (all but the span, the angles and the point `ics-ht` too); the wind left off fails `WindDrift` in 37 of `rc-wind2`'s 62 frames (no match shows it). Not shown to fail, after two runs each: Onett's street height made 4 (no island end lies between 4 and 5) and Hyrule Temple's search's filters, its ceiling and wall tests, the side it looks ahead to and its reach (no state found has an end within reach, five half widths ahead and less than twice the jump's height above, from a place with a ceiling over it: the roof's ends are all behind or over 80 above).

### The Ice Climbers with items

The items audit's last gap was Nana's computer player with items ([the player](player.md#items-toggle-whats-left)). `feat/cpu-switch-items` ported the CPU AI's item paths every computer kind shares (going for an item, `ftCo_800A866C`, and picking it up, `ftCo_800A3710`; a swung item's attacks by its reach, `ftCo_800B52AC`; the containers' monsters as targets, `ftCo_800A648C`, `ftCo_800B5AB0`; cloaked enemies, `ftCo_800A0F00`, `ft_80087A18`), taken here as they are. What's left, a shooting item used from range (`it_8026B924` in `ftCo_800B9A04`), Nana never reaches: `ftCo_800B9CBC` uses a held item from range only for a computer player allowed ranged attacks (`CpuFighter::xF9_b5`), which her way back to Popo clears each frame (`ftCo_800B0760`), and following him she runs no behavior.

Two Ice Climbers matches with items compare every tick with no difference and no stale read, `--fp hardware --heap`, to their ends, and pass `sync-test --rollback 7`: `ics-items-bf` (Ice Climbers against Fox on Battlefield, `BENCH_AI`, every kind at high; 8,991 ticks, a stock match's end; Nana goes for items 1,094 frames but holds none), made by ssbm-rs's recording run, and `ics-items-open-fd` (two openers on Final Destination, every kind at very high, two minutes; 7,428 ticks), which melee-hd records (`RECORDER=melee-hd`) and ssbm-rs's replay check plays back with no divergence in 98,294 events: Nana goes for items 3,949 frames and holds a Home-Run Bat (685 frames, 237 apart from Popo, where her attack picks the bat's swings six times), a Ray Gun (1,573 frames, 96 apart from Popo), a motion-sensor bomb, a cloaking device, a Poké Ball and a capsule. `ics-items-bf`'s replay check parts from its recording at frame 1,507 (the seed, then positions; 733 events), as the other items matches' recording runs do ([seed searches](#oracle-sets), "melee-hd records them"); its trace is playback's own match.

leaf-check's `nana` runs two more sets of saved calls of `ics-items-open-fd` (`local/calls/ics/fd-items-bat`, 250 fields from 6,560: the bat held apart from Popo and its swings; `fd-items-gun`, 100 from 1,190: the Ray Gun held apart from Popo) against the machine code, as saved and with each change.

### Special Melee

Milestone 5's task 12(a): what VS mode's Special Melee modes change in the match's start data, played by generator matches ([the `special` set](#oracle-sets)) recorded and traced as milestone 4's are (`$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/bench/oracle-m5-special/`, `traces/v5-mixed/oracle-special-*.trc`). Each mode's VS entry (`gmVsMelee_EnterVs` with its callbacks, or Stamina Mode's `gm_801B931C`) only fills in start data that the match already reads: the player blocks' scale, attack and defense ratios and size class (Tiny and Giant Melee, `xB`, which picks the voices' pitch), starting and respawn damage (Super Sudden Death's 300%), invisibility (a permanent cloak, `x2226_b3`), and stamina's HP and HP display (`xC_b7`); the rules' single button, game speed, unpause hook and match start. Most of what reads them was ported with the match; this task ported the rest:

- **Stamina Mode's knockout** (`ft_0C8C.c`): `ftCo_800C8C84` after every hit's damage, `fn_800C8E74`, the fall once the hit ends (`ftCo_800C8D00`), the cry (`fn_800C8E40`), and `gm_80167470`, which tells the game mode (only Stamina Mode's, `gm_801B97C4`, does anything: a slowdown to 0.4 for 100 frames). The match start Stamina Mode sets (`fn_801B9850`) and its process (`fn_801B96E8`, `gm_801B9600`), which ends the match 100 frames after one player or team is left, with `gm_SetGameSpeed`, `gm_ResetGameSpeed` and `gm_8016B33C`.
- **Fixed-Camera Mode's unpause hook**, `gm_80165290`, which the match's start calls too.
- **The game's speed**: `gm_SetGameSpeed`, and the pad alarm set again at a faster game's shorter tick (`lb_80019628`'s `OSSetPeriodicAlarm`, [the engine notes](engine.md#state-the-render-pass-writes)), which Lightning Melee's 1.25 needs. melee-sim also takes a new speed up only at a round's start, as the original's pad check does.
- **The modes' VS entries** as data (`vs_mode::Special`), which the launcher starts its modes with ([the player](player.md#special-melee-the-player-offers)).

Slippi's playback runs every replay in the debug VS mode and clears the rules' callbacks from 0x40 on (the match start among them), so the oracle's matches check what the start data carries, not what only the mode's game mode does: Stamina Mode's match start and slowdown, and Super Sudden Death's music (`gm_8016B3A0`), run only in their modes. leaf-check checks those against ssbm-rs's ports instead (below), and task 12(b)'s sessions through the menus will play them. In playback a stamina fighter out of HP lies down and is never counted out, so `special-stamina-ps` plays on to its last recorded frame (30,887).

| Match | Ticks | Diff (`--fp hardware --heap`) | `sync-test --rollback 7` |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `special-tiny-bf` (Peach, Samus) | 7,429 | no difference, no stale read | 7,318 ticks, no difference |
| `special-giant-fd` (Captain Falcon, Pikachu) | 7,428 | no difference, no stale read | 7,318 ticks, no difference |
| `special-ssd-dl` (Sheik, Falco) | 7,425 | no difference, no stale read | 7,318 ticks, no difference |
| `special-invisible-ys` (Mario, Luigi) | 7,430 | no difference, no stale read | 7,318 ticks, no difference |
| `special-single-button-fod` (Link, Ness) | 7,429 | no difference, no stale read | 7,318 ticks, no difference |
| `special-fixed-camera-bf` (Jigglypuff, Ganondorf) | 7,430 | no difference, no stale read | 7,318 ticks, no difference |
| `special-slomo-fd` (Yoshi, Donkey Kong) | 14,756 | no difference, no stale read | 14,642 ticks, no difference |
| `special-lightning-ps` (Zelda, Bowser) | 7,404 | no difference, no stale read | 7,318 ticks, no difference |
| `special-mix-bf` (Mewtwo, Young Link) | 7,411 | no difference, no stale read | 7,318 ticks, no difference |
| `special-stamina-ps` (Fox, Marth) | 31,011 | no difference, no stale read | 31,005 ticks, no difference |
| `special-stamina-ko-fd` (Marth, Captain Falcon; Captain Falcon out of HP at frame 352, in the air) | 7,427 | no difference, no stale read | see below |

ssbm-rs's replay checks of the recordings find only what its other oracle matches find: a few positions' last bit in the entry before "GO", and post-frame "hitstun" (`+0x2c`, the misc action state word) on some frames ([Recordings](#recordings)); the controllers alone play every match again with no divergence but Slo-Mo Melee's, where `BENCH_PADS` doesn't reproduce the recording (31,671 events diverge; ssbm-rs's harness at half speed, not looked into). The trace is of the recording's playback, which the replay check finds whole.

**A slowed game's replay.** Two things only a slowed game's playback shows, both Slippi's codes, not Melee's. Its first tick runs no game logic, so the playback's frame count still holds what the recording code set at the match's start, the frame before the first (now modeled in `playback_start`), and `diff` finds the match's first tick by that too. And Slippi's playback cuts nearly every sound effect in a slowed game: its sound log (`Playback/Core/Sounds`) takes a tick whose game clock isn't due for the frame before played again with no sound, and stops what that frame started ([the engine notes](engine.md#rulesets-in-the-state)). melee-sim models the log in a replay's playback only (`playback::SoundLog`); the player's own matches keep their sounds. Every replay's diff compares the log, and Big Blue's check the frame a sound the scene's entry starts is logged for (its first cars' engines, at tick 1): the count the scene before leaves, which `Match::start_from` sets for a replay. The boot from melee-sim's own audio setup (`--boot-audio`, `drive_pace_from_the_boot`) checks that the playback scene's start sound, before the match's scene, isn't logged.

**leaf-check.** `special`'s `player_callbacks` and `rules_callbacks` run each mode's callback on random player blocks and start data (500 and 300 each) against ssbm-rs's, field for field; `stamina_entry` runs Stamina Mode's VS entry from random VS data in the save's VS mode table. `player`'s `gm_accessors` gains the game speed (`gm_SetGameSpeed`, `gm_ResetGameSpeed`), the ending kind (`gm_8016B33C`) and Stamina Mode's count of players left, knockout, process and `gm_80167470` in each game mode, on random statics over captured matches; `stamina_match_start` checks the match start's statics and the GObj and process it makes. `vs`'s `stage_music_flags` runs the music choice (`Ground_801C28AC`) with every flag set `Stage_80225074` passes, Super Sudden Death's included, from 16 captured match states.

**Mutations** (one a check, each in code only that check reaches). Caught: Tiny Melee's defense ratio 0.5 to 0.55, Lightning Melee's speed 1.25 to 1.2 and Stamina Mode's 150 HP to 149 (`special`'s three tests); the mode's process ending a frame early and a sub-fighter's knockout counted (`gm_accessors`), the process's priority 0x15 to 0x14 (`stamina_match_start`); Super Sudden Death's usual song marking itself the alternate (`stage_music_flags`); the fixed camera's hook leaving the standard camera (`special-fixed-camera-bf`, the camera's mode from the first tick); the faster tick's pad alarm set a period late (`special-lightning-ps`, the audio timeline); the sound log never stopping (`special-slomo-fd`, at tick 4); the scene's count of game ticks the playback scene leaves kept at 0 (the Big Blue diffs, at tick 1) and the sound codes' scene check dropped (`drive_pace_from_the_boot`'s diff, at tick 1); the stamina knockout leaving the fighter's `x2225_b6` unset, and its cry played from the character's sound word 0x8 rather than 0xC (`special-stamina-ko-fd`, each at the knockout's tick, 476). ssbm-rs's call log of that match (`CALLS=`) has `fn_800C8E74` from `ftCo_800C8C84` and the fall from `ftCo_800C8D00` at frame 352; the cry runs there as `fn_800C8E74`'s inline copy of `fn_800C8E40`, whose symbol only `ftCo_800C8D00`'s branch for a knockout without knockback calls. Missed, both equivalent where the checks run them:

- Super Sudden Death's usual song read from the stage's own song word (`x4`) instead of the single player's (`xC`): on Battlefield and Final Destination, the captured states' stages, the two words hold the same song (Battlefield's entry 0x51 and 0x51, its alternates 0x26; Final Destination's 0x4E and 0x4E, alternates 0x27; read from the stage parameters in the traces' memory). The mutation of the same branch's alternate flag is caught.
- The stamina knockout's color animation 0x7A to 0x79, in `special-stamina-ko-fd`: Captain Falcon's knockout comes with knockback, so it starts there inside the hit's damage, and later in the same hit the hit's flash (color animation 4, priority 100, 0x7A's priority too and above 0x79's 60) starts on the same overlay (`x408`) and replaces whichever started; the trace's `x408` holds 4 at the end of that tick (476). A knockout without knockback starts 0x7A after the hit (`ftCo_800C8D00`) and would keep it; no match has one.

## Adding a stage

The recipe the milestone's stage tasks follow, as Yoshi's Island N64 (`groldyoshi.c`, task 4) proved it end to end. A stage joins the roster once its oracle match diffs whole, and the player offers it once its audit finds nothing a match on it can reach unported ([Stages the player offers](player.md#stages-the-player-offers)).

**The oracle.** Each stage has its match in the `stages` set ([Oracle sets](#oracle-sets)): `$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/bench/oracle-m4/stage-NAME.slp`, its replay check (`stage-NAME.check.log`), its controllers check (`stage-NAME.pads.log`) and its trace (`$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/traces/v5-mixed/oracle-stage-NAME.trc`, `--fp hardware`). Use them only once `stage-NAME.times` is there (the campaign writes it last). The trace is of ssbm-rs's playback, which is what melee-hd matches; a divergence the replay check shows between the recording and the playback is the recording run's, not melee-hd's, but say what it is: so far they have been fighters' off-screen flags on a doubled round (below), the playback's and the recording run's rounds doubling at different ticks.

**The code.** In melee-sim (`game/gr`):

1. `mod.rs`: the stage's `GR_KIND_*` (the decompilation's `Gr_Kind_*`, `gr/forward.h`), a `Globals` field for its `yakumono_param` static, and a `vars_kind` line for each map whose `Ground::u` member its code reads.
2. `vars.rs`: each member as a struct (the decompilation's `gr/types.h`), with its `VarsKind` and `GroundVars` variants, its `import_as` arm, an accessor that turns fresh bytes into the member (a pointer field must be null there; `alloc_user_data_ground` gives a fresh block at stage creation) and its `compare` arm. Bit fields stay one word with accessors, the bits nothing writes kept.
3. `data.rs`: the `StageCallbacks` table (`grXx_StageCallbacks`: setup, `callback1`, process, destroy callback and flags, by map id) and the `StageData` (`grXx_StageData`: archive, setup, demo setup, load, start, `callback4`, `on_touch_line`, shadow check, `flags2`, the stage's own collision joints), with its arm in `stage_data`. Every stage's `callback1` and `callback4` are dead: nothing reads them (`ledger/dead.txt`).
4. `<stage>.rs` (`oldyoshi.rs` is the model): `run` (the processes and callbacks by address, empty ones in one aliased `empty`), `run_event` (setup, load, start), `create` through `setup::create_from_callbacks`, the setup, the processes, the shadow check and whatever the objects' collision joints call. The setup's frame (`create`'s last argument) is the stack address the scale joint's descriptor has in the original: `setup::SETUP_FRAME` for a setup as shallow as Yoshi's Story's; a deeper one (Dream Land's, Pokémon Stadium's transformations) shows up as an ID table difference at the first tick, whose stack key names the frame to use.
5. Wiring: `code::run_proc`, `code::run_event` and `code::run_demo_init`; the shadow check in `gr::display::stage_receives_shadow`; `on_touch_line` in `ft::attack::line_hazard` (every stage so far answers no hazard); a collision joint's callback (`mpJointSetCb1`) in `ft::coll::ObjCollHooks::joint_callback`, which has the stage objects (`grounds`) to write; the `yakumono_param` static's address (`ssbm_types::tu::melee__gr__grXX::yakumono_param`) imported and compared in `tools/capture/src/stage.rs`. Search for `GR_KIND_` and for the stage's name elsewhere: buffers its stage asks the preload cache for (`vs_match::preloaded_files`), the camera, the match's end, the items' frequency and the CPU's searches have per-stage switches.
6. Shared code the stage calls that no stage reached before: the stage objects' sounds (`Running::object_sound` and its siblings), `stage_info`'s settings and queries (`gr::ground`, `gr::stage`), the spawner's switches (`gr::zako`), `grLib_801C9EE8` (`grlib::point_near_object`) and `rand_range` (`grlib::rand_range`) and the bob-omb rain's starting point (`ground::bomb_rain_point`) are ported and checked. Stage objects with a camera of their own (`lb_80013B14`, `grDisplay_801C5F60`; Corneria and Venom), the generator configs (`grZakoGenerator_801CA394`, `801CA43C`; Corneria), the stage items' glue in `grmaterial.c` and the enemies and trophies the spawner makes are not: they come with the first stage that reaches them, and their state's shape with it.

**The checks.**

1. `melee-oracle diff TRACE stage-NAME.slp --fp hardware` to the trace's end with no difference and no stale read, then again with `--heap`. Where it stops at an unported function or a difference, port or fix and run again; never accept a difference.
2. `melee-oracle sync-test stage-NAME.slp --fp hardware --rollback 7` to the replay's end.
3. A probe run (a local `eprintln!` in each branch, not committed) shows which branches the match reaches, for the leaf check to vary the rest.
4. Captures for leaf-check from the same match: a function list (`NAME-funcs.txt`, `ADDR NAME` per line, the stage's functions with code and `Ground_801C0C2C` for running states), every other function's address (`done-NAME.txt`), and ssbm-rs's runner with `CAPTURE` into `$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/calls/stages/oracle-stage-NAME` (`tools/leaf-check/tests/stages.rs` has the command; Yoshi's Island N64's 69 calls took seven and a half minutes of one core and 359 MB, 17 MB once `pack-calls` packed them). Remove the `.call` files once `pack-calls --verify` passes.
5. A test per stage in `tools/leaf-check/tests/stages.rs`: each function from its saved calls, the processes also with their variables set to each branch's (`leaf_check::calls::check_calls_with`'s prepare), and the callbacks no capture reached run by address from another function's calls (`retarget`); pure functions also on random inputs (`leaf_check::each_mode`). Each port gets its line in `ledger/checks.txt` (or a `fns::NAME` call in the test).
6. Mutation-test every port: break one constant, comparison or branch at a time and see a check fail.
7. The roster line (`game::roster::STAGES`, in name order, by its `StKind`, Slippi's external stage ID); `melee-player`'s `every_roster_stage_plays` starts it from the player's settings and plays a minute. The line starts at `audit: None`, which the launcher and the command line don't offer. The audit comes next: the stage's units (its `gr*.c`, its stage items' and any fighter states only it starts), its modules, what of them no player's match reaches with why, and the gaps one does reach; melee-sim's `audited_stages_reach_nothing_unported` holds it to the port ledger and the modules' stops, and the table in [Stages the player offers](player.md#stages-the-player-offers) gets its row. Look beyond the stage's own code too: the CPU AI's stage switches (Nana's, with the Ice Climbers), devices that catch fighters, winds, the camera's stage objects.
8. The docs: the stage's paragraph under [Stages](#stages) with what its match ran and its replay check's divergences, Melee's bugs the port found ([Melee's own bugs](melee-bugs.md)), the milestone's task status, and `cargo run --release -p ledger -- --check`.
9. Frames: the oracle can't see a drawing difference, so the stage's match gets frames checks against ssbm-rs's (`tools/render-check/tests/documented.rs`, references in `$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/player/ref-stage-NAME`), at frames that show its moving parts, hazards and backgrounds, identical draw for draw ([the player](player.md#checking-frames)).

## Sync test

`melee-oracle sync-test REPLAY.slp [--rollback N] [--fp hardware|slippi] [--ticks N] [--draw-offscreen-fighters]` checks that melee-hd is rollback-ready ([the architecture](architecture.md#netplay-and-other-features)): restoring an earlier state and playing the ticks since again must reproduce the straight run bit for bit. It plays the replay from its settings, as `melee-bench native` does (its own boot, no trace), and keeps the state before each tick in a ring of snapshots (`clone_from` into the ring's storage). After each tick a second match restores the snapshot from N ticks before (`clone_from`, as a rollback restores) and plays those N ticks again with the same inputs, display passes included. Its whole state must then equal the straight run's, and each replayed tick's output (sounds, rumble, draw commands) the straight run's for that tick. The first difference stops the run with the tick, the replay frame and the field's path, for example `the state after tick 300, restored from before tick 294 and played again, differs from the straight run's at game.objs.jobjs.pool.slots[101].rotate.x: 0.16551262 (0x3e297c24) != 0.16551447 (0x3e297ca0)`. N is 1 by default, which plays every tick twice from the same state; Slippi's rollback window is 7 or 8 ticks. `--draw-offscreen-fighters` turns on that cosmetic toggle, as the player does in widescreen ([architecture](architecture.md#simulation-and-presentation)); the snapshots carry it with the rest of the state. A tick stopping at unported code stops the run like `diff`, with the number of ticks checked before it.

**What it compares.** `melee_sim::state_eq::StateEq`, which every type in the state derives (`melee_derive::StateEq`): the whole `Game`, field by field, floats by bit pattern (`state_diffs` lists every difference, where the sync test stops at the first). Every pool slot is compared, freed ones too, since a freed slot's contents are what `alloc_stale` hands out next; vectors are compared by length and elements, maps by their entries in any order. That takes in the allocator model (`hsd::heap`, which a step borrows from the state and gives back), the random seed, the audio driver, synth, AX voices, DSP and timeline, the effects, items and stage objects, the display pass's statics, the pad library and playback's state. Only fields marked `#[clone_from(keep)]` are left out: spare storage a restore leaves as the target had it, namely the mix log (`Audio::mix`, presentation output), the TEV compile's scratch lists (`MObjs::texp_scratch`) and freed shapes' envelope lists kept for reuse (`PObjs::spare_lists`, `spare_sets`). Anything the simulation read from outside the snapshot would show up as a difference, since the replayed ticks would find what the straight run left there. Outside `Game` a tick reads only:

- the archives (`Match::data`), loaded data a tick borrows shared (`Running`), so it can't change them;
- the float mode and the multiply-add path (`gekko_fp`), per-thread settings fixed for a match;
- the memo tables of `HSD_MtxSRT`, `sinf` and `cosf` ([performance](performance.md#speed-work)), keyed by every bit of their inputs and the float mode, which return what the call would compute;
- its inputs: the controllers, or a replay's frames (`melee_slippi::Device` holds nothing of its own).

The allocator's operation log (`--heap-log`) and the `stubs` feature's stand-ins are thread-locals only the checks set, and the simulation never reads the log.

`--perturb TICK` flips the lowest bit of the first fighter's x in the restored state before the rollback that ends at that tick, which the run must catch: a check of the check. (The random seed wouldn't do: Slippi's playback restores it every tick.) `tools/oracle/tests/sync.rs` runs the first 1,800 ticks of the Fox ditto benchmark match with `--rollback 7`, and again with that perturbation, with only `SSBM_DISC`. It also runs the first 1,800 ticks of one replay per ported character and tournament stage with `--rollback 7`: the other four benchmark matches, and twelve ranked replays in `$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/replays/hf` (skipped without it). The 18 tests take about 130 CPU-seconds: 26–45 s at four test threads, depending on load. The whole matches run with the diffs, in the verify script that checks each merge: one `sync-test --rollback 7` job per replay the diffs play, plus the replays below that no diff plays. Each job takes about a tenth of its replay's diff.

**Results (2026-10-16).** With `--rollback 7`, each replay in the oracle's verify list (the diffs' 83 replays) and each replay this page names was played to its end: 128 replays, 1,543,058 ticks checked. 127 compared with no difference:

- the six benchmark matches (`data/bench`, `--fp hardware`), Ice Climbers–Peach now among them, and the six CPU oracle matches for Ness and Samus (`local/bench/oracle-chars2`, `--fp hardware`);
- 18 of Sarah's Slippi replays (`--fp slippi`), the four-player Final Destination match among them;
- 97 ranked replays the oracle traces (`--fp slippi`), covering every character on main: Mewtwo (six), Mr. Game & Watch (six), Donkey Kong (eight), Bowser (six), the Ice Climbers (22, with their 3.16, UCF 0.8 and frozen-Stadium replays), Young Link, Peach, Yoshi, Link, Roy, Dr. Mario, Mario, Luigi, Pikachu, Pichu, Ganondorf and the others.

One stops at unported code: `hf-roy-samus-fd-56052455` reaches Samus's unported `it_802B7160` in tick 6340, with no difference in the 6,334 ticks checked before it (see the stops above). That covers every ported character and every tournament stage. Nothing differed, so the sweep needed no fixes. Each replay took 17–114 s, about 40 s on average, three at a time on a busy machine: 84 minutes of CPU for all 128. With `--rollback 1` the Fox ditto and Fox–Marth Final Destination matches compare clean too. A rollback of 7 costs eight ticks per tick.

What it turned up: fields marked `#[clone_from(if_changed)]` (rarely changing containers, among them the text system's blocks, texts, canvases and buffers) were restored only when they compared unequal with `PartialEq`, which takes 0.0 and -0.0 for equal, so a restore could leave a text's position or size holding the other zero's sign. They now compare bit for bit (`StateEq`), and a unit test restores 0.0 over -0.0. No replay had reached it. Nothing else differed: the state holds everything a tick reads.

## Golden hashes

A whole-replay diff spends almost all its time on the reference, not on melee-hd: on Rainbow Cruise's stage match (`stage-rainbow-cruise --heap`, 28,991 ticks, `--times`), finding the archives in the trace's memory each tick (`Files::refresh`) took 48% of the run, the comparison 40% (the allocator model's 1.3% of it), reading and decompressing the trace 3%, and the simulation 6% (`--times` prints the split). The archive scan now goes over only the pages a tick changed (below), which took it from 164 s to 11 s of that run. Once a replay's diff has passed, a golden keeps what that run proved, so later runs only simulate and hash.

**Writing.** `melee-oracle diff TRACE REPLAY.slp ... --write-golden` runs the full diff and, if it passes clean (to the trace's end, the match's end or `--ticks`, with no difference and no stop), writes the run's golden: after each compared tick, a 64-bit hash of melee-hd's state. A run with stale reads still writes one, keeping its stale-read report. The file goes to `$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/golden/TRACE.FP[.heap].golden` (the trace's file name without its extension, the float mode, `.heap` with `--heap`: one file per verify job), or where `--golden FILE` says; it is written whole and renamed over the old one, so an interrupted run leaves the old golden.

**Checking.** `... --golden-check` reads the trace only up to the match's first tick, for the boot state the match starts from (the first tick or two), plays the match, and compares each tick's hash with the golden's; nothing is compared with memory, and the disc's archive index isn't built. It exits 0 when every tick matches, printing `N ticks match the golden` and the stale reads the golden was written with; 1 at the first tick whose hash differs (with the tick and its replay frame), or where the match stops at unported code, or ends at another tick than it did; 3 when there is no golden or it is stale, saying why. On anything but 0, run the full diff, which names the field (and rewrites the golden with `--write-golden` when it passes).

**What a hash covers.** `StateEq::state_hash`, which `#[derive(StateEq)]` generates beside the sync test's comparison: every value the sync test compares, in its order, floats by bit pattern, a map's entries in any order, spare storage (`#[clone_from(keep)]`) left out. That is the whole `Game`, but for the allocator model (`hsd::heap`) without `--heap`, as the comparer takes it in only then, and the archives the match's start loaded by name. A value unknown until written hashes as the unknown it is in melee-hd's state (an item variable not yet written, `it::Var`; a word held as `None` until written), so the golden masks what the comparer masks. The hash is a superset of what the comparer reads: it also takes freed pool slots and bookkeeping the original keeps nowhere. So a golden can miss on a change the full diff would pass (the full diff then runs and refreshes it), but can't pass a state the full diff would fail: equal hashes mean the state the comparer compared is, field for field, the clean run's. The hash feeds each word through `(h.rotl(5) ^ w) * K`, one-to-one in each word, so any one changed word changes it; two changes cancelling each other is a 64-bit chance.

**Stale goldens.** The header names what the golden was written for, and a check refuses a golden when any of it differs:

| Line | What |
| --- | --- |
| `melee-oracle golden 1` | the format's version |
| `trace-size`, `trace-hash` | the trace's size and a hash of all its bytes (it also holds the boot state) |
| `replay-size`, `replay-hash` | the replay's |
| `options` | what changes what runs or what's compared: `--fp`, `--disc-speed`, `--heap`, `--boot-audio`, `--ticks`, `--stand-in`, `--ignore` (`--from` and `--against` can't write or check a golden) |
| `coverage` | a hash of the comparer's sources, which `tools/oracle/build.rs` takes at build time: `melee-capture`, the mirrored structs' comparisons (`game/mirror.rs`, `mirror_gen.rs`), `diff.rs`, `golden.rs`, `state_eq.rs` and the derive |
| `disc` | the disc files the match's start loaded: names, entry numbers and bytes |
| `first-tick` | the trace tick that ran the match's first tick |

The trace and replay paths are kept for people and not compared; `end` (trace, match or limit) and the `stale` lines say how the clean run ended and what it read stale.

**What a golden doesn't prove.** It proves the state is the one a full diff passed with the comparer of its coverage. A field the comparer starts checking later isn't covered until a full run: any change to the comparer's sources makes every golden stale, so the next verify runs every full diff. A change to melee-hd's state that the comparer can't see (a freed slot's bytes, a new field of bookkeeping) makes a golden miss and the full diff run, though it would pass. The check's stale reads are the golden's: the counters (`Objects::stale_reads`, `CollStatics::stale_reads`) and the items' and Pokémon's stale-read marks are in the hashed state, so equal hashes mean the same stale reads; their text is the one the full diff printed. A periodic full verify (the diffs with `--write-golden`) stays the backstop, and refreshes every golden.

**Results (2026-10-20).** Goldens written and checked on three replays of different kinds, two runs at a time on a machine other agents were also using (an earlier, busier round took 865, 127 and 540 s for the full diffs and 76, 10 and 20 s for the checks):

| Replay | Ticks | Full diff with `--write-golden` | `--golden-check` (simulation, hash) | Speed-up |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `stage-rainbow-cruise`, `--fp hardware --heap` | 28,991 | 418 s | 28 s (13 s, 12 s) | 15x |
| `items-ported-fd`, `--fp hardware --heap` | 7,427 | 121 s | 6 s (2.4 s, 2.7 s) | 20x |
| `Game_20260129T214824` (Slippi, Pokémon Stadium), `--fp slippi` | 14,212 | 213 s | 11 s (4.4 s, 5.6 s) | 19x |

Two one-value bugs, each a local edit built and reverted: a heart's heal one more (`itHeart_Logic8_Spawned`) fails the items match's check at tick 1744 (replay frame 1620) after 8 s, where the full diff stops at the same tick on `item.xDD4_itemVar.heart.xDD4_heal: ours 101, theirs 100`; Rainbow Cruise's planks falling one ULP faster fails its check at tick 11500 (frame 11376) after 41 s, where the full diff stops at the same tick on the plank's speed in `ground.u.rcruise.entries[7]` (`bcf5c290` against `bcf5c28f`). A check refuses (exit 3) a trace with one bit flipped (same size, its hash differs), the run without `--heap`, with `--fp slippi` or `--ticks 100`, another replay, an oracle built from other comparer sources, and a missing golden. `tools/oracle/tests/diff.rs` writes and checks a 300-tick golden of the Fox vs Sheik replay and sees other options make it stale.

**The archive scan.** The comparer pairs pointers into loaded files through the archives found in the trace's memory: a walk over memory at 32-byte steps that, where a disc file's first three header words are, picks the candidate file memory agrees with on most words (`best_match`) and goes on past its end. Scanning all 24 MB again every tick was half a full diff. `melee_capture::Rescan` keeps the walk's work between ticks, and the trace's image says which 4 KiB pages its ticks changed (`Image::take_changed`): a bit per step says whether a disc file's header is there (recomputed only in changed pages), and each header the walk met keeps each candidate's agreement counts page by page, so a changed page is counted again only within a candidate's span, and a header that changed starts over. The walk itself is the full scan's, passing over the steps with no header at once, and the pick at each header is made by the same code from the same counts (`choose`), so the archives found, their order and the archive ids handed out are a full scan's. `melee-capture`'s `rescan_agrees_with_a_full_scan` checks it over 3,000 random changes to memory (files loaded over each other, worn, their headers cleared, pages rewritten, `prefer` changing), each site's counts against a fresh count; broken on purpose (a changed page not counted again, a site kept when its header changed, a page's first or last word left out) it fails. `diff --check-rescan N` scans all of memory too every Nth tick and stops at the first tick the two differ.

| Replay (full diff, `--times`) | Ticks | Archives before | after | Whole diff before | after |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `stage-rainbow-cruise`, `--fp hardware --heap` | 28,991 | 164.4 s | 10.7 s | 524 s | 368 s |
| `items-ported-fd`, `--fp hardware --heap` | 7,427 | 37.7 s | 1.9 s | 103 s | 68 s |
| `Game_20260129T214824` (Slippi, Pokémon Stadium), `--fp slippi` | 14,212 | 78.4 s | 3.6 s | 206 s | 136 s |

(2026-10-21, two diffs at a time on a machine other agents were using; the comparison's own time varies with the load.) Each run's output is identical to the full scan's, and with `--check-rescan 1` the rescan agreed with a full scan at every tick of the three and of `cpu-bowser-ps` (`--heap`). The comparison is now most of a full diff.

**Hashing's cost.** About 0.4 ms a tick, as much as the simulation's own: the HSD objects' pools are most of it (the materials 0.13 ms), every slot hashed, freed ones too.

**The sync test.** On Rainbow Cruise's match with `--rollback 7` (28,918 ticks checked, 113 s), playing the seven ticks again took 70% of the run, the straight run 12%, the snapshots and restores 9% and the state comparison 9%: hashes would replace a pass over two states with a pass over each, the same work, so the sync test compares as before. `sync-test` prints the split (`times:`).

## Recordings

`melee-oracle record REPLAY.slp OUT.slp [--fp hardware|slippi] [--known FILE]` plays a replay in melee-hd with the recorder on ([the player](player.md#recording)), writes its recording, and compares it with the replay: the frames' events through `ssbm_slippi::compare` (pre-frame, post-frame, item, frame start, bookend and game end, keyed by frame, port and spawn ID), the game start field by field, and the code list. With `--known` it takes ssbm-rs's original-code baseline for the replay (`local/replays/known-names0/NAME.txt`: melee-hd's heap is laid out as a names-off run's), and the recording must diverge from the replay in exactly those lines, no more and no fewer.

**Results (2026-10-20).**

- **Benchmark matches** (`data/bench`, `--fp hardware`, as they were recorded): `fox-fox-fd` (95,826 events), `fox-marth-bf` (91,239), `fox-marth-fd` (92,122), `fox-marth-ps` (82,093) and `ganon-captain-fd` (72,403) record every compared event equal, nothing missing, the game end included. `ics-peach-ps` is equal through frame 10,136; from frame 10,137 Nana's CPU DI reads a byte of her `Fighter`'s address, which the recording run laid out differently from playback's layout, the one melee-hd plays a replay in ([performance](performance.md)): the simulation's known difference of start, not the recorder's.
- **Slippi replays** (`SLIPPI_REPLAYS`, `--fp slippi`): 21 replays spread over the six month folders, Slippi 3.19.0 and 3.19.1, two to four players. Two (2026-09-15's) stop at the match's start in `lb/audio.rs`, as `sync-test` does without the recorder: content not ported yet. The other 19 play to their end, 1,606,484 events compared: nine record every event equal, nine diverge from their replay in exactly the original code's baseline lines (5, 2, 1, 139, 4, 14, 4, 5 and 17 of them, among them heap addresses in the "hitstun" word, which the allocator model gives exactly), and one with no baseline yet (`Game_20261008T213605`) diverges in 15 lines of the baselines' kinds: the entry frames' off-screen flag (frames -122 to -120) and the last bit of position y as fighters enter (frames -113 to -102).
- **A recording recorded again.** melee-hd's recordings of `fox-marth-ps`, `ics-peach-ps`, `Game_20260531T180326` and `Game_20261008T213605`, played back with the recorder on, record every event the same, byte for byte, the game start included. A match of the player's own does too, but for its game start's scene and the pre-frame physical inputs (`crates/melee-player/src/record_tests.rs`, [the player](player.md#recording)); `melee-oracle record` on it compares 22,345 events, no divergence.

**What differs, and why.** Every field the comparisons find different, or leave out:

- `ssbm_slippi::compare`'s own exclusions: the pre-frame physical buttons and triggers (playback injects the processed inputs and polls controllers at rest), the items' misc bytes (heap leftovers in most items; melee-hd writes those of the variables it keeps as types, a missile's kind, a turnip's face, a charge shot's launch and power, and zero for others), the bookend's latest finalized frame (rollback bookkeeping; melee-hd writes the frame), and the LRAS initiator of a no contest.
- The game start's version, on 3.19.0 replays: melee-hd writes 3.19.1.
- The game start's scene: a replay's own (2.8 online, 2.2 from the VS mode), against Slippi playback's (14.1), the scene a replay's match runs in; a match of the player's own writes 2.2.
- The display names, connect codes, Slippi UIDs, session ID and game number: online only, which the codes write as zeros outside Slippi's online scene.
- The UCF toggles, on the benchmark recordings only: 3.19's codes write 1 whatever runs, and those matches' code lists (ssbm-rs's recording setup) run no UCF; melee-hd writes whether its rules run UCF, 1 on every Slippi replay checked.
- The metadata: `startAt` (when the recording was made), `names` (online names; melee-hd's are empty), `playedOn`, and the characters' frame counts on online replays with rollbacks, where Dolphin counted every post-frame update it received, resent frames included (equal on a replay without rollbacks, `Game_20260531T183349`). `lastFrame` is equal on every replay; ssbm-rs's benchmark recordings have `playedOn` alone.

**Slippi's parser.** `@slippi/slippi-js` 9.1.3 (Slippi's JavaScript SDK, installed outside the repository for the check, with Sarah's approval) reads every recording above and the scripted match of the player's own without an error: settings (stage, characters, ports, costumes, stocks, timer, seed, toggles), the code list, metadata, every frame's pre- and post-frame updates and items, the game end, its winners and its stats. Compared with its reading of the originals, the stage, characters, ports, frame counts, last frame, game end method and placements, stats (stocks, conversions, combos and their damage, per-player damage, kills, L-cancels, rolls, grabs, ledge grabs) and every item by frame and spawn ID are equal on every replay but `ics-peach-ps` (whose play parts at frame 10,137, above). What differs is what the comparisons above explain (version, scene, UCF toggles on the benchmarks, the metadata), and three things of slippi-js's own reading: its input counts (from the physical buttons, which playback doesn't reproduce), its item and character counts on online replays (it counts a frame resent after a rollback twice, which a recording without rollback can't), and, on the three replays whose game ended with L+R+A+Start, its winner, which it takes as the player who didn't quit: the online code that records who quit doesn't run in playback, so the codes write the pause byte's 0 where the original has the quitter. It also found that a replay's match, which playback ends when its inputs run out, ran that tick on after the game end: the recording now stops at the game end, which slippi-js reads as the stream's last event, as Slippi Dolphin's file writer leaves it.

The payload sizes and every split message of the code list are equal byte for byte (`Game_20261008T220403`, `Game_20260129T130802`, `fox-marth-bf`), the last message's bytes past its block included, which the codes' reused buffer keeps from the block before.

## Sessions

A replay starts at a match, so the boot, the menus and the modes have no replay to diff against. Their oracle is a session: every controller read from power-on, with the console's setup. `melee-oracle session TRACE INPUTS` boots melee-hd from power-on with a recorded session's reads and compares the state after every engine tick with ssbm-rs's trace of the same recording, stopping at the first difference or unported function as `diff` does, through the same trace reader, comparer and stale-read checks (`tools/oracle/src/session.rs`, melee-sim's `game::session`).

**Recording.** ssbm-rs's runner records a session with `--record FILE` (with `--card CARD`, the card as the session found it is kept beside it as `FILE.card`) and plays it back headless with `--inputs FILE` on a copy of that card, in virtual time, so the game reads the same things in the same order at any pace (`tools/run/src/inputs.rs` in ssbm-rs). The file is `SSBMINP1`: a version (1 or 2), the disc rate in bytes a second (0 for Slippi's fast speed), flags (1 the DSP mixed sound, 2 a card beside it); version 2 (ssbm-rs's `63b9293`, 2026-10-23) then adds the console's settings the boot reads: the clock at power-on (u64, time base ticks from 2000-01-01), the VI's DTV status (u8), and the SRAM's language and flags (u8 each), where version 1 has ssbm-rs's defaults (0, 0, 0 and 0x2C: 2000-01-01, no component cable, English, stereo); then a byte per `PADRead`: 0 for the controllers of the read before, or 1 and the four controllers (11 bytes each). ssbm-rs's `POWER_ON=SECONDS`, `DTV=1`, `SRAM_LANGUAGE=N` and `SRAM_FLAGS=N` set them for a recording (and over a playback's). Thirteen recordings are in `$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/lockstep/recordings`, all at a drive's 3 MB/s with sound and a card ([milestone 5](milestone-5.md#sessions)).

**Tracing.** From ssbm-rs's checkout, with memory from the first tick and the modes log:

```text
MODES=1 STATE_TRACE=local/traces/session/2026-10-06_17-44-10.trc STATE_TRACE_MEMORY=1 \
  target/alt/release/ssbm-run-rf.exe "$SSBM_DISC" --inputs local/lockstep/recordings/2026-10-06_17-44-10.inputs --port all
```

The run stops a second after the recording's last read, reporting that read meanwhile. The 73-second session `2026-10-06_17-44-10` (4,410 reads) took a minute and a half and made a 132 MB trace of 4,000 ticks; all thirteen took about four hours one at a time on a busy machine (the longest, 16.6 minutes of play, 25 minutes and 2.3 GB), with `MODES=1`'s log of the modes beside each trace (`NAME.log`).

**Comparing.** `melee-oracle session TRACE INPUTS [--card FILE] [--card-pull F[,G]] [--from TICK] [--ticks N] [--all] [--ignore TEXT,...] [--stand-in ADDR[=VALUE]] [--scenes] [--mutate scene-ticks|seed|power-on|read=N]`. `--scenes` lists the scenes the trace runs (game mode, state and frame function, from `state_machine` and the scene table), each with the read its first tick ended on, and stops: the modes log on melee-hd's side.

**What it seeds.** Nothing of the game: power-on is `Session::new`, a new state with `DbLevel` at the executable's `DbLKind_NoDebugRom`. The console's setup is explicit (`session::Setup`): the disc's pace from the recording's rate (0 fast, 3,000,000 a drive's; another rate is refused), the DSP model from its sound flag, the card's raw image (`FILE.card`, or `--card`; the native save reads and writes it, `lb::save`), when the card is pulled out and put back (`--card-pull F,G`, the video fields of ssbm-rs's `CARD_REMOVE=F,G`, which the recording doesn't keep), and the console's settings the boot reads, from the recording (version 1's defaults otherwise): the real-time clock at power-on (`Setup::power_on`), the DTV status (`Setup::dtv`) and the SRAM's flags (`Setup::sram_flags`: stereo for `OSGetSoundMode`, the synth's mode, and progressive scan for `OSGetProgressiveMode`). The SRAM's language isn't an input: Melee never reads it (its language is the save's). The clock is what ssbm-rs's is, an offset only the code's reads of the time base add (its `Regs::tb_power_on`): the timeline, the trace's time base and every hardware time still count from power-on, so a clock moves no disc read or retrace, and what the code computes from `OSGetTime` adds it (the boot's seed from `OSGetTick`, `lbTime_GetTimeInSeconds`, `__EXIProbe`'s tenths, the card library's stamps and the pad alarm's grid, `InsertAlarm`'s multiples of the period on the code's time base: `audio::clock::Clock::tb_power_on`). The alignment takes it off the alarm's time in memory, and `--from` off the times the importer reads (`import_clock_on`). The clock reaches gameplay (the boot's seed, the title's draws), so it can't be the host's. The controllers are the recording's reads, handed to the game in order as it reads them (`session::Console`): read 0 to the boot's `db_GetGameLaunchButtonState`, each later one to a pad alarm poll (`HSD_PadRenewRawStatus`, which the timeline queues: `scene::step_session_tick`); past the recording's end the last read repeats, as ssbm-rs's playback does.

**Aligning the ticks with the reads.** Before anything runs, the harness finds which reads each trace tick took, from the trace alone. The pad alarm fires every 675,000 time base ticks on a fixed grid, and each tick's memory says when it fires next, so between two ticks' ends it fired as often as the grid passed. It read as often or less: a firing handled late, with interrupts held off through a load, reads once for all the periods it missed (by tick 2 of the session the grid had passed 125 firings, and the game had made 108 alarm reads). And the newest poll in the pad library's queue is the last read taken. Each tick's last read is so bound to a range and to the reads equal to that poll; run forward over the trace and back, the constraints leave each tick the reads it can have ended on (`session::Plan`). A tick whose poll no read it can have taken equals refuses the recording, naming the tick, the range and the reads. On `2026-10-06_17-44-10` every tick's read is known exactly but tick 0, the memory card scene's first, which ended on one of 21 reads (71 to 91) the controllers sat still through, and tick 1. All thirteen recorded sessions, traced on 2026-10-23 into `$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/traces/session/` (8.6 GB, 246,582 reads, [milestone 5](milestone-5.md#sessions)), align with their traces the same way: every tick's read is known but ticks 0 and 1 and, on two of them, the last few hundred ticks, where the controllers sit still to the trace's end (`--scenes` names the open stretches). During a run, a tick that ends on another read than the trace's can have taken differs (`session reads`), besides the pad library's comparison.

**What it compares.** From power-on, `Session::boot` runs `main` as far as it's ported and stops at the first unported function. Until the boot reaches the first scene's loop, what it compares is what the boot's ported part left that nothing after it writes, against trace tick 0's memory: `db_gameLaunchButtonState` and `DbLevel`. Once it reaches the loop, each tick compares as `diff` compares a match's: the whole state through `melee-capture`'s comparer, the stale reads, the dangling pointers, and the reads each tick took; from power-on also the game's data and the menus' state (`melee-capture`'s `menus`: `gmMainLib`'s data and save, the mode and scene machine, the memory card scene's windows, `lbcardgame`, `lbcardnew`'s results, the preload cache with `lbMemory`'s and `lbHeap`'s heaps, the modes' statics), and the tick's end on the time base against the trace's (`timeline`). With `--heap` the allocator model compares too. With `--from TICK` the harness takes up the trace's state at a tick's end through the importer (`diff --from`'s, shared), compares the import back with memory, and plays on with the reads from the one after the tick's last, which the alignment must know exactly; a scene's end stops the run, since the change of scenes (`gm_801A4014`) isn't ported. `--mutate` changes melee-hd's run on purpose, to see the comparison catch it: `scene-ticks` (the scene loop's tick count one more at the start), `seed` (the random seed one more), `power-on` (melee-hd's console at 2000-01-01 whatever the recording's clock) or `read=N` (read N with A toggled on its first controller, for melee-hd only).

**Results (2026-10-23, the session `2026-10-06_17-44-10`).** The trace runs the memory card scene from tick 0, the opening movie from tick 2 (skipped with Start), the title from tick 204 (Start at read 394, tick 272), the main menu from 273, the character select from 357 and the main menu again from 710, then Tournament mode's setup and bracket (from 777, 2071, 2298 and 2962) and its match from tick 3245 to the trace's end.

- **Power-on** reads read 0 into the launch buttons (none held) and sets `DbLevel` to retail's 0 from the disc's files (no `/develop.ini`), both equal to trace tick 0's, runs `main` (milestone 5's task 4) and the memory card scene's two ticks (the save loaded on the native save, the Pikmin check, the scene's end), then the opening movie (milestone 5's task 5: the scene, `lbMthp`'s player with its alarm and frame reads, skipped with Start) and the title (from tick 204: its models, the emblem, the clock's draws, the attract demo's pick, Start at tick 272), and the main menu from tick 273 (milestone 5's task 6: the save written on the game's waits, the main list, the character select's entry at tick 357): `357 ticks compared, no difference; stopped in tick 357: game mode 0x2's loading isn't ported`. Until task 6 it stopped at the main menu's mode (`273 ticks compared`), as all thirteen recorded sessions did, each at its first tick in the main menu (63 to 877 ticks), with `--heap` too, timeline included; eight now run through the menu ([menu sessions](#sessions), below): every disc read, movie frame read and audio frame lands at ssbm-rs's time base (until 2026-10-23 the movie's read times were 16 off where a frame's read waited behind a music read, settled with ssbm-rs's `DVD_TIMELINE` probe, [the engine notes](engine.md#audio)).
- **The idle sessions** (`$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/lockstep/idle-sessions`, nothing pressed after the memory card scene): `idle-movie` plays the whole opening movie (ticks 2 to 6132, its mark at the movie's 454th tick and the logo's flash at its 950th) and `idle-title` the movie skipped and the title idle to its attract loop (ticks 177 to 797); both compare clean to the attract match's first tick, timeline and `--heap` included. Until task 6 they stopped there (`onEnterVs isn't ported`); now the attract match's scene starts (`onEnterVs`, the attract camera's process), and its first tick (6133 and 798) differs in the fields its setup leaves to the heap blocks the menus' scenes used (below, the menu sessions). The card session `damaged` goes on into the movie and stops there too (1,098 ticks). Version 2's `clock` and `clock-late` (the clock at 2025-01-01, the title's draws by its seconds) run clean through the movie and the title to the attract match (898 ticks each, whose first tick differs as the idle sessions' do). `progressive` answers the prompt (yes, the switch at tick 311: `OSSetProgressiveMode`'s SRAM write, 288 ticks), then runs the memory card scene, the movie and the title, clean to the trace's end (1,426 ticks, `--heap`). Until 2026-10-23 the memory card scene differed from its first tick: the comparer's scan took the prompt's freed `EfCoData.dat`, whose header was still in memory, as loaded, so the scene's files loaded over its range weren't found and their pointers didn't map (now a file in an OS heap's arena but in none of its allocated cells claims nothing: `melee_capture::Freed`); the load's last seven blocks each took the old fitted block's 24 ticks too many, with no event to realign them, so the tick ended 168 late ([saves](saves.md#the-native-save-on-the-timeline)); and the movie's first tick ended 16 late, from a time read melee-sim took setting the movie's alarm, where ssbm-rs's alarms are stand-ins that read no clock (`OSSetPeriodicAlarm`). By hand, the scan without the heaps' cells makes `progressive` differ in tick 711 again (the scene's models' pointers), and the alarm's time read put back in tick 713 (16 late).
- **The match, taken up from tick 3300** (on read 3770), compares back with no difference and plays 463 ticks with the players' reads with no difference and no stale read, every tick's end on the time base included, to tick 3764; taken up from 3250, 3450, 3530, 3540 or 3700 it runs to the same tick. Until 2026-10-23 every tick of the match ended 56 time base ticks (seven register reads) before the trace's: on a console with a card the pad alarm's handler probes the card slots, which took no time in melee-sim ([the engine notes](engine.md#audio)), and a match's ticks start right after it (the replay checks never compared a tick's end, and a replay's console has no card). So tick 3535's audio frame, due in that window, ran inside the tick in ssbm-rs and after it in melee-sim, and the match's runs ignored the timeline (`--ignore timeline`); taken up from 3530, the tick after also slept for a retrace it never waits for (the frame buffers' import, [the engine notes](engine.md#audio)). At tick 3764 Link's hookshot's new links (the second player's, read before as Samus's grapple beam) hold the menus' leftover bytes in fields their setup doesn't write (`coll_data.contact`, `prev_env_flags`, `x13C`, `lstick_x`): the import brings in the items' next blocks with what the heap holds (Debug VS heaps, above) but not the links' (Known limits, below). His parts table's entry 139, past his bones, of which the hookshot's spawn writes only the tip's model, comes in with the block's bytes before his first hookshot (until 2026-10-23 it came in only for Samus, once a beam had set it; now for Link and Young Link too, always). A run from power-on will need the heap's contents the menus leave, which is milestone 5's to model.
- **Mutations.** `--mutate read=0` differs from tick 0 (`db_gameLaunchButtonState: ours 0x0100, theirs 0x0000`); from tick 3300, `scene-ticks` differs in tick 3301 (`gm_80479D58.unk_0: ours 58, theirs 57`), `seed` in tick 3301 in 30 places (`HSD_RandSeedPtr` first, then the particles drawn from it), and `read=3780` in tick 3310, the tick that took read 3780 (the fighter's held buttons, the pad statuses and the button maps); `read=3801` in tick 3331. A copy of the recording with read 394 changed (A added to port 1's Start read) is refused: `trace tick 272 queued a poll that no read it can have taken is (reads 383 to 394)`. From power-on, `seed` differs in tick 0 (`HSD_RandSeedPtr`) and `scene-ticks` in tick 0 (`gm_80479D58.unk_0: ours 2, theirs 1`). `tools/oracle/tests/session.rs` runs each. The timeline's fixes perturbed back (2026-10-23, by hand): the alarm's card probes taking no time make every card session's tick 2 end 56 ticks early and the taken-up match's tick 3301; the audio DMA block due as the retrace handler ends waiting for the wake's reads makes `nocard` differ in tick 444 by 8; the frame buffers imported as the replay boot's makes the match taken up from 3530 differ in tick 3531 by 10,776.

**Card sessions (2026-10-23).** Six sessions recorded from power-on for the memory card scene's answers to a card, at a drive's pace with sound, each 1,200 fields with nothing pressed but A at field 600 (`$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/lockstep/card-sessions`, traced into `traces/card-sessions` with `MODES=1` and `CARD_STEPS=1`):

| Session | Card | What the scene does | melee-oracle session |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `nofile` | formatted, no save (`f10pmcard1.raw`) | the failed load resets the save (its first trophy drawn), asks to create one; A creates it | 513 ticks clean, stops at the save's creation (`lb_8001C8BC`) |
| `full` | another file leaves 8 blocks (`rf-run-full2.raw`) | too little room; A goes on without saving | clean to the trace's end (1,173 ticks) |
| `broken` | garbage directories (`faults/garbage.raw`) | asks to format; A on no goes on without saving | clean to the trace's end (1,173 ticks) |
| `nocard` | none | no card; A goes on without saving | clean to the trace's end (1,190 ticks) |
| `damaged` | name tag bank 8 failing its checksum (`rf-clv8e.raw`) | loads past the damage (the bank reset) and goes on to the opening movie | clean through the whole movie to the attract match (1,098 ticks) |
| `removed` | no save, pulled at field 300 and put back at 450 (`CARD_REMOVE=300,450`, nothing pressed, 900 fields) | asks to create the save, says there's no card while it's out, asks again once it's settled | clean to the trace's end (872 ticks, `--card-pull 300,450`) |

Every run compares the timeline exactly, with `--heap`. Until 2026-10-23 three ended early on time at the hardware: in `full` and `broken` tick 896, the first a few seconds after going on without saving that starts right after the pad alarm, ended 56 ticks early, the alarm handler's probes of the card slots ([the engine notes](engine.md#audio)), which the scene's probe had made up for with fitted reads until then; in `nocard` tick 444 ended 8 ticks early, an audio DMA block ending just as the retrace handler did, whose frame ran after the wake's first reads instead of at once. In `removed` the first probe after the pull read the time base too until 2026-10-23: ssbm-rs's status register kept the card's EXT bit from the last write that carried it until that probe cleared the insertion interrupt, where a console's line reads no card, and melee-sim mirrored it (`CardDevice::ext_held`). ssbm-rs's `87882a8` fixed its register; `removed` was traced again with its main (`4a1fe33`; the old trace is kept as `removed-ext-held.trc`), whose ticks 211 and 212 end 16 ticks sooner, and the mirror is gone: melee-sim's probe finds the slot empty from the pull on. With the mirror put back, `removed` differs in tick 211 (`timeline: the tick ended at time base 202712745 on ours, 202712729 on the trace's`). A seventh session pulls the card in the middle of the boot's load (`pulled`, `CARD_REMOVE_STEP=5,300`): the native save can't yet fail a list at a step, so it isn't compared.

The retrace, from ssbm-rs's checkout, with the recording's pull:

```text
MODES=1 CARD_STEPS=1 CARD_REMOVE=300,450 STATE_TRACE=local/traces/card-sessions/removed.trc STATE_TRACE_MEMORY=1 \
  target/alt/release/ssbm-run-rf.exe "$SSBM_DISC" --inputs local/lockstep/card-sessions/removed.inputs --port all
```

**Version 2 sessions (2026-10-23).** Recorded with ssbm-rs's main (`4a1fe33`) from power-on on a copy of `2026-10-06_17-44-10`'s card, at a drive's pace with sound, the console's clock at 2025-01-01 00:00:00 (`POWER_ON=789004800`) and a component cable (`DTV=1`), into `$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/lockstep/v2-sessions`, and traced into `traces/v2-sessions` as the card sessions are:

```text
DISC_RATE=3000000 POWER_ON=789004800 DTV=1 SRAM_FLAGS=0x28 MODES=1 MONKEY_HOLD=none@0-399,start@400-403,none@404-1200 \
  target/alt/release/ssbm-run-rf.exe "$SSBM_DISC" --monkey 1 --fields 1200 --card CARD --record local/lockstep/v2-sessions/clock.inputs
```

| Session | Setup | What it does | melee-oracle session |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `clock` | SRAM flags 0x28 (mono, no progressive scan), nothing held at power-on | the memory card scene (the boot's report reads 2025-01-01), the opening movie skipped with Start, the title, its attract loop | clean to the attract match (898 ticks, `--heap`; until task 5, to the opening movie) |
| `clock-late` | `clock`'s recording with its clock 300,007 ticks later (written into the header, not a whole second, a tenth or an alarm period), traced again | as `clock`, the pad alarm's grid moved before tick 0 | clean to the attract match (898 ticks, `--heap`) |
| `progressive` | the default SRAM, B held at power-on (fields 0 to 80) | the progressive scan prompt (mode 0x27) from tick 0, answered, then the memory card scene, the movie and the title | the boot's launch buttons (0x0200) and first game mode (0x27) as the trace's; clean to the trace's end (1,426 ticks, `--heap`; until 2026-10-23, through the prompt) |

`--mutate power-on` makes `clock` differ in tick 0 (`HSD_RandSeedPtr`, the seed from `OSGetTick`); by hand, the pad alarm's grid left on the timeline's multiples makes `clock-late` differ in tick 0 (the pad library's queue), stereo whatever the SRAM says makes `clock` differ in tick 0 (`HSD_Synth_804D7754`), and the DTV status dropped makes `progressive`'s boot start the boot's mode (`the first game mode: ours 0x28, theirs 0x27`). A version 1 session compares as before: every card session and all thirteen recorded sessions give the results above. A third copy, `clock-odd` (123,457 ticks later), runs clean to the attract match too (898 ticks, `--heap`): its pad alarm comes due at the memory card load's first step after the mount, which moves the steps so that an audio frame comes due inside a block's read. ssbm-rs runs a read as a chain of segments, each issued from the last one's transfer-complete interrupt with interrupts off, so the frame due at 60,637,177 runs at the next segment's boundary, 60,637,292, its handler ahead of the chain's next ([saves](saves.md#the-native-save-on-the-timeline), from ssbm-rs's probe of the load, `$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/research/card-chain`). Until 2026-10-23 melee-sim took the block as fitted register reads with interrupts on and ran the frame at once, and tick 0 ended 112 early (`60872695 on ours, 60872807 on the trace's`). By hand, a read's events held to its end makes `clock-odd`'s tick 0 end at 60,887,476, the card's interrupt taken ahead of the audio one at 60,885,492, and the mount's font encoding read dropped makes `nofile`'s tick 0 end 8 early.

**Menu sessions (2026-10-24).** Five sessions recorded for milestone 5's task 6 with ssbm-rs's main (`2f86fda`'s `ssbm-run-rf`) from power-on on a copy of `2026-10-06_17-44-10`'s card, at a drive's pace with sound, every field scripted (`MONKEY_HOLD` with `none` between the presses, so the monkey presses nothing else), into `$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/lockstep/menu-sessions`, and traced into `traces/menu-sessions` with `MODES=1`:

```text
DISC_RATE=3000000 MODES=1 MONKEY_HOLD=none@0-399,start@400-402,none@403-519,start@520-522,... \
  target/alt/release/ssbm-run-rf.exe "$SSBM_DISC" --monkey 1 --fields 5800 --card CARD --record local/lockstep/menu-sessions/menu-tour.inputs
```

| Session | Script | What it does | melee-oracle session |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `menu-tour` | Start at 400 and 520, then a press every 40 fields, 5,800 fields | the main menu from tick 381: the main list up and down, the C stick turning the camera, Data and its Records list, Options, Trophies, VS and its Special Melee list, 1P with Stadium and Regular Match, each entered and left with B; L, R and Start in a list restarting the menu on the main list; B to the title, left to its attract match | clean through the whole tour (`--heap`, timeline included), B's return to the title (tick 3940), the title and its attract loop to the attract match's first tick, 4561, which differs in the heap's leftovers (below); until 2026-10-24 tick 383, the save's first write's, ended 2,191 early, and tick 3940's bank move left an ARAM request's `next` link out (below) |
| `menu-idle` | Start at 400 and 520, B at 9,000, 9,300 fields | the main menu idle for 8,400 ticks (it has no timeout), then B back to the title | clean to the trace's end (9,148 ticks, `--heap`, timeline included): the 8,400 idle ticks, B's return to the title at tick 8794 (the same bank move as `menu-tour`'s), the title |
| `attract-a` | Start at 400, A at 1,600, Start at 1,800, B at 2,100, 2,400 fields | the title left to its attract match, A in the match back to the title, Start to the main menu, B back to the title | clean to the attract match's first tick, 898, which differs in the heap's leftovers (below) |
| `attract` | Start at 400, then nothing, 30,000 fields | the attract loop from the title: the match, then the title again | as `attract-a` |
| `gallery` | Start at 400 and 520, the Data list, its movies, A on the Special Movie, B, down, A on How to Play, Start, B back to the title, 3,100 fields | the Data menu's movies (`mngallery`), each played in the menu | clean through the movies' list and the Special Movie to tick 1484, where its movie's audio has run a frame further on the trace's side |

What stops them, each left as found:

- **The save's writes.** The main menu's entry starts the save's list on the game's waits ([saves](saves.md#the-save-written-from-the-menus)). Reads and writes run as the CARD library's interrupt chains, a write's every erase and page from ssbm-rs's probe of three sessions' saves (`$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/research/card-chain/write-*.log`). Until 2026-10-24 a sector written was fitted (`SAVE_WRITE_WAITS` 3, `SAVE_WRITE_WORK` 0), which made eight of the thirteen recorded sessions compare clean through the main menu; the menu sessions' tick 383 wanted 323 register reads on a write's third wait, which ended `2026-10-06_17-44-10`'s tick 274 early instead.
- **The attract match's heap.** The attract match's scene sets up its fighters and stage in blocks of the scene's heap that the menus' scenes used before, and the fields its setup doesn't write hold their bytes (the fighters' matrices and scripts' return stacks, the grounds' color overlays): 7,465 places in `idle-title`'s tick 798. melee-sim's heap holds no bytes, so a session's match from power-on needs the heap's contents modeled (task 7's match from the menus); a replay's match starts from a fresh heap or an imported one. The attract match's results after it (`fn_8016C4F4`, `plbonus`) are task 9's.
- **Leaving the menu.** Going back to the title makes room for the title's sound banks by moving bank 2's groups in ARAM (`fn_800267B0`, `HSD_SynthSFXBankDeflag`), which the sync now waits for on the timeline (`Wait::BanksMoved`). Each of a group's chunks after the first is posted from the ARAM interrupt's callback of the chunk before (its relay buffer's transfer on to ARAM, `HSD_DevComStdCallback`), and `__ARQInterruptServiceRoutine` keeps that transfer under way until its callback returns, so the chunk's first request (`devComARQR[0][1]`) queues behind it and the second is linked behind the first (`next`), as the trace has it. Until 2026-10-24 melee-sim took the transfer off the queue before its callback, the chunk's first request ran at once and the link was missing (`menu-tour`'s tick 3940, `menu-idle`'s 8794); the menu sessions' leaving the menu now compares clean ([the engine notes](engine.md#audio), the bank deflag's sync). Taken off before the callback again, `menu-tour` differs in tick 3940 (`devComARQR[0][1]`).

**Comparing the menus.** The comparer takes the main menu's statics and user data (`melee_capture::mainmenu`: the flow, the lists, the panel, the cursors, the light, the movies' list and cursors), the mode's (`gmmenumode`'s enter and exit data) and the attract loop's (`vs_enter_data`, the camera's step). Data in an archive a later scene freed compares by the archive's last place (`PastAddrs`' gone archives: the title's data the menus still point into, a particle bank set up from a freed effect file), and the scan takes a file the run loaded by name as its own wherever it lies, even where it found the same file before the run named it (the title's preload of the attract match's files). One field is left out with its proof: the panel's `x2` byte (`MainMenuPanelData`, 4 bytes from `HSD_MemAlloc`), which `mn_80229DC0` never writes and nothing reads, so it holds what the heap block held (the trace's 222 where melee-sim has 0).

**Recorded sessions through the main menu.** With the save's writer, `2026-10-06_17-44-10`, `12-42-06`, `12-04-48`, `16-32-11`, `17-11-18`, `17-29-05`, `2026-10-10_12-54-40` and `13-05-00` compare clean from power-on through the main menu (every list they visit, the save written on its waits, the card indicator) to their next unported mode (`game mode 0x2's loading`, the character select; 0x1b, Tournament; 0x4; 0xa), timeline and `--heap` included, and since the writes' chains (2026-10-24) so do `16-58-24` (to its Event list, `mnEvent_8024E838`, at tick 336), `17-13-21` (to its Data menu's snapshots, `mnSnap_80257F24`, at tick 930), `17-25-31` (0x1b at 508) and `2026-10-07_23-52-34` (0x2 at 131). Until then those four stopped on the fitted writes' timing (ticks 152, an audio frame on the wrong side of the tick's end, 559, 301 and 64). `17-37-52` does too, to Tournament's loading (0x1b) at tick 289: its whole save runs inside tick 211, the main menu's second, and the pad alarm fires in the list's last step's wait point, before the unmount, so its card probes find the channel still attached (24 ticks; ssbm-rs's probe, `research/card-chain/write-17-37.log`). Until 2026-10-24 the writer detached the channel as it handed out that step, and tick 211 ended 32 ticks late (321,985,261 on ours, 321,985,229 on the trace's), with the fitted writes as with the chains.

**The first stops toward the title screen** were these, in `main`'s order (`OSInit`, `VIInit`, `DVDInit`, `PADInit`, `CARDInit` and `OSInitAlarm` are the platform's); item 1 is the platform's video and the allocator model's layouts (`hsd::heap`), items 2 to 6 and the memory card scene are ported or replaced (milestone 5's task 4), and the next stop is the opening movie's scene (`gm_Scene_Opening_OnEnter`):

1. `HSD_SetInitParameter` (four calls), `db_SetupCrashHandler`, `HSD_AllocateXFB`, `HSD_AllocateFifo` (and melee-render's `GXInit`), `HSD_GXSetFifoObj`, `HSD_InitComponent` (`HSD_OSInit`'s heaps and HSD's own setup), `GXSetMisc` (melee-render's).
2. The random seed from `OSGetTick` (the time base on the boot's timeline, an input of the timeline's).
3. `lbAudioAx_8002838C` and `lb_80019AAC` with `gmMain_8015FD24`: ported (`game::boot`, `lb::pacing`, `input::controller`), on the timeline from the audio setup's start.
4. `HSD_VISetUserPostRetraceCallback`, `HSD_VISetUserGXDrawDoneCallback`, `HSD_VISetBlack`; `lbMemory_8001564C`, `lbHeap_80015F3C`, `lbDvd_80018F68`, `lbArq_80014D2C`, `lbCardNew_Init`, `lbCardGame_Init`, `lbSnap_8001E290`, `gmMainLib_8015FCC0`, `lbMthp_8001F87C`; `HSD_SisLib_803A6048` (ported); `gmMainLib_8015FBA4` (the save data's reset; its `lbAudioAx_80028690` is ported).
5. The boot's report (`db_InitScreenshot`, `lbLang_GetLanguageSetting`, `lbLang_GetSavedLanguage`, `lbMemory_800154BC`, `lbTime_GetTimeInSeconds`, the clock's, `gm_801692E8`), `db_EnableItemSpawns`, `db_ClearFPUExceptions`.
6. The modes' loop, `gm_801A4510`: `gm_GetAllGameModes` and every mode's `on_init`, `VIGetDTVStatus` and the launch buttons' B for progressive scan, then `runGameMode` (`lbDvd_80018F58`) and `gm_801A4014`: `preloadState` (`lbDvd_80018CF4`, `lbDvd_GetPreloadCacheScene`, `lbHeap_80015BB8`, `lbDvd_80018254`, `lbCardNew_ForgetMemory`, `lbCardGame_Reset`, `lbSnap_8001E27C`, `Toy_803127D4`, `tyDisplay_8031C8B8`), the boot mode's `bootOnLoad`, `gm_FindGameSceneHandler`, `gm_801A4BD4` (`vs_match::init_scene` has it, without an alias), `gm_801A4B88`, and the scene loop `gm_801A4D34` (`gm::scene` has its body).
7. The memory card scene (`gm_Scene_MemCard_OnEnter`, `gm_Scene_MemCard_OnFrame`, trace ticks 0 and 1), the opening movie (`gm_Mode_Opening_OnLoad`, `gm_Scene_Opening_OnFrame` and `lbMthp`'s player, ticks 2 to 203), then the title (`gm_Scene_Title_OnEnter`, `gm_Scene_Title_OnFrame`, from tick 204).

**Known limits.**

- The alignment follows only the 60 Hz pad alarm on its grid, and needs memory and the time base at every tick (`STATE_TRACE_MEMORY=1`): a trace whose alarm changes period or leaves its grid is refused.
- Where the controllers sit still the alignment may leave a tick several reads, and `--from` refuses such a tick. A recording changed only within such a stretch is refused only where its change shows in a queued poll.
- The boot's `VIWaitForRetrace` before its read isn't timed: the timeline starts at the audio setup (`audio::boot`).
- The memory card's image is taken but not read: card tasks and their timing are milestone 5's.
- `--from` carries what the importer models, which was built for matches: a menu scene's own state isn't imported, nor the heap leftovers new item links take (their blocks come from the object heap one at a time, between the GObjs' of the links themselves, so where each lies is the allocator's to decide as the run goes, not the import's to guess). Nor does it know whether a tick was the last of its round: it takes up a state as if the round ends with it.

## Rules

- Floats compare by bit pattern. One ULP is a divergence.
- External events (a disc read completing, a camera snapshot) are inputs, recorded and replayed like controllers, never compared as state.
- A known difference must be explained and listed per replay, as ssbm-rs's known-divergence files are; a blanket exception is never accepted.

## Running the checks

The checks read three things that aren't in the repository, each named by an environment variable with no default. A check whose data isn't there skips with a message naming the variable or path (`cargo test -- --nocapture` shows it), so without them `cargo test` runs only the checks that need nothing; the `--ignored` frame and mix checks fail instead, since they are run on purpose.
