| Variable | What | Read by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `SSBM_DISC` | The NTSC 1.02 disc image (GALE01, revision 2) | Every disc-backed check, `melee-oracle`, `melee-bench`, the player |
| `SSBM_RS_LOCAL` | ssbm-rs's `local/` folder, where its runner writes captures (`calls/`), state traces (`traces/`), references (`player/`) and the benchmark matches' start traces (`bench/NAME.start.trace`) | leaf-check's captured-state checks, `melee-oracle`'s `diff`, `sync` and `vanilla` tests, the frame and mix checks (`REFS` overrides), `melee-bench native` |
| `SLIPPI_REPLAYS` | A folder of Slippi replays, a folder a month as Slippi Launcher keeps them (the author's, not public) | leaf-check's `replay`, `slippi` and `audio` crowd checks, `melee-oracle`'s `diff` test, the Fox vs Sheik frames |

Each test's own variable (`FOX_CALLS`, `MATCH_CALLS`, `TRACE`, `REPLAY`, `BENCH_DIR` and the others its header names) still overrides its default. The benchmark matches' replays are in the repository, in `data/bench` ([performance](performance.md)). The data itself is made with ssbm-rs's runner (its `main` branch has every recorder these pages name) as each check's header and this page say.

The checks run before each change lands:

```sh
export SSBM_DISC=/path/to/GALE01.iso SSBM_RS_LOCAL=/path/to/ssbm-rs/local SLIPPI_REPLAYS=/path/to/Slippi
cargo fmt --all --check
cargo clippy --release --all-targets
cargo run --release -p ledger -- --check
for m in fox-fox-fd fox-marth-fd fox-marth-bf fox-marth-ps ganon-captain-fd; do
  target/release/melee-oracle diff "$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/traces/v5-mixed/$m.trc" data/bench/$m.slp --fp hardware
done
target/release/melee-oracle diff "$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/traces/v5-mixed/Game_20260129T170722.trc" \
  "$SLIPPI_REPLAYS/2026-01/Game_20260129T170722.slp" --fp slippi   # and the other Slippi replays above
target/release/melee-oracle sync-test data/bench/fox-marth-ps.slp --fp hardware --rollback 7   # for changes that touch the state's shape
# A replay whose golden is current: simulate and hash only, the full diff on a miss (Golden hashes, below)
target/release/melee-oracle diff TRACE REPLAY.slp --fp hardware --heap --golden-check ||
  target/release/melee-oracle diff TRACE REPLAY.slp --fp hardware --heap --write-golden
cargo test --release -p melee-sim -p melee-oracle -p melee-render -p melee-gpu -p melee-audio -p melee-input -p melee-player
cargo test --release -p leaf-check
cargo test --release -p melee-render-check -p melee-audio-check -- --ignored
```

`cargo test` also runs `path-guard`, which fails on personal paths or names, build output (`dist/`, `target/`) or files over 5 MiB among the tracked files, by the rules the git hooks share (`.githooks/path-guard.conf`, see [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md#git-hooks)); CI runs it first, on its own.

Run filtered leaf-check tests (`--test NAME`) in a command of their own: with several packages, cargo applies a `--test` filter to all of them and skips the rest.

### Capture packs

ssbm-rs's runner saves each call as a file of its own (`NAME-callN.call`, a whole memory snapshot compressed), but the calls of one run share nearly all their memory. `pack-calls` puts a directory's calls into one `calls.pack` beside them, holding each distinct 4 KiB page once (compressed 64 pages to a block) with each call's name, registers and pages, and keeps the pack only when every call reads back from it byte for byte. ssbm-rs's `local/calls`, 21,042 calls in 168 directories, went from 114.4 GiB to 3.5 GiB this way.

```sh
cargo run --release -p melee-capture --bin pack-calls -- -r "$SSBM_RS_LOCAL/calls"   # every directory under it
cargo run --release -p melee-capture --bin pack-calls -- --verify DIR               # check a pack against the files left
cargo run --release -p melee-capture --bin pack-calls -- --unpack DIR               # the pack's calls back out as .call files
```

`Capture::read` takes a call from its directory's pack when the pack holds it and from its file otherwise, and the checks list a directory's calls from both, so a call's path is `DIR/NAME.call` either way and new captures can stay files until the directory is packed again: packing a directory that has a pack keeps the pack's calls whose files are gone and adds the new files. Once a directory's pack is verified, its `.call` files can go: `--unpack` writes them back as ssbm-rs's runner wrote them (zstd level 3), and on Marth's `fox-marth-fd` set the rebuilt files came out byte-identical to the originals, compressed bytes included, and packed again into the same pack. Each block is decompressed once per process and shared, so reading a call is copying its pages.

### leaf-check's speed

A captured-state check spends almost all its time around the call, not in it: running the function on both sides took about 12 ms of a call's 355 ms in `ftcommon_entries`. The rest was reading the call (58 ms of zstd), finding the loaded archives in memory and importing the state (100 ms), comparing the import with memory (79 ms), and after the run finding the archives again and comparing the whole state (107 ms), each again for every float mode and every case run from the same call. Looking symbols up by name in a 35,000-entry list inside the comparison's loops took most of the comparing. Now symbols come from a map made once, the archive scan skips places no disc file starts at, calls come from packs, and a test that runs several checks from the same calls (`ftcommon_entries`' cases, the characters' stand-ins answering no and yes and their neighbours' rounds) runs them as variants of one pass (`calls::run_variants`): each call is read once and imported, its round trip checked, once for every check and mode that prepares it into the same state, bit for bit. The checks, the calls and the comparisons are the same: each binary's output, every count in it, came out identical before and after.

Provisional timings, until a run on an idle machine replaces them: each binary was timed before (`e88b421`, reading `.call` files) and after (the same tests with these changes, before main's new characters were merged, reading the packs) back to back, alternating which ran first, with `--test-threads=4` and the default `LEAF_CHECK_WORKERS=2`, on the machine in [performance](performance.md) while other builds kept it 20 to 90% busy (2026-10-14). The load varied within pairs, so the CPU times are the steadier comparison:

**Sets at once.** The character checks (and `common_stops`) each hold one test, since its assertions need every set the character has: each function compared clean from some state, and none of the blocked list does. Their hundreds of sets of saved calls (a capture set's calls of one function, with what each call runs) ran one after another, a few calls each on the two workers, so such a test kept about one core busy; `nana`'s capture sets, `ftcommon_entries`' processes, `effects`' sweeps and `items`' kind lists ran the same way. `leaf_check::each_set` runs a test's sets `LEAF_CHECK_SETS` at a time (4 by default), the largest first, and hands back their reports in the sets' order, so the tallies, the lists and the first failure are the ones running them in order gives; only the order of each set's summary lines changes, as sets finish. `items`, `camera`, `vs`, `heap`, `hud` and `sis`, which let three checks hold captured states at once, now let `LEAF_CHECK_SETS` do. And each worker's reference no longer reads the disc's executable and checks its hash again (19 of its 32 ms): the process reads it once. Each binary's test list, and every line of its output that counts (compared, skipped, blocked, the tests' results), sorted, came out the same as before, all 76 binaries passing.

Each binary was timed before (`bb67b7e7`, two binaries running at a time) and after (one at a time) with `--test-threads=4` and the default workers and sets, on the machine in [performance](performance.md) while other work kept it fully busy (2026-10-15), so the times are rough. The CPU time grew a fifth: more threads ran at once on a machine whose eight cores were already running two threads each.

Run the suite as `cargo test --release -p leaf-check -- --test-threads=4`: cargo runs the binaries one at a time, each with up to four tests at once, each test running its saved calls on `LEAF_CHECK_WORKERS` threads (2 by default, each with its own reference) and up to `LEAF_CHECK_SETS` sets at once. No binary depends on another, so they can also run side by side: `cargo test --release -p leaf-check --no-run` builds them, and each runs on its own from `tools/leaf-check`, as cargo runs it; the longest then bound the whole (`items`, whose tests each run one check at a time, goes faster with `--test-threads=8` and `LEAF_CHECK_SETS=8`). Memory, not cores, bounds it: most binaries peak under 4 GB of commit, `nana` and `ftcommon_entries` near 6 GB, `audio` near 9 GB (its crowd checks hold whole matches' audio state), and a pack's decompressed pages stay for the process (a few hundred MB per capture set).

### leaf-check's shards

A few binaries run far longer than the rest (`cpu_vs` 85 minutes, `items` 36, `nana` 34 in the verification of b3a5321b), so they bound the whole run however many run beside them. `LEAF_CHECK_SHARD=K/N` runs the K-th of N shards of a binary (`leaf_check::shard`), each its own process, so the shards run side by side; the N shards together run exactly what the binary runs unsharded, each part once. Unset, a binary runs whole.

A binary is cut into units, each a check with its own assertions, so no shard needs another's results: a whole test (`leaf_check::shard_test!()` at its start: `items`' 194 tests), or a test's own parts (`leaf_check::shard::units`: `cpu_vs_changed`'s 36 changes and `nana_tumble_and_shields`' 9, each asserting that it compared clean in some frame; `nana_cpu_frame`'s three variants; `cpu_vs_frame` whole). A test's unit `i` runs in shard `(hash(test) + i) mod N` (FNV-1a of the test's name): fixed by names, not by a list to keep up, so a new test or change lands in some shard, and a test's units spread from a shard its name picks. Each shard prints which units it runs. A new `items` test needs `shard_test!()` too, or every shard runs it.

`tools/leaf-check/shards.txt` lists the binaries the verification splits and into how many shards, each over about 20 minutes on its own: `cpu_vs` 6, `items` 3, `nana` 3. `leaf-select plan` lists every job with its environment ([selecting leaf-check jobs](#selecting-leaf-check-jobs)). From `tools/leaf-check`, as cargo runs a test binary:

```sh
for k in 1 2 3 4 5 6; do LEAF_CHECK_SHARD=$k/6 target/release/deps/cpu_vs-HASH --test-threads=4; done   # each its own job
for k in 1 2 3; do LEAF_CHECK_SHARD=$k/3 LEAF_CHECK_SETS=8 target/release/deps/items-HASH --test-threads=8; done
for k in 1 2 3; do LEAF_CHECK_SHARD=$k/3 target/release/deps/nana-HASH --test-threads=4; done
```

On `items`' timed tests the hash splits its 4.0 hours of test time 4,759 / 4,840 / 5,064 seconds over three shards, each bound below by its longest test (14 minutes, `effect_router`); each of `cpu_vs`' six shards runs six changes, its fourth `cpu_vs_frame` (4.5 minutes) too.

**The shards check what the binary checks** (2026-10-21, at b45accfe). Each binary ran whole and as its shards with `--nocapture`, three processes at a time on a machine other work kept busy. Every line of their checks' output that counts (each set's `N of M calls compared clean, K skipped` and its skipped reasons, each change's and variant's tally, `items`' `N captures agree` and their blocked lists, the round trips), sorted, came out the same whole and over the shards, but for one thread id in an unported function's panic message that `items`' `groundcoll` catches; every process passed.

| Binary | Counted lines | Whole | Over the shards | Whole (wall) | Shards (wall each) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| cpu_vs | 1,675 | 1,332 set lines, 278,406 calls compared clean; 36 change tallies summing 131,996 | the same | 152 min | 34, 32, 27, 36, 25, 32 min |
| items | 1,320 | 1,303 checks, 14,207 captures agree | the same | 48 min | 17, 12, 9 min |
| nana | 288 | 264 set lines, 91,728 calls compared clean; 12 change and variant tallies summing 35,534 | the same | 46 min | 22, 18, 17 min |

### Selecting leaf-check jobs

Every leaf-check binary links melee-sim, so keying a job on its binary reruns all of them on any change to melee-sim, though most change nothing a given binary runs. `leaf-select` (`tools/leaf-select`) keys each job (a binary, or a [shard](#leaf-checks-shards) of one) on the source of what it runs instead, from a coverage run, so a change that touches none of it keeps the job's last pass. Sarah, 2026-10-21: "i figure we can play this more loosely and just do a full audit periodically"; "weekly full runs sound good".

**The reach.** `leaf-select run JOB` runs a job from leaf-check's tests built with the workspace's crates instrumented (`leaf-select build`: `-C instrument-coverage` through cargo's workspace wrapper, into `target/coverage`), exactly as the verification runs it, and, if it passed, records its reach (`local/leaf-reach/JOB.reach` by default) from `llvm-cov`'s line counts and the data it read:

- what ran: each unit holding an executed line. A unit is an item cut out with `syn`: a function (each method on its own; a closure's lines are its function's), an impl's or trait's shell (header, associated constants and types), a struct, enum, const, static, macro definition or invocation, use. A generic function's lines count in every instantiation, inlined ones too, so an edit to a generic is an edit to what ran. A line in no unit takes in its whole file;
- what that names, transitively, by identifier: the structs, enums, consts, statics, type aliases, traits and macros of those names, the uses that import them in their files, any `const fn` of those names (it may run at compile time, where coverage can't see it), the shells of the impls of methods that ran;
- the signature of every function of a name it mentions that didn't run, so a function that appears, goes or changes its signature where it could take over a call (method resolution, shadowing) changes the reach;
- each unit's hash: its tokens, comments and doc attributes left out (neither changes the program);
- the harness, the data it read (every path under `SSBM_RS_LOCAL` or `SLIPPI_REPLAYS` it asked `melee-local-data` for, which logs them to `MELEE_LOCAL_DATA_LOG`), and when.

**The fingerprint.** `leaf-select fingerprint` prints `JOB<TAB>FINGERPRINT` for every job (`--explain` says why on stderr). It always takes in the job's harness: the compiler's version, `Cargo.lock` and the manifests, leaf-check's library, the test's own file and the `#[path]` modules it takes in, the crates keyed whole, the disc image's size and time; and the data its reach names, every file's size and time. Then, if the reach holds (the harness is the one it was recorded with, every unit that ran hashes as recorded, and what they name and the signatures, worked out again from today's source, come out the same), the reach itself: nothing the job runs or names has changed, so the key is the one its last pass had. Otherwise every keyed crate's source, byte for byte, so the job reruns on any change until a coverage run records its reach again.

**What's skipped, and why it's safe.** A job is kept only while none of the code it ran has changed, nor anything that code names, nor its test, leaf-check, the toolchain or its data. Code it didn't run can only start running through a change to code it ran or to its inputs, and either changes the key. The rules fail toward rerunning:

- no reach, or one for another harness: the whole source keys the job, and the data it reads is unknown, as when jobs were keyed on their binaries;
- one changed unit and the reach stops holding until it's recorded again: from then on any change reruns the job, not just changes to what it ran (a changed function may now call one the reach never saw). Put back, the key is the old one again, and its pass stands;
- a unit that's gone, renamed or added under a name the reach mentions: the reach doesn't hold;
- lines that ran in a file of the repository the tool doesn't key, or a source newer than the binary the coverage ran: the reach is recorded unsound and never holds;
- only a passing run records a reach (a failing one may have stopped early);
- whole crates: proc macros (`melee-derive`) and crates with a build script, whose code runs while building where coverage can't see it, and by choice `melee-capture`, `melee-data`, `melee-gekko-fp` and `melee-local-data` (`crates::WHOLE`): every job runs most of them (the importer and comparer, the archives, the float operations), and their hot loops ran about five times slower with counters in them, so they aren't instrumented, and any change to them reruns everything. melee-sim and melee-slippi are keyed by unit; their counters cost nothing measurable (`marth` single-threaded 78 s plain, 74 s instrumented).

Not tracked: the tests' own path variables (`FOX_CALLS` and the others) when set (the verification doesn't set them), and data in the repository (`data/bench`, `docs/port-ledger.csv`), which the verification keys on separately (its runtime data key).

**Shown** (2026-10-21, reaches of `fox`, `marth`, `stage`, `math` and `lbvector`; each edit made, fingerprinted, reverted):

| Edit | Changed | Kept |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A Brinstar function none runs (`gr::zebes::init_stage`) | | all five |
| Inside a function `fox` runs (`fox_appeal_count`) | fox | the other four |
| A comment in it | | all five |
| A shared engine function, generic over its host (`hsd::gobj::free<K, H>`) | fox, marth, stage | math, lbvector |
| A generic `#[inline]` function (`Memo<K, V, BITS>::get`) | fox, marth, stage, math | lbvector |
| A const only `math.rs`' functions name (`TWO_OVER_PI`) | fox, marth, stage, math | lbvector |
| `fox`'s test file | fox | the other four |
| With `fox_appeal_count` changed, also the Brinstar function | fox's key changes again | |
| Both put back | | fox's key is its old one |
| `fox`'s capture set (`calls/fox`, from a stand-in `SSBM_RS_LOCAL`) | fox (and stage, whose sets the stand-in folder lacked) | math, which reads none |

**Refreshing.** A reach goes stale only toward rerunning, so reaches can be refreshed whenever: the weekly full run runs every job through `leaf-select run` (`leaf-select build` once, then a job per test binary or shard, which checks what the plain run checks and records its reach), and a routine verification can do the same for each job it reruns, so a job is keyed by unit again from its next run on. `run` prints each passing job's new fingerprint (`fingerprint<TAB>JOB<TAB>FINGERPRINT`), the key the next verification looks its pass up by.

```sh
cargo build --release -p leaf-select
target/release/leaf-select plan                      # the jobs: NAME, TEST, ENV
target/release/leaf-select build                     # leaf-check's tests, instrumented (target/coverage)
target/release/leaf-select run --stdout --test-threads 4 fox cpu_vs.2of6   # run and record
target/release/leaf-select cover -j 6                # build, then every job, 6 at once
target/release/leaf-select fingerprint --explain     # JOB<TAB>FINGERPRINT
```
